# Project checklist (0.0.1 completion)

## dbspec schema language

- [o] T8 Replace the Mermaid schema source with dbspec, a neutral schema language that is the source of truth of a schema. Priority: first. Cause: the Mermaid dialect cannot express physical schemas losslessly; field names select behavior; generated names are rewritten; dialect features drop silently; hidden markers restore triggers and `.sql` sources. Acceptance: T8.0–T8.8 complete in Go/PHP/Rust/TypeScript with conformance and MySQL/PostgreSQL/SQLite evidence; work runs in worktree `orm-dbspec-T8` and merges into `main` only after every item passes. Evidence: every T8 item is complete and main holds the dbspec line (T8.8).
- [o] T8.0 Record every schema syntax and semantic difference between MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite and decide neutral support per feature (requires T8). Cause: dbspec supports only features with one common definition, the same meaning on all three databases, and restoration by introspection; that rule needs complete facts. Acceptance: `docs/dialects.md` and its Korean pair cover types, length/precision, null, defaults, identity, generated columns, keys, indexes, FKs, CHECKs, comments, collation, namespaces and identifier rules, time types and time zones, enum, unsigned, triggers, DDL transactions and catalog sources; each row states the syntax and meaning per database, the neutral definition and rendering or the unsupported reason, and the introspection source; each decision is verified by tracked shared `tests/*` cases against the `.runtime` databases. Evidence: `make dialect-facts-check TEST_ENV=<main checkout>/.runtime/servers/env` ran twice with no failure: 239 probes (MySQL 8.4.11: 98, PostgreSQL 17.11: 75, SQLite 3.53.4 through modernc v1.58.0: 66) in 1.705 s and 1.637 s, plus four SQLite file-name cases each time; every probe drops and verifies its own database, schema or file. `docs/dialects.md` and its Korean pair record per feature the syntax and meaning on each database, probe IDs, official sources, the neutral rendering or unsupported reason, and the introspection source, and list the SQLite library of each client (Go, PHP, Node 3.53.4; Rust bundled 3.51.3, not probed). dbspec supports local `datetime(p)` with a UTC connection rule (instants unsupported; `timestamptz` columns are converted, not mapped) and executor update-time stamping in `manifestHash`; the audit context definition is left for T8.1 review. Defects found against the facts are T8.0.1–T8.0.11, T8.0.13 and T8.0.14. `checklist-check`, `docs-rules-check`, `docs-check` and `docs-verify-idempotent` pass.
- [o] T8.0.1 Restore PostgreSQL column import per schema (requires T8.0). Cause: the column query joins `pg_class` by `relname` alone (`internal/ormgen/import.go:589`, `clients/php/src/SchemaImport.php:271`, `clients/rust/orm-build/src/catalog/reader.rs:204`, `clients/typescript/src/tools/introspect.ts:135`), but a table name is unique per schema only (`postgres.namespace.relname_per_schema`), so a relation with the same name in another schema repeats each imported column; the import itself was not executed because PostgreSQL was unavailable. Acceptance: T8.4 joins by `relnamespace`, and a tracked case with a same-named table in a second schema imports one column set in every client. Evidence: the dbspec introspection reads columns, constraints, indexes and triggers only where `relnamespace` is the current schema, so columns were never repeated, but a foreign key read its referenced table by `relname` alone. tests/dbspec/introspect.json gains `postgres-other-schema`: a same-named `orders` table with another column, a check, a unique key and an index in a second schema `{schema}_b`, and a local `lines` table whose foreign key references it; the four harnesses replace `{schema}` with the case's schema and drop the second schema. Before the fix Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust failed it with "document differs", restoring `foreign key fk_lines_order (order_id) references orders (id)`; each client now reads the referenced table only in the current schema and reports the key as unsupported, and `make dbspec-introspect-check`, `dbspec-introspect-php-check` (30 round trips, 8 unsupported cases), `dbspec-introspect-ts-check` (41 tests) and `dbspec-introspect-rust-check` pass with one column set of `orders`. docs/dialects.md states the rule and the placeholder.
- [o] T8.0.2 Report index features that import drops or rejects (requires T8.0). Cause: observed with `ormgen import`: a MySQL functional index and a SQLite expression index abort import with `converting NULL to string is unsupported` (`internal/ormgen/import.go:194-196`, `internal/ormgen/migrate.go:285-287`); a MySQL prefix index and a SQLite partial index are imported as full indexes (`import.go:187-207`, `migrate.go:263-283`); the PostgreSQL reader joins expression keys (`indkey` 0) to `pg_attribute` and reads neither `indpred` nor `indoption` (`import.go:653-691`; not executed). Acceptance: T8.4 reports prefix, partial and expression indexes as unsupported objects with locations, with tracked cases on their databases in every client. Evidence: the dbspec introspection replaces the import path. docs/dialects.md "Keys, indexes and foreign keys" makes prefix, partial, expression and full-text indexes unsupported, and the PostgreSQL index query reads `indpred`, `indexprs` and `indoption` instead of joining expression keys to `pg_attribute`. tests/dbspec/introspect.json asserts them as `[index, table, name]` entries of exact unsupported lists: `mysql-unsupported` the prefix index `ix_name_prefix` and the functional index `ix_name_expr`, `postgres-unsupported` the expression index `ix_lower` and the partial index `ix_partial`, `sqlite-unsupported` the expression index `ix_lower` and the partial index `ix_partial` (MySQL has no partial index, `mysql.index.partial_rejected`); none aborts introspection, and the descending `placed_at desc` key of the ddl.json round trip is restored on each database. `make dbspec-introspect-check`, `dbspec-introspect-php-check`, `dbspec-introspect-ts-check` and `dbspec-introspect-rust-check` pass these cases in Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust.
- [o] T8.0.3 Report generated columns instead of importing plain columns (requires T8.0). Cause: observed: MySQL `b int AS (a+1) STORED` is imported as a plain nullable `int` because `GENERATION_EXPRESSION` is not read (`internal/ormgen/import.go:130-131`); SQLite `pragma_table_info` omits a generated column, so import drops it (`internal/ormgen/migrate.go:225`). Acceptance: T8.4 reports generated columns as unsupported with locations on all three databases in every client. Evidence: the dbspec introspection reads MySQL `GENERATION_EXPRESSION`, PostgreSQL `attgenerated` and SQLite `pragma_table_xinfo.hidden` and reports a generated column as an unsupported `column` (docs/dialects.md "Null, defaults, identity and generated columns"). tests/dbspec/introspect.json now asserts it on all three databases with a type that dbspec would otherwise read: MySQL `next_id bigint AS (char_length(name) + 1) STORED` and `name_size bigint AS (char_length(name)) VIRTUAL` in `mysql-unsupported`, PostgreSQL `next_id bigint GENERATED ALWAYS AS (id + 1) STORED` in `postgres-unsupported`, and SQLite `s INTEGER … STORED` beside the virtual `g` in `sqlite-unsupported`; each is listed as `[column, orders, <name>]` and left out of the document. `make dbspec-introspect-check`, `dbspec-introspect-php-check`, `dbspec-introspect-ts-check` and `dbspec-introspect-rust-check` pass these cases in Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust.
- [o] T8.0.4 Reject names over the identifier limits instead of rendering them (requires T8.0). Cause: observed: a 65-character `%% index` name is rendered unbounded as a MySQL `KEY` and MySQL rejects the DDL (1059) (`internal/ormgen/ddl.go:138`, fulltext `ddl.go:141`); trigger and function names append suffixes to the table name without a bound (`internal/ormgen/triggers.go:61-66`, `101-102`, `165-167`), rendering a 77-character MySQL trigger name and a 70-character PostgreSQL trigger name, which PostgreSQL truncates to 63 bytes (`postgres.ident.truncated_to_63`). Acceptance: T8.3 rejects a declared or generated name over 63 bytes before rendering, with tracked cases in every client. Evidence: docs/dbspec.md "Names" limits every declared name and every name the renderer writes (`<table>$<column>`, `<table>$immutable_update`, `<table>$audit_insert` and the others) to 63 bytes and makes a longer one a `name.length` error, so a set with such a name has no statements. tests/dbspec/cases.json asserts it with names of exactly 64 bytes: `name-too-long` (a 64-byte column name), `generated-check-name-too-long` (a 64-byte renderer CHECK name), `generated-immutable-name-too-long` (a 64-byte `immutable` trigger name) and `generated-audit-name-too-long` (a 64-byte `audit` trigger name), each with its line, column and rule. `make dbspec-go-check`, `php clients/php/tests/dbspec_test.php`, `node --test` of clients/typescript/tests/dbspec.mjs, dbspec-rules.mjs and dbspec-render.mjs (168 tests), `make dbspec-rust-check` and `make dbspec-compare-check` (8 runs agree on 116 cases) pass them in Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust.
- [o] T8.0.5 Record the same audit changes when a foreign key action changes rows (requires T8.0). Cause: MySQL fires no trigger for rows changed by a foreign key action (`mysql.fk.cascade_skips_triggers`) while PostgreSQL and SQLite do (`postgres.fk.cascade_fires_triggers`, `sqlite.fk.cascade_fires_triggers`), so `mode=changes` audit triggers (`internal/ormgen/triggers.go:378-437`) record cascaded changes on PostgreSQL and SQLite only. Acceptance: T8.3 rejects an audit or immutable setting on a child table of a `cascade` or `set_null` foreign key, with a tracked case on all three databases in every client. Evidence: docs/dbspec.md "Settings" and "Audit" reject `immutable` and `audit` on a child of a `cascade` or `set_null` foreign key with a `setting` error at the setting keyword, so no statement of any dialect is rendered for such a table and the cascaded rows that MySQL triggers skip (`mysql.fk.cascade_skips_triggers`, while `postgres.fk.cascade_fires_triggers` and `sqlite.fk.cascade_fires_triggers` fire) never meet a generated trigger. The rejection precedes rendering, so it does not depend on the database. tests/dbspec/cases.json gains `immutable-on-cascade-child` (`on delete cascade`) and `audit-on-set-null-child` (`on update set_null`), each expecting one `setting` error at its line and column; they pass in `make dbspec-go-check`, `php clients/php/tests/dbspec_test.php` (81 invalid cases), `node --test` of clients/typescript/tests/dbspec.mjs, dbspec-rules.mjs and dbspec-render.mjs, `make dbspec-rust-check` and `make dbspec-compare-check`, where the four clients agree on them.
- [o] T8.0.6 Make the immutable guard equal for statements that match no row (requires T8.0). Cause: the PostgreSQL guard is a `FOR EACH STATEMENT` trigger (`internal/ormgen/triggers.go:110`, `clients/php/src/SchemaTriggers.php:101`, `clients/rust/orm-schema/src/triggers.rs:112`, `clients/typescript/src/engine/triggers.ts:81`) that fails an `UPDATE` or `DELETE` matching no row (`postgres.trigger.statement_fires_on_zero_rows`), while the MySQL and SQLite row guards do not fire (`mysql.trigger.zero_rows_not_fired`, `sqlite.trigger.zero_rows_not_fired`); it also covers `TRUNCATE` on PostgreSQL only. Acceptance: T8.3 renders row-level `UPDATE` and `DELETE` guards on all three databases, and a tracked zero-row case passes on all three in every client. Evidence: docs/dialects.md "Triggers" renders `immutable` and the `audit` delete rejection as `BEFORE UPDATE` and `BEFORE DELETE` `FOR EACH ROW` triggers on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite, and no `TRUNCATE` trigger on any of them, as the statements of the `immutable-and-audit` vector of tests/dbspec/ddl.json show. That vector gains zero-row steps: `UPDATE ledger SET amount = 7 WHERE id = 999` and `DELETE FROM ledger WHERE id = 999` on the immutable table and `DELETE FROM service WHERE id = 999` on the audited table must succeed, after the matching `UPDATE` and `DELETE` failed (MySQL 1644, PostgreSQL P0001, SQLite "table ledger is immutable"). `make dbspec-ddl-check` runs the 13 steps on MySQL 8.4, PostgreSQL 17 and SQLite and passes; the four clients render these statements byte for byte (`make dbspec-go-check`, `php clients/php/tests/dbspec_render_test.php`, clients/typescript/tests/dbspec-render.mjs, `make dbspec-rust-check`, and `make dbspec-compare-check`, where they agree).
- [o] T8.0.7 Keep the automatic key valid after an explicit key on PostgreSQL (requires T8.0). Cause: `GENERATED BY DEFAULT AS IDENTITY` (`internal/ormgen/ddl.go:539`, `clients/php/src/SchemaDdl.php:542`, `clients/rust/orm-schema/src/ddl.rs:230`, `clients/typescript/src/engine/ddl.ts:163`) does not advance past an explicit key, so the next generated key fails with 23505 (`postgres.identity.by_default_not_advanced`), while MySQL and SQLite continue (`mysql.auto_increment.explicit_value_advances`, `sqlite.autoincrement.explicit_value_advances`). Acceptance: clients reject an explicit automatic key and PostgreSQL apply steps that write explicit keys reset the identity sequence, with tracked cases on all three databases in every client. Evidence: dbspec renders PostgreSQL `GENERATED BY DEFAULT AS IDENTITY` (docs/dialects.md "Automatic key"), and every client already rejects an explicit automatic key before any statement runs: the request validators reject a primary key or identity column in `on_duplicate` ("on_duplicate cannot assign"), and the planners reject an identity column in an insert ("cannot set identity column") and a primary key or identity column in an update ("cannot update"), so no client writes a key past the PostgreSQL sequence. No test asserted the insert and update rules. New tracked cases run the four requests on the mysql, postgres and sqlite dialects and require `IR_INVALID` with the message: Go `TestKeyColumnsAreNotWritten` (engine/query_forms_test.go), PHP clients/php/tests/engine_test.php, TypeScript clients/typescript/tests/engine.mjs and Rust `engine::tests::key_columns_are_not_written`; all pass, and the PHP and TypeScript cases fail on all three dialects when the expected message is changed. No plan statement writes explicit keys on PostgreSQL (docs/plans.md copies rows only in the SQLite rebuild), so there is no PostgreSQL apply step to reset; the SQLite rebuild resets its own counter, recorded as T8.5.1.1. docs/dsl.md states the write rule.
- [o] T8.0.8 Render a binary collation for MySQL text columns (requires T8.0). Cause: DDL ends each MySQL table with `DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4` (`internal/ormgen/ddl.go:146`, `clients/php/src/SchemaDdl.php:134`, `clients/rust/orm-schema/src/ddl.rs:564`, `clients/typescript/src/engine/ddl.ts:360`), which selects `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` (`mysql.collation.default_accent_case_insensitive`), so MySQL unique keys and comparisons treat `a`, `A` and `á` as equal while PostgreSQL `C` and SQLite `BINARY` do not. Acceptance: T8.3 renders `utf8mb4_0900_bin` and PostgreSQL `COLLATE "C"`, with a tracked unique-key case on all three databases in every client. Evidence: docs/dialects.md "Inside `CREATE TABLE`" renders MySQL `varchar(n)` and `text` with `CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE utf8mb4_0900_bin` and the table with `COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_bin`, PostgreSQL text columns with `COLLATE "C"`, and SQLite text with its `BINARY` default, as the statements of tests/dbspec/ddl.json show. The `keys-indexes-foreign-keys` vector gains a unique-key case on `orders.code`: `a`, `A`, `á` and `a ` (trailing space) are inserted as four rows, a second `a` fails (MySQL 1062, PostgreSQL 23505, SQLite UNIQUE), `code = 'a'` matches one row, and `A` sorts before `a` and `á`; under the former `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` the `A` insert would fail (`mysql.collation.default_accent_case_insensitive`) and under a PAD SPACE collation `a ` would (`mysql.collation.utf8mb4_bin_pads`). `make dbspec-ddl-check` runs the 15 steps on MySQL 8.4, PostgreSQL 17 and SQLite and passes; the four clients render these statements byte for byte (`go test ./engine/dbspec -run TestRenderVectors`, `php clients/php/tests/dbspec_render_test.php`, clients/typescript/tests/dbspec-render.mjs, `make dbspec-rust-check`, and `make dbspec-compare-check`, where they agree), and introspection reads the collations back (`make dbspec-introspect-check` round trips).
- [o] T8.0.9 Render `datetime(p)` as a local date-time with the UTC connection rule (requires T8.0). Cause: `datetime` renders as MySQL `DATETIME`, PostgreSQL `timestamp with time zone` and SQLite text (`internal/ormgen/ddl.go:487` and the client ports), which introspection cannot restore and which `docs/dialects.md` now rejects; the SQLite `=now` default writes UTC (`ddl.go:553`, `sqlite.datetime.now_is_utc`) while the dialect rules state that SQLite date-times use the connection time zone. Acceptance: T8.3 renders MySQL `DATETIME(p)`, PostgreSQL `timestamp(p)` and SQLite text with its renderer CHECK; every client sets UTC on each connection; introspection reports `timestamptz` and MySQL `TIMESTAMP` as unsupported; tracked cases run on all three databases in every client. orm reports an existing `timestamptz` column as unsupported and does not convert it. Evidence: tests/dbspec/ddl.json renders `datetime(p)` as MySQL `DATETIME(p)`, PostgreSQL `timestamp(p)` and SQLite text in every client (T8.3, `make dbspec-ddl-check`); tests/dbspec/introspect.json reports a MySQL `timestamp(6)` column and a PostgreSQL `timestamptz` column as unsupported in every client (`make dbspec-introspect-check` and its PHP, TypeScript and Rust variants); T8.0.9.1 sets UTC on every Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust connection and conformance reports 25 vectors × 4 languages identical on the three databases.
- [o] T8.0.9.1 Read and write every Go, TypeScript and Rust connection in UTC (follows T8.0.9). Cause: the PHP client sets MySQL `time_zone = '+00:00'` and PostgreSQL `TIME ZONE 'UTC'` on every connection and accepts only `UTC` or `+00:00` as `timezone`, but the Go, TypeScript and Rust clients accept any zone and use the server or process zone without the parameter, so the MySQL and PostgreSQL clock defaults write the session wall clock (KST on the test servers) and Go binds a PostgreSQL `timestamp` from a value with an offset whose offset PostgreSQL drops (`postgres.timestamp.ignores_offset`). Evidence: the Go, TypeScript and Rust clients now follow the rule that PHP already kept: MySQL connections set `time_zone = '+00:00'`, PostgreSQL connections `TimeZone=UTC`, values are read in UTC, a value with an offset is bound as its UTC wall clock (Go no longer hands PostgreSQL a `time.Time` whose offset `timestamp` drops), and `timezone` accepts only `UTC` or `+00:00` with `CONFIG` "dsn timezone `<value>`: every connection reads and writes datetime values in UTC" otherwise; the named-zone code, the MySQL 1298 mapping and the `chrono-tz` dependency are removed. Red on the test servers (MySQL `SYSTEM` = KST, PostgreSQL `Asia/Seoul`): Go `TestParseDSN` and `TestParseDSNRejectsInvalidInput` failed on 5 and 3 cases, and `TestConnectionsUseUTC` read `2026-01-02 00:00:00 +0900 KST` for the instant `2026-01-02 00:00+09:00` on SQLite, MySQL and PostgreSQL without the parameter and `2026-01-02 00:00:00 +0000 UTC` on PostgreSQL with `timezone=UTC`; TypeScript clients/typescript/tests/dsn.mjs accepted `timezone=%2B09:00` and clients/typescript/tests/model.mjs (`connectionsUseUtc`) read `start_dt 2026-01-02 00:00:00.000000` and a MySQL `created_ts` nine hours ahead; Rust `connections_use_utc` and `non_utc_time_zones_are_rejected` failed. Green: those tests pass with the parameter absent, `UTC` and `+00:00`, the PHP `connection time zone` cases and clients/php/tests/dsn.php pass, `go test ./clients/go/orm ./clients/go/model` passes, the Rust zone, value_decode and sqlite_file_name tests pass, clippy and tsc pass, and the conformance runs on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite report 25 vectors × 4 languages identical. docs/config.md, interfaces.md, dsl.md, protocol.md, dialects.md, features.md, interface-implementation.md and usage.md state the rule.
- [o] T8.0.9.2 Read SQLite DSN paths percent-decoded in every client (follows T8.0.9.1). Cause: the Rust `parse_dsn` reads `url.path()` without percent-decoding, so a `sqlite://` path with a space or another escaped character opens a different file. Acceptance: shared DSN cases with escaped paths open the same file in Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust. Evidence: docs/config.md "Runtime connection" states the rule: a SQLite DSN path is percent-decoded as UTF-8 before the file opens (`%25`, `%23` and `%3F` give `%`, `#` and `?`), and a `%` without two hexadecimal digits, a decoded NUL byte or a decoded path that is not UTF-8 returns `CONFIG`. tests/dsn/sqlite-paths.json holds 11 shared cases (plain, space, escaped percent, hash and question mark, UTF-8, a space with a query, and four rejected paths); each client connects through its public connect to `sqlite://<new directory>/<path>` and requires exactly the named file (with its journal files) or the error and an empty directory: Go `TestSQLitePathCases` (clients/go/orm/sqlite_path_test.go), PHP clients/php/tests/dsn.php, TypeScript clients/typescript/tests/dsn.mjs and Rust `sqlite_path_cases` (clients/rust/orm/tests/sqlite_file_name.rs). Red: Go decoded the path and handed it to SQLite as a `file:` URI, which decoded it again and cut it at `#`, `?` and NUL, so `escaped-percent` opened `a b.sqlite`, `escaped-hash`, `escaped-question-mark` and `nul` opened `a`, and `invalid-utf8` failed with `unable to open database file (14)` instead of `CONFIG`; PHP opened the literal `a%20b.sqlite` for `space`; TypeScript threw `URIError: URI malformed` instead of `CONFIG` for `invalid-escape`; Rust failed 10 of 11 cases, opening every escaped path under its literal name and accepting the four invalid paths. Green: Go rejects NUL and non-UTF-8 paths and escapes the decoded path into the `file:` URI, PHP validates and `rawurldecode`s the path, TypeScript decodes it in one function that maps a decode failure and NUL to `CONFIG`, and Rust decodes it in `sqlite_path`; the four tests pass all 11 cases. `go test ./clients/go/orm`, `cargo test -p orm --lib --test sqlite_file_name --test zone` and `cargo test -p orm-build --features cli --test tool_limits` pass. Gates passed: client-unit-check, ts-check, rust-check, rust-fmt-check, go-fmt-check, interface-check, docs-rules-check and checklist-check.
- [o] T8.0.10 Import SQLite primary key nullability as declared (requires T8.0). Cause: observed: `ormgen import` writes `k TEXT PRIMARY KEY` as NOT NULL (`internal/ormgen/migrate.go:238`), but SQLite accepts NULL in that column (`sqlite.pk.nullable_non_integer`). Acceptance: T8.4 reads `notnull` without inferring it from `pk`, with a tracked SQLite case in every client. Evidence: the dbspec introspection reads SQLite `pragma_table_xinfo.notnull` for every column and never infers it from `pk` (docs/dialects.md "Types and columns", `sqlite.pk.nullable_non_integer`). tests/dbspec/introspect.json gains `sqlite-primary-key-null`: table `kp` declares `"k" varchar(8) NULL` as its primary key in renderer form and is reported as `[table, kp, kp]` (TypeScript reason "key: primary key column k is null", because a dbspec key column cannot be `null`), while table `kq` with `"k" varchar(8) NOT NULL` is restored as `k varchar(8)` with `primary key (k)`. `make dbspec-introspect-check`, `dbspec-introspect-php-check`, `dbspec-introspect-ts-check` and `dbspec-introspect-rust-check` pass the case in Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust.
- [o] T8.0.11 Report catalog types and actions that import maps to another definition (requires T8.0). Cause: PostgreSQL `timestamp` and `timestamptz`, `time` and `timetz`, `char(n)` and `varchar(n)`, `real` and `double precision`, and `json`/`jsonb` and text are each imported as one type (`internal/ormgen/import.go:845-877`) although their stored values differ (`postgres.char.padded`, `postgres.float.real_single_precision`, `postgres.json.text_jsonb_normalized`); foreign key update actions are not read and `RESTRICT`, `NO ACTION` and `SET DEFAULT` are imported as the default (`import.go:518-527`; observed: MySQL `ON UPDATE CASCADE` is dropped). Acceptance: T8.4 restores each supported definition exactly and reports the rest as unsupported, with tracked cases in every client. Evidence: the dbspec introspection maps only the catalog forms of docs/dialects.md "Types and columns" and reads both foreign key actions, accepting only `RESTRICT`, `CASCADE` and `SET NULL`. The round trips of `make dbspec-introspect-check` restore `on delete set_null on update cascade` and `on delete cascade on update restrict` on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite, so a MySQL `ON UPDATE CASCADE` is kept. tests/dbspec/introspect.json now reports the other definitions as unsupported: PostgreSQL `timestamptz` (`at`), `timetz` (`tz`), `char(4)` (`ch`), `real` (`r`), `json` (`j`) and `jsonb` (`jb`) and the keys `fk_refs_a` (`ON DELETE NO ACTION`) and `fk_refs_b` (`ON UPDATE SET DEFAULT`); MySQL `timestamp(6)` (`ts`), `char(4)` (`ch`), `float` (`fl`), `json` (`doc`), the `NO ACTION` key `fk_c` and the `SET DEFAULT` key `fk_refs_b`; SQLite the keys `fk_refs_a` (`NO ACTION`) and `fk_refs_b` (`SET DEFAULT`), while the rest of each table (`refs` with its columns, key and index) is restored. `make dbspec-introspect-check`, `dbspec-introspect-php-check`, `dbspec-introspect-ts-check` and `dbspec-introspect-rust-check` pass these cases in Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust.
- [o] T8.0.12 Complete the PostgreSQL probe evidence (requires T8.0). Cause: during T8.0 the PostgreSQL primary and replica of `TEST_ENV` stopped because their data directories were configured at a removed path; the servers have since been restarted at the current path by their operator, and `BENCH_SQLITE_DSN` now names the current path. Evidence: with the restarted servers all 75 PostgreSQL probes pass twice, including `postgres.index.varchar_640_four_byte_fits`, `postgres.render.time_of_day_check` and the `COLLATE "C"` catalog step of `postgres.collation.c_codepoint`, which had not executed before; no PostgreSQL fact differed from the record.
- [o] T8.0.13 Find the opener that created a SQLite file named with its DSN query (requires T8.0). Cause: the previous `.runtime/servers` held an empty file `orm_bench.sqlite?_pragma=busy_timeout(5000)&timezone=%2B00:00` (dated 2026-09-26), so some program opened `BENCH_SQLITE_DSN` with its query in the file name. `TestSQLiteFileNameWithQuery` reproduces the mechanism: PHP PDO, `node:sqlite` and the `sqlite3` shell create that literal file name, while `modernc.org/sqlite` parses the query. The current Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust conformance runners and the Go, PHP and TypeScript `import` tools, run against a copy of the bench file with the same DSN form, created no such file, so the code path was not found in the current tree. Acceptance: each client's DSN tests and each tool that opens a SQLite DSN assert that only the path names the file; a found opener is corrected with a tracked RED case. Evidence: no current opener names a SQLite file with its DSN query, and each one now asserts it. Every case opens `sqlite://<temporary directory>/named.sqlite?_pragma=busy_timeout(5000)&timezone=%2B00:00`, the form of the observed file name, writes or connects, closes, and requires that the directory holds `named.sqlite` and no name other than `named.sqlite` and its `-journal`, `-wal` and `-shm` files. The client openers: Go `TestSQLiteFileNameIsThePath` (clients/go/orm/sqlite_file_name_test.go, `orm.Connect` and schema install), PHP clients/php/tests/dsn.php (`Orm::connect`), TypeScript clients/typescript/tests/dsn.mjs (`Db.connect`) and Rust clients/rust/orm/tests/sqlite_file_name.rs (`Db::connect`, which bench/install and the Rust tool database also use through the Go and Rust clients). The schema tools that open a DSN themselves: Go `TestToolSQLiteFileNameIsThePath` (internal/ormgen `openToolDB`), PHP clients/php/tests/schema_tool_test.php (`SchemaImport::connect`), TypeScript clients/typescript/tests/sqlite-auto-import.mjs (`openToolDb`) and Rust `tool_sqlite_file_name_is_the_path` in clients/rust/orm-build/tests/tool_limits.rs (`tool_db::open`). All pass; the new Go and Rust files are listed under the `dsn_connection` feature of contracts/features.json. The opener that created the file on 2026-09-26 is therefore not in the current tree.
- [o] T8.0.14 Propagate a failed MySQL context reset in the Go client (requires T8.0). Cause: MySQL user variables survive `COMMIT` and `ROLLBACK` (`mysql.context.user_variable_session_scope`), and the Go client discards the error of ``SET @`orm.<key>` = NULL`` at transaction end (`clients/go/orm/utils.go:152`), so a failed reset leaves the audit operation id on a pooled connection without an error; PHP, Rust and TypeScript propagate it. Acceptance: a tracked Go RED case with a failing reset reports the error, and the other clients keep equivalent cases. Evidence: the Go client now returns a failed reset of ``SET @`orm.<key>` = NULL``: `clearLocals` returns the mapped error, `commit` rolls back and returns it, and a rollback after a callback error returns `CONFIG` `transaction failed (<callback error>) and rollback failed (<cleanup error>)`, the form the Rust client already used. Go `TestTransactionReportsFailedLocalReset` (clients/go/orm/context_reset_test.go) wraps the MySQL driver so that only the reset fails; before the fix it failed with "commit: want the reset error, got `<nil>`", and after it passes on MySQL. The equivalent cases found that PHP replaced the callback error with the reset error and TypeScript discarded the reset error on rollback: clients/php/tests/model_test.php (`failed local reset/mysql`, a PDO that rejects only the reset) failed with "rollback reports the callback and the failed reset: reset rejected by the test driver", and clients/typescript/tests/model.mjs (`mysql/failedLocalReset`, a transaction `control` that rejects only the reset) failed with "…: callback failed"; both now report the two errors in the `CONFIG` form and pass. Rust `tx::tests::failed_local_reset_is_reported` ends the transaction connection with MySQL `KILL` from another connection and asserts that `finish` and `rollback` report the failed reset. `go test ./clients/go/orm -run 'Transaction|Local|Cancel'`, `go test ./clients/go/model`, the PHP and TypeScript model tests on SQLite, MySQL and PostgreSQL and the Rust `tx::tests::` tests pass; docs/interfaces.md states the rule. The remaining discarded transaction-end results of the Go client are T8.0.14.1.
- [o] T8.0.14.1 Report every transaction-end error in the Go client (follows T8.0.14). Cause: after T8.0.14 the Go client still discards the result of `SELECT RELEASE_LOCK(?)` in `releaseLocks` (clients/go/orm/utils.go), of `tx.Rollback()` in `abort` and of `finishSQLiteMode` in `rollback` (clients/go/orm/tx.go), and of the rollback that a panicking or exiting callback triggers, while the PHP, TypeScript and Rust clients propagate a failed `RELEASE_LOCK` and rollback. Acceptance: each of these errors reaches the caller or the panic, with a tracked Red case in Go and equivalent cases in the other clients. Evidence: the Go client now reports every transaction-end failure. `releaseLocks` (clients/go/orm/utils.go) reads the `RELEASE_LOCK` result and returns a failed statement and a result other than 1 as `CONFIG` `lock <key> was not held at transaction end`; `rollback` (clients/go/orm/tx.go) runs `finishSQLiteMode`, `releaseLocks`, `clearLocals` and the native rollback and joins every failure; commit, begin and callback failures go through `rollbackAfter`, which returns `CONFIG` `transaction failed (<cause>) and rollback failed (<transaction end error>)` when the rollback fails too; a panicking callback and a callback that leaves through `runtime.Goexit` panic with that error when their rollback fails and keep the original panic value otherwise. `beginSQLiteMode` marks the mode before its first PRAGMA, so a begin that fails after `PRAGMA read_uncommitted = 1` restores it. The transaction keeps its pool connection as a `*sql.Conn`, and a native rollback on a connection the driver closed is complete: `TestWithContextCancelsInsideTransaction` showed that both drivers close the connection when a statement in the transaction is cancelled (MySQL rollback returned "context canceled", PostgreSQL "conn closed"), so the rollback recognizes `driver.ErrBadConn` (clients/go/orm/pg maps `pgconn.ErrConnClosed` to it) and `driver.Validator` and the cancelled statement still returns `CANCELED`. Go `TestTransactionReportsFailedLockRelease`, `TestTransactionReportsFailedRollback` and `TestTransactionReportsFailedSQLiteModeReset` (clients/go/orm/transaction_end_test.go) use a test driver (clients/go/orm/failing_driver_test.go) that rejects a chosen statement or the rollback of the MySQL or SQLite driver; before the fix 9 of their 11 subtests failed, among them "commit: want the release error, got `<nil>`", "panic: want CONFIG … got `<nil>`" and "rollback: want CONFIG … got callback failed", and after it all 11 pass. The equivalent cases found the same gaps in the other clients. PHP (`failed lock release/mysql`, `failed rollback/sqlite` in clients/php/tests/model_test.php, a PDO that rejects a chosen statement or rollback) failed with "a lock released early is reported: no error" and "begin reports the cause and the failed transaction end: rollback rejected by the test driver"; `Db::finish` now runs every cleanup step, checks the `RELEASE_LOCK` result and reports the cleanup and rollback failures, and a failed begin restores the modes and reports a failed rollback with its cause. TypeScript (`mysql/failedLockRelease`, `sqlite/failedRollback` in clients/typescript/tests/model.mjs) failed 5 checks, among them "a lock released early is reported: no error" and "commit reports the failed mode reset: DRIVER: sqlite: cannot start a transaction within a transaction", because a failed begin PRAGMA left the SQLite transaction open; `Db.finish` and the SQLite and PostgreSQL drivers now run every step, roll a failed begin back and report the rollback failure instead of dropping it. Rust (`tx::tests::` in clients/rust/orm/src/tx.rs) failed `lock_not_held_at_transaction_end_is_reported`, `every_failed_cleanup_step_is_reported` ("left: 1, right: 2") and `panicking_callback_ends_its_transaction` ("the panicked transaction released its lock: left: Some(0), right: Some(1)"), and the SQLite panic case failed with "the panic carries the transaction failure"; `finish` now runs every step and checks the `RELEASE_LOCK` result, and a panicking callback rolls back before the panic continues. `sqlite_transaction_end_failures_are_reported` denies ROLLBACK and PRAGMA statements with a SQLite authorizer. `go test ./clients/go/...`, the PHP model test (11 tests × 3 databases), the TypeScript model test on SQLite, MySQL and PostgreSQL and the 9 Rust `tx::tests::` tests pass; docs/interfaces.md states the rule. Found defects are T8.0.14.2, T8.0.14.3 and T8.0.14.4.
- [o] T8.0.14.2 Close a dropped Rust transaction connection over TLS. Cause: `CancellableConnection` (clients/rust/orm/src/driver.rs) ends a transaction connection that was not completed with `close_hard` in a spawned task; with the MySQL test server over TLS that close started and had not finished 5 s later, and the session kept its named lock, so a failed begin, a failed commit cleanup or a dropped transaction future can leave the open transaction and its locks on the server. Acceptance: a dropped Rust transaction future ends its server session and named lock over TLS, with a tracked Red case. Evidence: sqlx's `close_hard` flushes before the shutdown, and the rustls stream of sqlx-core 0.9.0 reads until the server sends data when it has nothing to write, so the spawned close of a TLS connection never finished. `CancellableConnection` (clients/rust/orm/src/driver.rs) now drops the detached connection in place, which closes the socket at once for MySQL and PostgreSQL and closes the SQLite handle as SQLite's `close_hard` did; the spawned task and its discarded close result are gone. Rust `tx::tests::dropped_transaction_closes_its_connection` takes a named lock in a transaction, drops the transaction future and takes the lock from another connection; it asserts the MySQL connection uses TLS (`Ssl_cipher` is not empty). Before the fix it failed on MySQL in two runs (5.02 s and 5.03 s) with "mysql: the dropped transaction released its lock: left: Some(0), right: Some(1)" (`GET_LOCK(?, 5)` timed out); after it the MySQL `GET_LOCK` returns 1, the PostgreSQL `pg_advisory_xact_lock` under `lock_timeout = '5s'` succeeds and SQLite `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` under `busy_timeout = 5000` succeeds with the inserted row rolled back. The 28 tests of `cargo test -p orm --lib` pass; docs/interfaces.md states the rule.
- [o] T8.0.14.3 Report every savepoint-end error. Cause: the Go `savepoint` (clients/go/orm/tx.go) discards the results of `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT` and `RELEASE SAVEPOINT` after a panic, a `runtime.Goexit` and a callback error, and the PHP and TypeScript savepoints replace the callback error with a failed savepoint rollback. Acceptance: every savepoint end error reaches the caller or the panic in every client, with tracked Red cases. Evidence: after a callback error, a panic or a Go `runtime.Goexit` every client now runs both `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT` and `RELEASE SAVEPOINT` and reports a failure of either as `CONFIG` `transaction failed (<cause>) and rollback failed (<savepoint end error>)` (the panic value for a panic), and returns a failed `RELEASE SAVEPOINT` after a successful callback: Go `savepoint`, `rollbackSavepoint` and `endSavepoint` (clients/go/orm/tx.go), PHP `Db::savepoint` and `Db::endSavepoint`, TypeScript `Database.savepoint`, and Rust `run_savepoint` and `rollback_savepoint` (clients/rust/orm/src/tx.rs), which serve `transaction`, `transaction_send` and `transaction_once`. The Rust savepoint also discarded `RELEASE SAVEPOINT` after a callback error and did not end on a panic; it now catches the panic, ends the savepoint and continues the panic. A Go savepoint of a transaction that already ended, through the cancelled transaction context or a connection that the driver closed, ended with it. Go `TestSavepointReportsFailedEnd` (clients/go/orm/transaction_end_test.go, the SQLite test driver rejects the chosen statement) failed 7 of its 8 subtests before the fix, among them "callback error: want CONFIG … got statement rejected by the test driver" and "panic: … got `<nil>`"; the nested case added to `TestWithContextCancelsInsideTransaction` failed on MySQL and PostgreSQL with "blocked read: driver: bad connection (code \"\")" because the failed `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT` on the closed connection replaced `CANCELED`; a holder transaction that ends before it holds the row now fails that test instead of blocking it. PHP `failed savepoint end/sqlite` (clients/php/tests/model_test.php) and TypeScript `sqlite/failedSavepointEnd` (clients/typescript/tests/model.mjs) each failed 4 checks, among them "ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT reports the cause and the failed transaction end: statement rejected by the test driver". Rust `tx::tests::savepoint_end_failures_are_reported` (a SQLite authorizer denies the savepoint statements) failed with "callback error 4: sqlx: error returned from database: (code: 23) not authorized"; the two authorizer tests now hold one lock because they share the process-wide denial setting, which had made them fail together. After the fix `go test -p 1 ./clients/go/...`, the PHP model test (11 tests × 3 databases), `make ts-check` and the 29 tests of `cargo test -p orm --lib` pass; the MySQL and PostgreSQL runs used dedicated databases on the test servers while concurrent runs held the shared ones. docs/interfaces.md states the rule.
- [o] T8.0.14.4 Clear MySQL session state of a cancelled Go transaction. Cause: when the context of a Go transaction is cancelled, database/sql rolls the transaction back itself and keeps the go-sql-driver connection, which implements `driver.SessionResetter` and `driver.Validator`, so `releaseLocks` and `clearLocals` cannot run on it and its named locks and user variables can stay on the pooled connection; this reading of database/sql has no executed case yet. Acceptance: a tracked Go case shows whether the cancelled transaction leaves session state, and a reproduced defect is fixed. Evidence: the defect reproduced. Go `TestCancelledTransactionClearsSessionState` (clients/go/orm/cancelled_session_test.go) cancels the context of a transaction on a one-connection pool after the callback took a named lock and a local value (a read-only transaction on SQLite). Before the fix every subtest returned "want CANCELED alone, got CONFIG: transaction failed (CANCELED: context canceled) and rollback failed (CANCELED: …)", because `releaseLocks`, `clearLocals` and `finishSQLiteMode` ran with the cancelled context; on MySQL `GET_LOCK(?, 5)` from another connection returned 0 and the next transaction read the user variable "tester"; on SQLite the next transaction failed with "READ_ONLY: attempt to write a readonly database (8)" because `query_only = 1` stayed on the pooled connection. `rollback` (clients/go/orm/tx.go) now closes a cancelled transaction's connection with `closeSession` instead of returning it to the pool, so the server ends the session with its locks, variables and modes, and the transaction returns `CANCELED` alone. After the fix the three subtests pass in two runs, `go test -p 1 ./clients/go/...` passes on dedicated MySQL and PostgreSQL databases of the test servers, and docs/interfaces.md states the rule.
- [o] T8.1 Specify dbspec in `docs/dbspec.md` and its Korean pair (requires T8.0). Acceptance: grammar; separation of definitions, `settings` and `diagram`; settings key table with `schemaHash`/`manifestHash` membership; neutral type and expression sets; naming correction table; cross-document imports; canonical order; limits; diagnostics; a keep/move/remove decision for every existing schema feature, including the PhysicalGraph records that carry dialect SQL text; shared vectors and per-client budgets. Evidence: `docs/dbspec.md` and its Korean pair define the header and document names, `use` against a declared document set without file paths, names, columns, the thirteen neutral types with their renderings, keys, indexes, foreign keys, the neutral check expressions, ten settings with their `schemaHash` or `manifestHash` membership, audit through an operation column and a history table (no session variable, transaction setting or context table; physical deletes rejected), diagrams, canonical form including literal forms, limits, sixteen diagnostic rules with locations, the naming changes and a keep/move/remove decision for every Mermaid feature including removal of the PhysicalGraph dialect-text records and the annotated Markdown path. `docs/dialects.md` records the audit decision. `tests/dbspec/cases.json` holds 4 canonical, 2 normalization and 17 invalid shared cases with computed error locations. The Rust parse budget is 300 ms for the 2000-table case; other clients are fixed at their first measurement. `make docs-check`, `make docs-rules-check` and `make checklist-check` pass.
- [o] T8.1.1 Settle the dbspec rules that the TypeScript, PHP and Rust parsers read differently (follows T8.1). Cause: the three implementations reported about 30 places where docs/dbspec.md was silent or open, and they chose differently (for example the column unit, the order of table lines, comments before a closing brace and at the end, f64 and decimal literals, use cycles, setting repeats, diagram coordinate range, stopping errors and diagnostic order). Evidence: docs/dbspec.md and its Korean pair now fix each point (reserved words, tabs, line ends, header position, constraint order, unclosed blocks, modifier and action order, literal forms, codec storage types, aes_version and blind_index rules, audit table shape, use resolution, name scopes against table names, written orders, comment placement, error locations and ordering), and tests/dbspec/cases.json gains 1 canonical, 4 normalization and 16 invalid cases that lock them (5, 6 and 33 in total). `make docs-rules-check` and `make checklist-check` pass. The client items realign their parsers to these cases.
- [o] T8.1.2 Settle the dbspec rules that the Go parser reported open (follows T8.1.1). Cause: docs/dbspec.md did not say how repeated `blind_index` lines are ordered, which rules a failed key or index line hides, or how a line that fails on a tab keeps its kind; Go chose one reading, while PHP and TypeScript kept `blind_index` lines in written order and reported a missing primary key, the identity rule, a leading-index error and a key-target error after a failed key line. Evidence: docs/dbspec.md and its Korean pair sort `blind_index` lines by AES column and state that a failed line keeps its kind and name and hides the rules that depend on its columns; tests/dbspec/cases.json gains blind-index-sorted, failed-primary-key-line, failed-index-line-under-foreign-key, failed-target-key-line, tab-in-primary-key-line and leading-tab-column-line (5, 7 and 38 cases). Before the client changes PHP and TypeScript failed them; now `make dbspec-go-check`, `make dbspec-php-check` and `make dbspec-ts-check` pass all 50 cases, and interface-check passes for go, php and typescript after recording the new PHP and TypeScript symbols.
- [o] T8.1.3 Settle where a `header` error points (follows T8.1.2). Cause: docs/dbspec.md required the exact form `dbspec 1 <name>` without saying where its errors point; Go, PHP and TypeScript pointed at an extra word, Rust at the space before it, and TypeScript accepted double spaces in the header. Evidence: docs/dbspec.md and its Korean pair point a `header` error at the first character that departs from `dbspec 1 <name>`, `<name>` being a run of ASCII letters, digits and `_`, or one past the line end; tests/dbspec/cases.json gains header-extra-word, header-double-space, header-tab, header-version, header-missing-name, header-leading-space and header-double-space-before-version (5, 7 and 45 cases). Go, PHP and TypeScript failed header-extra-word and TypeScript also the double-space cases before the change; the four client checks (`make dbspec-go-check`, `dbspec-php-check`, `dbspec-ts-check`, `dbspec-rust-check`) and the four-language interface-check now pass.
- [o] T8.1.4 Type the check predicates so that the three databases evaluate them alike (follows T8.1.3). Cause: docs/dbspec.md allowed `+`, `-`, `*` and `/` and any literal against any column, while integer division (2.5 on MySQL, 2 on PostgreSQL and SQLite), integer overflow (REAL on SQLite) and the scaled SQLite decimal give different results, and no client checked operand types. Evidence: docs/dbspec.md and its Korean pair define the predicate forms (comparisons with a column, `in`, `between`, `is null`, a `bool` column, `not`, `and`, `or`, parentheses), remove arithmetic, functions, the `null` literal and `bytes` columns, make a literal that meets a column a valid default of the column type written in its canonical default form, list which column types meet, and order the diagnostics: reading first, then columns and types in source order. tests/dbspec/cases.json gains the canonical `check-typed-predicates`, the normalize `check-literals-take-column-form` and 26 invalid `check-*` cases, and `every-line-kind` now writes `total >= 0.00` (6, 8 and 71 cases). Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust failed the 20 first new cases before their changes and pass all cases now; their rule tests replace the cases that used arithmetic and add typed cases. `make dbspec-go-check`, `dbspec-php-check`, `dbspec-ts-check`, `dbspec-rust-check` and `dbspec-compare-check` (8 runs agree on 89 cases) pass; stress medians are Go 45 ms, PHP 205-211 ms, TypeScript 87-90 ms and Rust 30 ms; gofmt, rustfmt, clippy and the four-language interface-check pass after recording the symbols.
- [o] T8.1.5 Bound the names that the renderer generates to 63 bytes (follows T8.1.4). Cause: docs/dialects.md "Rendered statements" names renderer CHECKs `<table>$<column>` and triggers `<table>$<event>`, which can exceed the 63-byte identifier limit for valid table and column names, and the renderer must not shorten a name. Evidence: docs/dbspec.md and its Korean pair list the generated names in "Names" and make a name over 63 bytes a `name.length` error at the column name or the `immutable` or `audit` keyword. tests/dbspec/cases.json gains the canonical `generated-names-at-63-bytes` and three invalid cases (check, immutable and audit names of 64 bytes); Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust failed the invalid ones before their change and pass all cases now (7, 8 and 74 cases). The five dbspec targets, gofmt, rustfmt, clippy and the four-language interface-check pass. Stress medians rose under a load average of 8.6; the Go stress measured 62-65 ms before the change and 53-63 ms after it in the same load, so the rule adds no measurable parse time.
- [o] T8.1.6 Reserve `and`, `or`, `not`, `in`, `between` and `is` (follows T8.1.4). Cause: these words read as predicate keywords but are valid table and column names, so `check ck (and > 0)` on a column named `and` is read as a column by the PHP parser and rejected by the Go parser; the predicate grammar is ambiguous unless the words are reserved. Evidence: docs/dbspec.md and its Korean pair add `and`, `or`, `not`, `in`, `between` and `is` to the reserved words, and the four clients add them to their reserved lists. tests/dbspec/cases.json gains the invalid `reserved-predicate-keywords` (a table named `or`, a column named `between`, both `name.format`), which the Go parser returned as a valid document before the change (the other clients were changed with it and not run before); all cases pass now in the four clients. The PHP check reader and renderer drop the branch that read a keyword as a column when the table had one, which reserved words make unreachable. The five dbspec targets, rustfmt and the four-language interface-check pass.
- [o] T8.1.7 Keep only the check predicate forms that introspection restores (follows T8.1.4). Cause: probes show that MySQL writes `NOT` into the comparisons it negates and rejects a bool column alone as a CHECK (3812), and that PostgreSQL writes `BETWEEN` as two comparisons, so a document with `not`, `between` or a column alone cannot be restored from the catalog; both catalogs also add parentheses that canonical form keeps as written. Evidence: six dialect probes record the catalog behavior and pass on MySQL 8.4.11 and PostgreSQL 17.11: mysql.check.bool_column_rejected (3812), mysql.check.not_pushed_down, mysql.check.between_kept, mysql.check.string_escaped_twice, postgres.check.between_rewritten and postgres.check.not_kept; docs/dialects.md cites them in the CHECK expression row. docs/dbspec.md and its Korean pair keep comparisons, `[not] in`, `is [not] null`, `and`, `or` and parentheses, remove `not`, `between` and a column alone with the reason, state the reading positions, and make canonical form keep only the parentheses that an `or` inside an `and` needs. tests/dbspec/cases.json rewrites `check-typed-predicates`, adds the normalize `check-redundant-parentheses` and the invalid `check-not-operator`, `check-between`, `check-bool-column-alone` and `check-literal-before-not` (7, 9 and 79 cases), and tests/dbspec/ddl.json rewrites `checks-in-dialect-form`, whose statements alone changed and still apply on the three databases. Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust failed the first four new cases before their change; `check-literal-before-not` was added after the TypeScript change showed that Go reported that literal instead of `not`, and Go was corrected. The parsers, typers, emitters and renderers drop the removed forms; the five dbspec targets, `make dbspec-ddl-check`, gofmt, rustfmt and the four-language interface-check pass.
- [o] T8.1.8 Let `audit` stand without `soft_delete` (follows T8.1.7). Cause: `audit` is a schema setting that introspection reads back from its triggers, while `soft_delete` is a manifest setting that no database holds; requiring it made every introspected audited table an invalid document. The `BEFORE DELETE` trigger already makes a physical delete fail. Evidence: the canonical vector `audit-without-soft-delete` failed in the four clients with the soft_delete diagnostic and passes after the rule is removed from Go `settings.go`, PHP `Parser.php`, TypeScript `parse.ts` and Rust `validate.rs`; the client rule tests drop that diagnostic; `make dbspec-compare-check` reports 8 runs agree on 115 cases; the interface check passes with the TypeScript symbols re-recorded.
- [o] T8.2 Implement dbspec parse and canonical emit in every client (requires T8.1). Acceptance: `emit(parse(s)) == s` for canonical input; located diagnostics; the 2000-table/60000-column/10000-FK case within budget; removal of the Mermaid source path and the T7.17.2.10.x document path; one CLI name across languages. Evidence: T8.2.1 to T8.2.6 are complete: every client parses and emits dbspec with `emit(parse(s)) == s` on the shared cases and located diagnostics; the 2000-table stress document passes each client budget in `make dbspec-<lang>-check`; `make dbspec-compare-check` finds the four clients identical; the Mermaid source path and the T7.17.2.10.x document path are removed and the CLI has one name.
- [o] T8.2.1 Implement dbspec parse, validation and canonical emit in the Rust client (requires T8.1). Acceptance: every case of `tests/dbspec/cases.json` passes; the 2000-table/60000-column/10000-foreign-key case parses within 300 ms in release mode and emits identically twice; errors carry rule, line and column. Evidence: orm-schema/src/dbspec implements parse, emit and Diagnostic with the signatures contracts/interfaces.json declares; the Red tests failed before the module existed. make dbspec-rust-check runs twice: tests/dbspec.rs passes all 50 shared cases (5 canonical, 7 normalize, 38 invalid), and tests/dbspec_rules.rs passes 52 rule cases, comment attachment and limits. The six T8.1.2 cases failed before the blind_index sort and failed-key rules were added. The release stress example parses five times with median 29-31 ms (min 28 ms, max 42 ms) and emit 8-9 ms on rustc 1.98.1, darwin arm64, under the 300 ms budget, and emits the document back unchanged twice. cargo fmt --check and cargo clippy -D warnings pass for orm-schema. contracts/symbols/rust.json now matches the native symbols: 715 added, 10 changed, and 37 removed from orm-gen db.rs and introspect.rs, which T7.17.1 deleted without updating the snapshot. `go run ./tests/interfaces/check` passes for all four languages.
- [o] T8.2.1.1 Expose the parsed dbspec model in the Rust client as Go, PHP and TypeScript do (follows T8.2.1). Cause: the Rust model types and fields were `pub(crate)`, so a tool outside the crate could read only the document name through `emit` and could not read or change tables, columns, keys or diagrams, while the Go model has exported fields, the PHP model public properties and the TypeScript client exports its model types. Evidence: `orm_schema::dbspec::model` is a public module whose types and fields are public, with `Value` of the literal; clients/rust/orm-schema/tests/dbspec_model.rs did not build before (`module model is private`, `field tables of struct Document is private`) and now reads a parsed document, adds a column and emits the canonical text that parses again; `make dbspec-rust-check` runs it; contracts/symbols/rust.json records the new public symbols and interface-check passes.
- [o] T8.2.2 Implement dbspec parse, validation and canonical emit in the Go client (requires T8.1). Acceptance: every shared case passes; the 2000-table case is measured and its budget recorded. Evidence: engine/dbspec implements Parse, Emit and Diagnostic with the signatures contracts/interfaces.json declares; the Red tests failed before the package existed. go test ./engine/dbspec passes 145 subtests: all 44 shared cases (5 canonical, 6 normalize, 33 invalid), 86 rule cases, 8 encoding and limit cases and 7 canonical forms. The stress document emits back unchanged twice with parse 48-53 ms and emit 6-10 ms on go1.27.0, darwin arm64, under the 100 ms budget of T8.2.4.1. interface-check passes for go.
- [o] T8.2.2.1 Report the identity rule in Go when a table has no primary key line (follows T8.2.2). Cause: Go validated the identity rule only inside the branch for a table with a primary key, so `table t { id i64 identity }` reported only `key`, while docs/dbspec.md requires the identity column to be the only primary key column and lets only a failed `primary key` line hide that rule; PHP, TypeScript and Rust report `column` at `identity` as well, and no shared case covered it, so the four-client comparison did not see the difference. Evidence: tests/dbspec/cases.json gains the invalid case `identity-without-primary-key` (`key` 3:7 and `column` 4:10); Go `TestSharedVectors` failed on it with only `key` and passes after validate.go reports the identity rule for a table without a primary key line; the plan parse case `target-diagnostic-located` now lists both diagnostics; `make dbspec-go-check`, `dbspec-php-check`, `dbspec-ts-check`, `dbspec-rust-check` and `dbspec-compare-check` pass.
- [o] T8.2.3 Implement dbspec parse, validation and canonical emit in the PHP client (requires T8.1). Acceptance: every shared case passes; the 2000-table case is measured and its budget recorded. Evidence: clients/php/src/Dbspec implements Orm\Dbspec\Dbspec::parse and ::emit and the diagnostic record as contracts/interfaces.json declares; the Red tests failed before the classes existed. make dbspec-php-check runs each test twice: all 44 shared cases (5 canonical, 6 normalize, 33 invalid) and 57 focused cases pass, and the stress document emits back unchanged twice with parse 218-308 ms, emit 24-32 ms and a 57 MiB peak within 128 MiB on PHP 8.5.10, darwin arm64, under the 400 ms budget of T8.2.4.1. interface-check passes for php.
- [o] T8.2.4 Implement dbspec parse, validation and canonical emit in the TypeScript client (requires T8.1). Acceptance: every shared case passes; the 2000-table case is measured and its budget recorded. Evidence: clients/typescript/src/dbspec (index, model, parse, emit) implements parseDbspec, emitDbspec and DbspecDiagnostic exactly as contracts/interfaces.json declares; the Red vector test failed with ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND before the module existed. make dbspec-ts-check passes 89 tests: 44 shared cases (5 canonical, 6 normalize, 33 invalid) and 45 focused cases, and the stress document (2,490,985 bytes) emits back unchanged twice, parse 105-156 ms and emit 15-26 ms on Node 26.8.1, darwin arm64. The TypeScript parse budget is 160 ms. contracts/symbols/typescript.json records the 279 dbspec symbols and its hash is updated; interface-check reports no dbspec failure for TypeScript, while 164 unrecorded physical_* and introspect symbols remain from before and leave with T8.2.6.
- [o] T8.2.4.1 Make the client budgets and the symbol snapshot true (follows T8.2.4). Cause: T8.2.4 cited a 160 ms TypeScript budget that no test asserted and that docs/dbspec.md did not state, and it was committed while interface-check failed on 164 TypeScript, 107 PHP and 514 Go symbols that the snapshot did not record. Evidence: docs/dbspec.md and its Korean pair state the budgets (Rust 300 ms, Go 100 ms, TypeScript 250 ms, PHP 400 ms within 128 MiB), and the Go, TypeScript and PHP stress tests fail above them; the Go test failed with a 1 ms budget (parse 50 ms) and passes at 100 ms (52 ms), TypeScript passes at 121-132 ms and PHP at 222-234 ms with a 57 MiB peak. contracts/symbols/{go,php,typescript}.json record every native symbol, including the physical_* ones that T8.2.6 removes, and `go run ./tests/interfaces/check -language` passes for go, php and typescript.
- [o] T8.2.4.2 Decide the parse budgets on the median of five parses (follows T8.2.4.1). Cause: the TypeScript stress test measured one parse; while other test binaries used a full core, one parse took 602 ms against the 250 ms budget, and 11 more runs took 111-131 ms, so one slowed parse decided the result. Evidence: docs/dbspec.md and its Korean pair state the median of five; the Go, TypeScript and PHP stress tests parse five times, print the shortest, median and longest parse and fail when the median exceeds the budget. Under the same load the medians were Go 45-47 ms (one loaded run 80 ms), TypeScript 94-107 ms and PHP 208-220 ms within a 59 MiB peak.
- [o] T8.2.5 Compare the four clients on the shared dbspec cases in one tracked target (requires T8.2.1-T8.2.4). Acceptance: the target runs each client twice and fails on any difference in emission or diagnostics. Evidence: `make dbspec-compare-check` builds the TypeScript client and the release Rust example, then runs tests/dbspec/compare/go, php.php, typescript.mjs and orm-schema/examples/dbspec_compare.rs twice each; every runner prints, for the 57 shared cases and the stress document, the emission or the `rule line column` diagnostics, and check.mjs fails on the first line that differs from the first Go run. The comparison test (check.test.mjs) failed with ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND before compare.mjs existed and passes its 4 cases; with the TypeScript runner shifted by one column the target exited 1 at invalid/missing-header, and as committed all 8 runs agree on 58 cases in 1.5 s. The five dbspec targets are part of `make check`.
- [o] T8.2.6 Remove the Mermaid schema source path, the PhysicalGraph dialect-text records and the annotated Markdown path, and give the CLI one name in every language (requires T8.2.5). Evidence: T8.2.6.1 to T8.2.6.4 are complete: the dbspec document set is the only schema source for every generator, runtime, schema tool and installation; the CLI is `orm-gen` in Go, PHP and TypeScript, and Rust generates models through build.rs without a CLI.
- [o] T8.2.6.1 Specify the manifest that generators read from a dbspec document and the `schemaHash` and `manifestHash` algorithms, with shared vectors from dbspec text to manifest and hashes (follows T8.2.6). Cause: the generators of every client read schema/schema.json, a manifest built from the Mermaid source with `auto`, `raw` and `pk` fields, and docs/dbspec.md names the two hashes and their coverage but neither the manifest format nor the hash computation, so removing the Mermaid path would leave the generators without input. Evidence: docs/dbspec.md and its Korean pair add "Manifest and hashes": the declared document set is the only schema source and no other manifest file exists; the manifest text is the canonical emission without comments and diagrams, concatenated in document name order for a set; the schema text keeps only the `immutable` and `audit` settings; each hash is `sha256:` and the lower-case hex SHA-256 of its text; generated code embeds the manifest text and its hash, runtimes parse it once at process start, and the PHP generator writes PHP arrays so no request parses it (the PHP parser takes 208-220 ms for 2000 tables, about 0.1 ms a table). This replaces the 16-hex hash over Go-style JSON and the nine manifest structs of the four clients. tests/dbspec/cases.json gains three `hashes` cases (comments, a diagram and manifest settings removed; `audit` kept in the schema text; a two-document set in name order) whose texts and hashes come from the textual rule, and whose documents emit back unchanged in TypeScript; the five dbspec targets still pass.
- [o] T8.2.6.1.1 Give the Korean manifest heading an ASCII anchor (follows T8.2.6.1). Cause: VitePress writes the id of `## Manifest와 hash` with decomposed Hangul, so the links `#manifest와-hash` in docs/dbspec.ko.md and docs/dialects.ko.md reach no anchor and `make docs-check` fails. Evidence: `make docs-check` failed with `Missing anchor: …/ko/dbspec.html#manifest%EC%99%80-hash` and passes with the heading `## Manifest와 hash {#manifest-and-hashes}` and both links pointing to that id.
- [o] T8.2.6.1.1.1 Point the Korean protocol link at the ASCII manifest anchor (follows T8.2.6.1.1). Cause: docs/protocol.ko.md links `dbspec.ko.md#manifest와-hash`, which T8.2.6.1.1 replaced by the id `manifest-and-hashes`, so `npm run docs:check` fails with `Missing anchor: …/ko/dbspec.html#manifest%EC%99%80-hash` at the current commit. Acceptance: the link uses `#manifest-and-hashes` and `make docs-check` passes. Evidence: T8.2.6.1.3 corrected the same two links of docs/protocol.ko.md to `#manifest-and-hashes`; docs/protocol.ko.md holds no `manifest와-hash` and `make docs-check` passes its 573 internal links and assets.
- [o] T8.2.6.1.2 Make `schemaHash` depend only on the tables (follows T8.2.6.1.1). Cause: the schema text concatenated the documents of a set with their headers and `use` lines, so moving a table to another document or renaming a document changed `schemaHash` while the database stayed the same, against its definition, and an introspected database could not be compared with a set by hash; migration plans record this hash. Evidence: docs/dbspec.md defines the schema text as one document `schema` with every table of the set in name order; the new hash vector `same-tables-in-one-document` puts the two tables of `document-set-in-name-order` in one document and must give the same schema text and `schemaHash` sha256:9d2367df…f140, which the previous definition did not; Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust pass the four hash vectors, `make dbspec-compare-check` and the four introspection checks pass, and the Go round trip compares with `ManifestOf(documents).SchemaText` directly.
- [o] T8.2.6.1.3 Point the Korean protocol page at the manifest anchor (follows T8.2.6.1.1). Cause: the client switch merge (T8.2.6.3) added a link `dbspec.md#manifest와-hash` to docs/protocol.ko.md, an anchor that no heading has, so `make docs-check` failed with "Missing anchor"; that merge did not run `docs-check`. Evidence: `make docs-check` failed with `Missing anchor: …/ko/dbspec.html#manifest%EC%99%80-hash` from docs/protocol.ko.md and passes with both of its links pointing to `#manifest-and-hashes`: 48 static pages and 573 internal links and assets.
- [o] T8.2.6.2 Compute the manifest text, the schema text and both hashes of a dbspec document set in the Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust clients; every client passes the `hashes` cases of tests/dbspec/cases.json and `make dbspec-compare-check` compares the four results (requires T8.2.6.1). Evidence: Go `ManifestOf`, PHP `Dbspec::manifest`, TypeScript `dbspecManifest` and Rust `manifest` emit each document with a view that drops comments and diagrams (manifest) or also every setting except `immutable` and `audit` (schema), concatenate the documents in name order and hash with SHA-256; a repeated document name returns one `name.duplicate` diagnostic at 1:10. contracts/interfaces.json declares the `Dbspec.manifest` rule and the `DbspecManifest` owner, and interface-check passes for the four languages. The new tests (Go manifest_test.go, PHP dbspec_manifest_test.php, TypeScript tests/dbspec.mjs, Rust tests/dbspec_manifest.rs) failed before the function existed and pass the 3 hashes cases in both input orders and the repeated name rule; each client target runs them. The compare runners print the hashes and both texts of every hashes case, and the 8 runs agree on 61 cases. One TypeScript stress run measured a 320 ms median while other processes compiled Rust; parse code was unchanged and the next runs measured 86-104 ms.
- [o] T8.2.6.2.1 Reject an invalid document set in manifest and rendering alike (follows T8.2.6.2). Cause: `ManifestOf` reports a repeated document name but not a used document missing from the set, the Go renderer skips a missing used document silently, and the PHP and TypeScript renderers throw for both; docs/dbspec.md states no rule for the set these functions take. Evidence: docs/dbspec.md and its Korean pair state the set rule in "Manifest and hashes" (each document once, every used document present; `name.duplicate` and `use` at 1:10 of the later or using document, documents and used names in name order, no manifest and no statements on a diagnostic), and docs/dialects.md points the renderer to it. One set check (Go `checkSet`, PHP `DocumentSet::check`, TypeScript `checkSet`, Rust `check_set`) now serves the manifest and the renderer, which returns statements or diagnostics in every client (Go `([]string, []Diagnostic)`, PHP `RenderResult`, TypeScript `DbspecRenderResult`, Rust `Result`). tests/dbspec/cases.json gains three `sets` cases (repeated name, missing used document, complete set); before the change Go did not compile against the new signature, PHP and TypeScript threw instead of returning a diagnostic, TypeScript and Go gave no `use` diagnostic, and Rust rendered an invalid set; all pass now. The TypeScript `DbspecDiagnostic` moved to model.ts, so contracts/interfaces.json names that owner. The five dbspec targets, gofmt, rustfmt, clippy and the four-language interface-check pass.
- [o] T8.2.6.3 Make every generator, runtime, schema tool and schema installation read the dbspec document set: generated code embeds the manifest text and `manifestHash` (PHP as arrays), runtimes build their model from it without a schema file, and the sources of schema/schema.json, schema/bench.mmd and the manifest fixtures of contracts/ and the client tests become dbspec documents (requires T8.2.6.2 and T8.3). Evidence: T8.2.6.3.1-T8.2.6.3.7 are complete, and T8.2.6.4 removed the Mermaid sources and manifests that T8.2.6.3.2 kept for the old tests.
- [o] T8.2.6.3.1 Specify the runtime model that a client builds from a dbspec document set and what generated code holds in each language: entity and field names, value types per dbspec type, codec stages, the default select set, the `updated`, `soft_delete`, `aes_version`, `blind_index` and audit operation behavior, and relations from `navigation` settings only; decide every field of the Mermaid manifest (requires T8.2.6.2.1). Cause: the runtimes read nine manifest structs whose fields (`auto`, `lazy`, `styles`, `relations` derived from `_seq` names, `fulltext`, `unsigned`, `enum`) have no dbspec counterpart or a different name. Evidence: docs/dbspec.md and its Korean pair add "Runtime model": entities per table in name and document order with names from `entity` or the table, fields with a per-language value type table for the 13 dbspec types (a new `i16` path; `date` as `YYYY-MM-DD` text in TypeScript, which today returns a date-time text; `time(p)` as text everywhere), keys, the default select set from `select explicit` only, database defaults for omitted columns, codec value types from the stages (styled values for `ordered_json`, `serialize`, `yaml`, `gz` and `base64`, which Rust today returns unstyled for `gz` and `base64`; strings for `aes`, `hex` and `ip`), the runtime behavior of every setting including the audit operation id, joins through match methods, and a connection without a schema path that carries `manifestHash`. A last paragraph maps every Mermaid manifest field and removes fulltext conditions and point values. The `navigation` setting now states that generated code does not read it, which matches the four generators: none of them uses the manifest relation names.
- [o] T8.2.6.3.2 Rewrite schema/bench.mmd, the Mermaid fixtures of the Go tests and contracts/fixtures, and the Rust manifest fixtures as dbspec documents, with every name-convention behavior declared as a setting and every foreign key indexed (requires T8.2.6.3.1). Evidence: schema/bench.dbs rewrites schema/bench.mmd: the `is_*` columns are `bool`, `updated_ts` is the `updated` setting, `aes_key_version` the `aes_version` setting, the name-prefixed columns declare their codecs (`aes hex`, `gz`, `ordered_json` for both `json_` and `jsons_`, `base64`, `serialize`, `ip`), the columns that the Mermaid builder left out of the default select set by type or style are listed in `select explicit`, `unsigned` becomes the check `ck_author_counts`, `enum(open_done)` a `varchar(8)` with `ck_task_state`, every relation line a named foreign key with a leading index (five indexes added), and the fulltext directive is dropped. contracts/fixtures gains decimal_schema.dbs, secret_config.dbs and zone.dbs from decimal_schema.mmd and the Rust aes_json and zone_schema manifests, and audit.dbs for the history-table audit; the Rust audit_* manifests describe the removed operation and change tables and are not rewritten. Every document parses and emits back unchanged, and the new TestSchemaDocumentsApply in `make dbspec-ddl-check` renders each of the 5 documents with the Go renderer and applies it to MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite: 15 probes pass. The .mmd files and the old manifests stay for the old tests until T8.2.6.4.

- [o] T8.2.6.4 Remove the Mermaid schema source path, the PhysicalGraph dialect-text records and the annotated Markdown path, and give the CLI one name in every language (requires T8.2.6.3). Evidence: 246 files are removed: Go 84 (engine/schema, internal/ormgen, the clients/go/orm physical records, tests/schema/record), PHP 46, TypeScript 45, Rust 45, 17 fixtures of contracts/, schema/bench.mmd, schema/schema.json, tests/schema, scripts/schema and scripts/db-test.sh. They are the Mermaid parser, the manifest JSON and `orm-schema-v1` SQL, every CLI command that read them (build, import, validate, ddl, diff, migrate, plan, apply, verify, recover, rollback), whose dbspec counterparts are the render, introspect, plan, apply and recover functions of each client, and the PhysicalGraph records. The CLI is `orm-gen` in Go (cmd/orm-gen with `gen` and `errors`; the model generator moved into package generator), PHP and TypeScript (`gen` only); the Rust `orm-gen` binary had only Mermaid commands and is removed, so Rust generates models in build.rs only. Generated files say `Code generated by orm-gen`. The interface check expands entity rules from schema/bench.dbs, scripts/decimal-db-setup.php renders contracts/fixtures/decimal_schema.dbs, and the PHP examples no longer pass the removed schema path. The targets schema-check, schema-cross-language-check, db-test and the physical-*-check targets are removed, and CI runs ts-model-check in place of schema-check. contracts/symbols drop 315 Go, 334 PHP (8 added for OrmGen), 824 TypeScript and 682 Rust symbols; contracts/features.json drops point_type and maps schema_definition to the dbspec tests. checklist-check, docs-rules-check, go-fmt-check, rust-fmt-check, interface-check, rust-check, dbspec-go-check, dbspec-php-check, dbspec-ts-check, dbspec-rust-check, dbspec-compare-check, client-unit-check, ts-check, ts-model-check, go-model-check, conformance-check (25 vectors and 4 languages on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite), docs-check (48 pages, 545 internal links) and `go test` of the generator, CLI, interface, conformance, dialect and engine packages pass. feature-check fails as at the previous commit, on the dsn_connection line of docs/features.md that differs from contracts/features.json and on the T15 coverage errors; its feature manifest errors fell from 156 to 69 and none was added. Two raw quotes of the checklist (`<value>`, `<nil>`) are now code spans, because VitePress stopped on them and docs-check failed.

Acceptance: the lock file matches the manifests and `cargo check --locked` passes. Evidence: with the committed lock file `cargo check --locked --offline` failed with that error; after the entry is removed it passes, and `make rust-check` passes.

- [o] T8.3.1 Specify the statements each dialect renders from a dbspec document set (statement order, type texts, renderer CHECK names and texts, collations, keys, indexes, foreign keys, and the triggers of `immutable` and `audit`) as shared vectors, and apply every vector on MySQL 8.4, PostgreSQL 17 and SQLite in the dialect probe environment (requires T8.2.6.2). The first run found that PostgreSQL stores the input hour 24 of a timestamp as the next midnight, which the record and the vector now state, and that a behavior query left the MySQL keyword `change` unquoted.

- [o] T8.3.5 Render the T8.3.1 statements in the Rust client (requires T8.3.1). One run while the files were being restaged exited 1; its log was overwritten before it was read, and four later runs, one with the Makefile of that moment, passed. fmt, clippy and the four-language interface-check pass after recording the Rust symbols.
