- [o] T8.4 Introspect MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite into dbspec in every client (requires T8.3). Acceptance: query count independent of table count; CHECK normal-form and SQLite CREATE parsing; restoration of rendered settings by exact renderer equality without markers; unsupported objects reported with locations. Evidence: T8.4.1–T8.4.6 with T8.4.2.1–T8.4.2.3 and T8.4.3.1: the four clients introspect MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite with 9, 7 and 3 catalog queries whatever the table count, restore the schema text of every ddl.json vector and schema document, report the unsupported cases of tests/dbspec/introspect.json, and agree on the 2000-table stress database within 5 seconds each.
- [o] T8.4.1 Specify how each dialect is introspected into a dbspec document (catalog queries whose count does not depend on the table count, the reading of types, defaults, identity, keys, indexes, foreign keys and checks, renderer CHECKs and triggers recognized by equality with the renderer output, and the unsupported objects reported with their location), with round-trip vectors: every vector of tests/dbspec/ddl.json and every schema document is rendered, applied, introspected and compared by schema text on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite, and objects that dbspec does not support are reported (requires T8.3.1). Evidence: docs/dialects.md "Introspection" and its Korean pair state the catalog queries, the type table per dialect, null, identity and default reading, keys, indexes and foreign keys, the renderer CHECK forms, the check decoding per dialect, trigger recognition, the unsupported kinds, their order and the removal of objects that refer to a left-out object; tests/dbspec/introspect.json holds one case per dialect with its documents, statements, expected document and unsupported list.
- [o] T8.4.2 Introspect into dbspec in the Go client (requires T8.4.1). Evidence: engine/dbspec/introspect*.go `Introspect(ctx, querier, dialect, name)` returns the document and the unsupported objects; the physical tests failed to build without it (`undefined: dbspec.Introspect`); `make dbspec-introspect-check` passes 30 round trips of the 5 ddl.json vectors and 5 schema documents on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite with equal schema text and no unsupported object, asserts 8, 7 and 3 queries for every set, and passes the 3 cases of tests/dbspec/introspect.json, whose assertions fail when an expected entry is removed.
- [o] T8.4.2.1 Report each unsupported object once and read SQLite table items exactly in every client (follows T8.4.2). Cause: a table rejected together with one of its objects made the Go introspector dereference a nil table; a multi-column MySQL foreign key with an unsupported action was reported once per column; an unrecognized SQLite table item was reported as kind `check` named by its text, and a table-level `UNIQUE` was reported again through its automatic index; a SQLite column with an inline `CHECK` or `REFERENCES` clause was read without it. The PHP, TypeScript and Rust ports copied this behavior. Evidence: tests/dbspec/introspect.json gains `mysql-dependents-once` (an `int unsigned` primary key column with an index, and a two-column `NO ACTION` foreign key) and `sqlite-table-items` (an unnamed `CHECK`, a table-level `UNIQUE`, an inline `CHECK` and an inline `REFERENCES`); before the fix Go and PHP stopped on the nil table and TypeScript and Rust failed both cases, and after it `make dbspec-introspect-check`, `dbspec-introspect-php-check`, `dbspec-introspect-ts-check` and `dbspec-introspect-rust-check` pass 5 unsupported cases and 30 round trips each; docs/dialects.md states the rules.
- [o] T8.4.2.2 Recognize MySQL renderer CHECKs after `ALTER TABLE` in every client (follows T8.4.2.1). Cause: the 2000-table comparison reported every `uuid` and `time` column of the MySQL stress database as unsupported: adding the foreign keys with `ALTER TABLE` made MySQL write `CHECK_CLAUSE` again with an `_ascii` introducer on the uuid pattern and no introducer on the time literals, which no longer equals the renderer form. Evidence: probe `mysql.check.alter_rewrites_introducers` records that `ALTER TABLE t ADD INDEX` rewrites `regexp_like(...,_utf8mb4\'...)` to `_ascii` and drops the time literal introducers; the vector `mysql-checks-after-alter` failed in Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust with "document differs" and passes once each client compares a renderer CHECK with its introducers removed; `make dialect-facts-check` and the four introspection checks pass.
- [o] T8.4.2.3 Read MySQL checks without the slow catalog join in every client (follows T8.4.2.2). Cause: the 2000-table comparison took 4 minutes 23 seconds to introspect MySQL; the query that joins `CHECK_CONSTRAINTS` with `TABLE_CONSTRAINTS` took 63.7 seconds alone on the stress database, while each catalog view read alone took under 0.1 second. Evidence: on the 2000-table MySQL stress database the joined check query took 63.7 seconds and the two queries that replace it 0.09 and 0.08 seconds; the four clients read `CHECK_CONSTRAINTS` and `TABLE_CONSTRAINTS` apart and join them by the database-unique check name, failing on a check without a clause; the four introspection checks pass and assert 9 MySQL queries for every set.
- [o] T8.4.2.4 Recognize the MySQL time CHECK of a `time(p)` column after `ALTER TABLE` in every client (follows T8.4.2.3). Cause: the first migration plan that raised a column to `time(3)` reported it unsupported on MySQL: the rewritten `CHECK_CLAUSE` reads `\'00:00:00.000\'`, which T8.4.2.2 did not remove. Evidence: probe `mysql.check.alter_writes_time_precision` records that `ALTER TABLE t ADD INDEX` writes a zero fraction of three digits into the time literal of a `time(3)` column CHECK; the vector `mysql-time-precision-after-alter` failed in Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust and passes once each client also removes the zero fraction of a time literal before comparing; the four introspection checks and `make dialect-facts-check` pass.

Acceptance: dependency-ordered plans, explicit renames in the plan chain, explicit permission for destructive changes. Evidence: T8.5.1 to T8.5.7 and their follow-ups are complete: docs/plans.md specifies plan documents, chains, the diff, the statement order and the comparison; the four clients pass the shared plan, invalid, chain, parse and comparison cases and agree in `make dbspec-compare-check`; `make dbspec-plan-check` and its client variants apply every case to the three databases.
- [o] T8.5.1 Specify plan documents, the plan chain, the diff, the statements of each dialect and the SQLite rebuild in docs/plans.md and its Korean pair, with the shared cases of tests/dbspec/plans.json (requires T8.4). Evidence: docs/plans.md and docs/plans.ko.md state the plan document, its canonical form, the chain, the diff with its changes, widenings, refusals and forced recreations, the ten statement steps and the SQLite rebuild; tests/dbspec/plans.json holds 9 cases with their changes and the statements of MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite and 8 invalid cases with their diagnostics; docs-rules-check passes 23 document pairs.
- [o] T8.5.1.1 Keep the SQLite automatic key counter across a rebuild (follows T8.5.1). Cause: the rebuild of docs/plans.md copies the rows into `"$rebuild"` with `INSERT … SELECT`, drops the table and renames the copy, which leaves `sqlite_sequence` at the largest copied key; after rows with the largest keys were deleted, the next insert reuses a deleted key (observed with `sqlite3`: keys 1, 2, 3, delete 3, rebuild, insert gives 3), while docs/dialects.md "Automatic key" states that keys are never reused. Acceptance: the rebuild carries the counter of `sqlite_sequence`, with a tracked plan case that deletes the largest key before the rebuild in every client. Evidence: docs/plans.md "Statements" states the carry: after `CREATE TABLE "$rebuild"`, a table with an `identity` column runs `INSERT INTO sqlite_sequence (name, seq) SELECT '$rebuild', seq FROM sqlite_sequence WHERE name = '<table>'` before the `INSERT … SELECT`, so the copy keeps the largest key ever given and SQLite updates that row instead of adding one (checked with `sqlite3` 3.51.0: keys 1, 2, 3, delete 3, rebuild, insert gives 4, and 4 also when every row was deleted). tests/dbspec/plans.json gains the statement in the 9 SQLite rebuilds of identity tables and the case `rebuild-keeps-key-counter`: table `k` gains a check, which rebuilds it on SQLite; before, rows 1, 2 and 3 are inserted and 3 is deleted; after, an insert must make `SELECT MAX(id) FROM k` 4 and no row has id 3. Red: Go `TestPlanVectors`, PHP clients/php/tests/dbspec_plan_test.php, TypeScript clients/typescript/tests/dbspec-plan.mjs and Rust `plan_vectors` failed on the SQLite statements (Go, TypeScript and Rust on 8 cases, PHP at its first, rename-table-and-column), and `make dbspec-plan-php-check`, `make dbspec-plan-ts-check` and `make dbspec-plan-rust-check`, which apply each client's own statements, failed on SQLite with `SELECT MAX(id) FROM k: got "3"; want "4"`. Green: the four planners write the statement; the four vector tests pass, and `make dbspec-plan-check` (the vector statements) and the PHP, TypeScript and Rust plan checks pass `rebuild-keeps-key-counter` on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite. Gates passed: dbspec-go-check, dbspec-php-check, dbspec-ts-check, dbspec-rust-check, dbspec-compare-check, dbspec-plan-check, dbspec-plan-php-check, dbspec-plan-ts-check, dbspec-plan-rust-check, dbspec-apply-check, dbspec-apply-php-check, dbspec-apply-ts-check, dbspec-apply-rust-check, dbspec-apply-pairs-check, ts-check, rust-check, rust-fmt-check, go-fmt-check, interface-check, docs-rules-check and checklist-check.
- [o] T8.5.2 Parse, chain, diff and write plans in the Go engine (requires T8.5.1). Evidence: engine/dbspec/plan*.go `ParsePlan`, `EmitPlan`, `Chain`, `Diff` and `PlanStatements`; the plan tests did not build without them (`undefined: ParsePlan`); `make dbspec-go-check` passes the 9 cases (changes, statements of three dialects and canonical emission), the 8 invalid cases and the 4 chain cases of tests/dbspec/plans.json; `make dbspec-plan-check` passes 27 runs: each case applied to MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite reaches its target schema text with its data steps.
- [o] T8.5.2.1 Keep the Go plan writer free of dropped results and unused state (follows T8.5.2). Cause: `EmitPlan` discarded the diagnostics of `ManifestOf` with `_`, and the renderer field `tables`, which `Render` and `PlanStatements` filled, was never read, so `PlanStatements` built a second renderer for nothing. Evidence: `ParsePlan` keeps the target schema text it validated and `EmitPlan` writes it, so no result is discarded; the renderer has no `tables` field and `PlanStatements` uses one renderer; `make dbspec-go-check` passes the plan cases, invalid cases and chains, and `make dbspec-plan-check` passes 27 runs.
- [o] T8.5.2.2 Reject plan names over 63 bytes and cover plan parse errors with shared cases (follows T8.5.2.1). Cause: the Go `ParsePlan` checked the `dbplan 1 <name>` name only against reserved words, so a 64-byte name passed although docs/plans.md requires the name rules, and tests/dbspec/plans.json had no case for the diagnostics of `ParsePlan`. Evidence: tests/dbspec/plans.json gains 12 parse cases ([rule, line, column, message], the message only for the rule plan); `TestPlanParseErrors` failed on `name-too-long` with no diagnostic before the fix and passes after `ParsePlan` rejects names over 63 bytes; `make dbspec-go-check` runs it.

Evidence: `clients/php/tests/dbspec_plan_test.php` fails without the plan classes (`Class "Orm\Dbspec\PlanStatements" not found`) and passes `cases=9 invalid=8 chains=4` in `make dbspec-php-check`; `make dbspec-plan-php-check` applies the 9 cases to MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite (`runs=27`) and reads each target back through PHP introspection.
- [o] T8.5.4 Parse, chain, diff and write plans in the TypeScript client (requires T8.5.1). Evidence: clients/typescript/src/dbspec/plan*.ts `parsePlan`, `emitPlan`, `chainPlans`, `diffPlan` and `planStatements`; the plan test failed before them because dist/dbspec/index.js did not export `chainPlans`; `make dbspec-ts-check` passes 190 tests, among them the 9 cases (changes, statements of three dialects and canonical emission), the 8 invalid cases and the 4 chain cases of tests/dbspec/plans.json and the unknown dialect TypeError; `make dbspec-plan-ts-check` passes 27 runs: each case applied to MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite reaches its target schema text with its data steps; contracts/symbols/typescript.json records the 107 new symbols and interface-check passes.
- [o] T8.5.5 Parse, chain, diff and write plans in the Rust client (requires T8.5.1). Evidence: orm_schema::dbspec `parse_plan`, `emit_plan`, `chain`, `diff` and `plan_statements` in clients/rust/orm-schema/src/dbspec/plan*.rs; the plan test did not build without them (unresolved imports); `make dbspec-rust-check` passes twice the 9 cases (changes, statements of three dialects and canonical emission), the 8 invalid cases and the 4 chain cases of tests/dbspec/plans.json; `make dbspec-plan-rust-check` passes 27 runs: each case applied to MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite through the Rust renderer, plan statements and `orm::dbspec::introspect` reaches its target schema text with its data steps.
- [o] T8.5.6 Compare the plans of the four clients in `make dbspec-compare-check` (requires T8.5.2-T8.5.5). Evidence: the PHP, TypeScript and Rust plan tests run the 12 parse cases of tests/dbspec/plans.json like Go `TestPlanParseErrors` ([rule, line, column, message], the message only for the rule plan); before the fix all three failed on `name-too-long` with no diagnostic, and now PHP, TypeScript and Rust reject plan names over 63 bytes with the Go message; `make dbspec-php-check` passes `cases=9 invalid=8 chains=4 parse=12` twice, `make dbspec-ts-check` passes 203 of 203 tests, `make dbspec-rust-check` passes `plan parse errors: 12 cases` twice and `make dbspec-go-check` passes the 12 `TestPlanParseErrors` subtests; the Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust compare runners take tests/dbspec/plans.json as a fourth argument and print 69 plan entries (emitted plan, changes and statements of three dialects for 9 cases, diagnostics of 8 invalid and 12 parse cases, order or diagnostics of 4 chains, with the message of every plan and chain diagnostic); `make dbspec-compare-check` passes `8 runs agree` with 186 cases per run, check.test.mjs passes 5 tests including a different plan message, and a Go runner printing table and name swapped failed at `plans/cases/create-from-empty/changes`.
- [o] T8.5.7 List the differences between two schemas in every client (follows T8.5.2-T8.5.5). Cause: the plan diff refuses type, identity, primary key and column order changes instead of listing them and needs an `allow drop` line for every drop, so a tool that compares a database with a model cannot show every difference. Acceptance: a shared comparison API and vectors list every difference between two schema texts as `[kind, table, name]`, including the changes a plan refuses, identically in the four clients. Evidence: docs/plans.md "Comparison" and its Korean pair specify the comparison, placed in the plan document because it shares the plan diff's kinds and widening: both sides are documents of schema texts, and a side that is not is a `compare` diagnostic naming it; tables and columns match by name; 21 kinds (`create_table`, `drop_table`, `drop_column`, `add_column`, `alter_column`, `change_column_type`, `change_column_identity`, `reorder_columns`, `change_primary_key`, `drop_`/`add_` of `unique`, `index`, `foreign_key`, `check`, `immutable` and `audit`) come in table name order, kind order and name order, and the comparison is empty exactly when the schema texts are equal. tests/dbspec/plans.json has 11 `comparisons` cases (same schema, tables from empty and on one side, columns, identity and primary key, column order, objects, settings and three sides that are not schema texts). Go `CompareSchemas`, PHP `Dbspec::compareSchemas`, TypeScript `compareSchemas` and Rust `compare_schemas` implement it. Before the implementations, with a stub that returned no difference, Go `TestCompareSchemas` failed 10 of 11 cases, the PHP plan test stopped at `plan/comparison/tables-from-empty`, the TypeScript plan test failed 10 of 45 tests and Rust `plan_comparisons` reported 10 failures; now `TestCompareSchemas` passes 11 subtests, PHP prints `comparisons=11`, TypeScript passes 45 of 45 and Rust passes `plan comparisons: 11 cases`. The four compare runners print every comparison as `plans/comparisons/<id>` with `| kind table name` lines or `! compare 1 1 <message>`; with the TypeScript runner of the commit before, `make dbspec-compare-check` failed with `typescript 1 differs from go 1 in plans/comparisons/same-schema` and runners.test.mjs failed its two new TypeScript cases (`comparisons` missing, `comparisons[0].target` not an array); now the 85 runner tests pass and the 8 runs agree on 213 cases. `TestCaseHarnessReportsOnlyFailure` also requires a FAIL line for a comparison case. `dbspec-go-check`, `dbspec-php-check`, `dbspec-ts-check`, `dbspec-rust-check`, `dbspec-compare-check`, `rust-check`, `rust-fmt-check`, `go-fmt-check`, `interface-check`, `docs-rules-check` and `checklist-check` pass.
- [o] T8.5.6.1 Reject a missing vector section or field in the dbspec compare runners (follows T8.5.6). Cause: the Go runner decodes the vectors into json structs and the Rust runner reads them with `into_iter().flatten()`, `unwrap_or_default` and `filter_map`, so a missing section, id or line becomes empty instead of an error, and runners that agree on the empty value pass the comparison. Acceptance: every runner rejects a missing or mistyped section, id, document or line with a located error and a nonzero exit; a test removes a section and a field and sees each runner fail. Evidence: tests/dbspec/compare/runners.test.mjs runs the Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust runners (tests/dbspec/compare/runners.mjs, shared with check.mjs) on 19 copies of the vectors, each with one section or field removed or mistyped (`canonical`, `hashes`, `canonical[0].id`, `invalid[0].main`, the main document, a document line, `crlf`, `hashes[0].documents`, ddl `cases` and `cases[0].id`, plans `chains`, `cases[0].source`, `invalid[0].plan`, `chains[0].plans[0]` and `parse[0].id`, mermaid `round_trip`, `export[0].documents`, `import[0].mermaid` and `invalid[0].mermaid[0]`), and requires a nonzero exit with `<file>: <location> <problem>` on stderr. Before the change 76 of its 77 tests failed: 48 runs exited 0 (Go 13, PHP 12, TypeScript 6, Rust 17), and the other 28 failed without the located error (Go json decoder messages without the file, PHP warnings and a TypeError, TypeScript TypeErrors, Rust `mermaid vectors: round_trip is not an array`). Each runner now reads every vector file through a reader that requires each section, case object, `id`, `main` and its document, document set, line array and string line and the `source` key (null or lines), takes an absent `crlf` or `mixed` as false and rejects a non-boolean one, and names the file of a JSON syntax error; the 77 tests pass. `make dbspec-compare-check` runs the test after the builds and the 8 runs agree on 202 cases; `make dbspec-go-check`, `dbspec-php-check`, `dbspec-ts-check`, `dbspec-rust-check`, `rust-fmt-check`, `go-fmt-check`, `rust-check`, `interface-check`, `docs-rules-check` and `checklist-check` pass.
- [o] T8.6 Apply plans with lock, step journal, step events, verification and recovery in every client (requires T8.5). Acceptance: apply then introspect then diff is empty on all three databases; an interrupted non-transactional step is detected from the catalog. Evidence: T8.6.1 to T8.6.6 and their follow-ups are complete: docs/plans.md "Apply" specifies the lock, history, state, statements, verification, events, recovery and failure shapes; `make dbspec-apply-check` and its PHP, TypeScript and Rust variants pass 17 runs each on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite, including MySQL recovery from the catalog; `make dbspec-apply-pairs-check` continues a chain across every ordered pair of clients in 48 runs.
- [o] T8.6.1 Specify applying plans in docs/plans.md "Apply" and its Korean pair: the lock, the history table `dbspec$plans` that introspection leaves out, the state and drift checks, the transactions of each dialect, verification, events and MySQL recovery from statement effects (requires T8.5.1). Evidence: docs/plans.md "Apply" and docs/plans.ko.md state the MySQL and PostgreSQL locks and the SQLite transaction, the history table and its columns, the state, drift and chain errors, the transactions of each dialect, verification with nothing unsupported, the five events, idempotent apply and recover, and recovery from the catalog effect of the interrupted MySQL statement; docs/dialects.md leaves `dbspec$plans` out of introspection; docs-rules-check passes.
- [o] T8.6.2 Apply and recover plans in the Go engine (requires T8.6.1). Evidence: engine/dbspec/apply.go `Apply`, `Recover`, `ApplyEvent` and `ApplyError`; the apply test did not build without them (`undefined: dbspec.ApplyError`); `make dbspec-apply-check` passes 16 runs: the two-plan chain with two `done` history rows, matching events and a second apply without events on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite; drift, lock and verify errors on the three; rollback after a failed statement on PostgreSQL and SQLite; MySQL recovery after a stop before and after a statement, which fails when the effect check is disabled; introspection leaves `dbspec$plans` out and `make dbspec-introspect-check`, `dbspec-plan-check` and `dbspec-ddl-check` pass.
- [o] T8.6.2.1 Report the cleanup errors of a failed Go apply (follows T8.6.2). Cause: when the apply body failed, Go dropped the error of `RELEASE_LOCK`, `pg_advisory_unlock`, `ROLLBACK`, `COMMIT` and `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON`, ignored the restore after a failed `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, accepted an advisory unlock that released nothing, read a MySQL effect query without a row as an absent effect, and dropped the error of closing the rows, against the rule that every error is reported. Evidence: engine/dbspec/apply_test.go `TestApplyReportsCleanupErrors` and `TestMySQLEffectRequiresRow` wrap a SQLite connection to inject failures; before the fix they failed (`stop, want both errors`; `locked: … begin failed, want locked and the restore error`; `no error`) and they pass after apply joins every cleanup error with `errors.Join`, rejects an unlock that released nothing and reports a missing effect row; `make dbspec-go-check` runs them and `make dbspec-apply-check` still passes 16 runs.
- [o] T8.6.2.2 Assert every apply cleanup error in every client (follows T8.6.2.1). Cause: only PHP has a case for a PostgreSQL unlock that released nothing (`unlock_not_held`); the Go, TypeScript and Rust cleanup behaviour is not asserted by equivalent cases, and the PHP `Dbspec::apply` documentation says every failure is an ApplyError although history write errors and event handler exceptions pass through unchanged. Acceptance: Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust each run the cleanup cases (failed ROLLBACK, failed restore after `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, effect query without a row, unlock that released nothing) and the client documentation states the failure shapes. Evidence: the new PostgreSQL run `unlock_not_held` of tests/dialects/apply_physical_test.go, clients/typescript/tests/dbspec-apply-physical.mjs and clients/rust/orm/tests/dbspec_apply.rs releases the advisory lock from an event; it failed in TypeScript (`Missing expected rejection`) and Rust (`Ok(())`), which ignored the result of `pg_advisory_unlock`, and passes after both reject an unlock that released nothing (Rust as `ApplyError::LockNotHeld`); the Go run failed (`<nil>`) with the check disabled once and passes restored. clients/typescript/tests/dbspec-apply-cleanup.mjs and clients/rust/orm/tests/dbspec_apply_cleanup.rs run 3 cases each (failed ROLLBACK, failed restore after a failed `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, effect query without a row through a scripted MySQL connection); they passed at once and failed with the cleanup join and the row check broken once (`Error: stop alone`, `Err(Event(Stop))`, `unexpected query UPDATE` / `unexpected statement UPDATE`), then passed restored. Go returned a failure without cleanup errors wrapped by `errors.Join`; the new check of `TestApplyReportsCleanupErrors` failed (`&errors.joinError{…}, want the event error itself`) and passes after `joinCleanup` returns a single error unchanged. Rust `ApplyError::Cleanup` holds its cleanup error as an `ApplyError`; docs/plans.md "Apply" states the failure shape of every client and the PHP `Dbspec::apply` docblock lists the errors that propagate unchanged. `make dbspec-go-check`, `dbspec-ts-check`, `dbspec-rust-check` (2 runs of 3 cases), `dbspec-php-check`, `dbspec-apply-check` (17 runs), `dbspec-apply-php-check` (17), `dbspec-apply-ts-check` (17), `dbspec-apply-rust-check` (17), `rust-check`, `rust-fmt-check`, `go-fmt-check`, `interface-check` and `docs-rules-check` pass.
- [o] T8.6.3 Apply and recover plans in the PHP client (requires T8.6.1). Evidence: clients/php/src/Dbspec/PlanApply.php behind `Dbspec::apply` and `Dbspec::recover`, with `ApplyEvent` and `ApplyError` (codes locked, interrupted, drift, chain, failed, verify); the apply test failed before them with `Call to undefined method Orm\Dbspec\Dbspec::apply()`; `make dbspec-apply-php-check` passes 16 runs: the two-plan chain with two `done` history rows, 2 plan, 2 verified and 2 done events with equal statement and applied counts, and a second apply without events on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite; drift, lock and verify errors on the three; rollback after a stopped statement on PostgreSQL and SQLite; MySQL recovery after a stop before and after a statement, and `recover_after_applied` fails with `Unknown column 'mail'` when the effect check always reports the effect missing; PHP introspection leaves `dbspec$plans` out with the table queries of the Go engine byte for byte; `make dbspec-php-check`, `dbspec-introspect-php-check`, `dbspec-plan-php-check` and interface-check pass.
- [o] T8.6.3.1 Report the cleanup errors of a failed PHP apply (follows T8.6.3). Cause: PlanApply copied the earlier Go behaviour: when the apply body failed it dropped the error of `RELEASE_LOCK`, `pg_advisory_unlock`, `ROLLBACK`, `COMMIT` and `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON`, ignored the restore after a failed `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, accepted an advisory unlock that released nothing, read a MySQL effect query without a row as the value `false`, and never checked closing a result, against the rule that every error is reported. Evidence: clients/php/tests/dbspec_apply_cleanup_test.php wraps a SQLite PDO (`FailingPdo`, `FailingStatement`) to inject failures; before the fix its 4 cases failed (`RuntimeException "stop" alone; want it with the cleanup errors`; `PDOException "restore failed" alone`, the locked error lost; `UnexpectedValueException "Catalog value 0 is bool, not an integer"`; `no error` for a result that cannot be closed) and the new PostgreSQL run `unlock_not_held` of clients/php/tests/dbspec_apply_test.php failed with `succeeded`; they pass after `PlanApply::finish` runs every cleanup step and throws the single error unchanged or an `ApplyCleanupError` whose previous throwable is the first failure and whose `cleanup` lists the later errors, `queryValue` reports a missing row and a failed close, and the PostgreSQL unlock rejects a lock it did not hold; `make dbspec-php-check` runs the cleanup test twice and `make dbspec-apply-php-check` passes 17 runs; interface-check passes with the recorded PHP symbols.
- [o] T8.6.4 Apply and recover plans in the TypeScript client (requires T8.6.1). Evidence: clients/typescript/src/dbspec/apply.ts `applyPlans`, `recoverPlans`, `DbspecApplyEvent` and `DbspecApplyError`; the apply test did not load without them (`does not provide an export named 'DbspecApplyError'`); `make dbspec-apply-ts-check` passes 16 runs: the two-plan chain with two `done` history rows, two plan, verified and done events, equal statement and applied events and a second apply without events on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite; drift, lock and verify errors on the three; rollback after a stop on PostgreSQL and SQLite; MySQL recovery after a stop before and after a statement, where recover_after_applied fails when the effect check always reports absent; introspection leaves `dbspec$plans` out with the Go query texts and `make dbspec-introspect-ts-check`, `dbspec-plan-ts-check`, `dbspec-ts-check`, `ts-check` and `interface-check` pass.

- [o] T8.6.6 Continue a chain that another client applied, in every pair of clients (requires T8.6.2-T8.6.5). Evidence: tests/dbspec/apply holds an apply runner per client (Go, PHP, TypeScript and clients/rust/orm/examples/dbspec_apply.rs) with the actions apply-first, apply, stop and recover; tests/dialects/apply_clients_physical_test.go `TestApplyChainAcrossClients` runs, in per-run databases named after the pid and a random value, the 12 ordered pairs on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite (client A applies create-from-empty, client B the chain) and 12 MySQL recoveries (A stops after statement 1 of rename-table-and-column, the row is `running 1`, B's apply is `error interrupted` and B recovers), requiring the 2 history rows with their hashes, `done`, steps and `applied_at`, and the introspected schema text of the chain's target. It passed at once; with PHP writing an empty `from_hash` instead of `empty` once, the 12 runs where PHP applies first failed (`go apply: "error chain" after 20ms, want "ok"` and differing history rows) and the other 36 passed, then all passed restored. `make dbspec-apply-pairs-check` (48 runs, in the `check` aggregate), `go-fmt-check`, `rust-fmt-check`, `rust-check`, `interface-check` and `docs-rules-check` pass, and no `dbspec_pair_` database remains on MySQL or PostgreSQL.
- [o] T8.6.7 Scope the apply lock to one database and accept an empty plan chain (follows T8.6.6). Cause: MySQL `GET_LOCK('dbspec$plans')` is server-wide and the PostgreSQL advisory lock covers every schema of a database, so applies to two databases or schemas collide with `locked`; and `chain` rejects an empty list with "no plan starts from empty", although a project without plans is a valid empty chain for a database without tables. Acceptance: docs/plans.md names the lock per database or schema and the empty chain, and every client follows it with shared cases. Evidence: docs/plans.md "Lock" names the lock of one database: MySQL `GET_LOCK(CONCAT('dbspec$plans$', LEFT(SHA2(DATABASE(), 256), 51)), 0)`, a 64-character name, the MySQL limit, for a database name of any length, and PostgreSQL `pg_try_advisory_lock(hashtext('dbspec$plans'), hashtext(current_schema()))`; a result other than MySQL `1`/`0` or PostgreSQL `true`/`false` is an error, where Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust had read a MySQL NULL as `locked` and PHP a PostgreSQL NULL as `locked`. "Chain" and "Recovery" state the empty chain: no plan is a valid chain, and its apply creates the history table, requires a database without a table and reports no event. tests/dbspec/plans.json adds the chain case `empty` (`plans: []`, `order: []`). Red: Go `TestPlanChains/empty`, PHP clients/php/tests/dbspec_plan_test.php, TypeScript `plan chain empty` and Rust `plan_chains` failed with "no plan starts from empty"; the new `other_database` scenario, which applies the chain to a second database (MySQL), schema (PostgreSQL) or file (SQLite) from an event of an apply that holds its lock, failed on MySQL and PostgreSQL in Go (`make dbspec-apply-check`), PHP, TypeScript and Rust with `locked: another session holds GET_LOCK('dbspec$plans')` and `locked: another session holds the advisory lock of dbspec$plans`, and the `lock` and `unlock_not_held` scenarios, which hold or release the new key, failed against the old one. Green: the four chain functions return the empty chain for no plan and the four appliers use the new lock name and key; `make dbspec-apply-check` passes, `make dbspec-apply-php-check` reports `runs=23`, `make dbspec-apply-ts-check` `apply runs: 23` and `make dbspec-apply-rust-check` `23 runs on three databases`, each including `other_database` and `empty_chain` (apply and recover of the empty chain without an event, zero history rows, `drift` after a table is created) on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite. The Go, PHP and Rust probe harnesses create and remove the second database, schema or file `<name>_b` (tests/dialects/README.md). Gates passed: dbspec-go-check, dbspec-php-check, dbspec-ts-check, dbspec-rust-check, dbspec-compare-check, dbspec-plan-check, dbspec-apply-check, dbspec-apply-php-check, dbspec-apply-ts-check, dbspec-apply-rust-check, dbspec-apply-pairs-check, client-unit-check, ts-check, rust-check, rust-fmt-check, go-fmt-check, interface-check, docs-rules-check and checklist-check.
- [o] T8.6.8 Apply plans with one step-by-step guarantee and rollback statements on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite (follows T8.6.2). Cause: docs/plans.md "Apply" gives each database a different guarantee: PostgreSQL rolls a plan back in one transaction, SQLite rolls the whole apply back, and MySQL commits every statement because DDL commits implicitly, so the same plan fails and recovers differently per database; the one-transaction PostgreSQL apply also holds an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock on every changed table until commit, which blocks every read and write of those tables for the whole apply, and failed on the first plan of a 2000-table round trip (20014 statements) with `out of shared memory`. An all-or-nothing schema change needs a maintenance window or a blue-green switch, which the operator chooses and orm cannot enforce. Acceptance: docs/plans.md states one guarantee for the three databases: DDL runs outside a transaction, statement by statement, each followed by its recorded history step; an interrupted apply stops at a known step and `recover` decides from the catalog whether the last statement took effect and continues; every statement has a rollback statement, and a rollback undoes exactly the applied steps in reverse order and can itself be interrupted and continued; statements that destroy data move it aside and a final step removes it, so everything stays reversible until that step; lock waits fail fast instead of queueing; the documentation states that atomicity is the operator's choice (maintenance window or blue-green). Every client follows it with shared cases that interrupt and roll back at each step on the three databases, and a tracked case applies the 2000-table plan on all three. Evidence: docs/plans.md "Steps" and "Apply" and the Korean pair specify one guarantee for the three databases: every statement commits on its own, outside any transaction, with its recorded step; every step has a rollback statement and an effect; dropped tables and columns are hidden under `dbspec$hold$<h>$<n>` until finalize, added columns are hidden on rollback and restored by a second apply, SQLite rebuilds copy rows both ways through `dbspec$rebuild`, a rollback of an applied plan fills the NULL rows of a column it makes non-null with the source default or stops with `nulls`, the only irreversible steps are `time` and `datetime` precision widenings, lock waits end after 5 seconds, and atomicity is the operator's choice. Go `PlanSteps`, `Apply`, `Recover`, `Rollback` and `Finalize`, PHP `Dbspec::planSteps`, `apply`, `recover`, `rollback` and `finalize`, TypeScript `planSteps`, `applyPlans`, `recoverPlans`, `rollbackPlans` and `finalizePlans`, and Rust `plan_steps`, `apply`, `recover`, `rollback` and `finalize` replace `PlanStatements`, the plan transactions and the MySQL statement-effect table; the introspection of the four clients leaves out every `dbspec$` table and column. tests/dbspec/plans.json lists the steps of 12 cases in three dialects, with the new cases `representative` and `drop-required-column`. Red: on the previous implementation the new tests did not build or load (Go `undefined: PlanStep`, PHP `Call to undefined method Orm\Dbspec\Dbspec::planSteps()`, TypeScript `does not provide an export named 'effectText'`, Rust `unresolved imports orm_schema::dbspec::plan_steps`), and with recover and rollback ignoring the catalog effect `make dbspec-apply-check` failed 69 of 119 runs (`mysql.apply.interrupt_00`: the rollback left an unsupported trigger). Green: `make dbspec-apply-check` passes 119 runs: the chain with history and events, drift, lock, a second database, the empty chain through the four commands, an unlock that released nothing, verification, `rows_between` (rows written after the apply survive the rollback with the hidden column's value or default, a second apply brings back the added column's values, finalize leaves no `dbspec$` table or column and a later rollback is `irreversible`), `nulls` (`has 1 NULL rows`, nothing changed) and the stop after every step of `representative` (25 MySQL, 31 PostgreSQL and 38 SQLite steps), each followed by a rollback to the source, a recover to the target and a stopped rollback that continues, with the history rows asserted each time; `TestApplyStressPlan` applies the 2000-table document as a first plan in 22000 steps on MySQL (15m9s) and on PostgreSQL with `max_locks_per_transaction` 64 (1m54s) and in 12000 steps on SQLite (46s), each ending with the history row `applied`. `make dbspec-apply-php-check`, `dbspec-apply-ts-check` and `dbspec-apply-rust-check` pass the same 119 runs; `make dbspec-plan-check` and its PHP, TypeScript and Rust variants pass 36 runs each and roll back and apply again every reversible case; `make dbspec-apply-pairs-check` passes 108 runs (chain, recover and rollback across every ordered pair of clients on three databases); `make dbspec-compare-check` requires equal steps with their rollback, effect, restore and null checks from the four clients. MySQL and PostgreSQL turn 03:04:05.6 into 03:04:06 when a `datetime(6)` column narrows to `datetime(0)` without an error, while narrowing a long `varchar` fails (MySQL ERROR 1265, PostgreSQL `value too long`), which makes precision widening the irreversible step; SQLite carries other tables' foreign keys through `ALTER TABLE … RENAME TO` only with `legacy_alter_table` off, which every command sets. Gates passed: dbspec-go-check, dbspec-php-check, dbspec-ts-check, dbspec-rust-check, dbspec-compare-check, dbspec-plan-check, dbspec-plan-php-check, dbspec-plan-ts-check, dbspec-plan-rust-check, dbspec-apply-check, dbspec-apply-php-check, dbspec-apply-ts-check, dbspec-apply-rust-check, dbspec-apply-pairs-check, rust-check, rust-fmt-check, go-fmt-check, interface-check, docs-rules-check and checklist-check.

- [o] T8.7.2.1 Correct the Mermaid defects of every client (follows T8.7.2). Cause: Go `ImportMermaid` panics (`index out of range`) on a relationship label whose column count differs from its referenced column count; Go `mermaidImportType` ignores the `strconv.Atoi` error, so a length or precision above the int range becomes 0, and the clients decide such a type at different points, so their dropped lists differ for a key column; export reports only table and column comments and drops the comments of primary key, unique, index, foreign key, check, settings and `use` lines, closing comments of blocks and trailing comments of the document silently; the import comment pattern accepts `nullidentity` as `null identity`; two relationships over the same FK columns add the index `ix_<table>_<columns>` twice and report it twice; the T8.7.2 evidence claims 16 objects for the schema/bench.dbs round trip, the engines report 17, and no test asserts it. Evidence: the cases of tests/dbspec/mermaid.json grew to 2 export, 6 import, 4 invalid and 1 round trip case. Before the fix Go panicked on `import/label-column-count` (`index out of range [1] with length 1`) and, with that corrected, failed `export/every-comment`, `import/type-ranges` (tables c and d dropped), `import/comment-spacing` (`nullidentity` read as `null identity`) and `import/repeated-relationship` (`ix_b_a_id` reported three times); PHP failed `export/every-comment`, stopped on `import/label-column-count` with `TypeError` (`Argument #2 ($n) must be of type string, null given`) and failed `import/type-ranges`, `import/comment-spacing` and `import/repeated-relationship`; TypeScript and Rust failed `export/every-comment`, `import/type-ranges` (table e with `varchar(16384) code PK` dropped), `import/comment-spacing` and `import/repeated-relationship`. All four pass the 13 cases after each client skips the columns of a label whose column count differs from its referenced column count, decides the dbspec type ranges (varchar 1-16383, time and datetime 0-6, decimal p 1-18 and s 0-p) before keys are read, reports every comment export leaves out with the kinds docs/mermaid.md now states, requires single spaces between the comment parts, and adds the index of a column list once. The round trip case asserts in every client that exporting schema/bench.dbs drops exactly the 17 listed objects and importing the export drops exactly the 9 listed objects and gives back the tables, columns, primary keys and foreign keys of schema/bench.dbs. `dbspec-go-check`, `dbspec-php-check`, `dbspec-ts-check`, `dbspec-rust-check`, `dbspec-compare-check`, `interface-check`, `go-fmt-check`, `rust-fmt-check`, `rust-check`, `docs-rules-check` and `checklist-check` pass.

- [o] T8.7.6 Compare the Mermaid results of the four clients in `make dbspec-compare-check` (requires T8.7.2-T8.7.5). Evidence: tests/dbspec/compare/check.mjs takes tests/dbspec/mermaid.json as a fifth argument, and the Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust runners (tests/dbspec/compare/go, php.php, typescript.mjs and clients/rust/orm-schema/examples/dbspec_compare.rs) print every export, import, invalid and round trip case: the Mermaid text or emitted document, the dropped objects as `= kind<TAB>table<TAB>name` without reasons, the diagnostics as `! rule line column`, and for the round trip the export of schema/bench.dbs and then its import. `make dbspec-compare-check` prints 200 cases per run instead of 186 and its 8 runs agree; with the TypeScript Mermaid source of the commit before T8.7.2.1 built, it fails with `typescript 1 differs from go 1 in mermaid/export/every-comment`. tests/dbspec/compare/check.test.mjs passes 6 tests, including a different Mermaid dropped object, and docs/mermaid.md and its Korean pair describe the comparison. `dbspec-go-check`, `dbspec-php-check`, `dbspec-ts-check`, `dbspec-rust-check`, `interface-check`, `go-fmt-check`, `rust-fmt-check`, `rust-check`, `docs-rules-check` and `checklist-check` pass.
- [o] T8.7.6.1 Make the dbspec test harnesses report failures and missing inputs (follows T8.7.6). Cause: Go `TestMermaidVectors` logs `PASS mermaid/<case>` after `t.Errorf`, so a failing case also prints a PASS line, and tests/dbspec/compare/php.php reads its inputs with `(string) file_get_contents(...)`, so an unreadable file becomes empty text. Acceptance: a failing case prints only its failure, and every runner rejects an unreadable input with its path and a non-zero exit. Evidence: engine/dbspec/harness_test.go `TestCaseHarnessReportsOnlyFailure` runs `TestFailingCaseFixture` as a child `go test` with `DBSPEC_FAILING_CASE_FIXTURE=1`, which checks the first Mermaid export, import, invalid and round trip case and the first plan, invalid plan, chain and parse case each with one wrong expectation, and requires a `FAIL <case>` line and no `PASS <case>` line for each of the 8. Before the change it failed for all 8 (`no FAIL line`, `PASS line for the failing case`), because the Mermaid and plan checks logged PASS after `t.Errorf` and never a FAIL line; the Mermaid checks also logged `deadline=5s` without a deadline. The checks of `TestMermaidVectors`, `TestPlanVectors`, `TestPlanChains` and `TestPlanParseErrors` now return their mismatches joined with `errors.Join` to `runTimed`, which enforces the 5 s deadline and logs either PASS or FAIL, and report a failed JSON encoding; the test passes and `make dbspec-go-check` runs it. The other Go dbspec tests already logged through `runTimed` or `logResult`. tests/dbspec/inputs.test.mjs runs the four compare runners with a missing file and a directory in place of each of the 5 inputs and of the round trip document, the four apply runners with a missing and a directory plans file and the Rust stress example with a missing and a directory document, and requires a nonzero exit with `<path>: ` on stderr. Before the change 11 of its 58 tests failed: the PHP compare runner exited 0 for a directory stress document and a directory round trip document, which PHP reads as empty text, and the PHP stress document, the PHP apply plans file, the TypeScript compare stress and round trip documents and the TypeScript apply plans file failed without `<path>: ` (a PHP TypeError or `cannot read <path>`, uncaught Node errors whose EISDIR message has no path). tests/dbspec/input.php `dbspec_read_input` and tests/dbspec/input.mjs `readInput` reject a directory or an unreadable file with `<path>: <reason>` and exit 1; the PHP and TypeScript compare and apply runners read every input through them, and the 58 tests pass in `make dbspec-compare-check`, which builds the Rust apply and stress examples for them. `make dbspec-compare-check` (8 runs agree on 202 cases), `dbspec-go-check`, `dbspec-php-check`, `dbspec-ts-check`, `dbspec-rust-check`, `rust-fmt-check`, `go-fmt-check`, `rust-check`, `interface-check`, `docs-rules-check` and `checklist-check` pass.
- [o] T8.7.6.2 Read the engine test vectors strictly and comment the harnesses in Korean (follows T8.7.6.1). Cause: engine/dbspec/plan_test.go and mermaid_test.go decode tests/dbspec vectors into json structs, so a missing field becomes empty, and the comments of tests/dbspec/compare/{check.mjs, typescript.mjs, php.php, go/main.go}, clients/rust/orm-schema/examples/dbspec_compare.rs and the dbspec Makefile blocks are English, against the Korean comment rule. Acceptance: the engine tests reject a missing or wrongly typed vector field with its location, and those comments are Korean. Evidence: engine/dbspec/vector_reader_test.go reads a tests/dbspec vector file as JSON values and requires `version` 1, every section as a nonempty array of case objects with a string `id`, and every field the engine tests use with its type: string lines, line maps, `[kind, table, name]` triples, diagnostics of 4 items (`[rule, line, column, message or null]`) or 3 items for Mermaid, `{line, column, rule}` errors, a present `source` that is null or lines, `crlf` and `mixed` as absent or boolean, and a chain case with exactly one of `order` and `errors`; a missing or mistyped value is `tests/dbspec/<file>: <location> <problem>`. The loaders of plans.json (plan_test.go, also used by apply_test.go), mermaid.json (mermaid_test.go), cases.json (vectors_test.go, manifest_test.go) and ddl.json (render_test.go) decode through it instead of json structs. `TestVectorLoadersRejectMalformedVectors` removes or mistypes 15 values across the four files and requires that exact error; with the json struct decoding it failed all 15, 12 without any error and 3 with a json error that names neither the file nor the location, and now passes 15 of 15 in `make dbspec-go-check` with every vector test. The comments of tests/dbspec/compare/{check.mjs, typescript.mjs, php.php, go/main.go}, clients/rust/orm-schema/examples/dbspec_compare.rs, the 20 dbspec Makefile blocks and the English comments of engine/dbspec/{vectors, render, manifest, harness}_test.go are Korean. `dbspec-go-check`, `dbspec-php-check`, `dbspec-ts-check`, `dbspec-rust-check`, `dbspec-compare-check`, `rust-check`, `rust-fmt-check`, `go-fmt-check`, `interface-check`, `docs-rules-check` and `checklist-check` pass.
- [o] T8.9 Name dbspec files `.dbs` and make the header the file signature (requires T8.7). Cause: dbspec files used the extension `dbspec` only because DbSchema also uses `.dbs`; with a header signature, `.dbs` tells the two formats apart. Evidence: the ten tracked documents (schema/bench, contracts/fixtures audit, clock, clock_mark, decimal_schema, refusal, rollback, secret_config, zone, clients/rust/tests/nonnull) are moved to `.dbs` with `git mv`, and every reference follows: the Go `orm-gen gen --document`, PHP `orm-gen gen`, TypeScript `orm-gen gen --schema` usages, orm-build `MANIFEST_FILE` `orm_manifest.dbs`, the Makefile stress document `clients/rust/target/dbspec/stress.dbs`, contracts/features.json, contracts/rules.json, contracts/interfaces.json, tests/dbspec/mermaid.json, the scripts, tests, README files and documents with their Korean pairs; a `git grep` for paths with the extension `dbspec` lists only one CHANGELOG line about a past change. Each client has one reader: Go `dbspec.ReadFile` (engine/dbspec/file.go), PHP `Dbspec::readFile` with `ReadResult`, TypeScript `readDbspecFile` (src/dbspec/file.ts) and Rust `orm_schema::dbspec::read_file` with `ReadError`; a file that does not start with the bytes `dbspec ` gives exactly one `signature` diagnostic at 1:1 with the message `<path> is not a dbspec document` and no text, before any parse. - [o] T8.9.1 Check the dbspec signature on given bytes (follows T8.9). Cause: the file reader of each client takes only a path, so a caller that must read files under its own rules (such as one that reads with O_NOFOLLOW under a root and does not follow symbolic links) reads every file twice; the second read ignores the caller's rules, can judge a different version than the caller parses, and its message names whatever path it opened. The readers also differed on bytes that are not UTF-8 after the signature: Go and PHP passed them to the parser, TypeScript replaced them with U+FFFD and Rust returned an I/O error, although docs/dbspec.md requires an `encoding` error before parsing. Acceptance: each client offers a byte check that takes the file bytes and a caller-given name and returns the `signature` diagnostic `<name> is not a dbspec document` at 1:1, an `encoding` diagnostic `<name> is not valid UTF-8` at the line and column of the first invalid byte, or the UTF-8 text: Go `dbspec.ReadBytes`, PHP `Dbspec::readBytes`, TypeScript `readDbspecBytes`, Rust `dbspec::read_bytes`; the path reader of each client reads the file and gives its bytes to the byte check with the path as name; the shared file vectors run through both and `make dbspec-compare-check` compares them; docs/dbspec.md and its Korean pair, contracts/interfaces.json (rule `Dbspec.readBytes`) and the four symbol records state it. Evidence: tests/dbspec/cases.json adds the file vector `invalid-utf8-file` (tests/dbspec/files/invalid-utf8.dbs, `encoding` at 3:8). Red: the Go file test did not build (`undefined: ReadBytes`), and on that file the compare runners printed `! encoding 3 8` from the Go and PHP parsers without the reader message, `! name.format 3 7` from TypeScript, which had decoded the byte as U+FFFD, and the Rust runner exited with "invalid utf-8 sequence of 1 bytes from index 22". Green: every runner prints `files/<id>` from the path reader and `files/<id>/bytes` from the byte check with the same rule, position and `<name>` message, and `make dbspec-compare-check` passes ("8 runs agree", 1116 output cases per run); the four file vectors pass through both readers in Go `TestFileVectors` (8 subtests), PHP `dbspec_test.php` (`files=8`), TypeScript `dbspec.mjs` (245 tests, 0 failed) and Rust `shared_dbspec_files`. Owner checks run: go-fmt-check, rust-fmt-check, interface-check, client-unit-check, ts-check, dbspec-go-check, dbspec-php-check, dbspec-ts-check, dbspec-rust-check, dbspec-compare-check, rust-check, docs-rules-check, checklist-check and git-check; `node scripts/features/check.mjs` passes with 17 feature contracts.

Gates on "Merge branch 'main' into chore/integrate-T8.8" with TEST_ENV of the main checkout: checklist-check, docs-rules-check, docs-check (48 pages, 550 internal targets; four raw `<nil>` and `<value>` quotes of the checklist became code spans), interface-check, go-fmt-check, rust-fmt-check, rust-check, rust-driver-check, client-unit-check, ts-check, ts-model-check, go-model-check, conformance-check (25 vectors × 4 languages identical on each database), client-db-check, dbspec-go-check, dbspec-php-check, dbspec-ts-check, dbspec-rust-check, dbspec-compare-check, dbspec-ddl-check, the four dbspec-introspect checks, dbspec-plan-check and its PHP, TypeScript and Rust variants, dbspec-apply-check and its PHP, TypeScript and Rust variants, dbspec-apply-pairs-check, example-check, timing-check, `go vet ./...`, `go test -p 1 ./...` and git-check pass; feature-check fails only with the 15 "missing coverage kind" errors of T15, as at T10.

- [o] T8.8.4 Integrate main into feat/dbspec-T8 again (follows T8.8.3). Cause: `main` gained N18 (N18), N19 (N19) and N19.1 (N19.1) after T8.8.3. N18 connects to MySQL with TLS through `ssl-mode=VERIFY_IDENTITY` and an absolute `ssl-ca` and makes the PHP and TypeScript clients refuse a DSN parameter outside the set of each scheme; it also changed the PHP `SchemaImport` and TypeScript `tools/db.ts` tool connections, which T8.2.6.4 removed. N19 and N19.1 add `utils().schema().addColumns(manifestJson)` on the old schema path: the Mermaid import of the live tables with manifest facts (`internal/ormgen/add_columns.go`, PHP `SchemaColumns`, TypeScript `schema_columns.ts`, Rust `orm_schema::catalog` and `orm_schema::live`), the JSON fixtures `contracts/fixtures/add_columns_*`, and type, default, CHECK and index-name alignments; this branch removed every one of those paths, so a merge that resolves them as deleted drops the feature, its Go side included. Acceptance: `main` is merged; each feature works on the dbspec design in Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust with its tests on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite; the version stays 0.0.2; no old schema format comes back; the mapping and every part without a counterpart are recorded here. Evidence: Mapping. N18: Go `parseDSN`/`mysqlTLS` and `openSQL` (clients/go/orm/dsn.go, db.go), PHP `Orm::parseDsn`, `mysqlTls`, `mysqlTlsOptions` with `DSN_PARAMETERS` (parseDsn now returns the CA path as its sixth element), TypeScript `parseDsn`, `mysqlTls`, `mysqlSsl` with `DSN_PARAMETERS` (driver.ts), Rust unchanged (sqlx reads `ssl-mode` and `ssl-ca`); tests clients/go/orm/mysql_tls_test.go (`TestParseDSNMySQLTLS`, `TestMySQLTLSConnection`), clients/php/tests/mysql_tls.php, clients/typescript/tests/mysql_tls.mjs, clients/rust/orm/tests/mysql_tls.rs; `scripts/test-servers.sh tls` and `make test-servers-tls` are taken as they are. The PHP `SchemaImport` and TypeScript `tools/db.ts` changes have no counterpart: no tool of this branch opens a connection from a DSN; the dbspec introspect and apply functions take a connection that the caller opened. N19: `utils().schema().addColumns(schema)` (Go `AddColumns`, Rust `add_columns`) takes the generated schema value instead of manifest JSON, introspects the database of the connection, compares the existing tables of the document set with `compareSchemas` of their schema texts, and, when every difference is an `add_column` of a column that is null or has a default, runs the plan steps from those tables to the set; a new pure function writes those steps in each client's dbspec module: Go `dbspec.AddColumnSteps` (engine/dbspec/add_columns.go), PHP `Dbspec::addColumnSteps`, TypeScript `addColumnSteps` (src/dbspec/add_columns.ts, with `introspectCatalog` so the client introspects through its own pool), Rust `orm_schema::dbspec::add_column_steps`. The plan steps replace the audit triggers of a changed table (the dbspec audit trigger copies every column, so the history table gains the column too), add the renderer CHECK of each column, and on SQLite rebuild the table. Every other difference, including `reorder_columns` for a column added before an existing one and an unsupported object of a table of the set, is `SCHEMA_DIFFERS` with each `<kind> <table>[.<name>]` before any statement; `docs/errors.yaml` keeps the code with the dbspec wording, and `docs/schema.md` §5 states the call. The import fix for a foreign key of a table to its own key has its counterpart in dbspec introspection, which restores `fk_addcol_tag_parent` of `addcol_tag` on the three databases. Tests clients/go/orm/add_columns_test.go (`TestAddColumns*`), clients/php/tests/add_columns_test.php, clients/typescript/tests/add-columns.mjs, clients/rust/orm/tests/add_columns.rs with the cases `add_columns`, `add_columns_differs`, `add_columns_transaction` and `add_columns_edited_manifest`, on the dbspec fixtures contracts/fixtures/add_columns/*.dbs (`v1`, `v2`, the other set `log`, and `required`, `removed`, `changed`, `nullable`, `default`, `index`, `unique` and `reorder`); Go writes the rows and the audited update through the client with the version 1 and version 2 models, and the other clients through SQL. N19.1: no counterpart is needed: dbspec has one `bytes` type and no `char(n)`, a name has at most 63 bytes and is never shortened, and introspection restores the canonical type, default, check and index name; the fixture holds a `bytes` column and the 63-byte index `ix_addcol_item_enabled_created_at_at_the_sixty_three_byte_limit`, and the cases pass on PostgreSQL. Parts without a counterpart: SQLite adds columns outside a transaction and returns `CONFIG` inside one, as MySQL does, because the plan step that adds a column rebuilds the table with foreign keys off and SQLite does not change `foreign_keys` inside a transaction; it runs the steps in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction on one connection, requires `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` to return no row and turns foreign keys on again (the cases check that the same connection refuses a tag of a missing item afterwards). `install` after `addColumns` does not create a table that a set gains, because `install` here creates a set only whole and returns `CONFIG` for a partly installed set; the case requires that `CONFIG`. A schema-qualified table name (`CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED` on main) cannot occur in dbspec. The added columns come in table name order and then column order instead of manifest order. The Rust move of the catalog reads to `orm_schema::catalog` has its counterpart in `orm::dbspec::introspect`, which the runtime and the tools share; the TypeScript `DriverPool.session` of N19 replaces `unprepared` for MySQL install, and the SQLite pool has a `session` for the foreign key change. N16 and N17: their Go parts are in this branch: `clients/go/orm/fault_ormtest.go`, `rollback_fault_test.go`, `rollback_fault_entry_test.go` and the fault check in `tx.go` (N16); N17 has no Go part; nothing of either lived in internal/ormgen. Red: Go `TestAddColumns*` failed 12 of 12 with `AddColumns = [], <nil>` and no `SCHEMA_DIFFERS` against a stub that adds nothing; PHP failed 12 of 12 with `Call to undefined method Orm\SchemaUtils::addColumns()` on the branch utilities; TypeScript failed 12 of 12 against a stub (`addColumns = []`, `no such column: note`, `Unknown column 'note'`, `column "note" does not exist`, `required: no error`); Rust failed 4 of 4 against a stub. For N18 the PHP and TypeScript cases on the branch parsing accepted all 17 refused DSNs, read an empty `Ssl_version` and accepted both wrong servers, and the Go case did not compile without `sslCA` and `sslHost`. Green: the add-columns cases pass in the four clients on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite (Go 12 tests in 1.8 s, PHP 12 cases in 1.4 s, TypeScript 12 cases in 1.3 s, Rust 4 tests in 0.7 s) and the TLS cases pass in the four clients (`TLSv1.3`, both wrong servers refused); `node scripts/features/check.mjs --run --feature schema_install` and `--feature dsn_connection` pass with the new verification commands `install-add-columns-*` and `dsn-mysql-tls-*`; interface-check passes with the re-recorded symbol inventories and the `SchemaUtils.addColumns` entry of contracts/interfaces.json that takes `GeneratedSchema`. Check: every `make check` step passes on the merged tree. The free disk space fell to 1 GB while other worktrees built, so a full run was stopped twice to keep the shared servers writable and the Rust target was removed between runs: one run passed its first 29 steps (client-db-check 163 s, client-pooler-check 134 s, conformance-check 92 s, rust-check 66 s, ts-check 28 s) and was stopped in dbspec-introspect-rust-check after it passed, and `make check CHECK_TARGETS=<the other 15>` passed dbspec-introspect-compare-check (49 s), the dbspec plan and apply checks, dbspec-apply-pairs-check (32 s), ts-model-check, feature-check (448 s) and go-test-check; the databases of both stopped runs were dropped.
- [o] T8.8.5 Create the tables a new version of an installed set adds when the set is upgraded (follows T8.8.4). Cause: on `main` a module was upgraded by `addColumns` and then `install`, which created the tables new in the module (N19); on this branch `install` creates a set only whole and `addColumns` only adds columns, so a module version that adds a table could not be applied. Acceptance: the additive upgrade of an installed set creates every table of the set that the database lacks with its indexes, foreign keys, checks and audit and immutable triggers through the same plan steps, adds the missing null or defaulted columns, and refuses every other difference with `SCHEMA_DIFFERS` before any statement, in Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite. Evidence: the call states what it does and is renamed everywhere without an alias: `utils().schema().addTablesAndColumns(schema)` (Go `AddTablesAndColumns`, Rust `add_tables_and_columns`) with the pure step functions Go `dbspec.AddTablesAndColumnsSteps` (engine/dbspec/add_tables_and_columns.go), PHP `Dbspec::addTablesAndColumnsSteps`, TypeScript `addTablesAndColumnsSteps` (src/dbspec/add_tables_and_columns.ts) and Rust `orm_schema::dbspec::add_tables_and_columns_steps`; each compares the tables of the set that exist with the whole set, accepts `create_table` and the `add_column` of a null or defaulted column, and writes one plan from those tables (or from empty when none exists) to the set, so a created table gets the same steps as an applied plan. It returns a created table as `table` and an added column as `table.column` in table name order. The fixtures moved to contracts/fixtures/add_tables_and_columns: `v2` adds the audited table `addcol_extra` (index, foreign key to `addcol_item`, check) with its history table, and `required` adds the same table next to a non-null column without a default. Tests clients/go/orm/add_tables_and_columns_test.go (`TestAddTablesAndColumns*`), clients/php/tests/add_tables_and_columns_test.php, clients/typescript/tests/add-tables-and-columns.mjs and clients/rust/orm/tests/add_tables_and_columns.rs require the two tables in the result, an insert into `addcol_extra` recorded in its history with action `insert`, a refused insert of a missing item, a refused empty label and a refused delete, a repeated call that adds nothing, an `install` of version 2 afterwards that changes nothing, and after the eight `SCHEMA_DIFFERS` fixtures the full result again, so the refused `required` version created no table. docs/schema.md §5 (+ko), docs/usage, docs/interfaces, docs/errors.yaml with the regenerated Go, PHP and Rust codes, the PHP and Rust READMEs, contracts/features.json (verification `install-add-tables-and-columns-*`), the `SchemaUtils.addTablesAndColumns` entry of contracts/interfaces.json with the output `AddedTablesAndColumns`, and the re-recorded symbol inventories state it. Red: with the fixture and tests changed and the old implementation, Go failed 12 of 12 (`AddColumns = [addcol_item.note ...]` without `addcol_extra addcol_extra_history`), PHP 12 of 12 and TypeScript 12 of 12 (the same result and `no such table: addcol_extra`, `Table '...addcol_extra' doesn't exist`, `relation "addcol_extra" does not exist`), Rust 4 of 4. Green: Go 12 tests in 2.4 s, PHP 12 cases, TypeScript 12 cases and Rust 4 tests in 0.7 s pass on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite; `node scripts/features/check.mjs --run --feature schema_install`, interface-check, go-fmt-check and rust-fmt-check pass.
- [ ] T9 Implement the whole dbspec surface of the PHP client in a PHP extension written in C, as an independent fifth implementation of the same interface (requires T8.2.5). Cause: the item named only `Dbspec.parse`, `Dbspec.emit` and `DbspecDiagnostic`, so T9.1 to T9.5 built an ext-php-rs extension over the Rust dbspec with six methods and an opaque `Document`; the PHP client `Orm\Dbspec\Dbspec` (clients/php/src/Dbspec/Dbspec.php) is the specification, and the extension must provide every public method of it and every result and value class with the same names and property order, written in C like the ordered-json `php-extension`. Acceptance: `Orm\Dbspec\Native\Dbspec` provides parse, readFile, readBytes, emit, render, manifest, introspect, externalDifferences, parsePlan, emitPlan, chain, diff, compareSchemas, installedDifferences, addTablesAndColumnsSteps, planSteps, apply, recover, rollback, finalize, exportMermaid and importMermaid; `Native\Document` has the public mutable property graph of the PHP `Document`; contracts/interfaces.json declares every rule for the four languages and the extension; `make dbspec-compare-check` compares the extension on every input, the apply and introspect cross checks run it, its tests mirror the PHP plan, Mermaid and apply cleanup tests, the Rust extension and its cargo steps are gone, and CI passes on the last commit of the sub-items (T9.6 onward).
- [o] T9.1 Build the PHP extension orm_dbspec over the Rust dbspec and test it against the shared vectors and the PHP client (follows T9). Evidence: clients/php-extension is an ext-php-rs 0.15.15 cdylib, a Cargo workspace of its own with its Cargo.lock, that registers `Orm\Dbspec\Native\Dbspec` with readFile, readBytes, parse, emit, manifest and render over orm_schema::dbspec, the opaque `Document`, and the result classes Diagnostic, ReadResult, ParseResult, Manifest, ManifestResult and RenderResult as `final readonly` classes with typed readonly properties in declaration order and a private constructor, declared through the Zend API because ext-php-rs properties are untyped and writable and the order of its getter properties follows a HashMap of the proc macro; PHP code selects it by that namespace, with no automatic switch. A text that is not UTF-8 gives the encoding diagnostic of the PHP client. `make dbspec-php-extension-check` runs clippy, builds and copies the library, and runs twice clients/php-extension/tests/declarations_test.php (the Reflection of the loaded extension equals stubs/orm_dbspec.stub.php in 40 declarations; a stub with `?string` for emit failed) and dbspec_test.php (10 cases: 118 parse cases, 4 files read twice, 5 manifests and 51 renderings equal the vectors and the PHP client, encoding, argument and object rules; with the bare CR order reversed the bytes case failed). CI installs libclang in the setup step php-extension-tools (need libclang of feature-check); make install-rust fetches the crates of its Cargo.lock; repo-check, full-run-check, feature-unit-check, version-check, rust-fmt-check and interface-check pass.

The extension output equals the PHP output up to `plans/` (1125 lines). repo-check, full-run-check and feature-unit-check pass.
- [o] T9.3 Declare the PHP extension in the interface and symbol contracts (follows T9.2). Evidence: contracts/interfaces.json declares the extension `php-extension` under `extensions` (PHP syntax, the stub root clients/php-extension/stubs, the snapshot contracts/symbols/php-extension.json and its hash, the six `Dbspec` rules and the owners `DbspecDiagnostic` and `DbspecManifest`), and those rules and owners carry its adapters. contracts/validate.go checks each extension adapter against a `php-extension` column of the common inputs and outputs and refuses an adapter on a rule the extension does not list, a listed rule without one and an unknown rule (TestExtensionContractRejectsDrift: with the check bypassed 4 of its 5 mutations were accepted). The interface check runs `interfaces/php-extension` with the PHP extractor on the stub and compares rules, owners, prohibited symbols, the snapshot and its hash (39 symbols, 6 rules, 2 owners); TestExtensionDeclarations refuses a stub whose emit returns `?string`, a Manifest without externalText, a Model.gets adapter of the extension and a changed snapshot. `--language php-extension` checks it alone. go test ./contracts ./tests/interfaces/check, make interface-check, repo-check, feature-unit-check and docs-rules-check pass.

Evidence: runRunner selects inputs by `reads` only for a runner that declares it, refuses another count for such a runner with the fix, and passes the inputs of a runner without `reads` unchanged; a runner without reads received its four arguments, a compare runner its declared two, and a compare runner given one input was refused. check.test.mjs passes its 10 cases.

- [o] T9.7 Stop the PHP parser's endless recursion on a use cycle through a renamed document and its TypeError on digit-only column names of an audited table (follows T9.6). Cause: a differential run of the PHP client against the C extension of T9 over mutated shared vectors crashed the PHP client twice: a used document whose header names another document than its set entry and that uses its own entry recursed until memory ran out (Parser::usedDocument checked cycles by header names only), and an audited table with a column named `10` made checkAuditHistories pass the int key 10 to the string closure of auditLists; the Go, TypeScript and Rust parsers reported both documents with diagnostics. Evidence: tests/dbspec/cases.json gained the invalid cases `use-cycle-through-another-header-name` (one use error at 3:5) and `audit-digit-column-names` (name.format at 7:3 and 18:3, two setting errors at 24:16); clients/php/tests/dbspec_test.php failed the first with `Allowed memory size ... exhausted` before the fix; Parser::usedDocument now marks a document while it is read and reports a use cycle when it is used again in that time, and checkAuditHistories reads column names as strings; dbspec_test.php passes 100 invalid cases, `go test ./engine/dbspec`, `node --test clients/typescript/tests/dbspec.mjs` (141 tests) and `cargo test -p orm-schema --test dbspec` pass the new cases, and the php interface check, `make repo-check` and gofmt pass.

Cause: the ext-php-rs extension of T9.1 ran the Rust dbspec and gave PHP an opaque `Document`, so a document could not be built or changed in PHP code, its classes differed from the PHP client's and its build needed Rust and libclang. Evidence: clients/php-extension is C (src/*.c, config.m4, composer.json for PIE with build path src): a per-call arena C model, a parser, check expression reader, literal rules, emitter, document set check, manifest and renderer translated from clients/php/src/Dbspec, and convert.c, which turns the public mutable `Orm\Dbspec\Native` document graph into the C model and back; stubs/orm_dbspec.stub.php declares every class of the PHP client's dbspec surface (document, result and value classes with their constructors, methods and constants) and `Dbspec` with the six methods, and scripts/arginfo.php writes src/orm_dbspec_arginfo.h with gen_stub.php from it, leaving the array constants to src/orm_dbspec.c; scripts/build.sh builds with phpize into a temporary directory, refuses compiler warnings and publishes by rename; `make install-php-extension-tools` installs gen_stub.php with its PHP-Parser into .runtime/bin/gen-stub instead of libclang, and the Rust sources, Cargo files, clippy and rustfmt steps and the cargo fetch are gone. clients/php-extension/tests/dbspec_test.php now compares diagnostics with messages, emissions, files, manifests, statements, exceptions and documents built in PHP code with the PHP client (11 cases pass), declarations_test.php finds the 299 declarations of the loaded extension equal to the stub and checks the PIE package, `make dbspec-php-extension-check` passes, a differential fuzz of 40000 mutated documents and 15000 generated check and default lines found no difference from the PHP client after T9.7, repeated calls leave no memory behind, and `make interface-check` (with the re-recorded php-extension snapshot), `make repo-check`, `go test ./contracts ./tests/interfaces/check`, the owners, downloads, full-run and compare check node tests, `make docs-check` and gofmt pass.
