Cause: after T9.8 `Orm\Dbspec\Native\Dbspec` lacked externalDifferences, parsePlan, emitPlan, chain, diff, compareSchemas, installedDifferences, addTablesAndColumnsSteps and planSteps. Evidence: src/plan.c (plan text, chain, diff and object comparison, schema comparison, the set comparisons) and src/steps.c (the steps of three dialects with the SQLite rebuild) translate PlanText, PlanChain, PlanDiff, PlanObjects, SchemaComparison, PlanSteps and the set methods of Dbspec.php, and convert.c converts plans, steps, changes, differences and unsupported objects, returning the given Plan objects from chain; the stub declares the nine methods. tests/dbspec/compare/php-interface.php now holds the plan and Mermaid printers with the Dbspec class as argument, php.php calls them with `Orm\Dbspec\Dbspec`, and the extension runner reads the plan vectors (runners.mjs reads cases, stress, ddl and plans, until `mermaid/`); its output equals the PHP client's up to the first Mermaid case (2918 lines). clients/php-extension/tests/dbspec_plan_test.php has the body of clients/php/tests/dbspec_plan_test.php (51 cases pass), declarations_test.php checks that the bodies stay equal, and `make dbspec-php-extension-check` runs it twice; contracts/interfaces.json gives the nine rules and the owners DbspecPlan, DbspecPlanStep, DbspecEffect, DbspecNullCheck, DbspecChange, DbspecDifference, DbspecUnsupported, DbspecTableRename, DbspecColumnRename and DbspecColumnName their php-extension adapters with a re-recorded snapshot. A differential fuzz found no difference from the PHP client in 17000 mutated plans (parse, emit, diff, steps of three dialects, chain, comparison, installed differences, added tables and columns) and in 18000 random source and target schema pairs with renames, drops, widenings and new tables (diff and steps, 16444 with steps); repeated calls leave no memory behind; `make interface-check` (15 rules, 12 owners for the extension), `make repo-check`, `make docs-check`, the owners and compare check node tests and gofmt pass.
- [o] T9.10 Implement exportMermaid and importMermaid in the C extension and compare the extension on every input (follows T9.9). Cause: after T9.9 the extension lacked the Mermaid methods, so its compare runner stopped before the Mermaid vectors through `until`. Evidence: src/mermaid.c writes the erDiagram and reads one with scanners for the line forms of the PHP client's patterns (entity start, attribute, relationship, label, column suffix, Go TrimSpace white space, known types and the %q quoting), and src/catalog.c is the neutral intermediate model of the PHP client's Catalog and CatalogTable, which writes the document text, parses it and removes the rejected objects (introspection uses it too); the stub declares both methods. The extension runner now reads all five inputs, its output equals the PHP client runner's (3385 lines), and the `until` comparison of tests/dbspec/compare/compare.mjs, check.mjs and its four check.test.mjs cases are gone because no runner stops early; runners.test.mjs and inputs.test.mjs no longer say the extension skips vectors. clients/php-extension/tests/dbspec_mermaid_test.php has the body of the PHP client's Mermaid test (13 cases pass), declarations_test.php checks both mirrored bodies, and `make dbspec-php-extension-check` runs it twice; exportMermaid and importMermaid carry their php-extension adapters in contracts/interfaces.json (17 rules). A differential fuzz of 16000 mutated erDiagrams (876 imported documents per 4000) and the export of every shared case found no difference from the PHP client; repeated calls leave no memory behind; `make interface-check`, `make repo-check`, `make docs-check`, the owners and compare check node tests (6 cases) and gofmt pass.
- [o] T9.11 Implement introspect in the C extension and run it in the introspection cross checks (follows T9.10). Cause: after T9.10 `Orm\Dbspec\Native\Dbspec` had no `introspect`, so the extension could not read a database. Evidence: src/introspect.c sends the catalog queries of the PHP client's MysqlCatalog, PostgresCatalog and SqliteCatalog by calling `query`, `fetchAll`, `errorCode` and `errorInfo` on the given PDO through the Zend API (no pdo_dbh_t internals), wraps a PDOException in the RuntimeException "Catalog query failed: ..." with it as the previous exception, reports a failing statement in every error mode, rejects catalog values of another shape with the UnexpectedValueException messages of CatalogRows, decodes check expressions like CheckDecoder, recognizes immutable and audit triggers like CatalogTriggers, and builds the document with src/catalog.c; the stub declares `introspect(\PDO $connection, string $dialect, string $name): IntrospectResult` and the module requires pdo. clients/php-extension/tests/dbspec_test.php gains `php-extension/introspect`: the 5 ddl.json cases and the 4 SQLite cases of tests/dbspec/introspect.json applied to in-memory SQLite databases give the PHP client's document and unsupported objects and the vector's, and failing queries in three error modes, values of another type, exceptions thrown by a PDO subclass, an unknown dialect and a connection that is not a PDO give the PHP client's exceptions and previous exceptions. tests/dbspec/introspect/php-extension.php is the fifth runner of TestIntrospectCompare (ORM_DBSPEC_EXTENSION, built by `make dbspec-introspect-compare-check`), and clients/php-extension/tests/dbspec_introspect_test.php mirrors the body of the PHP client's introspection test, run on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite by the new `make dbspec-introspect-php-extension-check`; declarations_test.php checks three mirrored bodies, and Dbspec.introspect carries its php-extension adapter (18 rules). A differential fuzz of 24000 introspections (SQLite databases from the shared documents with mutated statements, and scripted MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite catalogs with mutated rows, value types and query failures) found no difference from the PHP client in documents, unsupported objects, exceptions or the sent queries, and 2000 repeated calls leave no memory behind. The server suites run in CI; locally `make interface-check`, `make repo-check`, `make docs-check`, the extension's declarations and dbspec tests, the owners and check node tests and gofmt pass.
- [o] T9.12 Stop rollback at an applied row whose recorded step lies outside its plan, in every client (follows T9.11). Cause: a differential run of the PHP client against the C extension over apply, recover, rollback and finalize on SQLite, with the history row changed between commands, found that rollback of an `applied` or `done` row read `step` without the range check that recover and an interrupted rollback apply: a row at step 99 of a 4-step plan made the PHP client read missing steps (warnings, then `LogicException "step 98 has no effect to read"`), Go and Rust panic with an index out of range, and TypeScript throw `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'rollback')`. Evidence: engine/dbspec/apply.go, clients/php/src/Dbspec/PlanApply.php, clients/typescript/src/dbspec/apply.ts and clients/rust/orm/src/dbspec/apply.rs now report such a row as the `chain` error "the recorded step 99 is outside the plan's 4 steps" before the drift check, and docs/plans.md (+ko) states the rule. The SQLite cleanup tests of each client gained the case `apply/rollback-step-outside` (apply create-from-empty, set the row's step to 99, roll back): before the fix Go failed with `index out of range [98] with length 4`, PHP with the LogicException, TypeScript with the TypeError and Rust with `index out of bounds: the len is 4 but the index is 98`; after it `go test ./engine/dbspec` (the three apply tests), clients/php/tests/dbspec_apply_cleanup_test.php (5 cases), `make dbspec-ts-check` (277 tests) and `make dbspec-rust-check/apply-cleanup` pass, and gofmt and `make docs-check` pass.
- [o] T9.13 Implement apply, recover, rollback and finalize in the C extension and compare its public classes with the PHP client's (follows T9.12). Cause: after T9.12 the extension lacked the four apply commands, the last methods of the PHP client's `Dbspec`. Evidence: src/apply.c follows PlanApply: the session lock, settings and their restore for MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite, the history table, position, settled state, verify through the extension's own introspection and manifest, resolve, forward, finalize, rollback with null checks, events through the `$events` closure, `applied_at` from the `$now` closure through `DateTimeImmutable`, and `finish`, which runs every cleanup step and throws the first error alone or an ApplyCleanupError with the cleanup errors in order (a nested ApplyCleanupError is flattened); it calls `getAttribute`, `prepare`, `exec` and the statement methods of the given PDO, so a PDO subclass sees the PHP client's calls, and a connection outside ERRMODE_EXCEPTION is the InvalidArgumentException of PlanApply.php. The stub declares the four methods and an @internal `PlanApply` with `EFFECT_QUERIES` (a nested constant that src/orm_dbspec.c registers) and `effectOn`; contracts/interfaces.json gives Dbspec.apply, recover, rollback and finalize their php-extension adapters, so the extension carries all 22 Dbspec rules. Tests: clients/php-extension/tests/dbspec_apply_cleanup_test.php and dbspec_apply_test.php have the bodies of the PHP client's tests (declarations_test.php checks 5 mirrored bodies from their first `const`), `make dbspec-php-extension-check` runs the cleanup test twice, the new `make dbspec-apply-php-extension-check` runs the apply scenarios on the three databases, tests/dbspec/apply/php-extension.php is the fifth client of TestApplyChainAcrossClients (180 pairs), and dbspec_test.php gains `php-extension/apply`: 108 scripts on SQLite files (each chain applied plan by plan, stopped at every event of the second plan and then recovered or rolled back, drift, changed history rows, finalize) give the PHP client's events, exceptions with their codes, cleanup errors and previous errors, history rows and schema, and so do the error mode, an unknown dialect, a split chain and a clock that returns text. declarations_test.php gains `php-extension/client`: the 297 public declarations of the PHP client's 43 public dbspec classes equal the extension's, in property order, and every other extension class is @internal in the stub; for that, the PHP client's helper classes CheckDecodeFailure, Expression, ExpressionFailure, Literal, ParseStop and SchemaComparison are now marked @internal. A differential fuzz of 3600 random command scripts on SQLite files (stops at random events, stray tables, changed history rows, row inserts) found no difference from the PHP client. The server suites run in CI; locally `make dbspec-php-extension-check`, `make interface-check`, `make repo-check`, `make docs-check` and gofmt pass.
- [o] T10 Depend on an ordered-json revision with one Cargo manifest for its package. Cause: every cargo command in clients/rust warns "skipping duplicate package `ordered-json`", because the ordered-json revision of clients/rust/orm/Cargo.toml and ordered-json main both define the package in Cargo.toml (library rust/src/lib.rs) and again in rust/Cargo.toml; cargo uses the first and skips the second. Acceptance: ordered-json keeps one manifest for the package, clients/rust/orm/Cargo.toml pins that revision, and cargo test in clients/rust prints no duplicate package warning. Evidence: ordered-json removes the root Cargo.toml so rust/Cargo.toml is the one manifest of the package, and its repository test `test_each_cargo_package_has_one_manifest` fails on two manifests; the Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust clients all use that ordered-json; `cargo test -p orm --lib` prints no "skipping duplicate package" line, and rust-check, client-unit-check, ts-check, conformance-check and the four dbspec checks pass.
- [o] T11 Fail the check when a tracked Go source is not gofmt formatted, and make the Go and Rust formatting checks pass. Cause: `make check` runs `rust-fmt-check` but no Go formatting check, `rust-fmt-check` fails with 82 rustfmt differences across the Rust workspace (rustfmt 1.9.0 of the default and of the 1.98.1 toolchain agree), and six tracked Go files are not gofmt formatted (bench/seedaes/main.go, clients/go/decimalmodel/decimal_physical_test.go, clients/go/orm/decimal_model_test.go, clients/go/orm/exec.go, engine/plan/plan.go, engine/planner/planner.go), among them "feat: preserve exact decimal model values" code with struct fields out of alignment and an `if` body on one line. Evidence: `make go-fmt-check` lists the tracked Go files that gofmt would change and fails; it failed with the six files before formatting and passes after `gofmt -w`, and `make check` runs it next to `rust-fmt-check`. `cargo fmt --all` formatted the Rust workspace and `make rust-fmt-check` passes. `go build ./...`, `go vet` of engine, clients/go and bench, `cargo check --workspace --all-targets --all-features` pass after the change. rustfmt added a trailing comma to two multi-line parameter lists of orm-build catalog/mod.rs (`read_only_query`, `read_only_grid_query`), whose recorded token signatures then differed; contracts/symbols/rust.json records them again and the four-language interface-check passes.
- [o] T12 Fail the check when the committed TypeScript models differ from the generator output, and scan only the sources that call models. Cause: `npm run build` of clients/typescript runs `npm run models`, whose `--scan tests` reads every test file, so the Playwright call `browser.newPage()` of tests/physical-document-render.mjs adds `newPage(value: unknown)` to the Author and User models; the committed models.ts lacks it and no target compares the two. Evidence: clients/typescript/tests/models-scan.mjs failed because the models script scanned 44 sources while only 8 import a generated model; the other 36 include tests/physical-document-render.mjs; the script now names those 8 and the test passes. With that scan the same test failed with `differs: src/models/models.ts`; regenerating removed the 12 `newPage(value: unknown)` declarations and it passes. `make ts-model-check`, part of `make check`, builds the client and runs both tests: the scan equals the sources that import a model module or class, and `orm-gen gen --check` with the script arguments exits 0 with no output. contracts/symbols/typescript.json drops the 12 `newPage` symbols and `go run ./tests/interfaces/check` passes; `make ts-check` passes with TEST_ENV.
- [o] T13 Check commit subjects against the commit rule of AGENTS.md. Cause: `make git-check` required `type: description` with the types build, perf and merge and 72 characters, while AGENTS.md requires `type(scope): Subject (#id)` with the types feat, fix, docs, style, refactor, test and chore and a 50-character subject, so the check failed on every commit that follows the rule and on the earlier commits without a type. Evidence: scripts/git/check.test.mjs failed because `subjectErrors` did not exist and passes 3 tests that accept `type(scope): Subject (#id)` and reject a missing type, scope or id, the types perf and the untyped form, a lower-case subject, a final period and a subject over 50 characters; contracts/rules.json holds the AGENTS.md types, the 50-character limit and the baseline `main`; merge commits are skipped; `make git-check` passes.
- [o] T14 Run the environment-bound Go tests only from their owning targets. Cause: `make client-db-check` runs `go test ./clients/go/...`, which also ran `TestDecimalPhysical*` and `TestCoverage*`; they need the DSNs that `decimal-physical-check` and `feature-check` provide, so `client-db-check` failed with "DECIMAL_MYSQL_DSN is required" and "selected database and DSN are required". Evidence: `TestDecimalPhysical*` carries the build tag `decimalphysical` and `TestCoverage*` the tag `featurecoverage`; scripts/decimal-physical-check.mjs and scripts/features/coverage.mjs pass the tags. `go test -count=1 ./clients/go/...` with TEST_ENV passes every package; `make decimal-physical-check` passes 24 runs (4 clients, 3 databases, 2 runs) in each of three consecutive runs after `make decimal-db-setup`; `node --test scripts/features/coverage.test.mjs` passes 10 tests. `make feature-check` stops before running tests on T15.
- [o] T14.1 Run the Rust DSN coverage test only from feature-check (follows T14). Cause: `cargo test --workspace` in `client-db-check` and `client-pooler-check` ran `coverage_dsn_connection`, which needs `ORM_FEATURE_DATABASE` and `ORM_FEATURE_DSN`, and failed with "ORM_FEATURE_DATABASE is required". Evidence: `coverage_dsn_connection` is `#[ignore]` with its owner named in the reason; `cargo test -p orm --test coverage_dsn` reports 1 ignored and passes; with `ORM_FEATURE_DATABASE=sqlite`, the bench SQLite DSN and `-- --exact --include-ignored`, the form scripts/features/coverage.mjs now runs, it passes 1 test; `node --test scripts/features/coverage.test.mjs` passes 10 tests.
- [o] T14.2 Run the Rust decimal and generated-model coverage tests only from their owners (follows T14.1). Cause: once `coverage_dsn_connection` stopped failing first, `cargo test --workspace` ran `decimal_mysql`, `decimal_postgres`, `decimal_sqlite` and `coverage_generated_model_connection`, which need the variables of `decimal-physical-check` and `feature-check`, and failed with "DECIMAL_SQLITE_DSN is required". Evidence: the three decimal tests and `coverage_generated_model_connection` are `#[ignore]` with their owners in the reason; scripts/decimal-physical-check.mjs passes `--include-ignored`; `cargo test --workspace` with TEST_ENV exits 0 with 1 and 3 ignored; `make decimal-physical-check` passes with the Rust runs.
- [o] T15 Declare a coverage kind and owner tests for every feature of contracts/features.json. Cause: `make feature-check` fails with "missing coverage kind" for 16 of the 17 features; only the DSN connection feature declares coverage, and no item tracked the rest. Evidence: every feature of contracts/features.json declares `coverage.kind` (11 database, 6 independent), shared case IDs and an owner test per supporting client under `clients/<language>`, 152 owner and dependent-part requirements that scripts/features/coverage.mjs executes twice each with equal results and equal database digests; database cases run on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite against the seeded bench database and write only to composite_account, composite_membership and the audit tables they install and drop. With TEST_ENV, `make feature-check` passes: "feature coverage: 17 contracts executed" and all 71 verification commands, "features: 17 feature contracts passed". The shared fixtures contracts/fixtures/planner.json, schema_definition.json and authenticated_encryption.json give the independent cases one expectation for four clients. Manifest errors are corrected at their cause: decimal_model.json takes the fixture form (`feature`, `operation`, `expected`) and belongs to model_writes, and its four readers failed on the old form and pass on the new one; the paths of removed tests are dropped and every unmapped test file names its feature; the dsn_connection and schema_install texts state the current behaviour and docs/features(.ko).md is generated from contracts/features.json; the new feature catalog_connection (Rust partial) owns the orm-build catalog tests; a verification command runs `node …/typescript/bin/tsc` instead of a `.bin` link. Owner tests must lie under `clients/<language>`, so bench/go moved to clients/go/bench. The four conformance runners take a repeatable vector selection. Defects found by the new cases and fixed Red then Green: the checker read the second Go run from the test cache (the new checker test counted 1 run of 2; `go test -count=1` now runs both); a SQLite RESTRICT foreign key violation reported as SQLite 1811 was DRIVER and is FOREIGN_KEY in the four clients and docs/errors.yaml, while another 1811 of a trigger RAISE stays DRIVER; CHECK violations were not CONSTRAINT in TypeScript, PHP and Rust; PHP kept the unsplittable plan of an IN list beyond the bind limit in its plan cache (memory held after the count fell from 87 MB to 5 MB); the Rust catalog read a MySQL `_bin` VARCHAR as binary and failed its insert check with ROW_WRITE_MISMATCH; the PHP schema install case read the manifest text without its hash after the T8.8.1 merge. Raw `<value>` and `<nil>` quotes of the checklists are code spans, because VitePress stopped on them and docs-check failed. Found and recorded: T18 to T22. On the tree merged with T8.6.8 and T8.8.2, `make feature-check` passes in one run: "feature coverage: 17 contracts executed", the 72 verification commands and "features: 17 feature contracts passed" in 1453 s, while clients/rust/target grows to 11.8 GB. Two shared-database defects that the merged tree exposed are fixed: the Rust `rollback_fault` case (clients/rust/orm/tests/rollback.rs) drops `rollback_probe` at its end, because a table left in the test database failed the `schema().empty()` case of the Rust integration binary ("mysql: schema().empty() on a database without tables"); and the schema set databases of clients/rust/tests/src/decimal_physical.rs are named with the process id and a counter, because two parallel MySQL cases created the same timestamp name and one failed with MySQL error 1007 "database exists"; the 9 cases of `decimal_physical` then passed three times in a row.
- [o] T16 Open the send-savepoint SQLite file beside TEST_ENV. Cause: the Makefile named it `.runtime/servers/send-savepoint.sqlite` under the checkout, which a worktree does not have, so `sqlite_send_savepoint` failed with "unable to open database file" in `client-db-check` and `client-pooler-check`. Evidence: with the old path `cargo test -p orm --lib sqlite_send_savepoint` failed with "unable to open database file" in this worktree; `SEND_SQLITE_DSN` is now `$(dir $(abspath $(TEST_ENV)))send-savepoint.sqlite`, which `make -n client-db-check` expands to the servers directory of TEST_ENV, and the three send_savepoint tests pass there.
- [o] T17 Fail the Rust client benchmark when its DSN is unset. Cause: clients/rust/tests/src/client_bench.rs replaces a missing `ORM_BENCH_MYSQL_DSN` with a built-in local DSN, a fallback that no specification names. Evidence: clients/rust/tests/tests/client_bench_dsn.rs runs the built `client_bench` with `ORM_BENCH_MYSQL_DSN` removed and with it empty. Before the change both tests failed: without the variable the bench ran against the built-in socket and exited 0, and with it empty the bench panicked on the DSN scheme without naming the variable. `client_bench` now exits 1 and prints "ORM_BENCH_MYSQL_DSN is required" in both cases, and the 2 tests pass in `cargo test -p orm-tests --test client_bench_dsn` and in `cargo test --workspace` with TEST_ENV. With TEST_ENV the bench ran 100 iterations against the seeded bench database and exited 0. No make target runs `client_bench`: `make perf-check` runs only the Go and PHP gates, and the DSN tests run in the `cargo test --workspace` of `client-db-check`, which stops on T17.1. `make rust-check` and `make rust-fmt-check` pass.
- [o] T17.1 Make the orm-schema time bounds pass in `client-db-check`. Cause: `cargo test --workspace` in `client-db-check` failed in two orm-schema targets under a load average of about 70: the debug build of `physical_json::tests::preflight_bounds` measured node-limit 15.15 s and `physical_graph_vectors` measured 45.9 s and 51.9 s, each against a 15 s bound. Evidence: the bound measured the wrong quantity. docs/schema.md fixes Rust debug builds and a 15-second graph-test deadline, and the tests compared wall-clock time with it; on this shared machine (11 cores, load average 16-92 during this work) wall-clock time includes the time other processes hold the processors. Run alone at a load average near 30, `physical_graph_vectors` failed twice at 17.31 s and 26.48 s wall-clock while the process used 10.60 s and 11.86 s of CPU (user plus system), and `preflight_bounds` used 6.11 s of CPU for 19.23 s wall-clock; the Go `TestPhysicalGraphVectors` failed at 64.46 s and 81.71 s wall-clock inside `go test ./clients/go/...` and took 2.92 s wall-clock and 4.13 s CPU alone. The deadline stays 15 s and now bounds CPU time: Rust `CaseClock` (clients/rust/orm-schema/tests/common/case_clock.rs, `clock_gettime(CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID)` through a libc dev-dependency) measures the thread that runs the case in the eight physical graph, document and JSON-limit tests and the 1-second record case bound, Go `caseClock` (clients/go/orm/case_clock_test.go, `getrusage`) and PHP `processCpuNs` (clients/php/tests/process_cpu.php) measure the test process, whose tests run one at a time, and every case prints both times. After the change `physical_graph_vectors` reported cpu=10.25 s wall=13.43 s and node-limit cpu=2.05 s, the Go graph test cpu=5.19 s, the three PHP tests pass, and `ORM_CLIENT_DB_LANGS=rust make client-db-check` (78 passing test results) and `ORM_CLIENT_DB_LANGS=go make client-db-check` pass with TEST_ENV at load averages 16-31. docs/schema.md states the corrected criterion. The other wall-clock deadlines are T17.6.
- [o] T17.2 Fail the example programs when `ORM_BENCH_MYSQL_DSN` is unset. Cause: examples/complex and examples/thin-slice replace a missing `ORM_BENCH_MYSQL_DSN` with a built-in local socket DSN in their Go, PHP and Rust programs, and both README files name that DSN as the default. Evidence: the Go, PHP and Rust programs of examples/complex and examples/thin-slice exit with status 1 and print "ORM_BENCH_MYSQL_DSN is required; it names the seeded bench database" without connecting when the variable is unset or empty, and both README files state it. Go `TestExamplesRequireBenchDSN` (examples/dsn_test.go) builds the Go programs and runs them and the PHP programs with the variable removed and empty; Rust `examples_fail_when_dsn_is_unset` and `examples_fail_when_dsn_is_empty` (clients/rust/tests/tests/example_dsn.rs) run the built `complex` and `demo`. Before the change all 8 Go subtests failed: the Go programs exited 0 against the built-in socket, and the PHP programs exited 255 with "Unknown named parameter $schemaPath", because they still passed the `schemaPath` that T8.2.6.3.4 removed from the PHP `Config`; after removing it and the schema argument the PHP programs also exited 0 against the built-in socket. Both Rust tests failed ("left: Some(0), right: Some(1)"). After the change the 8 subtests and the 2 Rust tests pass, and with TEST_ENV the six programs exit 0; the thin-slice outputs are byte-identical and the complex outputs are equal JSON values. The complex outputs differ in key order and indentation although its README compares them with `diff`, which is T17.4.
- [o] T17.3 Read the Rust native benchmarks' database from a declared DSN. Cause: bench/rust/src/native.rs and bench/rust/src/driver_compare.rs connect to the fixed socket /tmp/mysql.sock and database orm_bench instead of a declared DSN. Evidence: bench/rust/src/native.rs and bench/rust/src/driver_compare.rs read the database from `ORM_BENCH_MYSQL_DSN`, the DSN that the Go bench and `client_bench` use: sqlx parses it with `MySqlConnectOptions::from_str` and mysql_async with `Opts::from_url`, an unparsable DSN exits with status 1 naming the variable, and an unset or empty one exits with status 1 and "ORM_BENCH_MYSQL_DSN is required; it names the seeded bench database" without connecting. `benchmarks_fail_when_dsn_is_unset` and `benchmarks_fail_when_dsn_is_empty` (bench/rust/tests/dsn.rs) run the built `native` and `driver_compare`; before the change both failed because `native` connected to /tmp/mysql.sock and stopped with status 101 ("left: Some(101), right: Some(1)"), and after it both pass. `make rust-driver-check` now runs these tests. With TEST_ENV both programs connected to the declared database and stopped with status 101 at the first row: "Rust type `u64` (as SQL type `BIGINT UNSIGNED`) is not compatible with SQL type `BIGINT`", which is T17.5.
- [o] T17.4 Make the complex example outputs byte-identical. Cause: examples/complex/README.md compares the Go, PHP and Rust outputs with `diff`, but the outputs are equal JSON values with different key order and indentation, so the documented comparison fails. Evidence: `make example-check` builds the Rust `complex` and `demo` programs and runs `TestExampleOutputsAreIdentical` (examples/outputs_test.go, build tag `examples`), which runs the Go, PHP and Rust programs of examples/complex and examples/thin-slice against the seeded bench database and requires byte-equal stdout. Before the change the complex subtest failed: Go wrote 2241 bytes with sorted top-level members and two-space indentation, PHP 3121 bytes in declared order with four-space indentation, and Rust 2243 bytes with every object's members sorted and `"read_sum": 456000.0`, because `json!` turns each row into a sorted map and serde_json writes an integral f64 with a fraction; thin-slice passed with 200 bytes each. The three complex programs now write compact JSON whose members follow one declared order (a Go struct, the PHP array, a Rust `Serialize` struct that keeps the client's row JSON) and write `read_sum` and `like_avg` as their shortest decimal without a forced fraction, as Go and PHP do; Rust returns serialization errors from `main` instead of unwrapping them. After the change both subtests pass with 1183 bytes and 200 bytes from each language, and the README states the format and the check. `make example-check` is part of `make check`.
- [o] T17.5 Decode the bench schema types in the Rust native benchmarks. Cause: bench/rust/src/native.rs and bench/rust/src/driver_compare.rs decode `seq` and the other keys as `u64` and the booleans as `u8`, while schema/bench.dbs declares signed `i64` keys, so both programs stop at the first row against the seeded bench database; `make rust-driver-check` only compiles them. Evidence: `native_reads_the_seeded_database` and `driver_compare_reads_the_seeded_database` (bench/rust/tests/seeded.rs) run the built programs with 30 and 10 iterations against `ORM_BENCH_MYSQL_DSN` and require status 0 and a statistics line for every workload. Before the change both failed with status 101 at column 0: "Rust type `u64` (as SQL type `BIGINT UNSIGNED`) is not compatible with SQL type `BIGINT`". Both programs now decode the declared types: `i64` keys, `i32` counts, `bool`, `datetime(6)` as `NaiveDateTime` through the sqlx `chrono` feature, and `varchar` as `String`; `native` propagates every column decode error instead of replacing it with an empty value, its relation step binds only the keys its SQL has placeholders for instead of two extra values, and both programs reject an iteration argument that is not an integer of at least 3 (`native`) or 10 (`driver_compare`) instead of replacing it with the default. After the change `make rust-driver-check` runs the two DSN tests and the two seeded tests one at a time with TEST_ENV, and all 4 pass. docs/perf.md states what the check runs.
- [o] T17.6 Bound the other test deadlines on CPU time. Cause: the deadlines that the remaining tests assert after a case finishes (for example the 1-10 s dbspec and 1-3 s physical bounds of orm-schema and their Go and PHP counterparts) still compare wall-clock time, which on a shared machine includes the time other processes hold the processors, and the TypeScript graph tests bound wall-clock time with their 15-second `node:test` timeouts. Evidence: `make timing-check` runs tests/timing/preempted.test.mjs, which runs each test below in its own process group and stops the group with SIGSTOP for 90 ms after every 10 ms of running, so it receives one tenth of wall-clock time. Before the change the four stress budgets failed there: Go median parse 523 ms over 100 ms, Rust 518 ms over 300 ms, PHP 3110 ms over 400 ms and TypeScript 1232.5 ms over 250 ms; the Rust orm-schema vector deadlines and the six other PHP dbspec tests passed. Every limit that a test sets on its own computation now bounds CPU time and every case prints CPU and wall-clock time: the new workspace crate clients/rust/case-clock (`CaseClock`, `clock_gettime(CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID)`) measures the case thread in the seven orm-schema vector tests, the dbspec_stress example and the two orm-build accessor and page tests; Go `caseClock` (engine/dbspec/case_clock_test.go) measures the OS thread that the stress test locks, because the process CPU time also holds the garbage collector workers (median 135 ms and 120 ms of process CPU against 71-80 ms of thread CPU); clients/php/tests/case_clock.php (`caseClockStart`, `caseClockElapsed`) measures the single-threaded PHP process in the seven dbspec tests; the TypeScript stress test measures `process.threadCpuUsage` (process CPU median 228 ms against 135 ms of thread CPU). After the change all 11 preempted tests pass with medians of 76.7 ms (Go), 49.1 ms (Rust), 391.0 ms (PHP) and 153.1 ms (TypeScript) of CPU time, and `make dbspec-go-check`, `make dbspec-php-check`, `make dbspec-ts-check` and `make dbspec-rust-check` pass. The other deadlines keep wall-clock time: the timers that stop a stuck case (Go `runTimed`, `runWithDeadline` and `exec.CommandContext`, Rust `tokio::time::timeout` and `run_probe`, the `node:test` timeouts, including the 15000 ms dbspec vector timeouts) measure a hang, which uses no CPU; the limits on work that a database or another process does (the Rust 300-600 s apply and introspection suites, the PHP apply, plan-apply, introspection and runtime tests, the introspection comparison budget) measure time spent outside the test process; and the cancellation and lock-wait assertions test timing behavior itself. The unused Go `caseClock` that T8.2.6.4 left in clients/go/orm is removed, AGENTS.md states the rule, and docs/dbspec.md states the CPU budgets and the check.
- [o] T17.7 Make the native benchmark writes and binds match the bench schema. Cause: `relation4` and `listN` in clients/go/bench/native_test.go bind two `bench-salt` values that their SQL has no placeholder for, so `go test ./clients/go/bench -bench Relation4` fails with "sql: expected 1 arguments, got 3", and the `native` insert of bench/rust/src/native.rs writes the plain text `ins@example.com` into the AES column `aes_hex_email`, which the Go baseline encodes with `HostEncode`. Evidence: Go `TestNativeStatementsEqualClient` (clients/go/bench/statements_test.go) compares the native PK and 100-row statements with the statements the generated client runs, and `TestNativeWorkloadsRead` runs every native read workload against the bench database; before the change both failed: the native statements selected raw hex AES columns and `ip` while the client selects `UNHEX` columns, `price` and the hidden `aes_key_version`, and `listN` failed with "sql: expected 1 arguments, got 3". The native statements now equal the client's, the native scan decodes the AES columns with the row's key version through one keyring, and `relation4` and `listN` bind only their keys; both tests pass and `make perf-check` runs them before the hot-path checks. The mismatch was what the `list100` hot-path check measured: at the 2026-09-27 commit "docs: record client overhead measurements" the ratio was 1.13 to 1.18 and at HEAD 1.22 to 1.32 under the same load, with client allocations unchanged (4438 against 4440 per call); with the corrected baseline five runs measured 1.10 to 1.15 against the bound 1.25 and PK 1.08 to 1.13 against 1.35. Rust `insert_email_is_an_aes_hex_envelope` (bench/rust/src/native.rs) failed because the value was not hex and passes now that `native` writes the hex AES ciphertext of `bench-salt`, as the Go baseline's `HostEncode` does. docs/perf.md states the same-SQL check.
- [o] T18 Load a relation over every component of a composite foreign key. Cause: a relation request carries one key pair, so `composite_membership` cannot be loaded from `composite_account` by `tenant_id` and `account_id` together in any client; the T15 `composite_key_rows` cases join on one component and filter the other, while contracts/features.json `composite_keys` claims relations that preserve every key component. Evidence: a relation request carries `keys`, one `{left, right}` pair per key component in key order, in place of `left` and `right` (docs/protocol.md 1.2 and its Korean pair; `IRRelation` and the new `IRKeyPair` in contracts/interfaces.json). The Go, PHP, Rust and TypeScript validators reject an empty `keys`, a pair without a column and a column used twice on one side with `IR_INVALID` and an unknown column with `COLUMN_UNKNOWN`; the planners bind every pair, so the relation step reads `(tenant_id, account_id) IN ((...))`. In generated code each `match<L>With<R>()` adds one pair in call order (Rust `Core::add_match` replaces `set_match`, which overwrote the pair), and the relation of a child with its own connection keys and filters by every component (a tuple condition for two or more). The `composite_key_rows` cases of Go, PHP, Rust and TypeScript now write tenant 990004 (accounts 1 and 2, memberships member and owner) and tenant 990006 (account 2, membership guest), load the memberships of the tenant 990004 accounts by `matchTenantIdWithTenantId().matchAccountIdWithAccountId()` in the same statement and through a child with its own connection, and expect exactly `990004/1:990004/1/member` and `990004/2:990004/2/owner`; they delete every row they write. Red: before the change all four failed on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite with the extra row `990004/2:990006/2/guest`, because the second match replaced the first and the relation joined by `account_id` alone. Green, each on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite against the seeded bench database: Go `TestCoverageCompositeKeyRows` 3/3, PHP and TypeScript `composite_key_rows` 3/3 each, Rust `coverage_composite_keys::coverage_composite_key_rows` 3/3, and the `model_query_rows` relation cases of the four clients 12/12. Owner checks of the changed code, all passing: `go test ./engine/...` (7 packages, with the new `TestCompositeRelationKeys`), 14 Go `clients/go/orm` relation, key and shape tests, Go `TestJoinsAndRelations` on 3 databases; TypeScript `tsc --noEmit`, tests/engine.mjs (new composite relation cases), tests/generate-scan.mjs (2 cases), tests/models-scan.mjs (2 tests), tests/model.mjs on 3 databases; PHP tests/engine_test.php (new composite relation cases), tests/relation_keys.php, tests/model_test.php (11 tests × 3 databases); Rust `engine::tests` (4 tests, new `composite_relation_keys`), orm-build `generate` (17 tests), integration `joins_and_relations` and `writes` on 3 databases, clippy `-D warnings` of orm, orm-build and orm-tests; interface-check with the recorded symbols and hashes, docs-rules-check, checklist-check, git-check, go-fmt-check and rust-fmt-check. The regenerated Go and TypeScript bench models carry `MatchTenantIdWithTenantId` and `matchAccountIdWithAccountId`.
- [o] T19 Report a failed type assertion in the generated Go relation getters. Cause: generator output such as `GetMemberships` in clients/go/model/composite_account.go reads `v, _ := x.m.Related(...).(...)`, which drops a mismatched relation value instead of reporting it. Acceptance: a generated Go relation getter returns `(<result>, error)`: the stored relation value, a nil result without error when the row holds no related row, and an `INTERNAL` error naming the relation result and the requested type when the stored value has another type; every generated Go model is regenerated and none reads a relation with `v, _ :=`; the Rust relation getters, which return `None` for a failed `downcast_ref` or the other relation kind, follow the same rule with `orm::Result<Option<..>>`; tracked Go and Rust unit cases store a mismatched relation value and receive `INTERNAL`, and the Go and Rust generator tests check the emitted getter; PHP and TypeScript return the stored relation value without a type conversion, so they cannot drop it. Evidence: Red before the change: `go test ./generator -run '^TestGoRelationGetterReportsMismatch$'` failed because the generated getters returned one value ("not enough return values, have (*model.BrandModel) want (*model.BrandModel, error)"); `TestRelatedAsReportsMismatch` of clients/go/orm did not compile ("undefined: RelatedAs"); the orm-build test `relation_getters_report_a_mismatched_relation_value` failed with `generated source lacks pub fn get_owner(&self) -> orm::Result<Option<&super::user::User>>`; the orm unit test `relation_results_report_a_mismatched_value` did not compile because `related_one` returned `Option<&M>`. The Go generator writes `return orm.RelatedAs[<result>](x.m, "<name>")`, and `orm.RelatedAs` returns the zero value for a row without the result or without a related row and `INTERNAL` `relation result <name> holds <stored type>, not <requested type>` for another type; `related_one` and `related_many` return `Result<Option<..>>` and report another model, a collection read as a row or a row read as a collection as `INTERNAL` with the result name and the requested type. clients/go/model is regenerated (author, composite_account, service and user; clients/go/decimalmodel has no relation and stays equal), no Go source reads a relation with `v, _ :=`, the call sites of the Go and Rust tests and conformance runners propagate the error, docs/interfaces(.ko).md states the rule, and contracts/symbols/go.json and rust.json record the new signatures with their hashes in contracts/interfaces.json. The new clients/go/orm/related_test.go belongs to the model_queries feature of contracts/features.json. Owner checks run, all green: the four Red cases; `go test ./generator` (all generator tests, 70.6 s); `go test ./contracts ./tests/interfaces/check`; `TestJoinsAndRelations` of clients/go/model on SQLite and on MySQL and PostgreSQL databases created for the run and dropped after it; the featurecoverage cases `TestCoverageModelQueryRows` and `TestCoverageCompositeKeyRows` on the three bench databases; the Rust `orm-build` generate tests (18 passed); the Rust integration case `joins_and_relations` on SQLite and on created and dropped MySQL and PostgreSQL databases; the Rust coverage cases `coverage_model_query_rows` and `coverage_composite_key_rows` on the three bench databases; `cargo clippy -p orm -p orm-build -p orm-tests --all-targets -D warnings`; `make interface-check`, go-fmt-check, rust-fmt-check, docs-rules-check, checklist-check and git-check.
- [o] T20 Reject an invalid iteration argument of the Rust `client_bench`. Cause: clients/rust/tests/src/client_bench.rs replaces an argument that is not a number with 3000 (`.parse().ok().unwrap_or(3000)`), a default that no specification names. Acceptance: `client_bench` requires its iterations argument: without it, or with a value that is not an integer of at least 1, it exits with status 1 before it connects and prints an error naming the iterations argument and the value it received; the bench/rust `native` and `driver_compare`, which reject a non-number but run 3000 and 1000 iterations without an argument, require the argument the same way; tracked process cases run the three programs with a missing, a non-number and a too small argument; the Go bench and the PHP performance test take no iteration argument and TypeScript has no bench program, so they hold no such default. Evidence: Red before the change: clients/rust/tests/tests/client_bench_args.rs failed both cases, because `client_bench` without an argument and with "many" went on to the DSN check ("ORM_BENCH_MYSQL_DSN is required ..."); in bench/rust/tests/dsn.rs `benchmarks_require_the_iterations_argument` failed because `native` without an argument reached the DSN check, and `benchmarks_reject_an_invalid_iterations_argument` failed on the old message "iterations must be an integer of at least 3, got \"many\"", which did not name the argument. Each of the three programs reads its first argument with one `iterations(minimum)` function and has no default count: a missing argument prints `usage: <program> <iterations>; the iterations argument is required` and a value that is not an integer of at least the minimum (1 for `client_bench`, 3 for `native`, 10 for `driver_compare`) prints `the iterations argument must be an integer of at least <minimum>, got <value>`, both with status 1 before connecting; docs/perf(.ko).md states it, and the new clients/rust/tests/tests/client_bench_args.rs belongs to the performance_gate feature of contracts/features.json. Owner checks run, all green: `cargo test -p orm-tests --test client_bench_args --test client_bench_dsn` (4 passed); in bench/rust `cargo test --test dsn` (4 passed) and `cargo test --test seeded` on the seeded MySQL bench database (2 passed); `client_bench 10` on the bench database printed its three statistics lines with n=10; `cargo clippy -D warnings` of orm-tests and of bench/rust; rust-fmt-check, docs-rules-check, checklist-check and git-check.
- [o] T21 Decode MySQL and PostgreSQL date and time cells in the Rust catalog grid. Cause: `GridCell` has no temporal value, so `table_page` of a table with a `datetime` column fails with "Unsupported catalog cell type" on MySQL and PostgreSQL; the T15 `catalog_read` case pages `user` for that reason. Acceptance: `GridCell` has `Date`, `Time` and `DateTime` cells that hold the dbspec text forms `YYYY-MM-DD`, `HH:MM:SS` and `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS` with fraction digits; MySQL `DATE`, `TIME` and `DATETIME` and PostgreSQL `date`, `time` and `timestamp` without time zone decode into them, while the time zone types (MySQL `TIMESTAMP`, PostgreSQL `timestamptz` and `timetz`) stay unsupported, because every connection reads zone-less values in UTC; a read of a described table (table page, row lookup and insert reads) writes exactly p fraction digits, p being the declared precision of the column (MySQL `datetime` without p is 0, PostgreSQL `timestamp` without p is 6), and a read-only grid query, which has no column declaration, writes six; a time of day outside 00:00:00 to 23:59:59.999999 fails instead of wrapping; a temporal primary key cell fails explicitly as a row identity bind; SQLite has no temporal storage class, so its cells keep the storage class of the stored value as its decimals do, and a dbspec column reads as its checked text; a tracked live-db test pages a table with `date`, `time(0)`, `time(3)`, `datetime(0)` and `datetime(6)` columns and reads the same values through a grid query on the three databases; the `catalog_read` case pages `author`, which has `datetime(6)` columns, on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite; only the Rust client has a catalog connection (feature catalog_connection), so no other client holds the defect. Evidence: Red before the decoders, with the three cells added to `GridCell`: the new live-db test clients/rust/orm-build/tests/grid_temporal.rs failed on MySQL and PostgreSQL with "table page: Err(\"error occurred while decoding: Unsupported catalog cell type\")" and the same error for the temporal key page, while SQLite passed; `coverage_catalog_read`, changed to page `author`, failed on the MySQL and PostgreSQL bench databases with "author page: \"error occurred while decoding: Unsupported catalog cell type\"" and passed on SQLite. The MySQL decoder reads `DATE`, `DATETIME` and `TIME` through sqlx and rejects a negative or 24-hour `TIME`; the PostgreSQL decoder reads the binary `date`, `time` and `timestamp` values itself, because the sqlx chrono decoders turn 24:00:00 into 00:00:00; clients/rust/orm-build/src/catalog/temporal.rs cuts the six decoded fraction digits to the declared p for the table page, the row lookup and the three insert reads and fails with `GRID_TEMPORAL_PRECISION` when a cut digit is not zero. The grid query of the test reads `03:04:05.000000` and `2026-01-02 03:04:05.000000` where the page reads `03:04:05` and `2026-01-02 03:04:05`; a row with temporal cells takes an update of its key; `CAST('25:00:00' AS TIME)`, `CAST('24:00:00' AS time)`, PostgreSQL infinity values, a MySQL `TIMESTAMP` column and a PostgreSQL `timestamptz` fail; a `DATE` primary key update fails with "temporal row identity is not supported". A catalog write of a temporal column still binds text, which the read-back temporal cell does not equal, so it fails with `ROW_WRITE_MISMATCH` instead of succeeding. docs/interfaces(.ko).md and docs/schema(.ko).md state the cells, the new test belongs to the catalog_connection feature of contracts/features.json, and contracts/symbols/rust.json with its hash records the new functions. Owner checks run, all green: grid_temporal on SQLite, MySQL and PostgreSQL (each run dropped the tables it created); `coverage_catalog_read` and `coverage_catalog_row_mutation` on the three bench databases; the whole `cargo test -p orm-build --features live-db` (21 test targets, unit and doc tests included); the two unit tests of the precision cut; `cargo clippy -D warnings` of orm-build with live-db and of orm-tests; `make interface-check` and `go test ./contracts ./tests/interfaces/check`; `node scripts/features/build.mjs --check`; rust-fmt-check, docs-rules-check, checklist-check and git-check. `node scripts/features/check.mjs` lists only clients/php/tests/sqlite_without_mysql.php of the T8.8.3 merge as a test file without a feature.
- [o] T21.1 Give SQLite date and time cells of a described table the temporal grid cells (follows T21). Cause: on SQLite a `date`, `time` or `datetime` column of a described table reads as `Text`, while MySQL and PostgreSQL give `Date`, `Time` or `DateTime` for the same dbspec column, so the catalog grid differs by database for one neutral type. Acceptance: reading a described table (table page, row lookup, insert result) gives the same temporal cell on the three databases for the same column and value; a read-only grid query, whose columns have no declaration, keeps the stored text on SQLite. Evidence: Red before the change: grid_temporal, now expecting the temporal cells from the SQLite page, failed on SQLite with "sqlite: table page: Ok(TablePage … Text(…))" and with loose values that the page accepted as text, and `coverage_catalog_read` on the SQLite bench database failed with "sqlite: first author page" (left `Text("2026-06-01 00:00:00.000000")`, right `DateTime(…)`). clients/rust/orm-build/src/catalog/temporal.rs turns a `Text` cell of a SQLite column declared `DATE`, `TIME` or `DATETIME` into `Date`, `Time` or `DateTime` when it is in the dbspec form (a calendar date from 0001-01-01 to 9999-12-31, a time of day with 0 to 6 fraction digits), keeps NULL, and fails every other value with `GRID_TEMPORAL_VALUE: <column> holds a value that is not a dbspec <kind>`; the SQLite renderer CHECK fixes the fraction digits at p, so the cell equals the MySQL and PostgreSQL cell of the same column and value. The read-only grid query still returns `Text` on SQLite. A SQLite `DATE` key now fails as a row identity bind like the others. docs/interfaces(.ko).md states it, and contracts/symbols/rust.json with its hash records the new functions. Owner checks run, all green: grid_temporal on SQLite, MySQL and PostgreSQL (it rejects `2026-01-02T03:04:05Z`, `20260102`, `2026-02-30 03:04:05`, `2026-01-02 24:00:00` and seven fraction digits on a SQLite page; the tables it created were dropped); the orm-build unit tests (12, among them the dbspec form cases); `coverage_catalog_read` and `coverage_catalog_row_mutation` on the three bench databases; the whole `cargo test -p orm-build --features live-db`; `cargo clippy -D warnings` of orm-build with live-db and of orm-tests; `make interface-check` and `go test ./contracts ./tests/interfaces/check`; rust-fmt-check, docs-rules-check, checklist-check and git-check.
- [o] T21.2 Write date and time cells through the catalog grid (follows T21). Cause: a catalog write binds a temporal value as text, so reading the row back gives a temporal cell that differs from the written one and the write fails with `ROW_WRITE_MISMATCH` on every database. Acceptance: a catalog insert and update of `date`, `time` and `datetime` columns succeed on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite and the read-back cell equals the written cell at the column's declared precision. Evidence: Red before the change, with the new write case of grid_temporal binding the values as text: the insert failed with "ROW_WRITE_MISMATCH: stored inserted values differ" on SQLite and MySQL and with "column \"d\" is of type date but expression is of type text" on PostgreSQL, and the update of a `DATE` key failed with "ROW_UPDATE_INVALID: temporal row identity is not supported" on the three databases. `P` and `ParamType` gain `Date`, `Time` and `DateTime`; their text must be in the dbspec form (`TOOL_BIND_INVALID` otherwise, before execution); MySQL and PostgreSQL bind chrono date, time and timestamp values and PostgreSQL declares those parameter types, SQLite binds the text; a temporal grid cell converts to and from these binds, so a temporal row identity binds too. The case inserts `2026-01-02`, `03:04:05.120` and `2026-01-02 03:04:05.123456` into `date`, `time(3)` and `datetime(6)` columns, updates them to `2027-12-31`, `23:59:59.999` and `0001-01-01 00:00:00.000001`, reads each back as the written cells from the insert, the update and the page, rejects `2026-01-02T03:04:05`, and updates a row keyed by a `DATE`. The typed NULL case covers the three kinds. docs/interfaces(.ko).md states the binds, and contracts/symbols/rust.json with its hash records them. Owner checks run, all green: grid_temporal on SQLite, MySQL and PostgreSQL (the tables it created were dropped); the whole `cargo test -p orm-build --features live-db`, typed_binds included; `coverage_catalog_read` and `coverage_catalog_row_mutation` on the three bench databases; `cargo clippy -D warnings` of orm-build with live-db and of orm-tests; `make interface-check` and `go test ./contracts ./tests/interfaces/check`; rust-fmt-check, docs-rules-check, checklist-check and git-check.
- [o] T22 Remove the root `schema:check` npm script. Cause: package.json runs `node scripts/schema/check.mjs`, which T8.2.6.4 removed. Acceptance: the root package.json has no `schema:check` script and no tracked file outside the history files names it; `make repo-check` fails when a root npm script names a repository path (a word with a `/`) that is neither a tracked file nor a directory of tracked files, naming the script and the path; a tracked node test covers a missing path, a tracked file, a tracked directory and a word without a `/`, and `make repo-check` runs it. Evidence: Red before the removal: with the new path check in scripts/repo/check.mjs, `make repo-check` failed with "package.json script schema:check names scripts/schema/check.mjs, which is not a tracked file or directory" and "repository check: 1 script path problems remain". The `schema:check` line is removed from package.json, and no other tracked file names the script or scripts/schema/check.mjs outside CHANGELOG and the checklists. scripts/repo/scripts.mjs holds `scriptPathErrors`, which treats every word with a `/` of a root npm script as a repository path and accepts a tracked file or a directory that holds one after a leading `./` is removed; scripts/repo/check.test.mjs covers the four cases and the Makefile target runs it before the check. Owner checks run, all green: `make repo-check` (the node test, then "repository check: 890 tracked paths inspected"), docs-rules-check, checklist-check and git-check.
- [o] T24 Make every check report each case as it runs, with its own time limit (AGENTS testing rule). Cause: `make feature-check` runs about 25 minutes and prints nothing until coverage.mjs ends; `dbspec-introspect-compare-check` runs 5-11 minutes and prints only the start and end of each database; the physical Go checks in the Makefile are bounded only by whole-run limits (`-timeout 70m` for dbspec-apply-check, `40m` and `35m` for the conformance runner checks, `0` for the dialect facts check). Acceptance: every check prints, for each case, its start, its result (passed or failed with the reason) and its elapsed time while it runs; every case carries its own time limit and no whole-run limit replaces it; a case that runs longer than a minute prints its steps with their elapsed time; a test asserts the output form of each runner. Evidence: T24.1-T24.5 survey every Makefile target and runner script. Go tests (`check`, the dbspec physical checks, dialect-facts, the conformance checks, example, fuzz, dbspec-go, client-db and perf scripts, feature commands): `GO_TEST` (`go test -v -timeout 0`) and internal/testcase cases. Node tests and runners (checklist, repository, git, docs-rules, docs-build, docs-check, docs-static, docs-verify-idempotent, feature-check, feature-docs, dbspec-ts and the TypeScript physical checks, dbspec-compare, ts-check, ts-min-check, ts-model, timing, group-rows, unselected-column and decimal physical checks): tests/testcase.mjs. PHP tests (client-unit, dbspec-php and the PHP physical checks, client-db, perf, php-without-mysql, conformance-result, tests/codec/check.php): tests/testcase.php; tests/conformance/run_result_tests.py is removed. Rust tests and programs (dbspec-rust, the Rust physical checks, rust-send-savepoint, rust-driver, client-db's integration program): orm-testcase. Interface and conformance CLIs: `testcase.Run`. Build, format, lint and package commands: tests/run-case.mjs. Not checks and unchanged: test-servers, test-servers-stop, docs-dev, decimal-db-setup, decimal-bench-sqlite, scripts/bench-db.sh, scripts/build-artifacts.sh, scripts/typescript/node-min.sh and the stress and vector generators. `make testcase-check` asserts the form of the Go, JavaScript, PHP, Rust and command harnesses, and coverage.test.mjs that of coverage.mjs. The full suite runs once after the checklist is complete.
- [o] T25 Give every case that needs an empty database a database of its own. Cause: the `schemaEmpty`, `conditions` and `joinsAndRelations` cases and the other cases that assert `schema().empty()` before installing run on the fixed shared database of `ORM_TEST_MYSQL_DSN` and the `public` schema of `ORM_TEST_POSTGRES_DSN`, so any table that another run or an interrupted run left there fails them; on 2026-10-03 the full run failed five feature commands (model_queries PHP and TypeScript, parameter_chunking Rust, PHP and TypeScript) and ts-check on `a database without tables` while the clients were correct. Acceptance: every case that asserts an empty database or installs a schema creates its own uniquely named database (MySQL) or schema (PostgreSQL), or its own SQLite file, and drops it when it ends, also after a failure; no case assumes the shared database is empty; a test leaves a table in the shared database and shows that the cases still pass and leave nothing of their own behind. Evidence: T25.1 (Go, internal/testdb), T25.2 (PHP, clients/php/tests/case_database.php), T25.3 (TypeScript, clients/typescript/tests/case-database.mjs) and T25.4 (Rust, clients/rust/case-database) give each case that checks an empty database or installs a schema the database `orm_case_<pid>_<n>`, created through ORM_TEST_MYSQL_DSN or ORM_TEST_POSTGRES_DSN as the admin connection, or the SQLite file `orm-case-<pid>-<n>.sqlite`, and drop it when the case ends, also after a failure, reporting both as STEP lines. PostgreSQL gets a database instead of the schema this item named, because `schema().empty()` counts every schema other than public as content, so a case schema inside a shared database cannot pass an empty check. Survey (grep for `empty()`, `install(`, `ORM_TEST_*_DSN` and `DROP TABLE` in the tests of the four clients and in the feature and coverage runners): the cases that checked emptiness of a shared database were TypeScript `schemaEmpty` and `install()` (every installing model.mjs case), PHP `schema empty` and Rust `schema_empty`; the cases that installed into a shared database are listed in T25.1-T25.4; the feature commands model_queries and parameter_chunking failed through their verification commands, which run model_test.php, model.mjs and the Rust `integration` program; the coverage cases (`coverage_*`) run on the seeded bench DSN that feature-check supplies and whose state it reads before and after each run, and stay there; the dbspec physical tests and the schema-set tests already used databases of their own (the schema-set tests now use the helpers). The statement timeout cases through the PgBouncer entry `orm_test_single`, which reaches only `orm_test`, install under a table name of their own there and drop only that table. `make case-database-check` (scripts/case-database-check.mjs, part of `make check`) builds the TypeScript client and the Rust `integration` program, creates a table `orm_leftover_<pid>` with one row in the shared MySQL database and PostgreSQL public schema, reads the shared state digests of tests/conformance/check, the PostgreSQL schemas, the `orm_case_` databases and the `orm-case-` files, runs Go TestSchemaEmpty and ./clients/go/model, PHP model_test.php, TypeScript model.mjs and the Rust `integration` program, requires each to pass and every value read before to be unchanged, and drops its table in `finally`. With that table and the leftover tables `routed` and `unowned` of other runs in the shared databases it passed: Go TestSchemaEmpty 1 and ./clients/go/model 18 cases, PHP 66, TypeScript 57 and Rust 30 passed with no FAIL, the cases created and dropped 2, 18, 35, 33 and 20 databases, the shared state stayed mysql 238574e7… and postgres 636d2bfd…, no `orm_case_` database and no `orm-case-` file remained, and the table was dropped.
- [o] T24.1 Report every Go test and Go check runner case by case with its own deadline (part of T24). Cause: Go tests printed only package results without `-v`, the physical checks logged ad hoc `start`/`result` lines or none, and their only limits were whole-binary `-timeout` values. Acceptance: every Go test case prints its start with its deadline, its steps when it can run longer than a minute, and its result with the elapsed time while it runs; every case carries its own deadline; no Makefile, script or feature command uses a whole-binary limit in its place; a test asserts the output form. Evidence: the new package internal/testcase gives one form: `RUN <case> deadline=<d>`, `STEP <case> elapsed=<d>: <step>`, `PASS <case> elapsed=<d>`, `FAIL <case> elapsed=<d>: <reason>` (a test failure names the errors logged above it), `SKIP <case> elapsed=<d>`, and `RUN <group> group` for a test whose subtests carry the deadlines. `Start(t, deadline)` gives a context that ends at the deadline and a watchdog that, 5 s (`Grace`) after it, prints `FAIL … deadline <d> exceeded` with every goroutine stack and ends the binary; `Run(name, deadline, work)` reports a case of a program that is not a test; `StepWriter` turns each nonblank output line of a child process into a STEP line. The deadline classes state their basis: `Compute` 1m (memory and repository files only; the largest such case, `TestStressDocument`, took 2.4 s), `Database` 2m, `Process` 5m (cold `go run`/`go build`). Every top-level `Test` and `Fuzz` function of the 120 other Go test files starts a case: a class by what the test does, or its own deadline: probe cases of TestDialectFacts, TestDDLVectors, TestSchemaDocumentsApply, TestIntrospectRoundTrip, TestIntrospectUnsupported, TestPlanApply, TestApplyChain and TestSQLiteFileNameWithQuery 1m (probe 30 s plus cleanup 30 s) with a start step and each observed note as a step; TestApplyStressPlan 21m per database with a step every 2000 applied steps; TestIntrospectCompare 15m per database with a step every 1000 statements and per runner; TestApplyChainAcrossClients 7m per client pair with a step per runner action; the conformance physical tests a build case (40m and 30m) and a 32m case per language and database with first run, repeated run and comparison steps, and 6m per database for the state checks; TestExampleOutputsAreIdentical 5m per example; the hot path gates 5m; TestCPULoad starts a case only in the parent process, because its load child runs until the parent ends it. `go run ./tests/conformance/check run` reports the cases `conformance/<db>/build`, `/<language>` and `/compare`, with each command and its output lines as steps; `go run ./tests/interfaces/check` reports `interfaces/manifest`, `interfaces/rust-build`, `interfaces/<language>` and `interfaces/results`, with a failure's reasons in its FAIL line. The Makefile runs every Go test with `GO_TEST = go test -v -timeout 0` in place of `-timeout 10m`, `70m`, `30m`, `40m`, `35m`, `3m` and `0`; scripts/client-db-test.sh, scripts/perf-test.sh and the Go commands of contracts/features.json use `-v -timeout 0` (the `-timeout 45s` and `90s` are gone). `make testcase-check` runs TestReportForm, which runs fixture tests in a child `go test -v` and asserts the lines and their order for a passing case with a step, a failing case after its error, a skipped case, a group with two cases, `Run` passing with a step and child output lines without the blank one, failing with its reason, ending at its context deadline and stuck past it, and a case past its deadline that prints FAIL, a goroutine stack and no PASS. Owner checks run, all green: `make testcase-check`; `go test -v -timeout 0` of ./engine/..., ./contracts, ./tests/interfaces/..., ./tests/conformance/..., ./tests/dialects and ./generator (24 generator cases, longest 11.0 s); `TestSQLiteFileNameWithQuery`; the SQLite probes of TestDialectFacts, TestApplyChain and TestPlanApply on TEST_ENV; TestApplyStressPlan on SQLite (`STEP TestApplyStressPlan/sqlite elapsed=2.249s: 2000 of 12000 steps applied` … `PASS TestApplyStressPlan/sqlite elapsed=1m15.03s`); TestIntrospectCompare on SQLite (13 statement steps and four runner steps, `PASS TestIntrospectCompare/sqlite elapsed=1m17.936s`); TestPhysicalCounterCleanup on SQLite; `TestClockMicrosecondsSQLite`; `go run ./tests/conformance/check run -driver sqlite` twice (`PASS conformance/sqlite/build elapsed=3m7.386s` on a cold build, then each language and the comparison); `go run ./tests/interfaces/check -language go --self-test`; `go vet` with the tags physical, featurecoverage, ormtest, examples and decimalphysical; go-fmt-check, docs-rules-check, checklist-check and git-check.
- [o] T24.2 Report every JavaScript test and Node check runner case by case with its own deadline (part of T24). Cause: `make feature-check` printed nothing until coverage.mjs ended; the TypeScript tests and Node runners printed ad hoc `start`/`result`/`ok` lines or only a summary, and plain test scripts stopped at their first failure without a deadline. Acceptance: as T24.1 for every Node test file and Node runner, with a test that asserts the output form. Evidence: tests/testcase.mjs gives the Go form (`RUN <case> deadline=<d>`, `STEP`, `PASS`, `FAIL … : <reason>`, durations written like Go's `time.Duration`) with `runCase` (an AbortSignal ends at the deadline and the case fails 5 s later), `caseTest` (a node:test test that runs a case), `cases()` (a script's sequential cases and its exit code), `sections()` (uninterruptible code regions: FAIL and exit after the deadline, FAIL when the process ends inside one), `runGroup` (a command whose runner reports its own cases) and `stepLines` (child output lines as steps). Classes: `COMPUTE` 1m, `DATABASE` 2m, `PROCESS` 5m. coverage.mjs reports each native run as `<feature>/<role>/<language>/<database> run <n>` with a deadline of the run's process limits added up (state readers and native commands, each 10m), a step per state digest and per command, and the stderr lines of cargo and go as steps, DSN redacted; features/check.mjs reports each verification command as a group with its output lines as steps and gains `--feature <id>`. Every node:test file uses `caseTest` with its former timeout or `COMPUTE` (clients/typescript/tests dbspec, dbspec-rules, dbspec-render, dbspec-plan, dbspec-mermaid, dbspec-apply-cleanup, dbspec-apply-physical, dbspec-plan-physical, dbspec-introspect, dbspec-stress, dbspec_runtime_db, models-scan; scripts checklist, git, repository and features tests; tests/conformance/result_typescript.test.mjs; tests/dbspec/compare check and runners tests; tests/dbspec/inputs.test.mjs; tests/timing/preempted.test.mjs, which streams the preempted child's lines as steps); the plain scripts aggregate_numeric, decimal_model, group_rows, dsn, engine, generate, generate-scan, codec-vector, styled-value, clock, driver-error, rollback, schema-set, sqlite-concurrency and model run each section as a case and set the exit code after all of them; the group-rows, unselected-column and decimal physical runners, tests/dbspec/compare/check.mjs and the checklist, git, repository, docs-rules, feature-docs, docs build, docs idempotence and docs static checks report their cases. The owner files whose output coverage.mjs or a physical runner parses (coverage_*.mjs, group_rows_db.mjs, decimal_model_db.mjs) keep their exact `CASE <id> PASS` output and are reported by that runner. The docs build stopped on raw `<program>` and `<name>` placeholders in the T19 and T20 records; they are code spans now. `make testcase-check` also runs tests/testcase.test.mjs, which asserts in child processes the lines and their order of `runCase` passing with a step, failing with its reason, ending at its signal and stuck past the deadline, of `runGroup` with child lines and without the blank one, of `sections` passing, failing, ending inside a case and stuck past the deadline with exit 1, and of `caseTest` under `node --test`; coverage.test.mjs asserts the nine lines of two passing runs and a failing one. Owner checks run, all green: `make testcase-check`; the node:test files above (265 tests); the TypeScript physical tests on their SQLite cases (83); the compare and input tests of the TypeScript runner (34); the preempted Go and TypeScript timing cases; dsn, engine, generate, generate-scan, codec-vector, styled-value, aggregate_numeric, decimal_model, group_rows and sqlite-concurrency; clock, driver-error, rollback and schema-set (33 cases on SQLite, MySQL and PostgreSQL); model.mjs (57 cases, longest 1.724 s); `node scripts/features/coverage.mjs --feature planner` (8 runs); `node scripts/features/check.mjs --run --feature planner`; the unselected-column (9) and group-rows (15) physical checks; docs build, idempotence (two builds equal in 179 files) and static check; checklist, git, repository and docs-rules checks.

Cause: the PHP tests printed `RUN`/`PASS` lines in several forms (`cpuMs=`, `elapsedMs=`, `ok   `, plain summaries), most stopped at the first failure without a FAIL line, the render, manifest, introspect and runtime cases had no deadline, and runtime_model and runtime_db bounded the whole script (30 s, 120 s) instead of each case; tests/conformance/run_result_tests.py printed its own form. Acceptance: as T24.1 for every PHP test script, with a test that asserts the output form. Evidence: tests/testcase.php gives the Go form with `testcase_run` (a closure case), `TestCases` (a script's cases and exit code), `testcase_begin`/`testcase_step`/`testcase_end` (a code section; an uncaught error that ends the script prints its FAIL line from the shutdown handler with the message without the `Uncaught <class>:` prefix and the file) and the classes `TESTCASE_COMPUTE` 60 s, `TESTCASE_DATABASE` 120 s and `TESTCASE_PROCESS` 300 s. With pcntl a SIGALRM at the deadline interrupts the case (system calls are not restarted) and 5 s later prints FAIL and exits; without pcntl (the official PHP image of php-without-mysql-check) and for deadlines below the alarm's second, a case past its deadline fails when it ends. The full error goes to stderr and its message to the FAIL line. case_clock.php's `cpuCaseBegin`/`cpuCaseEnd` keep each CPU-time limit as the assertion and stop a stuck case after ten times that limit in wall-clock time, because timing-check gives the process a tenth of the CPU. Converted: dsn, relation_keys, hostcodec, engine, runtime_model (its 30 s whole-script limit is gone), orm_gen, aggregate_numeric, decimal_model, group_rows, unselected_column, styled_value, aes_json, runtime_db (its 120 s whole-script limit is gone), sqlite_concurrency, sqlite_without_mysql, perf_gate (5 min per measured query, with the ratios as steps), model (each of 13 sections), clock, driver_error, rollback, schema_set, and every dbspec test (vectors and rules with their CPU totals as `cpu-total` cases, manifest, render, mermaid, plan, stress, apply cleanup, introspect, introspect stress, plan apply, apply); tests/conformance/result_php.php is a case. Classes `COMPUTE` 60 s, `DATABASE` 120 s, `PROCESS` 300 s, and `wall_for_cpu(limit)` = ten times a CPU-time limit for tests that assert one (timing-check gives the process a tenth of the CPU). - [o] T26 Make the rollback failure cases pass through the poolers. Cause: through ProxySQL, the cases `rollback_failed/mysql` and `savepoint_rollback_failed/mysql` of clients/php/tests/rollback_test.php and clients/typescript/tests/rollback.mjs and the MySQL rollback failure cases of clients/rust/orm/tests/rollback.rs end without the error they assert, because the KILL that the case sends through ProxySQL does not end the server session that holds the transaction; through PgBouncer the Rust case's own session connection fails with `unsupported startup parameter: extra_float_digits`; so `make client-pooler-check` fails while the Go cases pass. Acceptance: the rollback failure cases of every client pass in `make client-pooler-check` with the failure they assert on MySQL and PostgreSQL. Evidence: ProxySQL takes a text-protocol `KILL <id>` as a command for its own client sessions: a probe through ORM_TEST_PROXYSQL_DSN got OK for `KILL <id>` of a backend thread in a transaction and the session stayed alive, while a prepared `KILL ?` reached the server and ended it, which is why the Go case (`raw.Exec("KILL ?", id)`) passed. The Rust case opened its session connection with `sqlx::PgPool::connect`, whose defaults send the startup parameter `extra_float_digits`, which PgBouncer rejects unless `ignore_startup_parameters` lists it; the client itself sends none (`extra_float_digits(None)` in orm::db, since PostgreSQL 12 prints float8 exactly by default), so the client is right for deployments behind a default PgBouncer and the pooler configuration stays unchanged. Change: the make targets export `ORM_TEST_MYSQL_SERVER_DSN` and `ORM_TEST_POSTGRES_SERVER_DSN`, the server DSNs of TEST_ENV, in WITH_TEST_ENV, which client-pooler-check does not replace; the rollback failure cases of Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust open the case database on that server and end the session that holds the lock on its `rollback_probe` (the case database name marks the transaction behind the pooler; PostgreSQL also filters `pg_locks` by the current database) with `KILL` or `pg_terminate_backend`, and fail naming the variable when it is unset; the Rust session connection is a client connection (`Db::connect`). The cases still assert the same ROLLBACK errors. Red before the change through ProxySQL and PgBouncer (ORM_TEST_MYSQL_DSN and ORM_TEST_POSTGRES_DSN set to the pooler DSNs): PHP rollback_test.php failed `rollback_failed/mysql` and `savepoint_rollback_failed/mysql` on "transaction raises nothing", TypeScript rollback.mjs the same two on "transaction raises undefined", and Rust `rollback_failed_mysql` and `savepoint_rollback_failed_mysql` on "transaction: ()" and `rollback_failed_postgres` and `savepoint_rollback_failed_postgres` on "unsupported startup parameter: extra_float_digits", with and without `test-faults`. Green, directly and through ProxySQL and PgBouncer: PHP rollback_test.php 9 cases, TypeScript `node --conditions=orm-test` rollback.mjs 9, Rust `cargo test -p orm --test rollback` 7 and with `--features test-faults` 10, Go TestRollbackFailed*, TestSavepointRollbackFailed* and `-tags ormtest` TestRollbackFault* 10; without ORM_TEST_MYSQL_SERVER_DSN the Rust MySQL cases fail with "ORM_TEST_MYSQL_SERVER_DSN is required"; no `orm_case_` database, `rollback_probe` table or `orm-case-` file remained. go-fmt-check, rust-fmt-check, `cargo clippy -p orm --features test-faults --test rollback -- -D warnings` and `php -l` passed.

Cause: T24 made checks report each case but kept their durations: `make feature-check` takes about 40 minutes (the full run of 2026-10-03 had 107 cases done after 12 minutes; the first Rust coverage case took 2 m 40 s because each Rust coverage command runs `cargo` and compiles), and case limits of 30 minutes remain (`rustBuildDeadline` of the conformance check, coverage case deadlines).
