  - [o] T29.1 Stop dbspec apply, recover, rollback and finalize when the connection does not keep one server session (follows T29). Cause: the lock of a command must cover statements that run outside transactions one by one, and a session advisory lock does not survive transaction pooling; through PgBouncer (`pool_mode = transaction`) apply ran without the lock and no error: an apply whose server connection another client had left holding the lock succeeded, and an apply whose statements moved to another server connection ran every step and failed only at the unlock. Evidence: in Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust the command reads the server session (MySQL `CONNECTION_ID()`, PostgreSQL `pg_backend_pid()`) and whether it already holds the lock before taking it, the lock statement returns the server session, and the statement of every step is preceded by a read of the server session; a shared or changed session is a `session` error whose message names the requirement (a direct or session-pooled connection), raised before the next statement; docs/plans.md (+ko) state the requirement. Each client's apply check runs both cases through PgBouncer in a database of its own and requires the `session` error (before: the shared lock applied without error, the moved session ended in `the advisory lock of dbspec$plans was not held at unlock`); the other apply scenarios and the dbspec probe servers of the Rust tests use ORM_TEST_MYSQL_SERVER_DSN and ORM_TEST_POSTGRES_SERVER_DSN, and the Rust schema-set cases create and drop their databases through them (PgBouncer rejected the raw sqlx `extra_float_digits` startup parameter). dbspec-apply-check, dbspec-apply-php-check, dbspec-apply-ts-check, dbspec-apply-rust-check, rust-check, rust-fmt-check and `make client-pooler-check` pass. Commit T29.1 was made without the native symbol snapshots of the new private symbols, so feature-check failed in interface_contract (`go: unexpected engine/dbspec/apply.go::applier.checkSession` and the same in the other three clients) until contracts/symbols and the hashes of contracts/interfaces.json were recorded in the next commit.
- [o] T30 Let an audit setting select the recorded columns. Cause: the audit triggers copied every column of an audited table into its history table, so a large or sensitive column could not be kept out of the history. Acceptance: `audit into <history> operation <col> action <col> previous <col> [exclude (col, ...) | include (col, ...)]` with one list at most; the operation column is always recorded; the history table holds exactly the recorded columns with their names and types besides its identity key, action and previous columns; the triggers copy only the recorded columns on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite; introspection restores the setting from the triggers without a marker; invalid uses are diagnostics with rules; docs/dbspec.md Audit (+ko) states that a value that needs encryption is encrypted by the codec before the write, so the triggers copy ciphertext; shared vectors compared by dbspec-compare-check and physical cases on the three databases in Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust. Evidence: Go (engine/dbspec: `AuditSetting.Exclude`, `Include`, `Records`, `Excluded`, the parser lists and `validator.auditLists`), PHP (`Setting::$exclude`, `$include`, `records`, `excluded`, `auditLine`, `Parser::auditListTokens`, `auditLists`), TypeScript (src/dbspec/audit.ts with `auditRecords`, `auditExcluded`, `auditLine`, and the parse lists) and Rust (`Setting::Audit { lists }`, `AuditList`, `Setting::records`, `audit_line`) parse, check, emit, render and introspect the lists alike. A listed column must be a column of the table and appear once, both lists are rejected at the second keyword, and the operation column is rejected in either list, because it is always recorded and one recorded set has one spelling; each is a `setting` error at the listed column. The schema text writes the setting with the `exclude` list of the unrecorded columns in column order, or without a list, because the database holds only the recorded columns: the manifest text keeps the written list and an `include` setting and its `exclude` equivalent have one `schemaHash`; introspection reads the recorded columns from the `audit_insert` insert and restores that form. The comparison adds `drop_audit` and `add_audit` when the recorded set of the columns that both tables have differs, so a new column that a setting does not record stays an `add_column` (docs/plans.md, +ko). The history table check reports each mismatch at the history table name in all four clients; the PHP and Rust checks had reported one combined error, so their own rule cases `setting-audit-shape`, `immutable-and-audit-on-cascade-child` and `audit-history-shape` now expect one error per mismatch, as Go and TypeScript report. Vectors: tests/dbspec/cases.json `audit-exclude`, `audit-include`, `audit-list-spacing`, nine invalid cases (`audit-list-unknown-column`, `audit-list-repeated-column`, `audit-both-lists`, `audit-excludes-operation`, `audit-includes-operation`, `audit-history-has-excluded-column`, `audit-history-differs-from-included-columns`, `audit-list-without-parentheses`, `audit-list-trailing-word`) and the hashes case `audit-list-in-schema-text`; tests/dbspec/ddl.json `audit-columns` with the statements of the three dialects and behavior steps (insert, update and soft delete of `card`, which excludes `secret`, and insert and update of `tag`, which includes `label`, each recorded in a history table without the other columns, and a refused delete); tests/dbspec/plans.json comparison `audit-recorded-columns`. Physical: contracts/fixtures/audit_columns.dbs and the coverage case `audit_selected_columns` of the feature `audit_triggers` (Go `TestCoverageAuditSelectedColumns`, clients/php/tests/coverage_audit_columns.php, TypeScript and Rust `coverage_audit_selected_columns`) write through the client with operations 7 and 8 and require the history columns and rows on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite. Red: with the vectors added and the previous code, Go failed 16 subtests of TestSharedVectors, TestRenderVectors and TestCompareSchemas (`unexpected 'exclude', expected the end of the line`), TypeScript 17 of 197 dbspec tests, PHP stopped in dbspec_test, dbspec_manifest_test, dbspec_render_test and dbspec_plan_test at the first new case, and Rust `shared_dbspec_vectors` panicked. Green: dbspec-go-check, dbspec-php-check, dbspec-ts-check, dbspec-rust-check, dbspec-compare-check (the four runners agree twice), dbspec-ddl-check and dbspec-introspect-check with `ddl_audit_columns` and `schema_audit_columns` on the three databases, dbspec-introspect-ts-check, dbspec-introspect-php-check, dbspec-introspect-rust-check, `node scripts/features/coverage.mjs --feature audit_triggers` (four clients, three databases, two runs each), `node scripts/features/check.mjs --run --feature audit_triggers`, interface-check with the re-recorded symbol inventories, rust-check, ts-check, go-fmt-check and rust-fmt-check pass. Correction: T30 was committed while `make interface-check` and the feature-check interface_contract case failed, because rustfmt rewrapped the doc comment of the Rust `Setting` enum after its symbols were recorded; T30 records the formatted symbols, after which interface-check and `make feature-check` (17 feature contracts) pass.
- [o] T31 Let every transaction entry point take an operation id. Cause: an audited write needs an operation id, but Rust `Db::transaction_once` (runs once, no retry, keeps the callback error type) took none: `operation(id)` existed only on the `transaction` and `transaction_send` builders, so every audited write through `transaction_once` failed with `CONFIG`. Acceptance: `transaction_once` is a builder that takes `operation(id)` and existing `.await` call sites stay unchanged; every transaction entry point of the four clients is checked to carry an operation id, with owner cases on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite, docs/usage.md, docs/interface-implementation.md, the interface and symbol records. Evidence: Rust `Db::transaction_once(f)` returns the builder `TransactionOnce` with `operation(id)`; awaiting it runs `TransactionOnceFuture` (clients/rust/orm/src/tx.rs), a state machine that begins the transaction or the savepoint, runs the callback once in the task-local frame, commits, releases or rolls back, and keeps the previous results: `TransactionOnceError::Orm`, `Callback` and `Rollback`, the rollback fault and a continued panic. The callback bound is `FnOnce() -> Fut` with `Fut: Future<Output = Result<T, E>>`, which `async ||` closures satisfy, so existing `.await` call sites compile unchanged; the steps the ORM awaits are boxed `Send` futures, so the future is `Send` when the callback, its future, `T` and `E` are. A nested `transaction_once` with an operation id returns `TransactionOnceError::Orm` with `CONFIG`. Go `Transaction`, PHP `transaction` and TypeScript `transaction`, also through the Go `WithContext` and TypeScript `withSignal` handles, already take the operation id; they are the only entry points of those clients. Red: `cargo test --test audit` with `transaction_once(...).operation(10)` in `audit_operation_id` failed to compile with "no method named `operation` found for opaque type `impl Future<Output = Result<(), TransactionOnceError<orm::Error>>>`". Green: `audit_operation_id` (clients/rust/orm/tests/audit.rs) passes on SQLite, MySQL and PostgreSQL case databases with `transaction_send` and `transaction_once` writes recorded under operations 9 and 10, `transaction_once` without an id returning `Callback` with `CONFIG` and a nested one with an id returning `CONFIG`; the 33 orm library tests pass, among them the send savepoint cases, which now require the `transaction_once` future with `operation(7)` to be `Send`, and the one-shot and savepoint-end cases; the 10 rollback tests pass. The coverage case `audit_operation_entry_points` of feature audit_triggers (Go `TestCoverageAuditOperationEntryPoints`, PHP and TypeScript `coverage_audit_triggers`, Rust `coverage_audit_operation_entry_points`) writes through every entry point of its client and checks item_history and the nested `CONFIG`; it passes in the four clients on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite databases of this run. interface-check passes with the recorded Rust symbols, and the audit_triggers feature check, feature docs, clippy with test-faults, rustfmt, gofmt, docs-check, docs-rules-check, version-check and checklist-check pass. docs/usage.md, docs/interface-implementation.md and the Rust README pairs state the builder. Superseded: T35 replaces `operation(id)` with `.audit(values)` on the three builders; the builder form of `transaction_once` stays.

Cause: `buildRust` of tests/interfaces/check returned tests/interfaces/rust/target/debug/orm-interface-symbols, but the Makefile gives every cargo command `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=clients/rust/target`, so cargo built the tool there; in a new worktree `go run ./tests/interfaces/check --self-test` failed with "fork/exec …/tests/interfaces/rust/target/debug/orm-interface-symbols: no such file or directory", and a checkout that kept an older tool ran that stale build (the main checkout held one from 2026-10-02). Evidence: `buildRust` builds with `--message-format json-render-diagnostics` and returns the executable that cargo reports for orm-interface-symbols, or an error when it reports none. `TestBuildRustReturnsBuiltExecutable` (tests/interfaces/check/rust_tool_test.go) sets `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` to a temporary directory; before the change it failed with "buildRust = …/tests/interfaces/rust/target/debug/orm-interface-symbols, want the executable cargo built in CARGO_TARGET_DIR …", after it passes, and `go run ./tests/interfaces/check --self-test` passes in the new worktree; go vet and gofmt pass.
- [o] T32 Restore a soft-deleted row explicitly. Cause: a table with `soft_delete` keeps the unique key values of soft-deleted rows and an audited table rejects a physical `DELETE`, so re-adding a removed association (a link row with a unique key) failed with `DUPLICATE_KEY`, and no call read or restored a soft-deleted row because the planner always adds the soft delete filter. Acceptance: an explicit restore, in the four clients, of a soft-deleted row found by its primary key or one unique key, with new values of its other columns, clears the soft delete column with an `UPDATE` (an audit records it with its operation like any update) and returns the restored model; a row that is not deleted and a missing row have a defined result stated in the docs; default reads do not change; shared planner and conformance vectors; owner cases on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite including an audited table with an exclude list. Evidence: the IR kind `restore` (engine/ir, PHP Validator, TypeScript and Rust validate) takes a `where` of `eq` predicates that names every column of the primary key or of one unique key once and a `set` of new values checked like an `update`; the planners (Go `restoreStep`, PHP `Planner::restoreStep`, TypeScript `restoreStep`, Rust `restore_step`) render `UPDATE <table> SET <new values>, <soft delete> = NULL[, <operation> = ?] WHERE <key> AND <soft delete> IS NOT NULL` and reject a table without `soft_delete`, another predicate, a partial or other key and an assignment of a primary key, identity, soft delete or operation column with `IR_INVALID`. The model method `restore` (Go `Core.Restore` and generated `Restore`, PHP `Model::restore`, TypeScript `Model.restore`, Rust `orm::model::restore` and the orm-build template) takes the primary key when every primary key column has a set value, otherwise the first unique key in name order whose columns all have set values (`CONFIG` without one), writes every other set value with the restore, then reads the row by the key: a row that is not deleted is returned unchanged with nothing written, a missing row is `NO_ROWS`, and an audited restore without an operation id is `CONFIG`. `restore` is a reserved column name in the four generators and docs/dsl.md. Red: Go `TestRestorePlansGuardedUpdate` failed with "IR_INVALID: unknown kind \"restore\"", the Go owner test failed to compile with "Restore undefined (type *orm.Core has no field or method Restore)" and `TestColumnNameRules` with "restore: got `<nil>`, want \"reserved\""; PHP engine_test and TypeScript engine.mjs failed with "IR_INVALID: unknown kind \"restore\"", the PHP and TypeScript generator tests accepted a column named `restore`, and the PHP and TypeScript database cases failed with "Restore is not a column of membership"; Rust failed with "IR_INVALID: unknown kind \"restore\"", "cannot find function `restore` in module `orm::model`" and "reserved_method_names_are_not_columns: restore: ()". The change to new values failed first in the four clients with "IR_INVALID: restore takes no set or optimistic" and "restore of membership names every column of its primary key or of one unique key once with an eq value". Green: the shared scenario on contracts/fixtures/restore.dbs (membership with the unique key (team_id, member_id) and an audit that excludes note, label with the unique key (name) and no audit: the re-insert is `DUPLICATE_KEY`, the default read is `NO_ROWS`, the restore writes the new note under operation 4 and the history holds insert 1, update 2 deleted and update 4 live, a second restore leaves the row, a missing key is `NO_ROWS`, a restore without key values is `CONFIG`) passes as Go `TestRestoreSoftDeletedRow`, PHP runtime_db_test, TypeScript dbspec_runtime_db and Rust `restore_soft_deleted_row` on SQLite, MySQL and PostgreSQL case databases, and as the coverage case `soft_delete_restore` of audit_triggers in the four clients on the three databases twice; the planner coverage cases `planner_restore` and `planner_restore_rejects_non_key` (contracts/fixtures/planner.json) pass in the four clients; the conformance vector `restore` (soft_record by seq with a new name, in a transaction that rolls back) is recorded after `go run ./tests/conformance/check run` reported 26 vectors × 4 languages identical on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite of a bench database of this run, and soft_record is a declared counter table. interface-check passes with the rule `Model.restore`, the recorded symbols and the `restore` sequence; `--results` on the three outputs passes after the `delete_recursive` sequence expectation was corrected from `false` to the recorded `NO_ROWS`. Feature checks, go-model-check, ts-model-check, go vet, gofmt, rustfmt, the Rust clippy variants, docs-check, docs-rules-check and version-check pass. docs/usage.md, docs/protocol.md, docs/dbspec.md, docs/interfaces.md, docs/interface-implementation.md and docs/dsl.md (+ko) state the call.
- [o] T33 Set the operation id inside a running transaction. Cause: a unit of work whose operation id is the key of a row it inserts first (an unaudited operation row) has the id only after its transaction began, but the four clients took the id only when the transaction began, so its audited writes failed with `CONFIG`. Acceptance: in the four clients a call on the running transaction sets the id before the first audited write; an audited write before it is `CONFIG`; setting a different id, also from a nested transaction, is a defined error stated in the docs; owner cases on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite. Evidence: Go `db.Utils().SetOperation(id) error`, PHP `$db->utils()->setOperation(int|string $id): void`, TypeScript `db.utils().setOperation(id: OperationId): void` and Rust `db.utils().set_operation(id)?` set the operation id of the active transaction of the connection. Outside one they fail with `CONFIG`; an id of another type (Go: not `int64` or `string`, TypeScript: not a safe integer or non-empty string) is `CONFIG`; the id the transaction already has, from the option or an earlier call, changes nothing, and another id is `CONFIG` `the transaction already has the operation id <old>; it cannot change to <new>`, also inside a nested transaction. The id belongs to the transaction: a nested transaction uses it, and a savepoint that rolls back (callback error, Go `runtime.Goexit`, Go and Rust panic, Rust `transaction_once`) restores the id it began with, because the rows written under it roll back too; a released savepoint keeps it. Rust keeps the id in a mutex of the transaction and clones it before each statement. Red: Go `go vet` failed with "db.Utils().SetOperation undefined (type *orm.Utils has no field or method SetOperation)", PHP with "Call to undefined method Orm\Utils::setOperation()", TypeScript with "setOperation outside a transaction" and "TypeError: db.utils(...).setOperation is not a function", Rust with "no method named `set_operation` found for struct `Utils<'a>`". Green: the shared scenario (outside a transaction `CONFIG`; an audited insert before the call `CONFIG`; id 7 set, the same id again unchanged, id 8 and a nested id 9 `CONFIG`; a nested transaction that sets 10 and fails leaves the next audited update `CONFIG`; id 11 then writes; a transaction with the option 12 accepts 12 and rejects 13; the history holds 7, 7, 11 and 12) passes as Go `TestSetOperationInTransaction`, PHP runtime_db_test, TypeScript dbspec_runtime_db and Rust `set_operation_in_transaction` on SQLite, MySQL and PostgreSQL case databases, and as the coverage case `audit_operation_set_in_transaction` of audit_triggers in the four clients on the three databases twice; the Rust case also rolls back a nested `transaction_once`. The orm library tests with the `Send` check of `transaction_once`, the rollback tests, interface-check with `setOperation` in the Utils methods and the recorded symbols, the feature checks, docs-check, docs-rules-check, gofmt, rustfmt, the Rust clippy variants and version-check pass. docs/usage.md, docs/interfaces.md and docs/dbspec.md (+ko) state the call and its rules. Superseded by T35: an audit record that the transaction inserts from the handle's audit defaults and its values replaces the operation id, so `setOperation` and the operation id are removed.
- [o] T34 Publish TypeScript declarations without driver types. Cause: @polyspec/orm-typescript 0.0.2 published dist/dbspec/apply.d.ts and introspect.d.ts that imported types from `pg` (whose types are only a devDependency), `mysql2/promise` and `node:sqlite`, so a type check of code that imports the package without `skipLibCheck` failed (TS7016 with `pg` installed and no `@types/pg`, TS2307 without the driver), also with only the MySQL or SQLite driver installed. Acceptance: the public declarations reference no driver module; the needed connection types are the client's own; a test type-checks code that imports the packed package with `skipLibCheck` off and no pg types (Red first); mysql2 and node:sqlite are checked the same way. Evidence: clients/typescript/src/dbspec/connections.ts declares `DbspecMySqlConnection`, `DbspecPostgresConnection` and `DbspecSqliteConnection` (with `DbspecMySqlQueryResult` and `DbspecSqliteStatement`) with only the methods that apply and introspection call; the apply connection types are aliases of them, apply runs its MySQL and PostgreSQL statements through the object forms of `query`, and the runtime pool checks keep their driver imports, which stay out of the declarations. tests/typecheck.ts requires a mysql2 connection, pool connection and pool, a pg client, pool client and pool, and a node:sqlite `DatabaseSync` to fit the types. `clients/typescript/tests/package-declarations.mjs` (in `npm run typescript:test`) rejects a declaration file that imports pg, mysql2 or node:sqlite and packs the package with `npm pack`, installs it with ordered-json and @types/node but without pg, @types/pg and mysql2, and runs tsc with `skipLibCheck: false`. Red: "declaration files that import a driver module: dist/dbspec/apply.d.ts, dist/dbspec/introspect.d.ts" and "error TS2307: Cannot find module 'mysql2/promise' …" and "Cannot find module 'pg' …" in apply.d.ts and introspect.d.ts. Green: both cases pass; `npm run typescript:check`, dbspec-introspect.mjs (63), dbspec-apply-physical.mjs (122) and dbspec-apply-cleanup.mjs (3) pass; package-check, interface-check with the recorded TypeScript symbols, the feature checks, docs-check and docs-rules-check pass. docs/plans.md (+ko) states the types.
- [o] T35 Record an audited unit of work as a row of a declared audit table (replaces the operation id of T31 and T33). Cause: an audited write carried a bare operation id that no table defined, so nothing guaranteed that the id named a recorded unit of work, and the "operation" vocabulary differed from the audit record table that the setting names. Acceptance: the setting names the audit column and the audit record table, the column is a real restrict foreign key to the record's primary key on the three databases and is restored by introspection; a connection handle carries the audit defaults and a transaction takes the values of its change, and the client inserts one record and writes its key into every audited row it inserts, updates, soft deletes or restores; a nested audit and an audited write without an audit are CONFIG; "operation" leaves dbspec, the docs and the four client APIs; vectors, fixtures and coverage cases in four clients on three databases. Evidence: `audit into <history> column <col> references <table> action <col> previous <col> [exclude (...) | include (...)]` replaces the operation form in the four parsers, emitters, renderers, comparisons and trigger introspection (Go engine/dbspec, PHP Dbspec, TypeScript src/dbspec, Rust orm-schema); the validators require a non-null audit column, a record table of the set that is not the audited or history table and is not audited itself, a one-column primary key of the column's type and the declared foreign key `(<col>) references <table> (<key>) on delete restrict on update restrict`, and the previous column of the audit column's type; introspection takes the record table from the one foreign key of the audit column. tests/dbspec/cases.json, ddl.json and plans.json use the new form with an `audit` table, the foreign key and its index (behavior steps insert audit rows first and a raw insert with audit_seq 99 fails), and add the invalid vectors audit-record-table-unknown, audit-record-table-is-history, audit-record-key-of-two-columns, audit-column-type-differs, audit-column-without-foreign-key, audit-foreign-key-updates-in-cascade and audit-column-nullable; the fixtures audit.dbs, audit_columns.dbs, restore.dbs and add_tables_and_columns/*.dbs follow. The runtime models carry the audit column and the record entity, the planners bind the slot `audit` with the record entity, and the executors write the transaction's record key or fail with `CONFIG` ("a write of an audited table needs an audit: run it in a transaction with audit values", or another record entity). API: Go `db.Audit(defaults) *DB` and `orm.Audit(map[string]any)`, PHP `$db->audit(Model $defaults): Db` and `transaction(..., ?array $audit = null)`, TypeScript `db.audit(defaults): Db` and `TransactionOptions.audit`, Rust `Db::audit(defaults) -> Db` and `.audit(pairs)` on `transaction`, `transaction_send` and `transaction_once`; `audit(defaults)` returns a handle of the same connection and does not change the connection; the transaction validates the values before it begins (no defaults, or a value that is not a column of the record table, is `CONFIG`), inserts the defaults with its values (a value wins) in every attempt before the callback, and a nested transaction keeps the outer audit (`audit` there is `CONFIG`). The operation id, its options, Go `SetOperation`, PHP and TypeScript `setOperation`, Rust `set_operation` and `OperationId` are removed. Red: the four dbspec suites failed on the new vectors (Go `unexpected 'operation', expected 'column'` in the rule cases, PHP "expected `operation`", TypeScript 34 failures in dbspec-ts-check, Rust "'column' is not allowed here"), the Go owner test did not compile (`orm.Operation` undefined after the API change, then `cannot use auditOf(...) as map[string]any`), PHP failed with "Unknown named parameter $audit", TypeScript with "db.audit is not a function", Rust with "no method named `audit` found for struct `orm::Transaction`". Green: the owner case auditCase (records [[1 create] [2 default]], history audits 1, 1, 2, a raw insert with audit_seq 999 rejected, a failed callback leaves no record, no defaults and an unknown value `CONFIG`) passes in Go `TestAuditRecordTransaction`, PHP runtime_db_test, TypeScript dbspec_runtime_db and Rust `audit_record_transaction` on SQLite, MySQL and PostgreSQL case databases, with the restore and add-tables-and-columns cases; the coverage cases audit_history, audit_transaction_entry_points, audit_selected_columns and soft_delete_restore pass in the four clients on the three databases twice; dbspec-go-check, dbspec-ddl-check (with the foreign key behavior steps), dbspec-compare-check, dbspec-introspect-compare-check and the PHP, TypeScript and Rust dbspec, introspection, plan and apply checks pass; interface-check passes with `audit` in the Db methods, the new PHP transaction signature and the recorded symbols; feature checks, docs-check, docs-rules-check, go-model-check, ts-model-check, gofmt, rustfmt, the Rust clippy variants and version-check pass. docs/dbspec.md, usage.md (Audited writes), protocol.md, interfaces.md, interface-implementation.md, dialects.md and plans.md (+ko) and the Rust README pair state the design. Changed in part by T35.2: the audit values come from an audit source of the connection config, and `audit(defaults)` and its handle are removed.
  - [o] T35.1 Let a document set use tables of another set without owning them (part of T35). Cause: a set had to hold every document that its documents use, and every document of a set was equal: the manifest, the rendered statements, install, addTablesAndColumns and code generation covered all of them. A module set whose audited tables reference the `audit` table of the core set therefore either listed the core document, and then owned `audit` (installing it after the core set failed with `CONFIG` because only some tables existed, and the generated code repeated the core models), or omitted it and failed with a `use` diagnostic. Acceptance: a set is its owned documents plus external documents given separately; external documents are parsed and validated but never rendered, installed, altered, compared or generated; the manifest hash covers the owned documents and the definitions of the used external tables; install, addTablesAndColumns and connect verify the used external tables and fail with `CONFIG`; the all-or-none rule, comparisons and plans cover owned tables only; generators take the external documents; shared vectors and owner cases in four clients on three databases. Evidence: dbspec documents carry an external flag (Go `Document.External`, PHP `$external`, TypeScript `external`, Rust `external`); an external document that no owned document reaches through `use` is a `use` error at its header. The manifest text holds the owned documents and the new external text the used tables of each external document with their columns, primary key and unique keys; `manifestHash` covers both, so a change of an unused table of the external document keeps it; the schema text holds the owned tables and one `use` line per external document. Render skips external documents; the schema text check needs no external document; a plan whose target has a `use` line is a `plan` error (`a plan targets a whole database, and its target uses the external document <doc>: install a set that uses external documents with install or addTablesAndColumns`), because plan apply treats the database as one schema, and addTablesAndColumns builds its plan without parsing a plan text. `ExternalDifferences` (PHP `externalDifferences`, TypeScript `externalDifferences`, Rust `external_differences`) names a missing table, a missing column, another type or nullability, another primary key and a missing unique key; install, addTablesAndColumns and the connect with a generated schema value (Go `ConnectSchema`, PHP `Orm::connectSchema`, TypeScript `Db.connectSchema`, Rust `Db::connect_schema`) introspect the database only when the set has external documents and fail with `CONFIG` "the tables that the set uses from external documents differ from the database: ..." before any statement. The runtime models load the manifest text with the external text and have no entity for an external table; the generated schema value carries the external text (Go `orm.Schema.External` and `ExternalText`, PHP `EXTERNAL_TEXT`, TypeScript `externalText`, Rust `Schema::with_external` and `orm_external.dbs`), written only when it is not empty, so existing generated code is unchanged. Generators: Go and PHP `orm-gen gen --use <file.dbs>`, TypeScript `orm-gen gen --use <file.dbs>`, Rust `Builder::uses`. Red: Go `go vet` failed with "document.External undefined (type *Document has no field or method External)" and "undefined: runtimemodel.LoadFileSet"; PHP with "Undefined property: Orm\Dbspec\Manifest::$externalText", "FAIL plan/parse/target-uses-external-document" and "flag provided but not defined: -use"; TypeScript `make dbspec-ts-check` with "FAIL plan parse target-uses-external-document" and "FAIL sets external-document-unused", the owner case with "SCHEMA_INVALID ... document ext_core is not in the declared document set" and the generator with "Unknown option '--use'"; Rust with "error[E0609]: no field `external` on type `Document`", "error[E0599]: no associated function or constant named `with_external` found for struct `Schema`" and "no method named `uses` found for struct `orm_build::Builder`". Green: tests/dbspec/cases.json adds the sets external-document-used, external-document-unrelated-table-changed (same `manifestHash`), external-document-used-table-changed (another hash) and external-document-unused with their texts, hashes and created tables, and tests/dbspec/plans.json the parse case target-uses-external-document; dbspec-go-check, dbspec-php-check, dbspec-ts-check, dbspec-rust-check and dbspec-compare-check pass. The owner case on contracts/fixtures/external (Go `TestExternalDocuments`, PHP and TypeScript `external_documents` in schema_set_test and schema-set, Rust `external_documents`) passes on SQLite, MySQL and PostgreSQL case databases: before the core set every call is `CONFIG` naming ext_account and ext_audit and creates nothing; the member install creates only ext_post and ext_post_history, twice; an audited create records the source's actor in the core's ext_audit; member_v2 adds exactly ext_post.summary and ext_post_history.summary; a drifted external definition is `CONFIG` naming ext_account.nick. The coverage case schema_install_external_documents of schema_install passes in the four clients on the three bench databases, and the generator tests generate only the owned models. interface-check with the external text fields and the recorded symbols, the feature checks, go-model-check, ts-model-check, the TypeScript type check and declarations, gofmt, rustfmt, the Rust clippy variants, docs-check, docs-rules-check and version-check pass. docs/dbspec.md (External documents), plans.md, schema.md and usage.md (+ko) state the design.
  - [o] T35.2 Take the audit values from a source of the connection (part of T35). Cause: T35 put the audit defaults on a connection handle (`audit(defaults)`), so every request had to build a handle and pass it to the code that writes, and the record table came from the handle's model instead of the audit setting that names it. Acceptance: `audit(defaults)` and its handle are removed in the four clients; the connection config takes one audit source per process that returns the audit values of the current request, and a transaction takes the values of its change; the record table is the table that `references` names; a missing source, a value that is not a column of the record table and a record with no value are `CONFIG` before the transaction begins, and an error of the source fails the transaction unchanged; owner and coverage cases on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite. Evidence: Go `Config.AuditSource func(ctx context.Context) (map[string]any, error)`, called with the transaction's context; PHP `new Config(..., auditSource: \Closure)`; TypeScript `connect({ ..., auditSource: () => Record<string, unknown> })`; Rust `Config.audit_source: Option<AuditSource>` with `AuditSource = Arc<dyn Fn() -> Result<Vec<(String, Param)>> + Send + Sync>`; the per-call `orm.Audit(map)`, PHP `audit:`, TypeScript `{ audit }` and Rust `.audit(pairs)` stay. Before BEGIN the transaction finds the one record table of the audited tables of the registered sets (none, more than one, one outside the registered sets or one without a one-column primary key is `CONFIG`), calls the source once, merges the source values with the call's values (a call value wins), and checks the columns and that a value exists; in every attempt it inserts the record before the callback. The runtime models and the planner slot `audit` carry the record table name, and an audited write checks it against the transaction's record. Red: the updated tests against the previous code failed: Go `go vet` with "unknown field AuditSource in struct literal of type orm.Config", PHP runtime_db_test with "Unknown named parameter $auditSource" in its 12 cases, TypeScript dbspec_runtime_db with "an audit transaction whose source fails returns the source error" and "CONFIG: the transaction has audit values but the connection has no audit defaults: set them with db.audit(defaults)", Rust with "error[E0425]: cannot find type `AuditSource` in crate `orm`". Green: the owner case auditCase (no source `CONFIG`, a source value that is not a column `CONFIG`, a failing source returns its error, a call value wins over the source, records [(1, create), (2, default)]) passes in Go `TestAuditRecordTransaction`, PHP runtime_db_test, TypeScript dbspec_runtime_db and Rust `audit_record_transaction` on SQLite, MySQL and PostgreSQL case databases, with the restore and add-tables-and-columns cases; the coverage cases audit_history, audit_transaction_entry_points, audit_selected_columns and soft_delete_restore pass in the four clients on the three databases; the Go orm tests, the Rust workspace tests with test-faults and live-db, `transaction_once` and `transaction_send` futures that stay `Send`, interface-check with the Db methods without `audit` and the recorded symbols, the audit_triggers feature check, dbspec-go-check, dbspec-php-check, dbspec-compare-check, go-model-check, ts-model-check, docs-static-check, docs-rules-check, version-check, gofmt, rustfmt and the Rust clippy variants pass. docs/usage.md (Audited writes), config.md, interfaces.md, interface-implementation.md, protocol.md and dbspec.md (+ko) and the Rust README pair state the source.
  - [o] T35.3 Render the audit fixture in the schema definition fixture as T35 declares it. Cause: T35 changed contracts/fixtures/audit.dbs to the audit record table, but the `dbspec_render_ddl` case of contracts/fixtures/schema_definition.json kept the statements of the old fixture (`operation_id`, `ix_item_operation`, no `audit` table and no foreign key), so the schema_definition coverage of the four clients failed in `make feature-check`. Evidence: the expected MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite statements are the Go `dbspec.Render` of the current audit.dbs, with the `audit` table, `fk_item_audit` and the triggers on `audit_seq`. Red: `make feature-check` failed in schema_definition for Go, PHP, Rust and TypeScript; Rust `coverage_dbspec_render_ddl` with "assertion `left == right` failed: mysql" whose expected side held `operation_id`. Green: `node scripts/features/coverage.mjs --feature schema_definition` runs the coverage of the four clients without an error.
  - [o] T35.4 Plan the audit record of a set registered by its schema value in PHP. Cause: PHP built the runtime model of every registered set from its generated classes (`Registry::model`), so on a connection where a module set was registered by schema value (`utils()->schema()->install($schema)`) before or without its generated classes, every audit transaction failed with `SCHEMA_HASH_MISMATCH: no loaded generated models registered manifest ...` in `Db::auditRecord`; Go, Rust and TypeScript build the runtime model from the manifest text. Acceptance: in the four clients a set registered by schema value has its runtime model from its manifest text and external text, and the audit record table is resolved from the set that owns it; a Red test of that registration order on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite. Evidence: PHP `Registry::schema(Schema)` records the texts of a set registered by value (`Db::registerSet` calls it); `Registry::model` uses the generated classes once the set's bootstrap ran and otherwise builds the model once from `RuntimeModel::loadSet`; `Registry::generated` drops a model built before. External tables are not entities, so the record table comes only from the owning set. Go (`Schema.Model` with `runtimemodel.LoadSet`) and Rust (`Schema::manifest` with `Manifest::load_set`, `audit_record` in tx.rs) already build every registered set from its texts: their external owner cases now also install the module set contracts/fixtures/external/board.dbs by value and pass unchanged. Red: clients/php/tests/audit_external_sets_test.php (core and board registered by value, member generated after them or registered by value before its bootstrap, then an audited create) failed in its six cases with "SCHEMA_HASH_MISMATCH: no loaded generated models registered manifest sha256:0f94ef9772a2f21531b12cbe7e5563ba5a830d4e7ae91d9f2fd4b9a76fc59ffe". Green: the six cases pass on SQLite, MySQL and PostgreSQL with one ext_audit record of the source's actor and its key in ext_post_history; Go `TestExternalDocuments` and Rust `external_documents` pass on the three databases; PHP runtime_db_test, schema_set_test, add_tables_and_columns_test (12 each), model_test (66) and dbspec-php-check (556) pass. `make owner-check` on the change selected schema_install, audit_triggers and interface_contract and passed their six steps.
  - [o] T35.5 Check table existence in the Go external owner case without a failing query. Cause: the Go case `TestExternalDocuments` tested a table by reading `SELECT COUNT(*)` from it through the native connection; through PgBouncer in `pool_mode = transaction` the read of the still missing ext_post left the driver's named prepared statement on a server session, so the read after the member install failed with "FATAL: prepared statement name is already in use (SQLSTATE 08P01)" and the case reported that ext_post did not exist. Evidence: the case asks the catalog (`information_schema.tables` of `current_schema()`, MySQL `information_schema.TABLES` of `DATABASE()`, SQLite `sqlite_master`), which never fails for a missing table. Red: `make check` failed in client-pooler-check with "external_documents_test.go:141: ext_post does not exist after the member install" on postgres. Green: `make client-pooler-check` passes in the four clients, and `make owner-check` selected schema_install and passed its verification and coverage.
- [o] T36 Select owner checks from the changed files. Cause: owner checks were chosen by hand from the feature whose behavior changed, so T35 and T35.2 were committed while the schema_definition coverage of the four clients failed: contracts/fixtures/schema_definition.json renders contracts/fixtures/audit.dbs, which T35 changed, and no command connected the two. Acceptance: one command lists and runs every feature whose declared inputs include a changed file, with fixtures that name the changed file, RUN, PASS or FAIL and elapsed time per step and a deadline per step; a changed audit.dbs selects schema_definition; AGENTS.md states how to run it. Evidence: `scripts/features/owners.mjs` (`make owner-check`, `PATHS="<paths>"` or the files that differ from `HEAD` and the untracked files) selects the features whose `fixtures` or `tests` contain a changed file and those whose declared fixture files name one, prints the paths that selected each, and runs per feature the verification commands (`scripts/features/check.mjs --run --feature <id>`) and the coverage (`scripts/features/coverage.mjs --feature <id>`) as steps with a 10-minute deadline that ends the step's process group; `--list` only prints. `scripts/features/owners.test.mjs` runs in `make feature-check`. AGENTS.md and docs/feature-coverage.md (+ko) state the rule. Red: `node --test scripts/features/owners.test.mjs` against a selection of declared fixtures and tests only failed with "FAIL a changed fixture selects every feature whose fixtures render it elapsed=3ms: selected audit_triggers". Green: the three cases pass (audit.dbs selects schema_definition, named by schema_definition.json, and audit_triggers; a test file and tests/dbspec/cases.json select their features; README.md and docs/checklist.md select nothing). `make owner-check` on the T37 change selected model_queries and interface_contract and passed their four steps (23 and 2 verification commands, each coverage executed). docs-check and docs-rules-check pass.
  - [o] T36.1 Follow the files that named JSON files name when selecting owner checks. Cause: T36 selected a feature only when one of its declared fixtures named the changed file; interface_contract declares contracts/interfaces.json, which names contracts/symbols/rust.json, and that snapshot names every Rust source file, so a change of clients/rust/orm/src/tx_send_tests.rs (T38) selected nothing and its changed symbols failed interface-check only in `make check` ("rust: changed clients/rust/orm/src/tx_send_tests.rs::state"). Evidence: `selectOwners` follows each named JSON file and selects the feature for the files it names, with the reason `<path> (named by <json file>)`; a source file of any client now selects interface_contract. - [o] T37 Give the internal transaction helpers of the utilities one name and one place in the four clients. Cause: the helper that runs a utility statement in the active transaction of the connection or in a new one was Go `Utils.run`, Rust `Utils::run`, PHP `UtilsSql::run` in a separate internal class and TypeScript `inTx`, a module function; the helper that reads through the active transaction or the connection was Go `Utils.read`, Rust `Utils::reader`, PHP `UtilsSql::scalar` and TypeScript a module `read`. One concept had several names and places since the first commit. Acceptance: in every client `Utils` owns `active` (require the active transaction, `CONFIG` otherwise), `run` (the active transaction or a new one without retry) and `read` (the active transaction or the connection); the schema, privilege and AES utilities reach them through the `Utils` they hold; no behavior change. Evidence: Go keeps `Utils.active`, `run` and `read`. PHP removes `UtilsSql`: `Utils` has the internal `active`, `run`, `read` (was `scalar`) and `exec`, and `SchemaUtils`, `PrivilegeUtils` and `AesUtils` take `(Db $db, Utils $u)`. TypeScript `Utils` has the internal `active`, `run` (was `inTx`), `read` (was the module `read`) and `db`, and the three utilities take `u: Utils`. Rust renames `Utils::reader` to `read` and makes the external table check a `Utils` method that introspects through `read()`. The checks that refuse a transaction (MySQL install, MySQL and SQLite addTablesAndColumns) stay, because they are another concept. Red: none, a rename without behavior; the interface check reported the renamed members (TypeScript `inTx` and `read` missing, `Utils.run` and `Utils.read` unexpected) until the symbols were recorded. Green: PHP runtime_db_test, model_test, add_tables_and_columns_test and schema_set_test (12 each) on SQLite, MySQL and PostgreSQL, aes_json_test, rollback_test and dbspec-php-check pass; TypeScript typescript:test (361), dbspec-ts-check (275), schema-set and add-tables-and-columns (12 each) and `package-declarations` pass; Rust orm lib tests (35), add_tables_and_columns, external_documents, aes_json and the integration program on the bench schema (30 cases on three databases with privileges and AES rotation), rustfmt and the three clippy variants pass; interface-check passes with the recorded symbols.
- [o] T38 Give the Rust send savepoint test a table that a transaction pooler keeps apart. Cause: `tx::send_tests` (clients/rust/orm/src/tx_send_tests.rs, from N9.2.1) wrote to `CREATE TEMPORARY TABLE orm_send_savepoint_probe` and relied on closing its connection to remove it. Through PgBouncer in `pool_mode = transaction` a temporary table stays on the server session after the client connection closes, and a later run can get that session, so `make check` failed in client-pooler-check with `postgres_send_savepoint` "outer commit must succeed: ... duplicate key value violates unique constraint \"orm_send_savepoint_probe_pkey\" ... Key (id)=(1) already exists" on schema `pg_temp_52`; the test also passed only while its statements happened to share one server session. Evidence: the test creates a regular table named `orm_send_savepoint_probe_<process id>_<nanoseconds>` for the run and drops it at its end, so every server session sees the same table and no run meets another's rows. Red: the failure above in `make check`. Green: `make client-pooler-check` passes in the four clients with `mysql_send_savepoint`, `postgres_send_savepoint` and `sqlite_send_savepoint`, the three send tests pass directly, rustfmt and clippy of orm with test-faults pass. The test's changed signatures are recorded in contracts/symbols/rust.json, and interface-check passes; `make owner-check` (with T36.1) selects interface_contract for the test file and passes its verification and coverage.
- [o] T39 Make the generated models independent of how a scan or output path is written (four clients). Cause: the generated models must depend only on the documents and the scanned sources. Rust `orm_build::Builder::try_generate` joined a relative `out_dir` to `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR` and wrote the manifest path into `include_str!` as written, so the same sources and documents gave a different generated source for `out`, an absolute path, `./out` or a path through `..`. The TypeScript, Go and PHP generators had no such dependence: TypeScript scan reads only file contents and resolves the output directory, Go loads package patterns into absolute file names and resolves `--out`, and PHP has no scan and writes no path. Evidence: Rust canonicalizes the output directory after creating it, so `include_str!` names one path for one directory. Each client has a case that generates from one fixture, a source in `src` and one in `tests` (PHP, which has no scan: a document), with the scan, output and document paths written relative, absolute, with `./` and with a trailing `/`, and requires byte-equal output that holds the calls of both directories: Go `TestGoGenerationScanPathSpelling` (generator/gen_go_scan_test.go), TypeScript `generate-scan/scan_path_spelling` (clients/typescript/tests/generate-scan.mjs), Rust `scan_path_spelling_keeps_the_generated_source` (clients/rust/orm-build/tests/generate.rs) and PHP `gen writes the same models however the paths are written` (clients/php/tests/orm_gen_test.php). The model_generation verification now also runs generate-scan.mjs and orm_gen_test.php. docs/usage.md and its Korean pair state the rule. Red: the new Rust case failed with "the generated source of the absolute paths differs from the relative paths": the `include_str!` path was `<CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR>/../../…/out/orm_manifest.dbs` for the relative spelling and `<dir>/./out/orm_manifest.dbs` for `./`; the Go, TypeScript and PHP cases passed before the change. Green: `make owner-check` on the changed paths selects model_generation and interface_contract and passes `owners/model_generation/verification`, `owners/model_generation/coverage`, `owners/interface_contract/verification` and `owners/interface_contract/coverage`; the four new cases pass in it. The Rust `coverage_model_generation_check` compares the built model with canonical temporary and `OUT_DIR` paths. clippy `-D warnings` of orm-build, `go vet ./generator`, gofmt, rustfmt and docs-rules-check pass.
- [o] T40 Register a schema set without database I/O and verify the database at install (four clients). Cause: a connection opened per request must have its schema sets registered, and the only public call that registered a set on an open connection was `install(schema)`, which on every call verified the hash, rendered the DDL, introspected the external tables and queried the existence of each owned table inside a transaction (39% of a PHP request, about 30 extra statements on PostgreSQL); the connect helper (`connectSchema`) also introspected the database for a set with external documents. And `install` did not prove that the database matched the set: it checked table names and external tables only, never columns, keys, indexes or foreign keys. Evidence: `utils().schema().register(schema)` (Go `Register`, PHP `register`, TypeScript `register`, Rust `register`, synchronous in Rust and TypeScript) checks the manifest hash and registers the set without a statement, and `connectSchema` registers the same way. `install` introspects the database once, checks the external tables, applies the rendered statements only when no owned table exists (all or none), introspects again and fails with `CONFIG` `the database differs from the document set: <kind> <table>[.<name>]; …` when the owned tables differ from the set; `addTablesAndColumns` ends with the same comparison. The comparison is the new schema operation `InstalledDifferences` (Go `engine/dbspec`, PHP `Dbspec::installedDifferences`, TypeScript `installedDifferences`, Rust `orm_schema::dbspec::installed_differences`), which shares the owned-table comparison of `addTablesAndColumnsSteps`. contracts/interfaces.json declares `SchemaUtils.register` with the output `Registered`, a synchronous result, and contracts/validate.go exempts it from the async rule of operations that execute statements. docs/schema.md (new section 6 "Schema registration" with when to call which), docs/protocol.md, docs/dbspec.md, docs/usage.md, docs/interfaces.md and their Korean pairs state the rule: the database is verified at install and upgrade, not per request. Shared vector: contracts/fixtures/install/changed_database.json (case `renamed_column` on contracts/fixtures/install/note.dbs, one expected message per database). Red: Go `TestInstallVerifiesTheDatabase`, PHP and TypeScript `install_verifies_the_database` and Rust `install_verifies_the_database` failed on SQLite, MySQL and PostgreSQL with `install over a changed column = <nil>` (Rust `Ok(())`); the register tests failed to compile or call (`Register undefined`, `Call to undefined method Orm\SchemaUtils::register()`, `register is not a function`, `no method named register`), and the Go test without the register calls failed with "connect with the schema: CONFIG: the tables that the set uses from external documents differ from the database: table ext_account does not exist; table ext_audit does not exist". Green: the register tests count the statements the connection sends with a driver spy (Go `countingDriver` through `RegisterDriver`, PHP `StatementCountingPdo`, TypeScript wrapped pool methods, Rust a logger of the `sqlx::query` records that `empty()` is shown to produce) and get 0 for `register`, and `connectSchema` sends what `connect` sends; the install tests get the expected messages on the three databases. `make owner-check` on the changed paths selects schema_install and interface_contract and passes `owners/schema_install/verification`, `owners/schema_install/coverage`, `owners/interface_contract/verification` and `owners/interface_contract/coverage`.
- [o] T41 Make `make owner-check` run the make targets whose inputs a change touches. The owner check selected only features whose fixtures or tests are a changed file, so a change to a document ran nothing, and the T40 commit left `make docs-check` red. `contracts/check-inputs.json` declares, for every target of `CHECK_TARGETS`, the repository paths it reads (`*` within a path segment, `**` across segments), and `scripts/features/owners.mjs` runs `make <target>` for each target whose inputs match a changed path, before the selected features, each with a 30-minute deadline and its RUN, STEP, PASS or FAIL lines. `make repo-check` and `scripts/features/owners.test.mjs` fail when a target of `CHECK_TARGETS` declares no inputs, when an entry names a target outside it, or when a pattern matches no tracked file. `AGENTS.md` and its Korean pair state the rule, together with searching the tests and scripts for every changed file name before a commit. Evidence: before the change, `make owner-check` with the changed checklist pair reported `select 0 feature(s)` and ran nothing; now `docs/checklist.md` selects `checklist-check`, `version-check`, `repo-check`, `docs-rules-check`, `docs-check` and `docs-verify-idempotent`; `scripts/features/owners.test.mjs` passes 7/7, including the pattern cases and the declaration check; `make owner-check` with the changed paths of this item selects seven make targets and passes them.
- [o] T41.1 Give the make targets that `make owner-check` selects the server environment, the decimal environment and the Rust target directory that owner-check receives. `scripts/features/owners.mjs` started each target with a plain `make <target>`, so in a worktree a target read the worktree's `.runtime/servers/env` and `.runtime/decimal-env`, which do not exist, and cargo built into the worktree's own `clients/rust/target`, even when owner-check was given the values of the main checkout. The `owner-check` recipe now passes `ORM_OWNER_TEST_ENV` and `ORM_OWNER_CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, and `makeArguments` gives each target `TEST_ENV=`, `DECIMAL_ENV=` and `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=` on the make command line, the only way a make variable overrides the Makefile; a worktree check then uses the servers and the target directory of the main checkout. Evidence: in a new worktree of main with no `.runtime`, `make owner-check PATHS=clients/go/orm/cancel_test.go` with `TEST_ENV`, `DECIMAL_ENV` and `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` of the main checkout failed 8 of its 12 selected targets, each with `<worktree>/.runtime/servers/env is missing; run make test-servers`, and left a 2.0 GB `clients/rust/target` in the worktree; with the change the same command passes all 12 targets and the `transactions` feature and creates no `clients/rust/target` in the worktree. `scripts/features/owners.test.mjs` passes 8/8, including `makeArguments`.
- [o] T42 Publish an event for every statement a client sends, in Go, PHP, Rust and TypeScript with the same semantics, in place of the `OnQuery` hook (docs/usage.md "Statement events"). Subscribers registered on the connection (Go `DB.Subscribe`, PHP `Db::subscribe`, Rust `Db::subscribe` with `Subscription::unsubscribe`, TypeScript `Db.subscribe`) receive the statement text, its masked binds, its kind by origin (`select`, `insert`, `update`, `delete`, `begin`, `commit`, `rollback`, `savepoint`, `release`, `rollback_to`, `schema`, `utility`), the tables it names, its elapsed time, the number of its transaction on the connection or none, and its error, synchronously after the statement and before the operation continues; every handle of the connection shares them; a subscriber that fails stops the others and fails the operation with the new code `SUBSCRIBER` whose cause is its error. Every plan step of the four planners carries `tables`, recorded where the planner renders a table name (`contracts/fixtures/planner.json` states them, with the new case `planner_tables`); the renderers give each install statement its table and an add step names the table of its effect. Every client sends the same transaction control statements as its own SQL (MySQL `START TRANSACTION`, PostgreSQL one `BEGIN` with its options, SQLite `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, the SQLite row-lock table once per connection before its first transaction, `COMMIT`, `ROLLBACK`, savepoints), so the Go client no longer uses `database/sql` transactions and PHP and TypeScript no longer use the driver's transaction calls; a cancelled MySQL or PostgreSQL transaction closes its session and a cancelled SQLite transaction rolls back; the five table privileges are read with one statement in every client. The conformance runners record every statement as its event, with the transaction renumbered within the vector. Evidence: Red: before each client's implementation its vector test failed every case (`db.subscribe is not a function`, `Call to undefined method Orm\Db::subscribe()`, Rust `no method named subscribe`), and the old TypeScript planner failed the planner cases with `tables: undefined`. Green: the shared vector `tests/events/vectors.json` (fixture `contracts/fixtures/statement_events.dbs`, eight cases: model statements, a committed savepoint, a rolled back transaction, a rolled back savepoint, a statement the database rejects with `DUPLICATE_KEY`, a failing subscriber, unsubscribing, and the statements of `install` that name a table) passes 8/8 on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite in each of Go (`TestStatementEvents*`), PHP (`statement_events_test.php`), Rust (`--test statement_events`) and TypeScript (`statement-events.mjs`); the planner cases pass 6/6 in each client; `go run ./tests/conformance/check run` gave the same output from the four runners for all 26 vectors on each database, the expectations were recorded from them, and their model statements, binds and results equal the previous expectations; the interface check passes for the four languages. Check: the coverage case `statement_events` (each client's runner of the vector on the database that feature-check selects) passes twice on each database for Go, PHP, Rust and TypeScript; the Go coverage case first reported its eight subtests as tests of their own and now runs them inside `TestCoverageStatementEvents`, which passes exactly once per run on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite. The broad checks are not run per item; they run in the final full suite.

Cause: the static site builds successfully twice and local static checks pass, and the existing Pages URL responds with HTTP 200, but this repository is local-only and the development rules prohibit pushing or publishing the current commit. Evidence: the `docs-pages` run of G5.24 built, checked and deployed the site; its Pages artifact (`github-pages/artifact.tar`) holds 179 files, and every one of them, fetched from https://polyspec.github.io/orm/ at the same path, including `index.html`, `dbspec.html`, `checklist.html` and `ko/checklist.html`, is byte-identical to the artifact (179/179, HTTP 200). The coordinates of the Mermaid diagrams depend on the fonts of the platform that builds them, so builds are compared on one platform (`make docs-verify-idempotent`) and not across platforms.

## Stage 1 — Model syntax

- [o] M1 Implement model creation and `connect`, chains, connectors, groups, operators, value shapes, column comparisons, tuples, and subqueries in the four clients.
- [o] M2 Implement relations, joins, column selection, order, group, limit, finders, aggregates, and pages.
- [o] M3 Implement writes: `set`, `setRaw`, `new<Name>`, `plus`, `minus`, `create`, `creates`, `duplication`, `update`, `update(true)`, `save`, and `delete`.
- [o] M4 Implement flow-scoped transactions, savepoints, retry, options, row locks, and `connection.utils()`.
- [o] M5 Reject reserved column names and names that collide with generated methods.

## Stage 2 — In-process planning and generators

- [o] N1 Port validation, planning, dialects, and DDL to PHP, Rust, and TypeScript; the Go client calls the engine packages directly.
- [o] N2 Provide one generator per language: `ormgen gen --lang go`, `vendor/bin/orm-gen`, the `orm-gen` npm bin, and the `orm-build` crate.
- [o] N2.1 Distinguish Rust Result handling from model calls after fallible styled setters. Priority: required for generated-model controller compilation. Acceptance: tracked expect/unwrap source cases fail before correction, generation retains the model for later calls without generating Result methods, and unknown model methods still fail. Rust source analysis is client-specific; runtime and other clients remain unchanged. Evidence: the owner Result-handling case first failed on an invalid expect column method; all ten generation cases and targeted Clippy now pass. A generated controller compiles its styled-setter expect calls and its configuration cases pass within 68 CLI cases. Checklist checks pass; existing document rule findings remain.
- [o] N2.1.1 Distinguish Result transformations after fallible Rust model setters. Priority: before controller compilation. Acceptance: tracked RED reproduces Result methods being read as columns; preserve model calls after error mapping and extraction, distinguish success mapping from the original model, and reject unknown model calls and Result methods on infallible setters. Owner generation and generated controller cases must pass. Scope: Rust source analysis. Depends on: N2.1. Evidence: intended generation RED rejects map_err and map as columns; fourteen generation cases and one generated-model case pass, the strict Clippy checks of both parts pass, and paired documentation checks pass.
- [o] N3 Implement `utils().schema().install()` on MySQL, PostgreSQL, and SQLite in the four clients.
- [o] N3.1 Load the generated PHP models of several schemas in one process and plan each model request with the engine of its own schema on the connection. Priority: required to use a core schema and module schemas on one connection. Cause: the PHP registry keeps one schema hash for the process, a connection holds one engine, and `install()` creates tables without registering the manifest. Acceptance: each generated PHP model class carries its schema hash; a connection starts with the engine of its connect schema; `utils().schema().install()` verifies the manifest hash against its content and adds its engine to the connection; a request of a schema the connection has not registered fails with `SCHEMA_HASH_MISMATCH`; one-schema use is unchanged. Owner cases execute the several-schema connection in Go, PHP, Rust, and TypeScript on MySQL, PostgreSQL, and SQLite. Evidence: the PHP owner case first failed while loading the second generated bootstrap with `SCHEMA_HASH_MISMATCH: models of schema ... are loaded together`. After the change, `several_schemas`, `unregistered_schema`, and `edited_manifest` pass twice in the PHP and TypeScript owner tests on MySQL, PostgreSQL, and SQLite; Go `TestSchemaSet*` and Rust `schema_set_*` pass twice on the three databases with the core and decimal schemas on one connection. PHP unit, model, schema database, SQLite concurrency, grouped-row, and unselected-column checks pass; the PHP interface symbols, feature pages, documentation rules, and static documentation checks pass for the changed symbols and pages.
- [o] N3.2 Make the four clients follow the several-schema rule of `docs/protocol.md`: a connection plans only the schema sets registered on it. Cause: after the T8.8.1 integration every client planned a request with any set the process loaded: Go `engineFor` built an engine for the schema of any model on any connection, TypeScript and PHP planned every set whose generated code was imported or bootstrapped, and Rust planned with the manifest the model embeds, so a connection to a database that lacks a set, or holds another version of it, planned the request and failed only at execution or ran it against the wrong tables; Go `DB.plan` also read its plan cache before it checked the schema, so a request of edited generated code ran from the cached plan of the same hash and shape. Criteria: in the four clients a request of a schema that the connection has not registered fails with `SCHEMA_HASH_MISMATCH` before execution, also with a cached plan; the generated connect helper and `install()` add the set to that connection and a raw connection adds none; an edited manifest fails with `CONFIG`; the cases `several_schemas`, `unregistered_schema` and `edited_manifest` pass in all four clients on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite. Depends on: N3.1. Evidence: rulings and reasons: (1) a raw connection (Go `orm.Connect`, PHP `Orm::connect`, Rust `Db::connect`, TypeScript `Db.connect`) starts with no set, because nothing has told it what the database holds; the generated code of a set has a connect helper (Go `model.Connect`, PHP `Polyspec\Orm\Tests\Model\connect`, Rust `model::connect`, TypeScript `connect`) that opens the connection and registers its own set through `connectSchema` (Go `orm.ConnectSchema`, PHP `Orm::connectSchema`, Rust `Db::connect_schema`, TypeScript `Db.connectSchema`), one call with nothing to forget and the schema taken from the code that holds it; code with models moves to the helper and raw connect stays for tools; no schema parameter is added to the raw connects. (2) `install()` and the helper take the generated schema value, the manifest text with its declared `manifestHash` (Go `*orm.Schema`, PHP `Orm\Schema`, Rust `&orm::Schema`, TypeScript `{ manifestText, manifestHash }`), because the dbspec manifest text carries no hash and only a declared hash lets an edited text fail; a mismatch is `CONFIG` before any statement, and install registers the set it installs. (3) the schema check runs before the plan cache in Go (`DB.plan` calls `engineFor` first, which checks the text of the request's schema and its registration), so an edited manifest never plans from the cache; Rust `Db::plan` already checked the manifest before its cache and now also checks the registration there, and TypeScript and PHP check the registration before their caches. Red: at T24 the Go cases `TestSchemaSetUnregistered*` (a model of a set no package registered) and `TestSchemaSetEditedManifest*` (an edited manifest after the same request was planned) failed 3/3 each on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite with `<nil>`: the requests planned and ran. With the registration check disabled, the new `unregistered_schema` cases failed in Rust (`DRIVER` instead of `SCHEMA_HASH_MISMATCH` on a raw connection, MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite), PHP and TypeScript (bench read on a raw connection, decimal read and write on the bench connection). Green on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite: Go `TestSchemaSet*`, `TestSchemaSetUnregistered*`, `TestSchemaSetEditedManifest*` 9/9; Rust `schema_set_*` 9/9; PHP clients/php/tests/schema_set_test.php and TypeScript clients/typescript/tests/schema-set.mjs 3 cases × 3 databases each. `installed_elsewhere` is folded into `unregistered_schema`: a connection does not use a set that another connection installed. Owner checks of the changed code and callers, all passing: `go test -tags ormtest ./clients/go/orm ./clients/go/decimalmodel` (112 tests), `go test ./clients/go/model ./generator` (41 tests), Go `featurecoverage` `TestCoverage*` 26 per database on the bench databases; TypeScript `tsc --noEmit`, tests/engine.mjs, generate.mjs, generate-scan.mjs, dsn.mjs, models-scan.mjs (2), model.mjs, clock.mjs, driver-error.mjs, dbspec_runtime_db.mjs, sqlite-concurrency.mjs and rollback.mjs (`--conditions=orm-test`) on 3 databases, and the coverage files composite_keys, constraints_and_relations, model_queries, model_writes, parameter_chunking, transactions (2), schema_install (2), audit_triggers, dsn and interface_contract on 3 databases; PHP schema_set, engine, relation_keys, dsn, hostcodec, runtime_model, orm_gen, model (11 × 3), aes_json, clock, driver_error, runtime_db, sqlite_concurrency and rollback tests, the 17 coverage files on 3 databases and `make php-without-mysql-check`; Rust `cargo test -p orm --features test-faults` (91 tests), `orm-build` (18), the integration binary (30 database runs), its 10 coverage cases and the orm coverage_dsn, coverage_audit_triggers, coverage_authenticated_encryption and coverage_interface_contract cases on 3 databases, clippy `-D warnings` of the workspace and with `test-faults` and `live-db`; `make example-check` (complex and thin-slice), `make group-rows-physical-check` (12 runs), `make unselected-column-physical-check` (6 runs), `make decimal-physical-check` (24 runs), the PHP, TypeScript and Rust conformance runners on the `relations` vector on 3 databases, `go test ./contracts/...`, interface-check with the recorded symbols and hashes (`Db.connectSchema` added, `SchemaUtils.install` takes `GeneratedSchema`), docs-rules-check, checklist-check, git-check, go-fmt-check, go-model-check and rust-fmt-check. docs/protocol.md, dbspec.md, schema.md, usage.md, interfaces.md, interface-implementation.md, config.md, the generated component diagram and the PHP and Rust READMEs, with their Korean pairs, state the rule.
- [o] N3.3 Provide `utils().schema().register(manifestJson)` in the four clients. Priority: required to install a schema once and register it on every later connection. Cause: the only public call that adds a manifest to a connection is `install()`, which also creates objects; registering a set otherwise needs the internal `registerEngine`. Acceptance: `register` verifies the manifest hash against its content and fails with `CONFIG` when they differ, executes no statement and creates nothing, and adds the engine of the manifest to the connection exactly as `install()` does after its statements; `install()` uses the same registration. The cases `register_schema` and `register_edited_manifest` pass in Go, PHP, Rust and TypeScript on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite. Depends on: N3.1. Evidence: the PHP and TypeScript cases failed before the change because `register` did not exist, and the Go and Rust cases did not compile. After it `register_schema` and `register_edited_manifest` pass twice in `clients/php/tests/schema_set_test.php`, `clients/typescript/tests/schema-set.mjs`, Go `TestRegister*` and Rust `register_*` on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite, together with the earlier schema set cases. Before installation a registered connection reads the missing module table with `DRIVER`, so `register` creates nothing. PHP and TypeScript reject a module request before `register` with `SCHEMA_HASH_MISMATCH`; Go and Rust do not yet keep the schemas of a connection, which N3.2 records, and the Rust `register` verifies the manifest only. Rust `install` now also returns `CONFIG` for an edited manifest. `contracts/interfaces.json` holds `SchemaUtils.register`, its native signatures pass `contracts/validate.go`, and the snapshot hashes match the recorded symbols.
- [o] N3.3.1 Record that this branch provides no `utils().schema().register(manifestJson)` (follows N3.3). Cause: N3.3 is complete on `main`, where `register` adds an installed manifest to a connection, but the T8.8.1 integration replaced it, and this branch has no such call in any client; a record that names it as provided misleads, and a second registration call would give two ways to do one thing. Decision: a set reaches a connection in two ways only. The generated code of a set provides a connect helper that opens the connection and registers its own set on it, and `utils().schema().install(schema)` creates the tables of the generated schema value and registers that set on the same connection; N3.2 implements both. Reason: using a set on every later connection after one install is covered by the connect helper, which registers the set on each connection it opens from the code that holds the set; a public `register()` would add an API without a behaviour that orm lacks. Evidence: `git grep -n "schema().register\|register(manifest\|registerEngine"` finds the call only in the N3.3 records of docs/checklist.md, docs/checklist.ko.md, CHANGELOG.md and CHANGELOG.ko.md, which describe `main`, and in the T8.8.1 records; no document under docs/ other than the checklists, no file of contracts/ (interfaces.json and the four symbol records) and no test names it. Checks: docs-rules-check (24 English documents, 24 Korean translations), checklist-check and git-check pass.

- [o] N5.1.1.4 Read SQLite state when no table uses `AUTOINCREMENT` and `sqlite_sequence` does not exist. Treat the verified absence as zero counters, still detect row changes and sequence changes when the table exists, and reject other query errors.

- [o] N5.1.2 Make all four conformance runners propagate unexpected errors, clean write state on failure, and enforce the common aggregate scalar rule on all three databases. SQLite stores scaled signed integers, while MySQL and PostgreSQL store decimal values. Scalar aggregates retain their separate finite binary64 rule.
