- [o] N10 Write the client clock with microseconds in the four clients. Cause: the TypeScript client reads its clock from `Date`, which has millisecond resolution, so a `now` bind slot stores `.mmm000` in a `datetime(6)` column while the Go, PHP and Rust clients store six real fraction digits. Acceptance: `docs/protocol.md` states one rule for the `now` bind slot: the wall clock with microsecond resolution, truncated to six fraction digits, read once per statement; the TypeScript client reads microseconds from the wall clock and the monotonic clock; the case `clock_microseconds` passes in Go, PHP, Rust and TypeScript on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite. Depends on: none. Evidence: the TypeScript case `clock_microseconds` failed on SQLite before the change, with every stored value ending in `000`; after it the case passes twice in `clients/typescript/tests/clock.mjs`, `clients/php/tests/clock_test.php`, Go `TestClockMicroseconds*` and Rust `clock_microseconds_*` on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite. The TypeScript model and engine suites, Rust Clippy and formatting, PHP syntax, the feature manifest and the documentation rules pass.
- [o] N11 Report every driver error as an ORM error with a catalog code in the four clients. Cause: the PHP client returns a driver error that the catalog does not list as a raw `PDOException`, so a write that an `orm:audit` or `orm:immutable` trigger refuses has no ORM code; the Go client returns such an error unchanged, the Rust client reports `SQLX`, a code the catalog does not hold, and the Go, PHP and Rust clients report the SQLite trigger refusal 1811 as `FOREIGN_KEY`; the PHP, Rust and TypeScript clients do not map a CHECK violation to `CONSTRAINT`. Acceptance: `docs/errors.yaml` holds `DRIVER` for every driver error that it does not list, with the driver message and the driver error kept as the cause; SQLite 1811 is not `FOREIGN_KEY`; the cases `trigger_refused` (`DRIVER`) and `check_refused` (`CONSTRAINT`) pass in Go, PHP, Rust and TypeScript on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite. Depends on: none. Evidence: before the change the PHP cases raised `PDOException` on all three databases, Go reported no code on MySQL and PostgreSQL and `FOREIGN_KEY` on SQLite, Rust reported `SQLX` and `FOREIGN_KEY`, and TypeScript reported `DRIVER` for the CHECK violation; after it `clients/php/tests/driver_error_test.php`, `clients/typescript/tests/driver-error.mjs`, Go `Test(Trigger|Check)Refused*` and Rust `refusal` pass twice on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite. The PHP model, schema set and clock suites, the TypeScript model, schema set, SQLite concurrency, engine and DSN suites, the Go client, engine and generator packages and the Rust workspace pass except the cases that need the decimal and coverage environments; Rust Clippy, the symbol records of the changed declarations and the documentation rules pass.
- [o] N12 Type only the method calls of model chains in the TypeScript generator scan. Cause: `--scan` collects the name of every method call of a source file, so a call such as `this.enabled()` of a class that is not a model adds the method `enabled` to every model with a column `enabled`, and removing that call removes it. Acceptance: `docs/usage.md` states which receivers the scan resolves to a model; a call whose receiver does not resolve to a model adds no method; the generator owner cases show that a non-model call adds nothing and that model calls through `new`, bindings, typed parameters, callbacks, rows and collections add their methods to their own model. Depends on: none. Evidence: `clients/typescript/tests/generate-scan.mjs` failed before the change with 14 failures: calls on other receivers declared `name`, `isClose`, `readCount`, `aliasGhost` and `getGhost` on models, and `ltSeq` and `neName` were declared on every model; after it both cases pass twice. The repository build had also declared `newPage`, a call of the browser test, on every model. The generator, TypeScript type check, model, schema set, SQLite concurrency, clock, driver error and styled value suites pass; a project with 18 models type-checks with the regenerated models, which only lose declarations of calls on other receivers. The symbol records of the scanner and the generated models, the feature manifest and the documentation rules pass.
- [o] N13 Report both errors when a transaction or savepoint callback fails and its rollback fails too, in the four clients. Cause: the PHP client replaces the callback error with the rollback error, the TypeScript client drops the rollback error, the Go client discards the transaction rollback error and returns only the savepoint rollback error, and the Rust client reports both only as `CONFIG` text and returns only the savepoint rollback error. Acceptance: `docs/errors.yaml` holds `ROLLBACK`; the error of such a failure has the code `ROLLBACK`, names both errors in its message, and keeps the callback error and the rollback error; the ORM tests end a transaction on SQLite with a trigger that raises `ROLLBACK` while the callback runs only model calls, and on MySQL and PostgreSQL by ending the transaction's server session from a test connection; the cases `rollback_failed` and `savepoint_rollback_failed` pass in Go, PHP, Rust and TypeScript on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite. Depends on: N11. Evidence: before the change PHP raised the raw rollback `PDOException` on MySQL and PostgreSQL and no `ROLLBACK` code on SQLite, TypeScript reported the callback error alone and ended the process with an unhandled PostgreSQL error event, Go reported the callback error alone in all six runs, and Rust had no `Error::Rollback`. After it `clients/php/tests/rollback_test.php`, `clients/typescript/tests/rollback.mjs`, Go `Test(Savepoint)?RollbackFailed*` and Rust `rollback` pass twice on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite. The Go cancellation case inside a transaction now expects `ROLLBACK` that keeps the cancellation on MySQL and PostgreSQL. The PHP, TypeScript and Go client suites and the Rust workspace pass except the cases that need the decimal and coverage environments; Rust Clippy, Go vet, the symbol records and hashes, the feature manifest and the documentation rules pass.
- [o] N14 Write the database clock with microseconds on MySQL and keep the fraction of the bound clock in SQLite relative value functions. Cause: the MySQL dialect renders soft deletion as `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` and the `now` value function and its relative forms with `NOW()`, so a soft deletion stores whole seconds in a `datetime(6)` column and a condition `created_ts <= now()` misses a row created earlier in the same second; the SQLite relative forms render `datetime(clock, modifier)`, which drops the fraction of the bound clock. PostgreSQL stores and compares microseconds. Acceptance: `docs/protocol.md` states one rule: an ORM write of the database clock to a column uses the declared fraction digits of the column (`CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(p)` on MySQL) for the update time and soft deletion, the MySQL `now` value function and its relative forms use `NOW(6)`, and the SQLite relative forms keep the six fraction digits of the bound clock; the cases `clock_soft_delete_microseconds` and `clock_now_condition` pass in Go, PHP, Rust and TypeScript on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite; the conformance expectations hold the new SQL. Depends on: none. Evidence: before the change `clock_soft_delete_microseconds` failed on MySQL in all four clients with every stored value ending in `.000000`, and `clock_now_condition` failed on MySQL for `now()` and `secondsLater(0)` and on SQLite for `secondsLater(0)`; PostgreSQL passed both. After it Go `TestClock*`, `clients/php/tests/clock_test.php`, `clients/typescript/tests/clock.mjs` and Rust `clock` pass twice on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite, together with `clock_microseconds`. The Go engine and planner tests, including a soft deletion case for `datetime(6)` and `datetime` columns, the PHP and TypeScript engine and model suites, the Rust `orm` library tests and Clippy, and the documentation rules and build pass. `functions` is recorded with `NOW(6)` and the SQLite relative form from four agreeing outputs; MySQL and PostgreSQL ran on separate databases seeded from `bench/sql`, because the shared `orm_bench` databases differ from `bench/sql` on `main` as well.
- [o] N15 Store the times of the migration ledger with microseconds on every database. Cause: the ledger `orm_schema_migrations` declares `timestamp` on MySQL and `TEXT` written with `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` on SQLite, so `started_at` and `finished_at` keep whole seconds there, while PostgreSQL keeps microseconds. Acceptance: `docs/protocol.md` states one rule: MySQL declares `timestamp(6)` written with `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6)`, PostgreSQL `timestamptz` written with `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP`, and SQLite `TEXT` with a `CHECK` for six fraction digits that takes the tool clock in UTC; before a command uses an existing ledger, the tool verifies its time columns, and a ledger with other time columns fails with `MIGRATION_HISTORY_PRECISION` and stays unchanged; `docs/usage.md` states the statements that convert an earlier MySQL or SQLite ledger; the cases `migration_ledger_microseconds`, `migration_ledger_whole_seconds` and `migration_ledger_earlier` pass in Go, PHP, Rust and TypeScript on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite. Depends on: none. Evidence: before the change `migration_ledger_microseconds` failed on MySQL and SQLite in all four clients with whole-second times and passed on PostgreSQL, and `migration_ledger_whole_seconds` failed on all three databases in all four clients because the migration ran on the whole-second ledger. After it Go `TestMigrationLedger*`, `clients/php/tests/ledger_test.php`, `clients/typescript/tests/ledger.mjs` and the Rust `cli` cases `migration_ledger_*` pass twice on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite, on separate databases. The Go `internal/ormgen` tests, the full Rust `cli` tests, the PHP schema database test and the TypeScript schema tool parity test pass. The symbol inventories hold the new symbols of the changed files; the interface check still reports the unrecorded symbols of other work, and Clippy reports one finding in `tests/unit/typed_bind_validation.rs`, neither of which this item changes.
- [o] N16 Provide a test fault that makes the rollback of a transaction fail on every database, in the four clients. Cause: orm reports a callback error and a failed rollback together, but offered no supported way to make a rollback fail; its own tests end the server session on MySQL and PostgreSQL or install a SQLite trigger written in SQL, which a test without SQL cannot do. Acceptance: `docs/protocol.md` §3.1 states the rule: only the test entry point of a client arms the fault, and no DSN, configuration value or environment variable arms it; the Go function `FailNextRollback` exists only with the build tag `ormtest`, the Rust module `orm::testing` only with the feature `test-faults`, the TypeScript subpath `@polyspec/orm-typescript/testing` resolves only under the Node condition `orm-test`, and the PHP class `Orm\Testing\Faults` is outside the package autoloader. The next rollback of a transaction whose callback failed runs and is then reported with the new catalog code `FAULT`, so the transaction returns `ROLLBACK` with the callback error and the `FAULT` error; a commit does not consume the fault, and the next failing transaction returns its callback error alone; the case `rollback_fault` passes in Go, PHP, Rust and TypeScript on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite, and each client checks that its production entry point does not reach the fault. Depends on: N13. Evidence: before the implementation, with the entry points that only arm the fault, `rollback_fault` failed in all twelve runs: Go `TestRollbackFault*` and the PHP, TypeScript and Rust cases reported the callback error alone instead of `ROLLBACK` on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite. After it Go `TestRollbackFault*` (build tag `ormtest`), `clients/php/tests/rollback_test.php`, `clients/typescript/tests/rollback.mjs` (condition `orm-test`) and Rust `rollback` (feature `test-faults`, including `transaction_send` and `transaction_once`) pass twice on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite, together with `rollback_failed` and `savepoint_rollback_failed`; the entry cases `TestRollbackFaultEntry`, `rollback_fault_entry` and the PHP and TypeScript entry checks pass. Rust Clippy with `test-faults`, Go vet with `ormtest`, the documentation build, idempotence and rules, the checklist, the feature manifest and the interface check of the new symbols pass. The checks that fail on `main` (feature coverage, Git subjects, the interface inventory of other work, TypeScript codec vectors, schema records, Clippy and formatting of `orm-build`, the Go decimal and coverage cases, PHP AES JSON and audit service, the TypeScript model suite, conformance results and the performance check) report the same failures with this change.
- [o] N17 Require no PDO driver extension in the PHP client. Cause: `clients/php/composer.json` required `ext-pdo_mysql` and only suggested `pdo_pgsql` and `pdo_sqlite`, so `composer install` refused a PHP without `pdo_mysql`, such as the official PHP image, although the client references `Pdo\Mysql` only when a `mysql://` DSN opens a connection. Acceptance: `composer.json` requires none of `ext-pdo_mysql`, `ext-pdo_pgsql` and `ext-pdo_sqlite` and suggests each; the case `clients/php/tests/sqlite_without_mysql.php`, which `scripts/php-without-mysql.sh` and `make php-without-mysql-check` run in the official PHP image `php:8.5.10-cli-trixie`, checks the manifest and creates and reads a row on SQLite without `pdo_mysql`. Depends on: none. Evidence: the case failed with `requires ["ext-pdo_mysql"]` before the change and passes after it; `composer validate`, the PHP unit tests and the checklist and document checks pass.
- [o] N18 Connect to MySQL with TLS through one DSN mode in the four clients, and refuse an unknown DSN parameter in the PHP and TypeScript clients. Cause: no client documented a MySQL TLS parameter; the PHP and TypeScript clients ignored every unknown parameter, so `ssl-mode=VERIFY_CA&ssl-ca=<file>` connected without TLS, and the PHP driver cannot check a server certificate against a CA without also checking the host name, so the one mode that every client can implement is `VERIFY_IDENTITY`. Acceptance: `docs/config.md` states `ssl-mode=VERIFY_IDENTITY` with an absolute `ssl-ca` and a host name as the one shared MySQL TLS mode, with TLS that checks the CA and the host name and no RSA password exchange; the Go, PHP and TypeScript clients return `CONFIG` for another `ssl-mode`, a missing or relative `ssl-ca`, `ssl-ca` without `ssl-mode`, `socket` with `ssl-mode` and an IP address host; the PHP and TypeScript clients return `CONFIG` for a parameter outside the documented set of each scheme; the Rust client keeps passing its parameters to sqlx and is tested with `VERIFY_IDENTITY`; the TLS cases of the four clients connect to the TLS server of `make test-servers` and refuse a certificate of another CA and one that names another host. Depends on: none. Evidence: `scripts/test-servers.sh tls` issues a test CA, a certificate of the primary that names `localhost`, a certificate of the replica that names `orm-mismatch.invalid`, signed by the same CA, and a second CA, loads them with `ALTER INSTANCE RELOAD TLS` and writes `ORM_TEST_MYSQL_TLS_DSN`, `ORM_TEST_MYSQL_TLS_OTHER_CA_DSN` and `ORM_TEST_MYSQL_TLS_MISMATCH_DSN` with the host `localhost.`, which resolves over TCP. RED: `clients/typescript/tests/mysql_tls.mjs` and `clients/php/tests/mysql_tls.php` failed with every refused DSN accepted, an empty `Ssl_version` and both wrong servers accepted; `clients/go/orm/mysql_tls_test.go` failed to compile without the TLS fields of the parsed DSN. GREEN: the three pass, with `TLSv1.3` on the primary and refusals of both wrong servers; `clients/rust/orm/tests/mysql_tls.rs` passed at once (2 of 2), because sqlx implements `ssl-mode` itself. The PHP DSN parsing case, the TypeScript database suite, the TypeScript DSN, engine, generator and schema tool cases, the PHP schema, ledger, clock, driver error and rollback cases and the DSN coverage cases of Go, PHP and TypeScript on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite pass; the TypeScript codec vector case and the PHP AES JSON, audit service and empty schema cases fail on ORM main in the same way and do not read the DSN parameters. Follow-up: the clients differ in the other parameters that they accept: the Go client passes them to go-sql-driver/mysql and pgx, and the Rust client to sqlx, which also accepts `verify_ca`; refusing them in Go and Rust as in PHP and TypeScript would also refuse a MySQL DSN that gives `ssl-mode=verify_ca` to the Rust client.
- [o] N19 Add the missing nullable or defaulted columns of the existing tables of a manifest through the connection, in the four clients. Priority: required when an installation adds a column to a table that a live database already holds. Cause: `utils().schema().install(manifestJson)` creates only missing tables and keeps existing ones, and the migration tools plan one manifest against the whole live database through their own connection, a ledger and a log directory, so for the manifest of one module they plan the removal of every other table; the import also dropped a foreign key of a table to its own key, so such a table never matched its manifest. Acceptance: `docs/schema.md` §8 states `utils().schema().addColumns(manifestJson)` (Go `AddColumns`, Rust `add_columns`): it adds every missing column of an existing table of the manifest that is nullable or has a default, replaces the audit triggers of each changed table so that they record the new columns, returns the added columns as `table.column`, leaves the missing tables and the tables of other manifests unchanged, and returns the new catalog code `SCHEMA_DIFFERS` before any change for every other difference (a required missing column, a removed or changed column, a key or an index); a repeated call adds nothing; on MySQL a call inside a transaction returns `CONFIG`; §6 states that a foreign key of a table to its own key is imported as a relation of the table to itself; the cases `add_columns`, `add_columns_differs`, `add_columns_transaction` and `add_columns_edited_manifest` pass in Go, PHP, Rust and TypeScript on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite with the fixtures `contracts/fixtures/add_columns_*`. Depends on: none. Evidence: RED: `clients/php/tests/add_columns_test.php` and `clients/typescript/tests/add-columns.mjs` failed in all twelve runs because the call did not exist, and `clients/go/orm/add_columns_test.go` and `clients/rust/orm/tests/add_columns.rs` did not compile without it. With the call and the old import, the case `add_columns`, whose `addcol_tag` has a key to itself, failed with `SCHEMA_DIFFERS` in the four clients on the three databases: SQLite planned a rebuild of `addcol_tag`, and MySQL and PostgreSQL planned `ADD CONSTRAINT fk_addcol_tag_parent_addcol_tag_seq`. The first PHP run also showed that the import reads a MySQL decimal default with the digits of its scale and a PostgreSQL string default with its quotes; the call compares these defaults by value. GREEN: the four cases pass twice in PHP, TypeScript, Go `TestAddColumns*` and Rust `add_columns*` on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite: the rows are kept, the new columns read NULL or their default, an audited update records `{"note":"later","rank":4}`, the log tables are unchanged, the missing table stays absent until `install`, and each of seven other differences returns `SCHEMA_DIFFERS` with no column added. The Rust catalog reads and the live import moved from `orm-build` to `orm_schema::catalog` and `orm_schema::live`, so the runtime and the tool read the catalog through one `Catalog` trait. The PHP schema, schema tool, schema database, ledger and schema set cases, the TypeScript schema tool, schema case, SQLite import, ledger, engine, schema set, generator, DSN and driver error cases, the Go `internal/ormgen`, engine and client packages, the Rust workspace and `orm-build --features cli` tests, the TypeScript type check, Rust Clippy of the workspace and of `test-faults`, and the documentation records pass, except the cases that need the decimal, coverage and send-savepoint environments, the timing bound of `physical_graph_vectors`, the TypeScript model suite, whose empty-database cases find tables of other tests in the shared databases, and the formatting and `cli` Clippy findings of `orm-build`, which fail on ORM main in the same way.
- [o] N19.1 Compare the PostgreSQL column types and the bounded index names of the existing tables by their stored form in addColumns, in the four clients. Cause: PostgreSQL stores `char(n)` as `varchar(n)` and every blob type as `bytea`, and stores an index name longer than 63 bytes cut with a digest, so the import reads another raw type and another index name than the manifest declares; addColumns returned `SCHEMA_DIFFERS` for installed core and board manifests that hold a `longblob`, a `char(64)` and long index names. Acceptance: `docs/schema.md` §8 states that a column whose type the dialect stores as the declared type has the declared type and that an index whose physical name is the physical name of a declared index has the declared name; the fixtures `contracts/fixtures/add_columns_*` hold a `longblob` column, a `char(8)` column and an index whose PostgreSQL name is longer than 63 bytes, and the N19 cases pass with them in Go, PHP, Rust and TypeScript on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite. Evidence: RED: with the new fixtures the four cases failed on PostgreSQL in the four clients with `SCHEMA_DIFFERS`: `addcol_item.payload changed from raw=blob to raw=longblob`; with the type comparison and without the index comparison PHP `add_columns` failed on PostgreSQL with `DROP INDEX "addcol_item_ix_addcol_item_enabled_and_created_at__b10618c7774a"` and `CREATE INDEX "addcol_item_ix_addcol_item_enabled_and_created_at_stored_together"`. GREEN: the four cases pass twice in PHP, TypeScript, Go `TestAddColumns*` and Rust `add_columns*` on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite, and a plan for installed core, member, page and board manifests adds nothing and finds no difference on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite.

## Stage 3 — Schema tools per language

- [o] T7.17.2.4 Resolve bounded immutable physical graphs in four clients (requires T7.17.2.1–3). Cause: independently valid records do not prove reference membership or uniqueness. Evidence: missing APIs in four clients, PHP 128 MiB exhaustion and Go native integer version rejection are Red-to-Green. The reusable physical-graph-check passes 28 common graph and five count/byte-limit vectors twice per client, plus all 25 column and 26 FK regressions. Exact shapes, global IDs/names, ordered owner resolution, cycles/self/shared references, located value-free errors and detached snapshots are verified. Each client retains the connected 2000-table/60000-column/10000-FK case. PHP process allocation peak is 62 MiB with the unchanged 128M limit; this is not RSS or another client's memory measurement. Owner Rust 1.98.1 Clippy, TypeScript compilation on declared Node 26.10.0, paired records and diff checks pass on macOS arm64. This does not complete physical import, full physical schemas, DDL or DB conformance.

- [o] T7.17.2.3 Preserve physical foreign keys and share bounded record validation (requires T7.17.2.1/T7.17.2.2). Cause: logical relationships lose independent FK names, paired-column order, update/delete actions and deferral. Evidence: four missing-API Reds are Green. The reusable physical-fk-check passes all 26 FK and 25 existing column vectors twice per Go/PHP/Rust/TypeScript client, plus sparse/alias and 64/65-column checks. Preserve independent names, ordered pairs, self/shared references, distinct actions, unspecified states and exact options; reject contradictions safely. Shared record modules replace column-specific validation, including Rust RecordError without the obsolete ColumnError path. Rust 1.98.1 owner Clippy, declared Node 26.10.0 TypeScript compilation, PHP syntax, paired checklist, documentation rules and diff checks pass on macOS arm64. Each client creates/retains 2000 FK records twice; observed second-run times are Go 7.36 ms, PHP 9.50 ms, Rust debug 22.95 ms and TypeScript 12.27 ms. These are record-retention measurements, not connected graph, archive, memory, frame, database or other-platform acceptance. Graph reference resolution, imports, DDL and execution remain separate requirements.

- [o] T7.17.2.2 Preserve immutable physical column records in four clients (requires T7.17.2.1). Cause: logical columns cannot carry exact native type/default/generation/options without loss. Evidence: missing API Reds in all four clients, TypeScript type-narrowing compile failure and PHP reference-alias runtime Red are Green. The exposed physical-column-check passes all 25 shared exact-field/rejection vectors twice in Go/PHP/Rust/TypeScript, plus aggregate/field/option limits, invalid encodings, sparse options and detached input/output checks. TypeScript compilation using declared Node 26.10.0, owner Rust Clippy, PHP syntax, paired checklist, documentation rules and diff checks pass. Preserve stable IDs, raw SQL, absent/NULL/literal/expression distinctions, identity/computed generation and ordered options. This is structural interchange, not text parsing, SQL grammar validation, physical import, database conformance or execution authority.

- [o] T7.17.2.1 Preserve qualified physical identities without logical normalization. Cause: logical identifier rules cannot represent arbitrary physical names. Evidence: missing API Reds in all four clients and a TypeScript sparse-array rejection Red are Green. The tracked physical-identity-check runs all ten shared vectors twice per Go/PHP/Rust/TypeScript client with identical exact keys/rejections, plus immutable component, invalid encoding and UTF-8 byte-limit checks. TypeScript compilation, owner Rust Clippy, PHP syntax, paired checklist, documentation rules and diff checks pass. Preserve optional catalog/schema/column, required table, exact case/UTF-8 and unambiguous name keys without normalization. This primitive does not enable physical import, annotation parsing, DDL, migrations or database-dependent conformance.

- [o] N9.2.1 Remove the existing send-savepoint borrowed-Box warning. Cause: strict Clippy rejects savepoint_send in orm/src/tx.rs. Evidence: borrowed-Box lint and the tracked directly borrowed callback compile case fail before correction and pass after accepting &SendOperation with as_ref() at the caller. The reusable rust-send-savepoint-check runs Rust 1.98.1 strict library Clippy and all three owning MySQL/PostgreSQL/SQLite tests twice. Verify Send futures, direct callback borrowing, nested commit/rollback, original callback errors, restored transaction frames, outer rollback of released savepoints, exact retained fixture rows and connection reuse. Each test has its own 10-second deadline. Fixtures use connection-local temporary tables, never user tables; the shared Make environment declares the SQLite URI for existing test entry points. Owner library/tests Clippy, scoped formatting, paired records and diff checks pass on macOS arm64. This is a Rust implementation correction, not new transaction semantics or four-client DB conformance.

- [o] L1 Build `schema.json` from `.mmd` files in every language. Evidence: `make schema-cross-language-check` builds the same Mermaid source with Go, PHP, Rust, and TypeScript, compares the complete JSON manifests, and reports four equal outputs with 12 entities and schema hash `16198b563e2e3cae`.
- [o] L2 Provide migration and import tools in every language. Evidence: Go SQLite import tests normalize `DECIMALINT(13,4)` and `DECIMALINT(16,0)` to logical decimal types and build the manifest; PHP schema tools pass migration and import on SQLite, MySQL and PostgreSQL; Rust CLI import and TypeScript SQLite import tests preserve decimal precision and scale. The four SQLite import paths use the same logical mapping and reject no input silently.

## Schema and migration tools


- [o] T7.3 Implement deterministic `ormgen diff` and destructive-change checks.
- [o] T7.5 Implement YAML 1.2 and `point` conversions in Go, PHP, Rust, and TypeScript. Verify `point` DDL and SQL on MySQL, PostgreSQL, and SQLite.
- [o] T7.9 Compare Rust `mysql_async` 0.37.1 with sqlx 0.9 using equal SQL, binds, typed results, connection count, and fixture. Retain sqlx because neither measured workload shows the required 2x improvement.
- [o] T7.10 Exclude `multi_statement` from every public API because the supported databases cannot provide the same safe parameterized execution structure. G4 owns the executable exclusion check for all four clients.
- [o] T7.13 Validate AES version columns, persist the current version on writes, and provide status and transactional rotation APIs in Go, PHP, Rust, and TypeScript.
- [o] T7.14 Include table and column comments in the manifest, schema hash, import, DDL, diff, and SQLite metadata.
- [o] T7.15 Add migration execution locking, transaction boundaries, and detailed recovery states for MySQL, PostgreSQL, and SQLite.
- [o] T7.16 Replace semicolon splitting with a dialect-aware SQL statement parser and preserve statement-level failure locations.
- [o] T7.17 Accept MMD, manifest JSON, metadata-bearing ORM SQL, and live DB schema sources for DDL, diff, structured plans, verification, recovery, idempotent migration, and verified rollback.
- [o] T7.17.1 Expose Rust DSN-only catalog connections through the public library and make the CLI use the same implementation. Evidence: missing module compile Red corrected; missing SQLite file creation Red corrected. Four public API owner tests pass, including actual MySQL/PostgreSQL repeated catalog reads and SQLite FK/content preservation; existing SQLite CLI automatic-rowid import regression passes. This extracts existing behavior, not lossless physical import or arbitrary data decoding.
- [o] T7.17.1.1 Reject lossy catalog/tool cell decoding before extending public data access. Cause: unsupported cells become NULL, invalid UTF-8 is replaced and unsigned overflow wraps. Criterion: owning physical Red/Green cases on MySQL/PostgreSQL/SQLite, explicit errors without secret values, and unchanged valid catalog/CLI paths. Evidence: seven lossy cases Red → all three databases Green; four catalog tests and the SQLite CLI automatic-rowid case pass.
- [o] T7.17.1.2 Replace invalid numeric/boolean accessor defaults with checked results and propagate errors through owning catalog/migration paths. Linked to T7.17.1.1. Cause: malformed integer text becomes zero and malformed/NULL booleans become false. Criterion: required conversions return errors without values; only optional integers accept NULL; catalog and migration callers propagate failures, retaining transaction cleanup; owning accessor, real catalog and CLI regressions pass. Evidence: missing Result API compile Red → two accessor tests Green (including all three databases); four catalog, one raw-cell and five CLI regressions pass. Invalid SQLite history is rejected without value exposure or mutation; three-database plan/apply/rollback/recovery remains valid.
- [o] T7.17.1.3 Bound Rust tool query accumulation while streaming instead of fetching all rows before validation. Cause: catalog/tool fetch_all has unbounded retained results. Evidence: missing-budget API compile Red and multiple-statement runtime Red corrected; MySQL prepared unsigned-overflow regression reproduced and corrected. Nine owning query/catalog/accessor tests and eleven CLI tests pass. Actual MySQL/PostgreSQL/SQLite cases verify exact/overflow budgets, invalid ceilings, binds, empty results, secret-free failures and connection reuse. Defaults cap rows at 100,000 and JSON results at 64 MiB; reject rather than truncate and accept one statement. This bounds accumulated output, not driver packet memory/DB execution, and does not complete arbitrary SQL result types.
- [o] T7.17.1.4 Expose ordered bounded query column metadata, including empty results and duplicate aliases. Evidence: missing result API compile Red corrected; actual MySQL/PostgreSQL/SQLite cases verify ordered duplicate aliases, empty-result columns and public catalog queries. Ten owning integration tests, one post-implementation metadata-budget unit regression and eleven CLI tests pass. Prepared metadata/checked rows share one reserved connection; metadata limits are 2,048 columns and 64 KiB of name/type bytes. This is SQL-grid groundwork, not read-only isolation or complete data types.
- [o] T7.17.1.5 Expose bounded read-only catalog queries using dialect AST validation and database-enforced read-only scopes on discard-on-drop connections. Evidence: missing API compile Red corrected; eleven owning integration tests, two post-implementation policy/disposal unit regressions and eleven CLI tests pass. Actual three-database cases verify MySQL function-write rejection, PostgreSQL/SQLite read-only state, unchanged rows and recovery after query errors. Event-driven abort tests verify no scope connection pool reuse; cancellation latency is not measured. Reject mutation/transaction statements, write CTEs, SELECT INTO, locks and executable comments; preserve SQL text and errors and bound parser/visitor depth. Correct the initial MySQL session-variable test assumption without server changes; identified failed test fixtures were cleaned. Not an OS/function sandbox or complete SQL type support.
- [o] T7.19 Add AES blind-index schema declarations, keyed equality predicates, and write synchronization.
- [o] T7.20 Split oversized root `IN` predicates, preserve non-`IN` parameters, merge rows, sum count results, and reject unsafe query shapes.
- [o] T7.21 Add the `soft_delete` schema directive, apply active-row predicates to reads and updates, and convert deletes to timestamp updates.

- [o] T7.17.1.6 Add bounded Rust typed read-only grid results with byte-preserving binary cells separate from text. Cause: tool values reject arbitrary bytes or interpret valid binary UTF-8 as text; SQL grid values need faithful representation. Evidence: missing GridCell/API compile Red is Green; actual MySQL/PostgreSQL/SQLite binary/NULL/empty/text cases pass. Added post-implementation budget, mutation-policy, empty-metadata and bind regressions pass. Twelve owning integration tests, eleven CLI tests and five library tests pass; streaming/metadata budgets and enforced read-only scope/disposal share the existing implementation. Numeric/temporal types and other-client grid APIs remain pending; binary support alone does not complete query types.

- [o] T7.17.1.7 Preserve native Rust grid floating-point bits and unsigned integer range. Cause: strict catalog scalar decoding rejects these query values. Evidence: missing typed variants compile Red is Green; actual three-database float64, PostgreSQL float32/signed-zero/NaN and MySQL u64::MAX cases pass. Numeric NULL and byte-budget checks are added regressions. Thirteen integration, eleven CLI and five library tests pass, retaining strict catalog semantics and read-only disposal. Decimal/temporal types remain pending.

- [o] T7.17.1.8 Preserve finite Rust grid decimal precision and declared scale without float conversion. Cause: current grid values have no decimal tag and fixed-precision model decoding cannot represent arbitrary SQL numeric results. Evidence: missing decimal variant compile Red is Green on actual MySQL 65-digit decimals, PostgreSQL declared trailing-zero scale and SQLite native storage classes. Added PostgreSQL 200-digit/exponent, numeric NULL, byte-budget and nonfinite rejection regressions pass. Fourteen integration, eleven CLI and five library tests pass. Use pinned BigDecimal 0.4.11; restore PostgreSQL binary dscale after public driver decoding and reject any value-changing scale adjustment. Nonfinite temporal values remain pending.

- [o] T7.17.1.9 Expose qualified Rust table metadata with declared primary-key order, native types, nullability and generated columns for explicit table browsing/editing. Cause: logical catalog column flags lose composite-key order and cannot establish reliable row identity. Evidence: missing public API compile Red; actual MySQL/PostgreSQL/SQLite Green for reversed composite keys, generated columns, nullable SQLite keys and rowid aliases. Added views, DESC/WITHOUT ROWID keys, untyped columns and qualified-name/input regressions pass; 12 focused tests pass. This metadata does not authorize edits; mutations and optimistic conflict checks remain pending.

- [o] T7.17.1.9.1 Read qualified MySQL system-view metadata through the Rust catalog API. Cause: actual process inspection of a system catalog fails; the native SYSTEM VIEW kind was absent from the relation classifier. Evidence: actual-MySQL Red confirmed native kind and failed descriptor; Green classifies it as View without reliable row identity. Six focused metadata/catalog tests pass, including ordinary table/view cases on all three databases. The new system-view case changes no database contents; Rust scope does not establish four-client conformance.

- [o] T7.17.1.10 Add explicit qualified Rust table-page reads through the native typed grid API. Cause: arbitrary SQL results do not establish table/column/key provenance for row edits. - [o] G5.7 Run each check once in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: the steps `common interfaces` (`make interface-check`) and `hot-path regression gate` (`make perf-check`) are removed, because `make check` runs their commands, `PATH="$HOME/.cargo/bin:$PATH" go run ./tests/interfaces/check --self-test` and `./scripts/perf-test.sh`, as verification commands of `contracts/features.json` in `feature-check`. `make repo-check` fails when a workflow line equals a verification command that runs from the repository root, or runs a make target outside `CHECK_TARGETS` whose every recipe command is one (make's `$$` and `$(NAME)` read as the shell's `$` and `$NAME`, without the leading `$(WITH_TEST_ENV)`). The rule compares whole commands; tests that two different commands both select remain: `go test ./...` of the `Go` step and the Go and Rust lines of `codec vectors` run tests that feature verification commands also run with other filters. Evidence: with the new rule and the G5.5 workflow `node scripts/repo/check.mjs` failed with `ci.yml step "common interfaces" runs make interface-check, whose commands make check runs in feature-check` and the same error for `hot-path regression gate`; it now passes, `make repo-check` passes 22/22 node cases and the repository case, and Ruby `YAML.load_file` parses the workflow. `make owner-check` with the changed paths selects 0 features. Only a GitHub Actions run (G5) can show the workflow on the runner.
- [o] G5.8 Run every workflow job on the runner `ubuntu-26.04-arm`, declared once in `.github/runner`, and make each install step valid there. `ci.yml`: MySQL 8.4.11 from the Ubuntu 26.04 packages `mysql-server-core` and `mysql-client-core` (`8.4.11-0ubuntu0.26.04.1`, which hold `/usr/sbin/mysqld`, `mysql_tzinfo_to_sql` and `mysqladmin`) instead of the Oracle amd64 bundle, checked as ` Ver 8.4.11`; PostgreSQL 17 and PgBouncer from the PostgreSQL apt repository, whose `resolute-pgdg` holds `postgresql-17` 17.11 for arm64; ProxySQL 3.0.9 from `proxysql_3.0.9-ubuntu24_arm64.deb`, checked by its sha256 `e12b2721…eb2a0f`, because the ProxySQL repository has no `resolute`: on the runner its dependencies are satisfied and the extracted binary runs (`ProxySQL version 3.0.9-618-g7ddb3dc`). The build of SQLite 3.46.1 for PHP is removed: the image links PHP to the system SQLite 3.46.1, and the step now only checks that PHP reports 3.46 or later. PHP 8.4 for `make php-min-check` comes from a second `shivammathur/setup-php` step, which supports 8.4 on `ubuntu-26.04-arm`, reading the version that the step `php-min` writes from `scripts/php/php-min.sh --version`; the step of `.php-version` runs last so that PHP 8.5 is `php`. The static-php-cli `common` build of PHP 8.4.23 is not used: it links SQLite 3.45.2 on linux-aarch64 and macos-aarch64, below the 3.46 every client requires (`dsn.php` fails with `CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED: SQLite 3.45.2 is older than 3.46`), and the PHP 8.4 builds that setup-php installs are replaced under the same release URL, so no checksum can pin them. `docs-pages.yml` runs both jobs on the same runner. `make repo-check` fails when a job runs on another runner than `.github/runner` or declares none, when a setup-php step reads `steps.php-min.outputs.version` without the step `php-min` that writes it, when it installs the lowest release twice, and when the step of `.php-version` is not the last setup-php step. Evidence: with `.github/runner` the rule rejects the previous workflows with `ci.yml runs on ubuntu-24.04; .github/runner declares ubuntu-26.04-arm` and the same error for both jobs of `docs-pages.yml`; `make repo-check` passes 25/25 node cases and the repository case; Ruby `YAML.load_file` parses both workflows. `make owner-check` with the changed paths selects 0 features. Only a run on `ubuntu-26.04-arm` can show the installations, setup-php 8.4 and the servers there.
- [o] G5.9 Make `TestCIRequiresGeneratedChecks` (`tests/interfaces/check/prohibited_test.go`) check the workflow of G5.5 and G5.7. The test now requires that `ci.yml` runs `make check`, that `CHECK_TARGETS` holds `go-model-check` and `ts-model-check`, and that `contracts/features.json` verifies `go run ./tests/interfaces/check --self-test`, the command of `make interface-check`; a mutation removes each of the four and must fail. Evidence: `go test ./tests/interfaces/check -count=1` failed before the change and passes after it; `make owner-check` with the changed paths selects 0 features.
- [o] G5.10 Start every MySQL server of `make test-servers` with `--secure-file-priv=NULL`, the value the local checks had, instead of the compiled default of each build. The Ubuntu 26.04 `mysqld` defaults to `/var/lib/mysql-files`, a directory that only the `mysql-server` package creates, while the workflow installs `mysql-server-core`; the Homebrew build defaults to NULL. Evidence: a run of T40 on an Ubuntu 26.04 ARM64 machine failed in `make test-servers` with `mysqld: Error on realpath() on '/var/lib/mysql-files' (Error 2 - No such file or directory)`; locally `mysqld --secure-file-priv=/var/lib/mysql-files` fails to start with the same error and `[MY-010095] Failed to access directory for --secure-file-priv`, and with `--secure-file-priv=NULL` after it the server starts and reports `@@secure_file_priv` NULL. The run of the working tree with G5.10, G5.11 and G5.12 started every server and wrote `.runtime/servers/env` (`test-servers: started`). `make owner-check` with the changed path selects 0 features; `make repo-check` passes.
- [o] G5.11 Make `make test-servers` fail before it starts a server when a Unix socket path under `.runtime/servers` exceeds the platform limit, 107 bytes on Linux and 103 on macOS, naming the path, its length and the limit: `test-servers: socket path <path> is <N> bytes; <system> allows at most <limit>`. The five socket paths (MySQL, MySQL replica, the two ProxySQL admin interfaces and the ProxySQL PostgreSQL interface) are declared once. A deep checkout, such as a long worktree path, failed with the MySQL log line `The socket file path is too long (> 107)` after the servers began to start. Evidence: the new case `test-servers.sh refuses a socket path longer than the platform limit` of `scripts/repo/check.test.mjs` copies the script under a deep checkout, once where every socket is too long and once where only `proxysql-admin-pgsql.sock` is; with the previous script it failed with `Failed to start mysqld daemon` and `start failed; logs are in …` after starting `mysqld`, and now it passes in 85 ms with the exact message and no `.runtime/servers`. `make repo-check` passes; `make owner-check` with the changed paths selects 0 features.
- [o] G5.12 Read the output of ProxySQL and PgBouncer in `make test-servers` line by line as it arrives. `start_logged` copied the output through `awk` and waited for the ready line; the `awk` of Ubuntu, `mawk`, reads a pipe in large blocks and passes no line until the server exits, so on the Linux runner the start of ProxySQL waited until the run was stopped after 3981.5 s, and PgBouncer the same. A `sh` loop of `read` now copies each line to the log and reports the ready line; the read of the FIFO opens it again when the exit of a fast-ending server interrupts the open (`Interrupted system call` of the macOS `/bin/sh`). Evidence: on the Ubuntu 26.04 machine `(echo ready; sleep 5) | awk '{ print "got " $0; fflush() }'` prints `got ready` only after 5 s, ProxySQL writes 101 log lines within 6 s to a file, and the run of T40 with G5.10 and G5.11 waited in ProxySQL with no `.runtime/servers/proxysql.log`; the new case `start_logged reports a ready line while the server keeps running` of `scripts/repo/check.test.mjs` runs the function of the script with a server that keeps running after its ready line and with one that exits first, and passes in 36 ms six times in a row; the run of the working tree with G5.10, G5.11 and G5.12 started ProxySQL and PgBouncer and wrote `.runtime/servers/env`. `make repo-check` passes; `make owner-check` with the changed paths selects 0 features.
- [o] G5.13 Give every server start and every CI step its own deadline. `start_logged` in `scripts/test-servers.sh` takes a declared deadline per server (60 s for ProxySQL and PgBouncer, which log their ready line within seconds); a timer reports through the same FIFO, and a start that passes it fails with `test-servers: <name> logged no line with '<line>' within its deadline of <N> s; last lines of <log>:` and the last 20 lines of the log, and an exited server reports its last lines too. Every step of `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `docs-pages.yml` declares `timeout-minutes`, sized from the run of the working tree on `ubuntu-26.04-arm` (4 vCPU, 16 GB), where `make check` took 1150.1 s and every other step under 84 s, with room for cold caches and downloads: `make check` 90, Go and codec vectors 30, the installs 10 to 20, the rest 2 to 15. `make repo-check` fails on a workflow step without a positive `timeout-minutes`. Evidence: the run before G5.12 waited 3981.5 s in `database servers` without a deadline; the case `start_logged reports a ready line, an exit or its deadline` now fails a silent server after its 2 s deadline with the exact message and passes in 2.1 s, also 24 times in a row with six runs at a time (the timer stops only its `sleep`, so the shell reports no killed job); it fails against the previous script; with the new rule `make repo-check` failed with 27 errors `<workflow> step "<step>" has no timeout-minutes of its own` and now passes; Ruby `YAML.load_file` parses both workflows. `make owner-check` with the changed paths selects 0 features.
- [o] G5.14 Keep the documentation build green: the T40 items of `docs/checklist.md` and its Korean pair wrote `<kind> <table>[.<name>]` and `<nil>` outside code, which VitePress compiles as unclosed HTML elements, so `make docs-check` and `make docs-verify-idempotent` failed with `docs/checklist.md (209:2878): Element is missing end tag` locally and on an Ubuntu 26.04 ARM64 machine. The two messages are now inline code, and `make docs-rules-check` fails on an element outside code in a page under `docs/` other than `<br>`, which the generated feature pages use: `docs.element: <file> writes <tag> outside code; put it in inline code`. Evidence: with the new rule and the previous checklist pair `make docs-rules-check` failed with eight `docs.element` errors (`<kind>`, `<table>`, `<name>` and `<nil>` in each language); now it passes, `make docs-check` passes (48 pages, 546 internal links) and `make docs-verify-idempotent` passes. `make owner-check` with the changed paths selects 0 features; T41 makes owner checks run the documentation checks for a changed document.
- [o] G5.15 Fetch the crates of `clients/rust` and `bench/rust` before any cargo command that runs with `--offline`. The new target `rust-fetch` runs `cargo fetch --locked` for both lockfiles, and the 14 targets that run cargo offline, in their recipe or through `scripts/decimal-physical-check.mjs` and `tests/timing/preempted.test.mjs`, name it as their prerequisite; a target no longer depends on an earlier target having filled the cargo cache. Evidence: on a fresh Ubuntu 26.04 ARM64 machine, `make rust-driver-check` failed in 19 ms with `error: no matching package named mysql_async found` (`location searched: crates.io index`, offline); locally `cargo test --locked --offline` in `bench/rust` with an empty `CARGO_HOME` fails with the same error, and `make rust-driver-check` with an empty `CARGO_HOME` now passes (`PASS rust-fetch elapsed=12.701s`, then 0, 1, 4 and 2 passing tests). `make repo-check` and `TestCIRequiresGeneratedChecks` pass; `make owner-check` with the changed paths selects 0 features.
- [o] G5.16 Wait up to 60 s, instead of 10 s, for the MySQL replica to reach the binary log position of the primary in the primary and replica cases of Go, PHP, Rust and TypeScript. The replica applies the writes of every client that `make client-db-check` runs in parallel, one after another, and on the 4-vCPU Linux runner the PHP case failed with `the replica did not reach binlog.000001:358010684` after its 10 s wait; the deadline limits work that another process does, so it is wall-clock time, and the comment beside each wait states the reason. Evidence: the run of the working tree on `ubuntu-26.04-arm` failed `client-db-check` with that error; `make owner-check` with the four changed test files selects nine features (`dsn_connection`, `model_queries`, `model_writes`, `transactions`, `schema_install`, `composite_keys`, `authenticated_encryption`, `parameter_chunking`, `audit_triggers`) and passes 18/18 verification and coverage steps; gofmt, rustfmt, `php -l` and `node --check` pass.
- [o] G5.17 Run the PHP of the checks without a debugger or coverage driver, as the local PHP runs. Both `shivammathur/setup-php` steps of `ci.yml` set `coverage: none`, and `make repo-check` fails on a setup-php step without it (`<workflow> sets up PHP without coverage: none, so Xdebug or pcov can load`); `clients/php/tests/perf_gate.php` refuses to measure when Xdebug or pcov is loaded (`perf_gate: this PHP loads <extension>; …`), through `perfGateRefusedExtensions` of `clients/php/tests/perf_extensions.php`, whose case `perf_extensions_test.php` runs in `make client-unit-check`. Evidence: the run of the working tree on `ubuntu-26.04-arm` loaded Xdebug 3.5.3 into PHP 8.5.4 and 8.4.26, and the PHP hot-path regression check measured ratios 1.64 (pk, bound 1.35) and 1.28 (list100, bound 1.25); with the new rule `make repo-check` failed twice on `ci.yml` and passes after the change, also for a removed or `xdebug` value; `perf_extensions_test.php` passes 2/2 (the local PHP loads neither extension, so the refusal itself was not run here). `make owner-check` with the changed paths selects `performance_gate` and passes its verification and coverage; `make client-unit-check`, `make repo-check` and `TestCIRequiresGeneratedChecks` pass.
- [o] G5.18 Define `ORM_TEST_MYSQL_SERVER_DSN` and `ORM_TEST_POSTGRES_SERVER_DSN` in the environment file of `make test-servers`, the one definition of the variables the checks read. Only `WITH_TEST_ENV` of the Makefile set them, so the CI step `go test ./...`, which reads the environment file through `$GITHUB_ENV`, failed `TestRollbackFailed{MySQL,Postgres}` and `TestSavepointRollbackFailed{MySQL,Postgres}` with `ORM_TEST_MYSQL_SERVER_DSN is required` on the Linux runner. `write_env` writes the file, `WITH_TEST_ENV` no longer defines the two variables, and `start` writes the file again for running servers, so an existing file follows the definition. Evidence: with the previous environment file, `go test ./clients/go/orm -run TestRollbackFailedMySQL` failed with that message; after `make test-servers` the file defines both variables once (no variable twice) and the four rollback tests pass with only the file loaded. `make owner-check` with the changed paths selects the feature `transactions` and 24 make targets; all passed except `case-database-check`, whose TypeScript case `model/postgres/schemaEmpty` ended with an unhandled `terminating connection due to administrator command` from an idle pool connection, and which passed when run again; that intermittent failure is not caused by this change and is recorded for its own item.
- [o] G5.19 Give `docs-pages.yml` an explicit build step, so its stages are build, test and smoke as GitHub runs them. The job publishes `docs/.vitepress/dist`, but its only step that wrote it was `Build twice and compare every static file` (`make docs-verify-idempotent`), a test step. The workflow does produce an artifact, so it declares no skip: `Build the static site` runs `make docs-build`, the idempotence check builds twice more and compares every byte, and `Smoke test of the built site in a browser` runs `make docs-static-check` on the output before the upload. Evidence: `docs-pages.yml` now has the stages build, test and smoke; Ruby `YAML.load_file` parses the workflow; `make owner-check` with the changed paths selects `checklist-check`, `version-check`, `repo-check`, `docs-rules-check`, `docs-check` and `docs-verify-idempotent` and passes them.
- [o] G5.20 Run every MySQL test server with `lower_case_table_names=1`, declared once in `scripts/test-servers.sh`, and move existing data to it by a repeatable procedure. MySQL picks the value from the file system when it initializes a data directory (2 on macOS, 0 on Linux), and `make test-servers` did not set it, so `dialect-facts-check` failed on the Linux runner with `got "0"; want "2"`; 1 is the only value that both platforms accept. `make test-servers` passes `--lower-case-table-names=1` to every `mysqld` and writes `.runtime/servers/mysql.settings`. When the servers already run, `scripts/test-servers-mysql.mjs` compares the declared value with the marker, or with the running primary when there is none: the same value changes nothing; another value runs the migration steps preflight (names that the value would merge refuse the migration, with the list, before any change), dump (every non-system database with routines, triggers, events, the users and grants, and the per-database counts), stop, keep (the old data directories move to `.runtime/mysql-before-<stamp>` and are not deleted), start, restore, verify (primary and replica against the recorded counts) and record, each with RUN, PASS or FAIL, its elapsed time and its deadline; progress is recorded, so a run after a failure resumes and rebuilds only the new data directory. The MySQL facts of `tests/dialects/probes_mysql.go` and `docs/dialects.md` state 1. The new target `test-servers-check` (in `CHECK_TARGETS`, with its inputs) runs `scripts/test-servers-mysql.test.mjs`: servers initialized with the platform default migrate with equal counts and a second run prints `unchanged`; colliding names refuse the migration on Linux, and on macOS the server itself rejects such a pair (1050). Evidence: `make dialect-facts-check` failed with `got "2"; want "1"` against the local servers; `test-servers-check` passes 4/4 on macOS; on the local servers `make test-servers` found 19 databases and no collision, recorded 161 counts (among them a database of 44 tables and 63 triggers, `orm_bench` 12 tables, `dbspec_sub_probe` 40 tables, one user), migrated in about 33 s, verified all 161 counts on the primary and the replica, kept the earlier data in `.runtime/mysql-before-20261004-121451`, and a second `make test-servers` printed `lower_case_table_names=1 unchanged`; both servers report `lower_case_table_names` 1 and `secure_file_priv` NULL, and `make dialect-facts-check` passes.
- [o] G5.21 Keep the TypeScript client running when the PostgreSQL server ends a pooled connection that waits in the pool or that the pool is closing. The `pg` pool reports the server's message on such a connection as an `error` event of the pool and removes the connection; the client registered no listener, so Node ended the process with `Unhandled 'error' event`. That ended `case-database-check` once in `model/postgres/schemaEmpty` with `terminating connection due to administrator command` (57P01) from the pool's `idleListener`: `Db.close()` resolves when `pg-pool` 3.14 has started closing its idle connections, before their sockets close, and the case then drops its database `WITH (FORCE)`, which ends a connection that is still closing when the server handles the drop first; the PostgreSQL log shows that termination at 21:32:29.621 followed by the drop's checkpoint. `PostgresPoolDriver` now listens for the pool's `error` event; no statement runs on that connection, so no caller receives the error, and the next statement opens a new connection. The MySQL pool already survived the same end. Evidence: the new case `clients/typescript/tests/idle-connection-end.mjs` (in `npm run typescript:test` and the `dsn_connection` tests) runs a child process that keeps one idle pooled connection to a new case database, has the server end it (`pg_terminate_backend` on PostgreSQL, `KILL` on MySQL), waits until the server has closed the session (`KILL` returns before that, so the MySQL branch waits until `PROCESSLIST` no longer lists it) and for one turn of the event loop, runs a statement again and closes; before the change the PostgreSQL child ended with status 1 and `Unhandled 'error' event` on every run, and the MySQL child passed; now both pass, 40 of 40 runs on the minimum Node version. Without the wait, the MySQL statement failed in 4 of 30 runs with `PROTOCOL_CONNECTION_LOST` or `ECONNRESET`, because it reached the ended connection before the pool removed it.
- [o] G5.22 Run every program that cargo builds from `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, which the Makefile declares and exports. The checks named `clients/rust/target/...` directly: the Makefile targets `dbspec-apply-pairs-check`, `dbspec-introspect-compare-check`, `dbspec-stress-bench`, `example-check` and `timing-check` and the stress document paths, the `chunking-rust` command of `contracts/features.json`, `scripts/case-database-check.mjs`, `scripts/client-db-test.sh`, `tests/dbspec/compare/runners.mjs` and `tests/dbspec/inputs.test.mjs`; `scripts/check-rust-150.sh` and `scripts/package-check.sh` overrode the declared value with `$ROOT/clients/rust/target`, and `tests/conformance/check` fell back to `clients/rust/target` when the value was unset. With another target directory, such as the one a worktree shares with the main checkout, cargo built into it and the checks looked elsewhere (`clients/rust/target/debug/integration: No such file or directory`, `spawn clients/rust/target/debug/examples/dbspec_compare ENOENT`). Every path now comes from `$(CARGO_TARGET_DIR)` in the Makefile, `${CARGO_TARGET_DIR:?…}` in shell, `cargoTarget()` of `tests/cargo-target.mjs` in Node and `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` in Go, and each fails when the value is unset instead of guessing a directory. `make repo-check` rejects a run command in the Makefile, `contracts/features.json`, a workflow, `scripts/` or `tests/` that names `clients/rust/target` (also as `"clients", "rust", "target"`), except comments and the declaration itself; documents and example comments may describe the default location. Evidence: with the shared target directory the worktree owner check of T41.1 failed `chunking-rust` and `schema-compare` with those errors; with the new rule `make repo-check` failed with 17 lines naming `clients/rust/target`, and on the previous `tests/conformance/check/main.go` with its fallback line; now it passes, and `make owner-check` with the changed paths passes the selected feature and make targets.
  - [o] G5.22-1 Read the package directory of a Rust test when the test runs, not when cargo compiles it. Cause: 31 lines in 24 Rust files under `clients/rust` (integration tests and the unit tests of `orm/src/codec.rs` and `orm/src/engine/mod.rs`) located fixtures and the repository through `env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")`, which writes the absolute path of the checkout that compiled the test into its binary. A worktree builds into the main checkout's `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` (G5.22), and cargo records workspace sources by relative path and does not rebuild a binary when only the checkout differs, so the main checkout ran test binaries that the removed worktree `orm-test-design-G5.29` had built. The final `make check` failed `client-db-check` and `client-pooler-check` (`add_tables_and_columns`: `orm-test-design-G5.29/clients/rust/orm/../../../contracts/fixtures/add_tables_and_columns/log.dbs: No such file or directory`) and `feature-check` (the Rust coverage of `schema_definition`, `authenticated_encryption` and `interface_contract`), with 56 lines naming that worktree. Now `orm_testcase::manifest_dir()` reads the `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR` that cargo test sets for the run and panics when it is unset, and every such line uses it. `scripts/features/coverage.mjs`, which runs Rust test binaries directly, gives each the package directory of its crate as `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR`, as cargo test does. `make repo-check` (`manifestDirErrors` of `scripts/repo/target.mjs`) fails a Rust line outside comments that reads `env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")`; `CARGO_BIN_EXE_*` and `OUT_DIR` stay, since they name the shared target directory itself. Evidence: the new repository case failed before `manifestDirErrors` existed, and `node scripts/repo/check.mjs` then reported the 31 lines; the Rust probe of the coverage runner test, which asserts the runtime `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR`, failed with `left: Err(NotPresent)` and the owner check failed 7 Rust coverage parts with `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR is unset` before the runner gave the value. Now both repository runs pass, the coverage runner test passes, `make rust-fmt-check rust-check` passes with the shared target directory, and `make owner-check` with the 34 changed paths passes its 22 steps.
- [o] G5.23 Cancel the blocked read of `TestWithContextCancelsInsideTransaction` (`clients/go/orm/cancel_test.go`) once the server reports it waiting for the lock, not after a fixed 300 ms. The test expects ROLLBACK because the MySQL and PostgreSQL drivers close the connection of a cancelled statement, but under load the timer fired before the statement reached the server: `database/sql` then returns the cancellation without touching the connection, the rollback succeeds, and the result is CANCELED. That failed `client-db-check` once in an owner check with `blocked read: CANCELED: context canceled (code "CANCELED"), want ROLLBACK` for both PostgreSQL cases, while creating a case database took 15 s. The test now polls a server session of the case database (`performance_schema.data_locks` with `LOCK_STATUS = 'WAITING'` on MySQL, `pg_stat_activity` with `wait_event_type = 'Lock'` on PostgreSQL) and cancels when a session waits; no wait within 10 s fails the test. Evidence: with a 1 s delay before the statement, the old test failed all four cases with that message and the new test passes them; without the delay `go test -run '^TestWithContextCancel' -count=3` passes.
- [o] G5.24 Keep the first report of `start_logged` (`scripts/test-servers.sh`) when a signal interrupts its read. The function waited on a FIFO that it opened anew for every `read`; the end of a background job delivers SIGCHLD, which can interrupt that `read` (EINTR), and the report written after the interrupted `read` closed the FIFO had no reader and was lost. The deadline's `timeout` was then read in place of the server's `exited`, or, when both writers were lost, the function waited forever. That failed `make repo-check` once on its own with `fake logged no line with 'accepting connections' within its deadline of 2 s` where the case expects `fake exited before it logged 'accepting connections'`. The function now opens the FIFO once for reading and writing and keeps it open while it reads, so a report stays in the FIFO's buffer until a later `read` takes it; the timer always writes at the deadline, so the loop ends. Evidence: 72 parallel runs (3 rounds of 24) of the `start_logged` case of `scripts/repo/check.test.mjs` failed 32 times before the change, 28 with the deadline message in place of the exit and 4 with the shell still waiting at the 20 s spawn limit; with the change all 72 pass.
- [o] G5.25 Restore a PostgreSQL sequence of the conformance check by the table that owns it, read from `pg_depend`. `restoreCounters` in `tests/conformance/check/counters.go` took the table from the sequence name, `<table>_seq_seq`, but PostgreSQL truncates a sequence name to 63 bytes, so a long table name gave another name and its sequence was refused as undeclared. `readCounters` now reads, with each sequence, the table that owns it through `pg_depend` (deptype `a` for a serial column, `i` for an identity column), and `restoreCounters` checks that table against the declared write tables; a changed sequence that no table owns is refused with `owned by no table`. Evidence: the new case `TestPhysicalPostgresCounterOwner` (in `conformance-counter-check`) creates, in a case database, a 63-byte table whose serial sequence PostgreSQL names `conformance_counter_owner_xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx_seq_seq`, an identity table `task`, an undeclared table and a free sequence; before the change it failed with `undeclared postgres counter changed: "public"."conformance_counter_owner_xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx_seq_seq"`; now both declared counters are restored to their earlier values, and the undeclared table and the free sequence are refused.
- [o] G5.26 Show `{{` in a documentation page as written, and fail `make docs-build` when the server render of a page fails. Cause: VitePress compiles each page as a Vue template and marks only fenced code `v-pre`, so the inline code `if: ${{ !cancelled() }}` of G5.5 ran as an interpolation; the server render of the checklist page logged `TypeError: _ctx.cancelled is not a function` and, as a production Vue build only logs an unhandled error, wrote `checklist.html` and `ko/checklist.html` without their content while the build passed. The `markdown.config` of `docs/.vitepress/config.mts` now marks inline code and indented code `v-pre`, and wraps Markdown text that holds `{{` in a `v-pre` span; the theme's `enhanceApp` (`docs/.vitepress/theme/index.ts`) sets `app.config.throwUnhandledErrorInProduction` for the server render, so a render error ends `vitepress build` with exit 1. Red: before the change `make docs-build` printed the `TypeError` twice and exited 0, and the built `checklist.html` (21733 bytes) held no `G5.5`. Green: `make docs-build` exits 0 without an error, and `checklist.html` and `ko/checklist.html` hold `<code>if: ${{ !cancelled() }}</code>`; with only the theme change, the unchanged G5.5 made `make docs-build` fail with `build error: TypeError: _ctx.cancelled is not a function` and exit 2; in a scratch copy, an HTML block `<div class="scratch">{{ missing() }}</div>` made it fail with `TypeError: _ctx.missing is not a function`, and the Markdown text `{{ missing() }}` rendered as written.

An unknown `--feature` id of coverage.mjs failed with `unknown feature <id>` and named no valid id. Now every target in `contracts/check-inputs.json` declares `scope`: `owner` (per-file checks: format, checklist, documents, checker unit tests, with `inputs`) or `suite` (only `make check`). `scripts/features/owners.mjs` selects owner targets only and fails before running anything when a target of `CHECK_TARGETS` lacks a scope, an owner target lacks inputs or a suite target declares inputs; `make repo-check` reports the same errors. A change to `contracts/features.json` selects the features whose entry differs from `HEAD`. The make targets and feature steps run as groups without an outer deadline; each case inside keeps its own. The unit tests and the feature-document check moved from `feature-check` to the new owner target `feature-unit-check`, so `make check` still runs each once. AGENTS.md and its Korean pair state the rule: per item only the Red/Green tests of what changed and `make owner-check`; `make check` once when every active item is done, and on push in CI. Red: on main, `node --test scripts/features/owners.test.mjs` failed 5 of 11 cases: the T42-shaped set of nine paths across the four clients selected 30 targets including `client-db-check` and `feature-check` where the case expects `repo-check`, `go-fmt-check`, `rust-fmt-check`; a target without a scope was selected instead of failing; `manifestChanges` did not exist; and the two cases on the declaration shape failed. In `scripts/features/coverage.test.mjs` the unknown-id case failed with `unknown feature missing`; the single-feature case (only the `statement_events` owner specs on three databases) passed on main, since `--feature` already selected one feature. - [o] G5.28 Make the Rust transaction option `timeout_ms` follow its definition in `docs/interfaces.md` and `docs/usage.md`: PostgreSQL applies it as `SET LOCAL statement_timeout`, and MySQL and SQLite return `CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED`. Cause: the Rust client ran the callback of a transaction with `timeout_ms` under a client timer (`tokio::time::timeout`) on every database and, on expiry, returned `CANCELED` `transaction callback timed out` after it closed the session or, on SQLite, rolled back. A client timer does not stop a statement on the server, so the option claimed a bound on MySQL and SQLite that it did not have, and on PostgreSQL the timer raced the server's `statement_timeout`. Go, PHP and TypeScript already follow that definition with the same check before `BEGIN`. `begin` in `clients/rust/orm/src/tx.rs` now returns `CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED` `transaction timeoutMs is supported only by postgres` for a positive `timeout_ms` on MySQL and SQLite before it takes a connection, as Go's `begin` does; the timer and its `cancelled` path are removed from `Transaction` and `SendTransaction`, and the Rust README describes the option as the PostgreSQL statement bound. No shared vector covers transaction options: `tests/events/vectors.json` runs transactions without options. Red: the case `tx::tests::transaction_timeout_is_the_postgres_statement_timeout`, which replaces `callback_timeout_cancels_a_statement_and_rolls_back`, failed on the earlier code with `sqlite: got "CANCELED: transaction callback timed out" with callback run true` and the same for MySQL, where `CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED: transaction timeoutMs is supported only by postgres` without running the callback is expected; in one of two runs PostgreSQL returned the timer's `CANCELED: transaction callback timed out` in place of the server's `canceling statement due to statement timeout (SQLSTATE 57014)`. The `transactions` case of the Rust `integration` program, which now expects `CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED` for `timeout_ms(500)` on MySQL and SQLite, failed on SQLite with `left: "ok"`. Green: the lib case passes on the three databases in four runs, PostgreSQL with the server's 57014 error and the callback run, MySQL and SQLite with `CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED` and the callback not run, and `timeout_ms(0)` commits on all three; `integration schema/bench.dbs --case transactions` passes on SQLite, MySQL and PostgreSQL.

- [o] G5.31 Send every PHP statement on PostgreSQL in one round trip, and set the PHP session time zone as a startup parameter. Cause: `clients/php/src/Db.php` enabled `PDO::ATTR_EMULATE_PREPARES` only for MySQL, so on PostgreSQL pdo_pgsql prepared each new statement text as a named statement in a round trip of its own (Parse and Sync) before Bind and Execute, and PostgreSQL runs that Parse in an implicit transaction of its own. A PHP connection lives for one request, so every statement text of every request paid that round trip and transaction; `Orm::connect` also sent `SET TIME ZONE 'UTC'` on every PostgreSQL connection, a statement without an event. Red: the new `server_transactions` of `tests/events/vectors.json` runs on a PostgreSQL case database in Go (`TestStatementEventsServerTransactions`), PHP and TypeScript (case `server_transactions` of the statement events tests) and Rust (`statement_events_server_transactions`). It requires the connection's TimeZone to be UTC from a startup parameter (`pg_settings` source `client`), reads the backend's local transaction number from `pg_locks` before and after two runs of the steps of `model_statements` (8 statement events, 5 statement texts) on one connection, subtracts the measured cost of the probe, and compares the transactions of a run with its events. Before the fix PHP failed: TimeZone source `session`, first run 13 transactions for 8 events, second run 8. TypeScript spent 8 and 8, Go 13 and 8, Rust 15 and 8. Fix: on PostgreSQL the PHP client sets `Pdo\Pgsql::ATTR_DISABLE_PREPARES`, so pdo_pgsql sends the statement and its binds as one unnamed statement (`PQexecParams`) in one round trip, and `Orm::connect` puts `-c TimeZone=UTC` in the startup `options` beside `statement_timeout`, as Go (pgx runtime parameter `timezone`), Rust and TypeScript (`options`) already do. Emulated prepares were rejected: they write the binds into the SQL text as literals, which changes the server's type inference (a quoted literal is `unknown`, a bare number `integer` or `numeric`) and sends bytea as escaped text. With prepares disabled the binds stay server-side parameters: pdo_pgsql gives `PQexecParams` the same parameter types and formats that it gave `PQprepare`, so the server infers the same types. One statement with bytea (with a NUL byte, `PARAM_LOB`), boolean (`PARAM_BOOL`), numeric text, null, bigint and jsonb binds returned identical values with prepares enabled and disabled, and a bytea bind without a cast failed to infer its type in both modes. Go and Rust keep their first-use prepare: pgx and sqlx describe a new statement text in a round trip of its own to encode their binds in binary by the described parameter types, and sqlx also reads the origin of new result columns from the catalog (Rust 15 = 8 events + 5 prepares + 2 catalog reads); their pool keeps the connection and its statements for later pages, so `server_transactions` lists only PHP and TypeScript in `first_run_clients` and reports the first run of Go and Rust without comparing it. docs/perf.md (F4) and docs/config.md (+ko) state the PostgreSQL behavior. Green: `server_transactions` passes in the four clients with TimeZone `UTC`/`client` and 8 transactions for 8 events in both PHP runs; `php clients/php/tests/statement_events_test.php` passes 25 cases, `php clients/php/tests/model_test.php` 66, `php clients/php/tests/dsn.php` 13; `go run ./tests/conformance/check run -driver postgres` reports 26 vectors × 4 languages identical against the recorded PostgreSQL expectations; `php tests/codec/check.php` passes with the Go, Rust and TypeScript codec outputs of 80 vectors; a PHP connection through PgBouncer reads TimeZone `UTC` and `statement_timeout` `5s`. `make owner-check` with the 19 changed paths selected 10 owner make targets and the verification commands and coverage of `statement_events` and `interface_contract`, and passed all 14 steps.

The sub-items G5.30-1 to G5.30-16 undo the deadlines that G5.29 and earlier items put on long operations, server starts and the long work inside test cases included. For G5.30-11 and G5.30-12, rebased onto G5.32-1, `make owner-check` over their 8 paths selected 7 make targets and passed all 7 (`test-servers-check` 52.9 s, `docs-check` 24.0 s with the new page cases, `docs-verify-idempotent` 21.9 s); `package-declarations.mjs`, which only `ts-check` of the full suite runs, passed when run directly. Change: `cargo update --workspace --offline --manifest-path bench/rust/Cargo.toml` updates only the entries of the workspace members, adding `libsqlite3-sys` (0.37.0, already locked) to the dependencies of `orm` and changing no version.   - [o] G5.32-5 Return the Go PostgreSQL client to the pgx statement cache (revert of G5.32-1). Cause: G5.32-1 compared first runs only. A Go pool keeps its connections beyond a request, so each connection prepares a statement text once and then reuses it with the server plan; only PHP, whose connection lives for one request, runs every statement on a new connection. Measured on PostgreSQL with the bench database and a page of `GetBySeq`, `GetsByServiceSeqAndIsClose`, `GetCountByServiceSeq` and a 10-row list (pool of 4 connections, 4 workers, 800 to 1600 pages after 200 warm-up pages, three rounds): with the pgx `exec` query mode of G5.32-1 the steady-state p50 was 24 to 44 % slower (7.8 to 8.9 ms against 5.5 to 6.3 ms), the backend CPU per page 22 to 31 % higher (2.9 to 3.0 ms against 2.2 to 2.5 ms) and the throughput 20 to 33 % lower (386 to 466 against 572 to 697 pages per second); only the first page on a new connection was 0 to 9 % faster. The same comparison on MySQL (driver-side interpolation against server-side prepares) stayed within the noise of the loaded machine, so the MySQL clients keep their server-side prepares and no MySQL change lands. Change: the client DSN no longer sets `default_query_exec_mode=exec`, and `DB.stmt` and `txConn.stmt` prepare on every database again (`modelStatement` and `unprepared` are removed). The criterion of `server_transactions` is rewritten: a short-lived connection (PHP) sends each statement in one round trip, and a pooled client prepares a statement text once per connection and reuses it; `first_run_clients` lists PHP and TypeScript (`pg` sends the Parse with the first Bind and Execute), `first_run_prepare_clients` lists Go and Rust, whose first run spends one transaction per event plus one per statement text, and the Go test asserts it. docs/perf.md (F1, +ko) records the rule, the first-run and second-run counts of every client (Go 13 / 8, Rust 13 / 8, TypeScript 8 / 8, PHP 8 / 8) and the measurements; docs/config.md (+ko) names the Go client among the clients with named prepared statements again.
