  - [o] G5.32-6 Make the slow statements of the timeout and cancellation tests wait for a row lock that another connection holds. Cause: the tests made a statement slow with a raw condition: `pg_sleep`, `SLEEP`, a self-join of MD5 values on MySQL or a recursive CTE on SQLite (Go `clients/go/orm/cancel_test.go` `TestWithContextCancels` and `timezone_test.go` `TestStatementTimeout`, `TestStatementTimeoutThroughAPooler`; PHP `clients/php/tests/model_test.php` statement timeout and pooler cases; TypeScript `clients/typescript/tests/model.mjs` `statementTimeout`, pooler and `cancellation`; Rust `clients/rust/orm/tests/zone.rs` `statement_timeout`, `statement_timeout_through_a_pooler`, `dropping_a_query_cancels_it`, `dropping_a_transaction_frees_a_single_connection`). Those conditions use the raw API that G5.32 removes and functions that no neutral expression has. Change: a test opens another connection that updates every row of the table in an open transaction (Go `holdRows`, PHP `holdRows` and, for the pooler case, `holdRowsFor` in another process, TypeScript `holdRows`, Rust `HeldRows`); the bounded statement is a locking read in a transaction (`lockedRead`, `locked_read`), which MySQL bounds with `max_execution_time` and PostgreSQL with `statement_timeout`, and the cancelled statement is an update of a held row. On MySQL and PostgreSQL the Go cancellation waits until the server reports the statement waiting for the lock. SQLite waits for a lock in its busy handler, which a cancelled context does not interrupt, so Go cancels the SQLite context before the statement, as the TypeScript test already did. The pooler cases keep the rows held for a declared 400 ms, past the 200 ms bound, to show that the plain connection is not bounded. `internal/testdb.Open` opens a SQLite DSN with the SQLite driver. Evidence: the converted tests pass: Go `./clients/go/orm` (all tests on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite), PHP `model_test.php` 66, TypeScript `model.mjs` 57 and Rust `--test zone` 11; none of them uses the raw API or a sleep function. `make owner-check` with the changed paths passes.

Cause: the conditions that raw SQL served are written with the typed DSL, whose element set is the dbspec `check` predicate set (comparison, `in`, `is null`, `and`, `or`, grouping, columns and typed values) plus `between` and the fixed column and value functions; it had no `not`, so a negated condition needed raw SQL. Change: the IR group has `not` (Go `ir.Group.Not`, PHP, Rust and TypeScript `not`, the `IRGroup` record of `contracts/interfaces.json`); a nested group with `not` renders `NOT (…)` in the four planners, and a top-level `where` or `on` group with `not` is `IR_INVALID`. The models have `not(fn)`, `andNot(fn)` and `orNot(fn)` (Rust `not`, `and_not`, `or_not`): Go through the generator template and `Core.AddNegatedGroup`, PHP and TypeScript in the base model, Rust in `fixed.rs.txt` and `Core::add_negated_group`; the four generators reserve the names and the column name `not`. docs/dsl.md (+ko) adds section 2.1.1, the query expression grammar, which names its relation to the dbspec `check` grammar and its three query-only extensions, and docs/plan.md, usage.md, interfaces.md and interface-implementation.md (+ko) name the negated groups. The planner fixture adds `planner_not_group` and `planner_rejects_top_not`, and the conformance vector `expression_forms` runs `andNot`, a leading `not` and `orNot` in the four runners; its expectations are recorded on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite from the runs whose other 26 vectors matched their recorded expectations. Change: `shape.group` feeds `g.Not`. PHP, TypeScript and Rust key the cache by the serialized IR, which carries `not`. Red: `go test ./clients/go/orm -run TestShapeCoversEveryField` failed with `.Query.Relations[0].Query.Relations[0].Query.Where.Not: same shape key as .Query.Relations[0].Query.Relations[0].Query.Where!` and the same for `On.Not`. Green: the test passes. `make owner-check` with the changed paths passes.

Cause: the raw entry points carried SQL text into the IR (`expr` of a predicate, an output column, an order item and an assignment, and `group_by_expr`), and the planners bound the `?` of that text as slots without a type (`rawParam`), so a plan could not say what every slot binds and the Rust executor bound those values by their own type (`value_type`, `UntypedNull`). The raw API is removed; the typed DSL with the query expression grammar (G5.32-7) writes the conditions. Change: the IR has no SQL text: Go `ir.Expr`, `ir.GroupExpr`, `Query.GroupByExpr`, `Columns.Expr`, `Pred.Expr`, `Order.Expr` and `Assign.Expr`/`Ps` are removed, so a request with those fields is `IR_INVALID` (unknown field), and the PHP `Validator` records, TypeScript `ir.ts`, Rust `ir.rs` and the `IRExpr` and `IRGroupExpr` records and fields of `contracts/interfaces.json` follow. The four planners lose `renderExpr`, the placeholder filling (`fillPlaceholders`, `fill`) and the untyped `rawParam`/`raw_param` slot, and the dialects lose `CurrentTime` and the `$CURRENT_TIME` token that only fragments used. The clients lose `raw`, `andRaw`, `orRaw`, `orderByRaw`, `groupByRaw`, `setRaw<Col>`, `addRawColumn<Alias>` and the format string of `addColumn<Col>Alias<Name>`, which now takes a column function only (Go `orm.Func`, PHP `Func`, TypeScript `ColumnFunction`, Rust `orm::Func`), with their core functions (Go `Core.Raw`, `AddColumnFormat`, `AddRawColumn`, `OrderByRaw`, `GroupByRaw`, `SetRaw`; PHP `Request::raw`; TypeScript `Core.raw`, `addRawColumn`, `addColumnFormat`, now `addColumnFunc`; Rust `Core::raw`, `add_column_format`, `add_raw_column`, `order_by_raw`, `group_by_raw`, `set_raw`, `add_column_as`, `ColumnFormat`, `IntoColumnFormat`, `Binds` and `args::bind`). The four generators neither emit nor reserve those names, and `raw` is no longer a reserved column name; the Go and TypeScript models are regenerated. The Rust executor binds a PostgreSQL value only as its slot's type: `value_type` and `UntypedNull` are removed. The `columns` conformance vector, which added a format column, now adds `addColumnStartDtAliasStartYear(year())`; it showed that the Rust client could not read a MySQL `YEAR` cell, which `YEAR(col)` returns (sqlx's i64 does not accept `YEAR`), so `row.rs` decodes `YEAR` with `year_cell` without that type check. The client tests write `geReadCount(20)`, `seq(x)` and `orderBy<Col>Asc()` for their raw conditions and orders and drop the raw and format columns of the columns-and-subqueries cases (Go `clients/go/model/model_test.go`, PHP `model_test.php`, TypeScript `model.mjs`, `coverage_audit_triggers.mjs`, `dbspec_runtime_db.mjs`, `restore_case.mjs`, `schema-set.mjs`, Rust `tests/src/integration.rs`, `orm-build/tests/generate.rs`). The conformance vector `raw_forms` and its four runner blocks are removed, `columns` is recorded again on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite, and `check run|compare|record` take `-vector NAME` (tests/conformance/README.md, +ko). `contracts/features.json` drops the input `raw_sets`. docs/dsl.md, protocol.md (which also gains the `not` of a group that G5.32-7 left out), dialects.md, plan.md and usage.md (+ko) drop the raw forms.   - [o] G5.32-9 Compare the TimeZone source of `server_transactions` only on a direct connection. Cause: G5.31 made the case require `pg_settings` source `client` for TimeZone, to catch a client that sets the time zone with a SET statement outside the statement events. Through PgBouncer (`client-pooler-check`) the client still sends TimeZone as a startup parameter, but the pooler applies the startup parameters of its clients to its shared server connection with SET, so the source is `session` whatever the client does. The requirement is that the connection runs in UTC without a statement per request: the setting and the transaction counts show it on both paths, and the source shows the startup parameter only where the client reaches the server itself. PgBouncer cannot pass a client parameter to a server connection as a startup parameter (`track_extra_parameters` only adds parameters it applies with SET), so the test servers stay as they are. Change: the case reads the path from the environment (`ORM_TEST_POSTGRES_DSN` differs from `ORM_TEST_POSTGRES_SERVER_DSN` through a pooler) and compares the source only on a direct connection, in Go (`TestStatementEventsServerTransactions`), PHP (`statement_events_run.php`), TypeScript (`statement-events-run.mjs`) and Rust (`statement_events_server_transactions`); the note of `server_transactions` in `tests/events/vectors.json` says so. Red: through `ORM_TEST_PGBOUNCER_DSN` the Go case failed with `TimeZone setting and source = UTC, session, want UTC, client` (13 and 8 transactions, as on the direct path), and the PHP and TypeScript cases on main failed with `TimeZone setting and source = [["UTC","session"]]`. Green: the four cases pass on the direct path and through PgBouncer with the same counts (Go 13 and 8, PHP and TypeScript 8 and 8, Rust passes). `make owner-check` with the changed paths passes.
- [o] G5.33 Keep runs from different checkouts and sessions from breaking each other's shared test resources. A resource that many runs use at once is leased: users hold a shared lease, and an operation that destroys or rebuilds it holds the exclusive lease, which is refused while any lease is held, naming the holders; a resource that one run can use alone has one holder; a dead holder's lease is reported and removed only explicitly. Everything else becomes per run. The sub-items G5.33-1 to G5.33-11 cover the test servers, the bench and decimal databases, the shared Rust target directory with its test binaries and builds outside the Makefile, the remaining run files, the TypeScript build output and its readers, and a race of the lease program. Evidence: `make owner-check` over the 32 paths of the branch, rebased onto G5.32-3, selected 10 make targets; 9 passed (`test-servers-check` 1 m 29 s, `docs-verify-idempotent` 55 s) and `repo-check` failed on the stale `BENCH_SQLITE_DSN` expectation, which the second G5.33-2 commit corrects; `make repo-check` then passes its 54 cases. Evidence of the second round (G5.30-14 and G5.33-5 to G5.33-10): `make owner-check` over the 22 paths of the branch, rebased onto G5.32-5, selected 72 verification commands and 35 coverage parts of 9 features, 1 helper check and 11 make targets with the databases of its run, and passed all 32 steps (the longest `authenticated_encryption/verification` 2 m 5 s); a first run stopped at `dsn-mysql-tls-rust` on the lease race that G5.33-10 corrects, a second one stopped when the disk fell below 3 GB. After the rebase onto G5.32-4, which also changed `scripts/repo/check.mjs`, `node --test scripts/repo/check.test.mjs` passes its 56 cases. The shared bench and decimal resources that no run uses since G5.33-2 were removed from the running servers after checking that no connection or open file used them: the MySQL and PostgreSQL databases `orm_bench` (134 MB and 181 MB) and `orm_decimal_case` (the replicas applied the drops), `.runtime/servers/orm_bench.sqlite` (109 MB), `.runtime/servers/send-savepoint.sqlite`, `.runtime/decimal-env`, `.runtime/decimal-case.sqlite` and `.runtime/decimal-owned`. `orm_bench.sqlite` had last been written at 11:33, before G5.33-2 reached main at 11:46 and before the environment file of the servers was rewritten at 11:47, while that file still named it in `BENCH_SQLITE_DSN`; no orm path names it now, and the header of `scripts/test-servers.sh`, which still described the shared bench database, was corrected. Evidence of the third round (G5.30-15, G5.33-8 and G5.33-9): `make owner-check` over the 28 paths of the branch, rebased onto G5.32-4-1, selected 2 verification commands and 6 coverage parts of 3 features and 10 make targets with the databases of its run, and passed all 18 steps (`owners/make/testcase-check` 1 m 7 s, `owners/interface_contract/coverage` 1 m 5 s). G5.33-11 gives each run's environment its send-savepoint SQLite file; `make owner-check` over the 7 paths of G5.33-11 and G5.34-1 passed its 7 make targets.
  - [o] G5.33-1 Lease the test servers. Cause: the servers of `make test-servers` (`.runtime/servers` on the fixed ports 33171, 55471, 33181, 55481, 33182 and 55482) are used by the runs of every checkout through `TEST_ENV` and by other tools, but nothing recorded who used them: `make test-servers-stop` stopped them and removed `.runtime/servers`, and the MySQL migration of `scripts/test-servers-mysql.mjs` inside `make test-servers` stopped MySQL and moved its data, while other runs were in the middle of their cases. Now the new Go command `tests/lease` (`.runtime/bin/lease`, built by the Makefile as `$(RUN_LONG) go-build/lease`) keeps the leases of a directory as files written atomically (rename for shared, link for exclusive) with the holder's checkout, process id, start time and command; `hold` keeps a lease until a given process exits, observed through `internal/procevent` (the exit watch of G5.30-13-1, moved there, and a directory watch: kqueue on darwin, inotify on linux), `run` keeps one while a command runs and with `--wait` waits on directory change events instead of refusing, `list` names the holders and `clear-dead` removes the leases whose process is gone and names them; a dead lease is never taken over, and `--wait` refuses at once when one blocks. `scripts/test-servers.sh` writes `ORM_TEST_SERVERS_LEASES` (`.runtime/servers.leases`) into the environment file; `WITH_TEST_ENV`, which every make line that reads `TEST_ENV` starts with (and now `make check` and `make owner-check`), holds a shared lease for the life of its line, and fails naming the fix when the environment file has no lease directory; `stop`, a fresh `start` and the MySQL migration (before its first change, also when resumed) hold the exclusive lease and fail with exit code 3 naming each holder. `make test-servers-leases` and `make test-servers-leases-clear` list the leases and remove the dead ones. CONTRIBUTING.md and its Korean pair describe the leases. The running servers were not restarted; their environment file gets the lease directory on the next `make test-servers`, which keeps the running servers. Red: the new cases over the scripts of main: `stopping the test servers is refused while another run holds a lease, naming the holder` of `scripts/test-servers.test.mjs` failed because the stop printed `test-servers: removed .../.runtime/servers` while the lease was held, and `the MySQL migration is refused while another run holds a lease of the servers` of `scripts/test-servers-mysql.test.mjs` failed because the migration of a throwaway MySQL fixture ran all its steps; a make line of main's Makefile that read `TEST_ENV` and slept 6 s left an exclusive lease free (`exclusive-ran-during-main-run`). Green: both cases pass (113 ms; 15.2 s with the MySQL fixture on ports 39305 and 39306, its marker still `lower_case_table_names=2` and no `.runtime/mysql-migration`), the stop case also stops the servers once the holder ends; during the same make line of this Makefile, `lease run <servers.leases> exclusive` was refused with exit 3 naming `shared lease of pid ... (running) from .../orm-shared-resources-G5.33 since ...: /bin/sh -c ... sleep 6`, and the lease was gone after the line; `make dialect-facts-check` passes with a lease, and an environment file without the lease directory fails with `names no lease directory; run make test-servers to rewrite it`; `node scripts/repo/check.mjs` passes.

Cause: every checkout builds into the one `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` (the disk is tight, so it stays shared), and six make targets built a program there and then ran it from there: `dbspec-apply-pairs-check` (`debug/examples/dbspec_apply`), `dbspec-introspect-compare-check` (`dbspec_introspect`), `dbspec-compare-check` (`dbspec_compare`, `dbspec_stress`, `dbspec_apply`, through `tests/cargo-target.mjs`), `dbspec-stress-bench` and `timing-check` (`release/examples/dbspec_stress`), `example-check` (`debug/complex`, `debug/demo`) and `case-database-check` (`debug/integration`); between the build and the run another checkout could rebuild the program, so the run executed the other checkout's code. Now `summarize` derives `failed` (targets whose status is failed) and `incomplete` (running or not run) from the targets each time the record is written, the first record starts with `incomplete` holding every target and `failed` empty, a finished run fails when either list is not empty, and the refusal and the final line name both lists. `make rerun-failed` still selects every target that did not pass, which is the failed and the unfinished ones; a record without `incomplete`, as older ones are, is still read. Green: that case passes with `failed` `['b']`, `incomplete` `['c']` and a rerun of exactly `b` and `c`; `summarize separates failed from unfinished targets` passes, and `node --test scripts/check/full-run.test.mjs` passes its 11 cases.
  - [o] G5.34-2 Let `make rerun-failed` run on a commit that descends from the recorded one. Cause: `scripts/check/full-run.mjs` allowed a rerun only on the tree of the recorded full run, so a failure of the code found by the full run of G5.32-9 and fixed by a later commit could only get a new full suite; the targets that had passed would run again for a change that does not touch them. Now a rerun is allowed when the current commit is of the recorded tree or descends from the recorded commit, which is the commit of the last rerun or else of the full run (`git merge-base --is-ancestor`). It reruns the targets that did not pass (failed and unfinished) and the owner targets that the paths changed since that commit (`git diff --name-only`) select in `contracts/check-inputs.json`, with the selection of `make owner-check` (`selectTargets` of `scripts/features/owners.mjs`), and prints each owner target with its paths. It sets the selected targets back to `not run` before it starts, so a killed rerun leaves them unfinished, and records its commit, tree, base commit and owner targets, so the next rerun counts changes from it. A commit that does not descend is refused with both commits; every other refusal (items in progress, uncommitted changes, a running run) stays. `AGENTS.md` and the Makefile comment describe the new rule. Red: the three new cases of `scripts/check/full-run.test.mjs` failed with the previous guard, the descendant decision with `the recorded full run is of tree t1, not of the current tree t2`, and the rerun on a new commit of the stub checkout with `full-run: refused make rerun-failed`. Green: all 14 cases of the file pass, including a killed rerun whose owner target `c` stays `not run` and is rerun, a later rerun with nothing to rerun, and a checkout of the earlier commit that is refused.
- [o] G5.36 Keep PHP connections across the requests of a php-fpm worker, and report a lost connection with one code in every client. Cause: `Orm::connect` (`clients/php/src/Orm.php`) built a new PDO for every `Db`, and `Config` refused `poolIdleSize` and `poolLifetimeMs` because "the PHP client has none", so every request opened a server session with startup and authentication: 956 PHP page requests on PostgreSQL opened 956 server sessions, where the Node client used 20 pooled sessions. A connection that the server ended (a killed session, a restart, an idle limit) failed with `DRIVER` in every client, so no rule could tell it from another driver error without reading the message. Change: `poolSize` keeps its meaning of the other clients, the most connections a process holds for a database at once, and gives PHP a pool in the process (`clients/php/src/Connection.php`): a `Db` takes the first free slot of the pool of its DSN and connection settings, the first `poolIdleSize` slots (all when zero) are PDO persistent connections that stay in the process for the `Db` of a later request, a later slot closes with its `Db`, `close()` and the end of the `Db` return the slot, and a `Db` that finds every slot held by an open `Db` of the process gets `CONFIG`, because a PHP request cannot wait for itself; `poolSize` zero keeps one connection per `Db`, and `poolLifetimeMs` other than zero stays `CONFIG`, because PDO cannot close a kept connection. `poolSize: 1` is one connection per worker. The session settings are set only when a connection opens: PostgreSQL startup parameters as before, and MySQL `SET time_zone = '+00:00', SESSION max_execution_time = <ms>` as the init command of the connection instead of statements after the connect; SQLite runs its pragmas on every connect, in process. A request that ends inside a transaction (`exit`, a fatal error) has the transaction ended by a shutdown function that `Db::begin` registers once per request: the MySQL named locks are released, the MySQL local values cleared, the SQLite modes reset and the transaction rolled back. The new driver code `CONNECTION_LOST` (`docs/errors.yaml`, generated into `Code.php`, `codes.go` and `codes.rs`) names a connection the server ended or that broke: MySQL 2006, 2013 and 4031, PostgreSQL SQLSTATE class 08 and 57P01, 57P02 and 57P05, and the driver's error for a connection that is no longer open: pdo_pgsql's error without a server SQLSTATE on a connection whose backend pid is 0 (`OrmException::fromDriver` now takes the PDO), go-sql-driver `ErrInvalidConn`, `driver.ErrBadConn` and pgx `ErrConnClosed` in Go, an I/O error of end of stream, reset, abort, broken pipe or not connected in Rust, a fatal mysql2 error with a code of a lost connection or none, and the statement error of a node-postgres session that reported a connection error, in TypeScript. No client matches a message text for it. The PHP client sends a statement once more on a new connection when it fails with `CONNECTION_LOST` and no statement of the current `Db` has run on that connection (`Db::send`); a statement sent through the PDO handle of the `Db` counts as run, each attempt is a statement event, and every other error is reported unchanged. Red: `clients/php/tests/pool_test.php` (new) starts a PHP built-in server, one process that handles requests one after the other and starts the PHP state of each anew as a php-fpm worker does, with the router `pool_request.php`, and runs on a case database through the server DSNs. On the client of G5.32-9 (with only the generated `Code.php` of this item) it failed every case but `pool_request_end/sqlite`: `pool_reuse` mysql "the two requests use one session: 65554 and 65556", postgres 60596 and 60600, sqlite total_changes 0 in the second request; `pool_request_end` mysql and postgres on two sessions; `pool_lost_between_requests` found no session left to end; `pool_bounds` got `CONFIG: poolIdleSize and poolLifetimeMs configure a connection pool, and the PHP client has none`; `resend_first_statement` mysql `DRIVER: ... 2006 MySQL server has gone away` and postgres `DRIVER: ... 7 FATAL:  terminating connection due to administrator command`; `no_resend_after_statement` `[null,"DRIVER"]`. With the pool but without the request-end shutdown function, `pool_request_end` failed on mysql ("the local value of the ended transaction is cleared: 'left'", "the named lock ... is released: 65531") and sqlite (`READ_ONLY ... attempt to write a readonly database` after a read-only transaction that the request left), and passed on postgres, whose transaction-local values and advisory locks end with the transaction. The `rollback_failed` case of the rollback tests now also requires `CONNECTION_LOST` for the callback and the rollback error on MySQL and PostgreSQL; before the mapping it failed in every client: PHP `DRIVER: ... 2006 MySQL server has gone away` and `DRIVER: ... 7 no connection to the server`, Go `DRIVER: invalid connection`, `DRIVER: FATAL: terminating connection due to administrator command (SQLSTATE 57P01)` and `DRIVER: driver: bad connection`, Rust `DRIVER: error communicating with database: peer closed connection without sending TLS close_notify` and `DRIVER: terminating connection due to administrator command (SQLSTATE 57P01)`, TypeScript `DRIVER: mysql: Can't add new command when connection is in closed state` and `DRIVER: postgres: Client has encountered a connection error and is not queryable`. Green: `php clients/php/tests/pool_test.php` passes its 15 cases: `pool_bounds` (sqlite, mysql, postgres: a third `Db` of a pool of two is `CONFIG`, the first connection stays for the next `Db`, the one beyond the idle size closes with its `Db`, `close()` returns it), `pool_reuse` (one session for two requests, and in both the session settings of the connect: PostgreSQL `TimeZone` `UTC` and `statement_timeout` `4321` from the source `client`, MySQL `time_zone` `+00:00` and `max_execution_time` 4321, SQLite `foreign_keys` 1 and `busy_timeout` 5000), `pool_request_end` (same session, the left row rolled back, no MySQL local value or named lock left, a write after an ended read-only transaction), `pool_lost_between_requests` (mysql, postgres: a new session after the test ended the old one), `resend_first_statement` (mysql, postgres: events `[CONNECTION_LOST, null]` for the same statement and its result) and `no_resend_after_statement` (`CONNECTION_LOST` after one event `[null, CONNECTION_LOST]`). The rollback tests pass: PHP `rollback_test.php` 9 cases, Go `^Test(Savepoint)?RollbackFailed` 6, Rust `--test rollback rollback_failed` 6, TypeScript `rollback.mjs` 9, and TypeScript `idle-connection-end.mjs` 2. The new verification commands `pool-php` of `dsn_connection` and `rollback-go`, `rollback-php`, `rollback-rust` and `rollback-typescript` of `transactions` run these tests; `go run ./tests/interfaces/check -language` php, typescript and go pass after the PHP and TypeScript symbol snapshots were recorded. `make owner-check` over the 36 changed paths selected 21 verification commands and 14 coverage parts of 6 features and 10 make targets and passed its 21 steps; its first run failed `docs-rules-check` (Korean words that `docs.writing-style` forbids) and `queries-php` and `chunking-php`, whose `model_test.php` cases built a `Db` from a test PDO, which now passes through `Connection::fromPdo`.
- [o] G5.37 Let addTablesAndColumns create the missing indexes of existing tables, and refuse a missing unique key with its reason. Cause: the step functions of the four clients (`dbspec.AddTablesAndColumnsSteps` of `engine/dbspec/add_tables_and_columns.go` for Go, `Dbspec::addTablesAndColumnsSteps`, `add_tables_and_columns_steps` of `orm-schema`, `addTablesAndColumnsSteps` of the TypeScript `dbspec`) accepted only `create_table` and the `add_column` of a column that is null or has a default and returned every other difference as `SCHEMA_DIFFERS`, so a set that adds an index to an existing table could not be upgraded: adding `index ix_audit_request (request_id)` to a table `audit` of an installed set failed on every database with `SCHEMA_DIFFERS: ... add_index audit.ix_audit_request`. An index (`index`, which dbspec keeps apart from `unique`) refuses no row, so creating it cannot fail on the rows a table holds; a unique key can. Change: the four step functions accept `add_index` and plan it with the tables and columns in one plan (`CREATE INDEX`), and return the created index as `table.index` after the added columns of its table; a missing unique key stays a difference, now named `add_unique <table>.<name>: a missing unique key can fail on the existing rows; add it with a plan`, and the `SCHEMA_DIFFERS` message of the four clients says the set differs "beyond missing tables, missing columns that are null or have a default and missing indexes". docs/schema.md, usage.md, interfaces.md (en/ko), `docs/errors.yaml` with the generated `Code.php`, `codes.go` and `codes.rs`, the `schema_install` entry of `contracts/features.json` with docs/features.md, and the PHP and Rust READMEs state the rule. Red: the new case `add_tables_and_columns_index` of the four addTablesAndColumns tests installs version 1 of `contracts/fixtures/add_tables_and_columns` and applies `unique.dbs` and then `index.dbs` (a nullable column and `ix_addcol_item_label`); with the step functions of G5.33-13 it failed on SQLite, MySQL and PostgreSQL in every client: the unique key was named only `add_unique addcol_item.uq_addcol_item_label`, and the index set failed with `SCHEMA_DIFFERS: ... add_index addcol_item.ix_addcol_item_label` (PHP 3 failed cases, Go `TestAddTablesAndColumnsIndex{SQLite,MySQL,Postgres}`, TypeScript 3, Rust `add_tables_and_columns_index` on sqlite first). Green: the case passes on the three databases in every client: the missing unique key is `SCHEMA_DIFFERS` with the reason, the index set returns `[addcol_item.note, addcol_item.ix_addcol_item_label, addcol_item_history.note]`, a second call returns `[]`, and `install` of the index set then changes nothing, so the index exists as declared; `index` left the list of refused fixtures. The whole addTablesAndColumns tests pass: PHP `add_tables_and_columns_test.php` 15 cases, Go `^TestAddTablesAndColumns` 15, TypeScript `add-tables-and-columns.mjs` 15, Rust `--test add_tables_and_columns` 5 cases over the three databases each. `make owner-check` over the 33 changed paths of G5.37 and G5.37-1 selected 11 verification commands and 8 coverage parts of 2 features and 11 make targets and passed its 17 steps, `test-servers-check` among them (1 m 19 s).
  - [o] G5.37-1 Make the binary logs of the test MySQL servers expire. Cause: `scripts/test-servers.sh` started both mysqld with the compiled binary log retention, 30 days (`binlog_expire_logs_seconds` 2592000, source `COMPILED`) and `max_binlog_size` 1 GB; the expiry of 3600 s set earlier with `SET PERSIST` did not survive the rebuild of the data directories, and an expiry alone does not act, because the server removes expired logs only when it starts and when it rotates the log. Seven files of about 1.1 GB each had piled up on the primary and the replica, and the shared disk fell to 2 GB free. Change: `MYSQLD_BINLOG` (`--binlog-expire-logs-seconds=3600 --max-binlog-size=67108864`) is passed to both mysqld, so the log rotates every 64 MiB and the server removes a log an hour after its last write; the running shared servers are not restarted, and the next `make test-servers` applies it. Red: the new case `the test MySQL server removes an expired binary log by itself` of `scripts/test-servers.test.mjs` starts a fixture mysqld in a temporary data directory on a free port, with the retention options that the script declares scaled to an expiry of 2 s and a size of 4096 bytes, writes ten rows, waits 3 s and writes ten more; with the script of G5.33-13 it failed: `binlog.000001 (13196 bytes, last written more than 3 s ago) is kept past binlog_expire_logs_seconds=2: the server removes expired logs only when it rotates the log, and max_binlog_size=1073741824 was never reached; scripts/test-servers.sh sets no --binlog-expire-logs-seconds; scripts/test-servers.sh sets no --max-binlog-size`. Green: the case passes: the server removed `binlog.000001` itself, and both mysqld of the script take `$MYSQLD_BINLOG`.
- [o] G5.39 Make a docs page that does not load say why. Cause: in the full `make check` on G5.37, `docs-check` failed `docs-static/page/examples/complex-query.html` after 15.009 s with only `page.goto: Timeout 15000ms exceeded ... waiting until "load"`, while every other page loaded in about 100 ms; the case named no request, error or measurement, so the failure could not be explained from the log. The page has the same resources as every other page (the stylesheets, `vp-icons.css`, the preloaded font `inter-roman-latin`, `favicon.svg`; no image, frame or external resource of its own) and the build and the local server have no defect that the evidence shows. Reproduction with the same static context on the built site, 60 rounds of the 48 pages while the host load average was about 165, stalled three pages in a row once: `ko/dsl.html` with the font and `favicon.svg` pending since +60 ms, then the main documents of `ko/examples/complex-query.html` and `ko/feature-coverage.html` pending for 15 s each, so the whole path between the browser and the local server stopped for about 45 s rather than one resource of one page. A second reproduction with a request log in the server, 150 rounds while the load fell from 168 to 25, stalled none, and this branch's `npm run docs:check` loaded every page in 54 to 72 ms for the two complex-query pages. Now every page opening of the docs check (each static page, the search and the theme and mobile cases, and the mobile reload) runs through `scripts/docs/load.mjs`, which on failure adds to Playwright's message every pending request with its resource type, URL, the time it was sent and its age, and whether the local server received it and when it answered (the server of `scripts/docs/lib.mjs` now keeps a request log), every failed request with its HTTP status or browser reason, the console errors and page errors, the longest and 99th percentile event loop delay of the process that runs the local server, and the host load average with the CPU count. The per-page Playwright timeout of 15 s and the case deadline stay. Red: the new case of `scripts/docs/load.test.mjs` (a page with a stylesheet the server never answers, a missing image and a script that logs an error and throws) got only `page.waitForLoadState: Timeout 1000ms exceeded.` from the previous loading. Green: it gets the pending stylesheet with the server's view, `HTTP 404 image .../missing.png`, both console errors, the page error, the event loop delay and the host load, in every run; `npm run docs:check` passes with all 48 pages, links, search and theme and mobile.
- [o] G5.38 Run the full suite on GitHub CI after a push, never stop on a failure, and leave enough information to fix every failure before the next CI run. Cause: the full suite ran locally before a push, and a run could end early or silently: `scripts/check/run.mjs` skipped every target, unrecorded, when `databases/create` failed; a target recipe stopped at its first failing line, and scripts and runners inside targets stopped at their first failing command or test binary; the output of a target was only printed, so a failure on CI could be read only in the scrolling job log, and nothing collected the run directories, the environment or a summary. Acceptance: the full suite runs on CI after a push; every target runs unless a setup step it needs failed, which records it as `not-run` with the failed step and its first failure lines; inside a target, independent parts run after a failed one; each failure is recorded with its exact command, its inputs, its output with the expected and the actual value, the cases that did not finish and their last step, and the environment of the run; the run ends with a summary of every target's status, time and first failure lines, which CI publishes as the job summary and uploads with the report of that run id as an artifact, also when the runner crashes; the job fails when a target failed or did not run. Locally only the Red/Green tests and the owner check of the changed paths run. The sub-items G5.38-1 to G5.38-5 each carry one part. Evidence: `make owner-check` over the 29 paths that the five sub-items change selected 9 make targets and passed all of them: `checklist-check`, `full-run-check` (the 16 runner cases, the 2 recipe-part cases and the 2 continuation cases of `scripts/check/continue.test.mjs`), `version-check`, `testcase-check` (its five parts, `tests/cargo-test.test.mjs` among them), `repo-check`, `docs-rules-check`, `docs-check`, `docs-verify-idempotent` and `feature-unit-check`. Neither `make check` nor `make rerun-failed` ran: the first CI run after a push is the first full run under these rules.
  - [o] G5.38-1 Record a target that a failed setup step blocks as `not-run`, run the others, and write a report of every run. Cause: `runChecks` ran the targets only when `databases/create` passed, so a failed database setup left every target, including those that read no database, unrun and unrecorded; it printed the output of a target and kept no file of it, and the run directory of a failed target, the environment and a summary were nowhere. Change: the runner runs two setup steps, `servers` (it reads the server environment file itself, `export NAME='value'` lines, and holds the shared lease of the servers, in place of `$(WITH_TEST_ENV)` in the recipes of `check`, `rerun-failed` and `run-databases`) and `databases/create`; every target of `contracts/check-inputs.json` declares `needs` (`["databases"]` for the 26 targets that read the servers and the run's databases, `[]` for the 23 others), `checkInputErrors` of `scripts/features/owners.mjs` rejects a missing or unknown one, and a target whose setup step failed is recorded `not-run` with `the setup step <step> failed: <its first failure lines>` while the other targets run; the record calls a step that has not started `pending`. Each target runs as `make -k`. `scripts/check/report.mjs` writes the report `.runtime/check/<run id>/report` of the run, whose id is `ORM_CHECK_RUN_ID` (CI gives the run id and attempt) or a new name: `environment.txt` (commit, tree, OS, Node, Go, PHP, cargo, mysqld, postgres, disk), `targets/<target>.log` (the exact make command, the recipe of the Makefile, the declared inputs and the whole output), `targets/<target>/run/` (the run directory `.runtime/run/<target>-<make pid>` that a failed target left, without build targets, node_modules, database files and binary files, which `MANIFEST.txt` lists with their sizes), and `summary.md`. The record of each target keeps its log path, time and first failure lines: the `FAIL`, `--- FAIL`, `not ok` and panic lines, then each case that started and never ended, named with its last step, so a case stopped by its deadline or by a dead process says what was pending; a target whose cases report no failure keeps the last lines of its output and the way its command ended. A log or kept file larger than 1 MiB keeps its last 256 KiB, which its first line and the summary state. `scripts/check/summary.mjs` (the CI summary step) writes the summary of `ORM_CHECK_RUN_ID` to `GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` and the report; when the runner did not finish it says so and lists the steps as last recorded, and when the record has no such run it says that make check stopped before the runner recorded it. Red: with the runner of G5.37, the new case `a failed setup step blocks only the targets that need it, and the report explains every failure` of `scripts/check/full-run.test.mjs` (a stub suite of four targets, a and c needing databases, a failing database setup, b failing with `rows of t: expected 3, actual 5` and a case that does not finish, d passing) ran `['sh create', 'sh drop']` and none of the targets; the crash case could not find `scripts/check/summary.mjs`; the recipe case found `$(WITH_TEST_ENV)` in `check`; and `checkInputErrors` accepted a target without `needs` and one with `needs: ['servers']`. Green: the setup case runs `sh create`, b, d and `sh drop`, records a and c `not-run` with `the setup step databases/create failed: ... Access denied for user ...`, b `failed` with its FAIL line and `case b/pending did not finish; its last step: STEP b/pending elapsed=1ms: waiting for the lock of t`, and d passed, exits 1, and writes `environment.txt`, `targets/b.log` with the command and the output, the kept run directory with `big.log` as `[the last 262144 of 2097156 bytes of ...]`, and `summary.md` with every row, which the summary step also writes to `GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`; the crash case, killed in target c, publishes `result **crashed**` and `The runner did not finish: the runner process <pid> ended without recording the end of run ci_99_2` with b failed and c running, and a run without a record says `make check recorded no run ci_77_1`. `node --test scripts/check/full-run.test.mjs` passes its 16 cases and `node --test scripts/features/owners.test.mjs` its 15.
  - [o] G5.38-2 Run the independent commands of a target as parts that make -k runs after a failed one. Cause: make stops a recipe at its first failing line, and 13 targets of `CHECK_TARGETS` held commands that read nothing of each other in one recipe: the unit tests and the check of `checklist-check`, `version-check`, `repo-check` and `git-check`, the five runners of `testcase-check`, the type check and the tests of `ts-check`, the four cargo runs of `rust-check`, the three workspaces of `rust-fmt-check`, the two fuzz runs of `fuzz-check`, the probe and the physical tests of `dialect-facts-check`, the three checks of `feature-unit-check`, the coverage and the verification commands of `feature-check`, and the seven PHP tests of `client-unit-check`, which one `&&` chain joined; a failure of the first hid the result of the others until the next run. The other targets of more than one line build or prepare what their next line reads, so their order stays. Change: each of the 13 targets lists its parts as prerequisites, `<target>/<part>`, with one command each (40 parts, declared `.PHONY`); a part that needs a lease or a build (`ts-check/hold`, `feature-check/build`, `lease-tool`, `rust-fetch`) has it as its own prerequisite, so a failed setup part stops only the parts that need it; the runner (G5.38-1) and `make owner-check` (`makeArguments` of `scripts/features/owners.mjs`) run a target with `-k`. Red: with the Makefile of G5.38-1, the new case `a target of independent commands runs each as a part of its own` of `scripts/check/full-run.test.mjs` listed every one of the 13 targets with its commands in one recipe (`checklist-check: prerequisites checklist-check: instead of checklist-check/unit checklist-check/run` and so on), the case `the parts of a target after a failed part still run under make -k`, which runs the definition of `checklist-check` in a temporary Makefile with a `node` that fails the unit tests, ran only `--test scripts/checklist/check.test.mjs`, and `makeArguments` gave no `-k`. Green: the structure case passes for the 13 targets, the make -k case runs both parts and ends with `make --no-print-directory -k checklist-check exited with 2`, and `node --test scripts/check/full-run.test.mjs` passes its 18 cases and `node --test scripts/features/owners.test.mjs` its 15; `make -n -k checklist-check client-unit-check ts-check` lists the commands of every part.
  - [o] G5.38-3 Run the independent commands of the test scripts after a failed one. Cause: the scripts inside the targets stopped at their first failure: `scripts/client-db-test.sh` (`client-db-check`, `client-pooler-check`) ran each lane under `set -eu`, so a failing test file ended its lane before the other 10 PHP test files, the second Go run, the Rust integration program and the TypeScript tests; `scripts/typescript/sqlite-test.sh` did the same with its nine TypeScript tests; and `tests/cargo-test.mjs`, which runs the test binaries of every `cargo test` of the checks, returned at the first failing binary. No other runner stops at its first failure: `node --test`, `tests/go-test.mjs` (`go test` without `-failfast`), the PHP and TypeScript test scripts and the Rust cases continue after a failed case. Change: the lanes and `sqlite-test.sh` run each test through `keep`, which runs the command, adds it with its exit status to `FAILED` when it fails and goes on; each lane ends with `finish`, which prints `client-db-test: the <lane> lane failed:` and every failed command and exits 1; a build that the next command reads (the Rust integration program, the TypeScript build) still stops what needs it, and the Rust lane records a failed integration build as `the build of the integration program (exit <n>), so the integration program did not run`; `tests/cargo-test.mjs` runs every binary as `cargo test --no-fail-fast` does, prints `cargo-test: <n> of <m> test binaries failed: <target> (exit <code>), ...` and exits with the first failing code. The commands stay one per line, so `make repo-check` still finds every test file they run. The new cases run in `full-run-check`, whose inputs now include both scripts. Red: with the scripts of G5.38-2, the cases of the new `scripts/check/continue.test.mjs`, which put a `php` or `node` that fails the first test in front of PATH, ran 1 of 11 PHP tests and 2 of 10 TypeScript commands (the build and `model.mjs`), and the new case `cargo-test runs every test binary after a failed one and names the failures` of `tests/cargo-test.test.mjs` (a fake cargo and lease, three binaries, the second failing with exit 4) ran `one` and `two` only. Green: the PHP lane runs all 11 and prints `client-db-test: the php lane failed:` with `php clients/php/tests/model_test.php (exit 3)`, the TypeScript script runs all 10 and names `node clients/typescript/tests/model.mjs (exit 3)`, cargo-test runs the three binaries and prints `cargo-test: 1 of 3 test binaries failed: two (exit 4)` and exits 4; `node --test scripts/check/continue.test.mjs` passes its 2 cases and `node --test tests/cargo-test.test.mjs` its 4.
  - [o] G5.38-4 Let CI run make check after a failed server start, publish its summary and upload the report of that run id alone. Cause: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` stopped the job when `make test-servers` failed, so no target ran, not even those that need no server; after `make check` it published nothing, so a failure could be read only in the job log, and `ciAfterCheckErrors` of `scripts/repo/ci.mjs` rejected every command step after `make check` but accepted any action step there. Change: the `database servers` step has `continue-on-error: true`, so `make check` runs and records the targets that need the servers as `not-run` with the failure (G5.38-1); `make check` gets `ORM_CHECK_RUN_ID: ${{ github.run_id }}-${{ github.run_attempt }}`; after it, two `if: always()` steps follow: `summary` (`node scripts/check/summary.mjs`, which writes the summary of that run id, also of a runner that did not finish, to `GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` and to the report, and copies the run record as `record.json` and the logs of the test servers, `servers/*.log` without their data directories, into the report) and `report` (`actions/upload-artifact@v4`, artifact `check-<run id>-<attempt>`, path `.runtime/check/ci_<run id>_<attempt>/report/` only, `if-no-files-found: error`), so no file of another run is collected and no database data directory or build target is uploaded. The job fails when `make check` fails, that is when a target failed or did not run. `ciAfterCheckErrors` now requires the `ORM_CHECK_RUN_ID` of the make check step and exactly the declared `summary` and `report` steps after it (`AFTER_CHECK`), and names every other step, a changed upload path and a missing step. `full-run-check` holds the TypeScript build output with `$(READ_TYPESCRIPT)`, because its new test runs `scripts/typescript/sqlite-test.sh`, which `make repo-check` counts as a reader of it. Red: with `scripts/repo/ci.mjs` of G5.38-3, the new case `a workflow runs only the summary and the report upload after make check` of `scripts/repo/check.test.mjs` failed on the minimal workflow with the declared steps: `ci.yml step "summary" runs node scripts/check/summary.mjs after make check`; the previous case had allowed any action step after make check; and with `scripts/check/summary.mjs` of G5.38-3 the new case `the summary step keeps the record and the server logs in the report of the run` found no `record.json` in the report. Green: the repository workflow and the minimal one pass; a fuzz step, a `actions/cache` step, an upload of `.runtime/check/ci_<id>_<attempt>/` instead of its `report/`, a workflow without the two steps and a make check without `ORM_CHECK_RUN_ID` each return their error; the report holds `record.json` equal to the record, `servers/postgres.log` whole, `servers/mysql.log` as `[the last 262144 of 2097169 bytes of ...]` and no `servers/mysql` data directory. `node --test scripts/repo/check.test.mjs` passes its 59 cases, `node --test scripts/check/full-run.test.mjs scripts/check/continue.test.mjs` 21 and `node scripts/repo/check.mjs` passes.
  - [o] G5.38-5 State in AGENTS.md and AGENTS.ko.md that the full suite runs on CI after a push. Cause: AGENTS.md said that the full suite runs exactly once, locally, when every active item is complete, and that one then runs `make check` once before a push; the full suite now runs on CI, and one CI run must collect enough information to fix every failure it found before the next CI run. Change: the rule now reads, in both languages: the full suite runs on GitHub CI after a push; it never stops at a failure and completes the suite; each failure records its inputs, the exact command, its output, the expected against the actual value and the environment facts relevant to it, so it can be diagnosed without running it again locally, and a failing case states what failed, where and why; the run ends with the summary that CI publishes and uploads with the report of the run id, and the job fails when a target failed or did not run; a local `make check` remains possible but is no required step before a push; a push needs the owner's approval each time and happens only when no item is in progress; a CI failure is fixed as a checklist item, and no push follows a CI run in progress to react to it. The guard text of `make check` in AGENTS keeps its refusals and drops the instruction to run `make check` once locally; the comments of the `check` recipe and of `scripts/check/full-run.mjs` say where the full suite runs. `fullSuiteRuleErrors` of `scripts/repo/ci.mjs`, which `make repo-check` runs, requires the rule in both files and rejects the previous one. Red: the previous AGENTS.md and AGENTS.ko.md gave 12 errors, among them `AGENTS.md does not state the full-suite rule: "runs on GitHub CI after a push"` and `AGENTS.md still states the local full-suite rule: "runs exactly once, when every active checklist   item is complete"`. Green: both files pass, the case `AGENTS states that the full suite runs on CI after a push` of `scripts/repo/check.test.mjs` passes, and `node scripts/repo/check.mjs` passes.
- [o] G5.40 Count a test as a reader of the TypeScript build output only when it really reads it, and drop the lease that the wrong count asked for. Cause: `usesTypescriptOutput` of `scripts/repo/testcases.mjs`, which `typescriptReaderErrors` (`make repo-check`) follows, took every `clients/typescript/...mjs` path on a line with the word `node` for a file that the test runs; `scripts/check/continue.test.mjs` names `node clients/typescript/tests/model.mjs` only as the expected output of a fake `node`, so the rule counted it as a reader, and G5.38-3 added `$(READ_TYPESCRIPT)` to `full-run-check`, which serialized that target against the TypeScript builds for nothing. The same rule missed real reads: an import of `../dist/` whose name list spans lines (`clients/typescript/tests/model.mjs`), and a run through `process.execPath`. Change: a JavaScript file runs a TypeScript test only in the two forms that execute it (`nodeRuns`): a program and its argument array (`'node', ['<file>', ...]` or `process.execPath, ['<file>', ...]`), and the shell string of an `exec`, `execSync`, `spawn`, `spawnSync`, `execFile` or `execFileSync` call (`'node [options] <file>'`); a path named in an expected output, a stub, a regular expression or a comment is no run. An import of the build output is read also when its `{ ... }` name list spans lines; only names, `as`, commas and whitespace may stand in it, so a function body followed by a comment that quotes an import is no import. `full-run-check` loses `$(READ_TYPESCRIPT)` and its `lease-tool` prerequisite. No path is exempted. Red: with the rule of G5.38-5, the new case `a test that only names a TypeScript test path does not read the build output` of `scripts/repo/check.test.mjs` reported `scripts/names.test.mjs` (which names the path in an assertion, a regular expression and a stub) as a reader and did not report `scripts/exec-path.mjs` (`spawnSync(process.execPath, [...])`) and `scripts/multi.mjs` (a test with a multi-line import of `../dist/index.js`). Green: the case reports exactly `scripts/array.mjs`, `scripts/exec-path.mjs`, `scripts/shell.mjs` and `scripts/multi.mjs`, and neither `scripts/names.test.mjs` nor `scripts/comment.mjs`; on the repository `scripts/check/continue.test.mjs` is no reader, `scripts/case-database-check.mjs` and `clients/typescript/tests/model.mjs` are readers, `node --test scripts/repo/check.test.mjs` passes its 61 cases and `node scripts/repo/check.mjs` passes without the lease of `full-run-check`.
- [o] G5.41 Let a checklist state marker appear only as the state of an item. Cause: `docs/checklist.md` and `docs/checklist.ko.md` held a legend of the four markers on line 3 and quoted markers in the text of three items (English lines 454, 505 and 517, Korean 452, 503 and 515), so a stop hook that counted markers took them for items in progress, and `scripts/checklist/check.mjs` let any line hold a marker. Now `make checklist-check` fails on every marker of the four states that is not the leading state of an item or sub-item, in a heading, a legend, item text, continuation text or inline code, naming the file, line and column, with no exception or allow-list. The legend is removed from both checklists, which hold only items; the four states are defined in `AGENTS.md` and `AGENTS.ko.md`, which already did so, and they now also state that a marker appears only as an item's state. The three item texts say "in progress" or "marked complete" in words. Bracket markers remain in prose outside the checklists, in `AGENTS.md` and `AGENTS.ko.md` (4 lines each, the state definitions and the full suite rule), `CONTRIBUTING.md` and `CONTRIBUTING.ko.md` (1 line each) and `CHANGELOG.md` and `CHANGELOG.ko.md` (the G5.34 entry); the checker covers the checklists only. Red: the new case of `scripts/checklist/check.test.mjs`, with markers in a legend, item text, inline code and continuation text, got no error from the previous checker, and the new checker reported 24 locations in the previous checklists. Green: the case gets each marker with file, line and column, and the rewritten checklists pass.
  - [o] G5.41-1 Reject the GitHub task list markers x and X outside an item state too. Cause: G5.41 rejected only the four markers of this checklist, but GitHub task lists also read a bracketed x or X as a state, so a reader could still take one in item text for an item. Now `scripts/checklist/check.mjs` counts those two as markers as well and reports each outside the leading state with file, line and column; a leading x stays one `invalid state` error. Every non-blank line of a checklist is a heading (starts with `#`), an item or sub-item (`- ` and a state in brackets and an ID, optionally indented), or a line indented by two or more spaces after an item, which Markdown renders inside that item; any other line, a paragraph or a legend, is `unnumbered content in task tracker`, and an indented line after a heading is too. Red: the new case of `scripts/checklist/check.test.mjs` with an x in Korean item text and an X in a continuation line got no error. Green: it reports `docs/checklist.ko.md:3:22` and `docs/checklist.ko.md:5:8`, a leading x gives `invalid state`, and both checklists pass.
- [o] G5.42 Make every browser wait of the docs check say why it failed. Cause: G5.39 explained page loads only; during the owner-check of G5.41, `docs-static/theme-and-mobile` failed with `locator.click: Timeout 15000ms exceeded` and the call log line `waiting for getByRole('switch').first()`, which says neither whether the switch existed, was hidden or was covered, nor what the page held, so the failure could not be explained, and passing reruns prove nothing about it. Now `scripts/docs/steps.mjs` (`browserSteps`) is the one helper through which `scripts/docs/check.mjs` opens and reloads pages, clicks, fills, waits for elements, URLs and conditions, reads text and evaluates in the page, in the static page cases and in the search, theme, mobile and reload steps. On failure it adds to Playwright's message what was waited for, the number of elements the locator matches and for each one whether it is visible (with its display and visibility), enabled, its box and the element on top at its center (itself, or the element that covers it) with its HTML and its parent's, or, when none matches, the accessibility tree of the page; then the page URL and the record of G5.39 (pending and failed requests with the server's view, console and page errors, event loop delay and host load); page opening and reload keep the load record of `scripts/docs/load.mjs`. `rawWaits` finds a Playwright wait (goto, reload, click, fill, waitFor, waitForURL, waitForFunction, innerText, evaluate and the other waiting methods) in the docs check outside the helper, ignoring the code that runs in the browser, and a case of `scripts/docs/steps.test.mjs` requires none; the per-step Playwright timeout of 15 s stays. The cause of that one failure is not established: the static HTML of `interfaces.html` holds two switches, and the next failure will say which state the page was in. Red: a click on the switch of a page without one and of a page where an overlay covers it both gave only `locator.click: Timeout 1000ms exceeded.`. Green: the first says `no element matches getByRole('switch').first()` with the accessibility tree (`navigation`, `button "Menu"`, `main`, `heading "Docs"`), the second `1 element(s) match` and `#1 visible (display block, visibility visible), enabled, box 10,10 40x20, is covered at its center 30,20 by <div class="overlay" ...>`, both with the request, error, event loop and load lines; the three cases pass three times in a row, the raw wait case reports a direct click, `waitForFunction` and `innerText` of a fixture and none in `check.mjs`, and `npm run docs:check` passes all 48 pages, links, search and theme and mobile.
- [o] G5.43 Fix what the first CI full run (commit G5.41-1) showed of the runner and its report, and find what filled `/tmp` on the runner. Cause: the run crashed (runner pid 7516 ended without recording its end, `databases/drop` stayed running), its artifact held only `record.json`, `summary.md` and `servers/` although the summary named `environment.txt` and `targets/*.log`, the first failure lines of `client-db-check` were PASS and STEP lines, and 25 targets failed from 10:41:47 to the end with `disk quota exceeded` (errno 122) on `/tmp`, while the root filesystem kept more than 92 GiB free. Acceptance: a write that fails is recorded and never ends the runner; the report survives the run; the first failure lines are failure lines; the report records the free space of `/`, of `/tmp` and of the run and the largest directories at the start, after each target and at a failure, and a target that fails for lack of space says so with those numbers; the cause of the `/tmp` exhaustion is G5.44, which needs the measurements of G5.43-4 from a CI run. The sub-items G5.43-1 to G5.43-4 carry these parts. Evidence: the Red/Green of each sub-item; `make owner-check` over the 10 paths that the four sub-items change selected 7 make targets and passed all of them: `checklist-check`, `full-run-check` (29.7 s), `version-check`, `repo-check`, `docs-rules-check`, `docs-check` and `docs-verify-idempotent`. Neither `make check` nor `make rerun-failed` ran.
  - [o] G5.43-1 Record a failed report or record write and an unhandled error of the runner instead of ending the run. Cause: the job log of the run of G5.41-1 ends the runner with `Error: ENOENT: no such file or directory, open '.../.runtime/check/ci_<run id>_1/report/targets/databases-drop.log'` thrown from `Readable.push` and `Pipe.onStreamRead`: `scripts/check/databases.sh drop` ends with `rm -rf "$dir"`, the run directory, which since G5.38-1 also holds the report (G5.43-2), and the next output line of the drop step called `appendFileSync` on the removed log inside the stream callback of the child process, where the exception was not caught and ended the process; no handler recorded it, so the record kept `databases/drop` running and the summary step could only say that the runner ended without recording its end. Change: `reportWriter` of `scripts/check/report.mjs` does every write of the report (the logs, `environment.txt`, kept run directories, the log tails, the summary); a write never throws and never recreates a missing directory: it collects `report write failed: <path>: <code>: <message>`, which the runner stores in the record of the step it happened in (`reportErrors`) or of the run, prints, and counts as a failure of the run (exit 1, result failed); the summary lists them. The recorder of `scripts/check/full-run.mjs` writes the record the same way (`record write failed: ...`). `handleCrashes` of `scripts/check/run.mjs` turns an `uncaughtException` or `unhandledRejection` into `runner error: <stack>` in the record, the result `crashed` with that reason, the summary and exit 1; `scripts/check/summary.mjs` prints that reason for a crashed run. Red: with the runner of G5.41-1, the new case `a report write that fails is recorded and does not stop the runner` of `scripts/check/full-run.test.mjs` (target b removes the report directory and writes one more line) ran only `sh create`, a and b and ended the runner, and the case `an unhandled error of the runner is recorded as the reason of a crashed run` (target b throws outside any callback of the runner) left the run `incomplete`. Green: the first case runs a, b, c and `sh drop`, records b passed with `report write failed: .../report/targets/b.log: ENOENT: ...`, the run failed, prints the failed writes and exits 1 without a signal; the second records `crashed` with `runner error: Error: boom outside the runner` and its stack, clears the runner entry, exits 1, and the summary says `The runner did not finish: runner error: Error: boom outside the runner`. `node --test scripts/check/full-run.test.mjs scripts/check/continue.test.mjs` passes its 23 cases.
  - [o] G5.43-2 Keep the report of a run apart from the run's databases, and cap each target log while it is written. Cause: the artifact of the run of G5.41-1 held only `record.json`, `summary.md` and `servers/`, which `scripts/check/summary.mjs` writes after the runner. The runner had written `environment.txt` and every `targets/*.log`, but G5.38-1 placed the report in `.runtime/check/<run id>/report`, inside `.runtime/check/<run id>`, the directory that the runner gives `scripts/check/databases.sh` for the run's databases and that `drop` removes with `rm -rf "$dir"`; the drop step removed the whole report, the kept run directories of the failed targets with it, and then crashed the runner (G5.43-1). The files were neither left unwritten nor written elsewhere. A target log also grew without bound while it ran and was cut to its last 256 KiB only afterwards, which dropped its head: the command, the recipe and the inputs. Change: the databases of a run live in `.runtime/check/<run id>/databases`, which `databases.sh` creates and `drop` removes, and the report stays beside it in `.runtime/check/<run id>/report`, which nothing removes; `cappedLog` of `scripts/check/report.mjs` writes a target log through the writer of G5.43-1 up to 1 MiB, keeps only the last 256 KiB of the rest in memory and closes the log with `[... <n> bytes of output omitted; the last <m> bytes follow ...]` and that tail, so a log holds its head and its end and never exceeds 1.25 MiB, also while it runs; the record keeps the omitted bytes as `truncated`. A reserve file is not added: the report sat on the root filesystem, which kept 92 GiB free; the writer of G5.43-1 records any write that still fails. Red: with the runner of G5.43-1, the new case `removing the run databases leaves the report of the run` of `scripts/check/full-run.test.mjs` (a drop that removes its directory as `databases.sh` does) found `report/environment.txt is missing`, and the case `a target log keeps its head and its tail within the size limit` (target b prints 3 MiB) found a log of 3105026 bytes cut afterwards to `[the last 262144 of 3105026 bytes of this log]`, without its `target b` and `command:` lines. Green: after the drop `report/` holds `environment.txt`, `summary.md` and the logs of `servers`, `databases/create`, a, b, c and `databases/drop`, the `databases` directory is gone, and no write failed; the log of b starts with `target b` and its command, holds `line 0`, the omission line and `line 3071` at its end, stays within 1 MiB plus 256 KiB, and the record gives b a positive `truncated`. `node --test scripts/check/full-run.test.mjs scripts/check/continue.test.mjs` passes its 25 cases.
  - [o] G5.43-3 Take the failure and error lines of a target as its first failure lines, also behind lane prefixes. Cause: the summary of the run of G5.41-1 gave `client-db-check` the first failure lines `[rust] STEP joins_and_relations/mysql ... created / ... dropped / [rust] PASS ...`. `scripts/client-db-test.sh` prefixes every line of a parallel lane with `[<lane>] `, and `failures` of `scripts/check/report.mjs` matched `FAIL`, `RUN`, `STEP` and `PASS` only at the start of a line, so it found no failure in the target and fell back to its last 20 lines, which were the passing cases of the other lanes; it also knew no error-level line, so `[rust] sqlite: schema().install: DRIVER: disk I/O error`, the Rust panic, `(exit 101)` and `make: *** ... Error 1` were missed. Change: `failureLine` strips a lane prefix and a GitHub annotation and takes, in the order they appear, `FAIL` and `TIMEOUT` lines, `--- FAIL`, `not ok`, panics, lines with the word error (`error`, `Error`, `ERROR`), `failed:`, `failed lanes`, `(exit <n>)`, `exited with <n>`, and `disk quota exceeded`, `No space left on device`, errno 28 or 122, `ENOSPC` and `EDQUOT`; a `PASS` or `RUN` line is no failure; cases are tracked per lane, so a case of one lane that does not finish is still named with its last step. The last lines of the output stay the fallback only when a failed target printed none of these. The fixture `scripts/check/fixtures/client-db-check-lanes.txt` holds the last 25 lines of that target from the job log, without the runner's own step prefix. Red: with the extractor of G5.43-2, the new case `the first failure lines of a target are its failure and error lines` of `scripts/check/full-run.test.mjs` gave the last 20 lines of the fixture, starting with `[rust] STEP joins_and_relations/mysql elapsed=47ms: database orm_case_33354_8 created`, the `PASS` and `RUN` lines among them. Green: the case gives exactly the panic line, `[rust] sqlite: schema().install: DRIVER: disk I/O error`, the `[rust] FAIL columns_and_subqueries/sqlite ...` line, the two lines of the failed rust lane, `./scripts/client-db-test.sh: 107: echo: echo: I/O error`, `client-db-test: failed lanes: rust`, `make: *** [Makefile:549: client-db-check] Error 1` and the exit of make. `node --test scripts/check/full-run.test.mjs scripts/check/continue.test.mjs` passes its 26 cases.
  - [o] G5.43-4 Record the free space of `/`, `/tmp` and the run at the start and after every step, and say so plainly when a target runs out of space. Cause: the run of G5.41-1 printed only `free disk <n> GiB` of the root filesystem before each target, which stayed above 92 GiB, while 25 targets failed with `disk quota exceeded` on `/tmp`; nothing recorded the space of `/tmp`, its mount, what it held or which process held removed files open, and a target that failed for lack of space showed no line saying so. Change: `diskSnapshot` of `scripts/check/report.mjs` records `df -k` of `/`, `/tmp`, the temporary directory of the process (`TMPDIR`) and the run directory, `findmnt` of `/tmp` (on Linux: the tmpfs size and quota options), the 20 largest entries of `/tmp`, of `TMPDIR`, of `.runtime/run` and of `.runtime/check/<run id>` (`du -sxk`), and the files under `/tmp` that were removed but are still open (`lsof +L1`); a command that is missing is recorded as missing. The runner writes one snapshot at the start (`report/disk/00-start.txt`, `disk` of the run in the record) and one after every step (`report/disk/<n>-<step>.txt`, `disk` of the step, and a `free space after the step` line in its log); the snapshot after a failed step is the space at that failure. When the failure lines of a step hold `disk quota exceeded`, `No space left on device`, errno 28 or 122, `ENOSPC` or `EDQUOT`, the first failure line becomes `out of space: the target failed with ENOSPC or EDQUOT; / <n> GiB free, /tmp <n> GiB free, ...; the largest under /tmp: ...`. The `free disk` step line is gone. Red: with the runner of G5.43-3, the new case `a target that runs out of space says so with the free space of / and /tmp` of `scripts/check/full-run.test.mjs` (target b fails with `write /tmp/go-build.../_testmain.go: disk quota exceeded`) found the quota line itself as the first failure line and no `report/disk`. Green: b's first failure line is the out-of-space line with the free space of `/` and `/tmp` and the largest entries of `/tmp`, its second the quota line; the record holds `disk` for the run and for b; `report/disk` holds `00-start.txt` and the snapshots of servers, `databases/create`, a, b, c and `databases/drop`, each with df, the largest entries of `/tmp` and the `lsof +L1` section. `node --test scripts/check/full-run.test.mjs scripts/check/continue.test.mjs` passes its 27 cases; `runChecks` takes the snapshot function as `snapshot`, and the stub suites use a fixed snapshot except the space case, so `full-run-check` does not read `/tmp` for every stub step. `make owner-check` over the paths that G5.43-1 to G5.43-4 change passed (see G5.43).
- [o] G5.44 Find what fills `/tmp` on the CI runner during `client-db-check` and remove the cause. Cause: in the run of G5.41-1 the first `disk quota exceeded` (errno 122, EDQUOT) on `/tmp` came at 10:41:47, at the end of the Rust lane of `client-db-check`, and `/tmp` stayed full until the run ended at 10:46, so what filled it outlived the processes of that target, while the root filesystem kept more than 92 GiB free; the job log shows no df of `/tmp` and no listing of it. Acceptance: the snapshots of G5.43-4 (df of `/`, `/tmp` and the run, `findmnt /tmp`, the largest entries of `/tmp`, `lsof +L1` on `/tmp`) of a CI run name what grows; that writer is fixed so that `/tmp` holds no file of a finished target, with a Red/Green case of its own; a local measurement of the Rust lane of `client-db-check` (du of `/tmp` and `$TMPDIR`, `lsof +L1`, the run directories, before and after) is recorded when the free disk of the machine is at least 3 GB. Evidence: the disk snapshots of the CI run of G5.43-4 name the writer, the fixture mysqld of the binary log case of `test-servers-check`, whose removed log grew from 930 MB after that target to 6.5 GB at `client-db-check`, and G5.44-1 stops that server before its directory is removed, with a Red/Green that shows the server now exits and nothing is left; G5.44-2 and G5.44-3 fix the report defects that run showed. The local measurement of the Rust lane is not needed, because the snapshots found the writer outside that lane. The `/tmp` series of the next CI run confirms the fix; if `/tmp` still grows there, that becomes a new item with its snapshot.
  - [o] G5.44-1 Stop the fixture MySQL server of the binary log case before its directories are removed, and fail the case when the server does not stop. Cause: the CI run of G5.43-4 records `/tmp`, a 7.8 GiB tmpfs with `usrquota`, at 151,200 KiB used through `repo-check`, at 1,165,248 KiB right after `test-servers-check`, and at 6,606,232 KiB, its quota, at `client-db-check`, after which 25 targets failed with EDQUOT. In every snapshot from `test-servers-check` on, `lsof +L1` names the same process, mysqld 10079, writing to `/tmp/orm-binlog-AHN00Q/mysqld.log (deleted)` on fd 1 and 2: 930,269,067 bytes after `test-servers-check` and 6,493,765,632 bytes at `client-db-check`, with its InnoDB files open in the same removed directory. The largest entries of `/tmp` never showed it, because a removed file has no entry. The case "the test MySQL server removes an expired binary log by itself" of `scripts/test-servers.test.mjs` ran `mysqladmin shutdown` over TCP, which returns before the server exits, ignored its result and removed the data directory at once. A server whose data directory goes away while it shuts down never exits: `sample` of four such servers left on a development machine by earlier runs showed the InnoDB thread `log_files_governor` spinning, retrying its redo log file in the missing directory, and lsof put their removed logs at 6.9 to 9.2 GB by SIZE/OFF. Stopping the four freed about 15 GB, less than the 34 GB those offsets add up to, so SIZE/OFF overstates the blocks such a log holds. The case still passed. Change: the case stops the server with `STOP_PROCESS TERM <pid>` (tests/stop-process, which returns on the exit event of the process), asserts that it stopped and that the process is gone, and removes its directories only then; a server that does not stop fails the case and keeps its directories, so it does not spin. Red: with the old shutdown and a check before the removal, the case failed with `mysqld 26697 still runs after its shutdown returned`, and that server then exited by itself because its directory was kept. Green: the case passes in 7.9 s, no `orm-binlog` process or directory remains after it, and `make test-servers-check` passes.
  - [o] G5.44-2 Take the first failure lines of a target from failures and not from case names, and label a SQLite disk I/O error as out of space only when the snapshot shows a file system without free space. Cause: in the run of G5.43-4 the first failure lines of `client-db-check` were `[typescript] STEP driver-error/trigger_refused/sqlite ...: database file ... created` lines. The pattern `\b(?:error|Error|ERROR)\b` of `failureLine` matched `error` in the case name `driver-error/...`, and the first real failure, `[rust] FAIL audit_record_transaction ...: install: DRIVER: disk I/O error`, came later. That SQLite error (778) got no out-of-space line, and the out-of-space line named only du entries of `/tmp`, which cannot show a removed file. A disk I/O error has other causes than space, so the code alone must not make it out of space. Change: a step line (`STEP <case> ...: <message>`) is judged by its message only, and an error word counts only when no `-`, `/`, `.` or letter comes right before it. `spaceCause` puts the out-of-space line first for an ENOSPC or EDQUOT line as before, and for a `disk I/O error` line only when a file system of the snapshot after the step has 0 KiB free, naming that file system. `spaceLine` also names the largest removed but open file under `/tmp` from `lsof +L1`, with its SIZE/OFF and its process. Red: with the runner of main, the new case `a case name that holds an error word does not make its step lines failures` got the four `driver-error` STEP lines and an `every-error-in-order` STEP line among its first failure lines. The new case `a disk I/O error is out of space only while a file system has no free space` got the plain FAIL line first where `/tmp` had 0 KiB free. Green: the first failure lines are the Rust FAIL, panic, quota and compile error lines; with `/tmp` at 0 KiB the first line is `out of space: the target failed with a disk I/O error while /tmp had no free space; ...; the largest removed but open file: 6192.9 MiB by lsof SIZE/OFF, /tmp/orm-binlog-AHN00Q/mysqld.log (mysqld 10079)`; with free space the FAIL line stays first.
  - [o] G5.44-3 Keep the npm debug log of a failed target in its report and take its error lines as the first failure lines. Cause: in the run of G5.43-4 `package-check` showed only `npm error A complete log of this run can be found in: /home/runner/.npm/_logs/2026-10-05T11_42_43_213Z-debug-0.log`. That file is outside the report and the artifact, so the error of npm could not be read without running it again. Change: the runner notes every path that a step line names in `A complete log of this run can be found in:`. For a failed step it copies that file to `report/targets/<step>/` (its last 256 KiB when it exceeds 1 MiB), puts its `<n> error <message>` lines first among the first failure lines as `npm debug log <copy>: <message>`, and says so when the file is missing. Red: with the runner of main, the new case `a failed npm command keeps its debug log and its error lines in the report` got the `npm error A complete log ...` line first and no copy in the report. Green: the first failure lines are `npm debug log .runtime/check/ci_13_1/report/targets/b/2026-10-05T11_42_43_213Z-debug-0.log: code E404` and its 404 line, and the copy equals the debug log. `node --test scripts/check/full-run.test.mjs scripts/check/continue.test.mjs` passes its 30 cases, and `make owner-check` over the four changed scripts passed `full-run-check`, `repo-check` and `test-servers-check`.
- [o] G5.47 Remove the PHP connection pool of G5.36: the PHP client opens one connection per `Db` and keeps none across requests. Cause: G5.36 kept PDO persistent slots `orm:<pool>:<slot>` in each php-fpm worker. A slot is not shared across workers and cannot wait, so the server connections grow with workers × slots × connection settings, and a kept connection carries the session state of one request into the next. A php-fpm worker serves one request at a time and shares nothing with the next, so PHP cannot pool connections well inside the process. A pooler outside it can: PgBouncer in transaction mode and ProxySQL share a few server connections among all workers, `docs/config.md` "Connection poolers" describes both, and `make client-pooler-check` runs the four clients through them. Change: `clients/php/src/Connection.php` with its slots and `$held` is gone, and `Orm::connect` opens the PDO and gives it to the `Db`. `Db` loses `endRequest` and its shutdown function, `__destruct`, `$sent`, the resend of a first statement after `CONNECTION_LOST` and `reconnect`. A connection now closes with its request, and the server then rolls back an open transaction and releases its named locks and session values; a test MySQL session showed `IS_FREE_LOCK` 0 while it held `GET_LOCK` and 1 once its PDO was freed. With no reused idle connection, the first statement of a `Db` runs on the connection `connect` just opened, so the resend has no case left. `Config` keeps `poolSize`, `poolIdleSize` and `poolLifetimeMs`, and `Orm::connect` refuses any of them other than zero with `CONFIG`, naming PgBouncer and ProxySQL. Deleting the fields would shift the positional arguments after `poolSize` and silently change `statementTimeoutMs`. Ignoring a value would let a caller believe the connections are pooled when they are not. `Db::poolSize()` is gone, because a method that always returns 0 describes nothing. `stats()` reports `maxOpenConnections` 1, the true maximum of a `Db`. MySQL still sets its session with one init command. `finish` still releases named locks, clears local values and resets SQLite modes at the end of every transaction, because a `Db` runs several transactions in one request. `CONNECTION_LOST`, `OrmException::fromDriver($e, $driver, $pdo)` and `poolSize` of Go, Rust and TypeScript stay. `pool_test.php` and `pool_request.php` are gone, and `docs/config.md`, `usage.md`, `protocol.md` and the `dsn_connection` feature now say that PHP opens a connection per request and that PgBouncer or ProxySQL pool PHP connections. Red: with the PHP source of main, the case `pool options` of `clients/php/tests/model_test.php` connected with `poolSize: 3` and reported `maxOpenConnections` 3, not 1.
