The new case `first_statement_lost` of `clients/php/tests/rollback_test.php` ends the server session of a new `Db` before its first statement. On MySQL and PostgreSQL it found no error and the events `["CONNECTION_LOST",null]`, so the client sent the statement again. Green: `pool options` passes on SQLite, MySQL and PostgreSQL with `CONFIG` naming the poolers for `poolSize` 3 and -1, `poolIdleSize` 1 and `poolLifetimeMs` 1000. `first_statement_lost` passes on MySQL and PostgreSQL with `CONNECTION_LOST` and the single event `["CONNECTION_LOST"]`. `model_test.php`, `rollback_test.php`, `schema_set_test.php`, `driver_error_test.php` and `runtime_db_test.php` pass, and so does the scoped owner check.
- [o] G5.45 Return a connection to the pool only when its transaction ended cleanly, and close it otherwise, in the Go, TypeScript and Rust clients. Cause: a transaction ends by releasing its MySQL named locks, clearing its MySQL local values, resetting its SQLite modes and running COMMIT or ROLLBACK, because that state outlives the transaction on a pooled connection. After a failed step the state of the session is unknown. A failed RELEASE_LOCK can leave the lock held, and the next user of the pooled connection then holds a lock it never took. The clients still put such connections back. Go `txConn.rollback` (`clients/go/orm/tx.go`) returned the connection with `conn.Close()` whatever its cleanup and ROLLBACK reported, and `commit` rolled back through that path after a failed cleanup. TypeScript `finish` (`clients/typescript/src/database.ts`) discarded the session only when ROLLBACK or COMMIT failed, not when only the cleanup failed. Rust `rollback` (`clients/rust/orm/src/tx.rs`) marked the connection completed after a successful ROLLBACK even when the cleanup before it had failed; its commit path already closed the connection, because the failed cleanup returned before `end`. The rule is that a pooled connection is clean or discarded: a client that cannot show the session is clean closes it, and the server ends its transaction, locks and values with the session. Change: Go records a failed cleanup at commit in `txConn.unclean`, and `rollback` closes the session (`closeSession`) before `conn.Close()` when that flag is set or any cleanup step or the ROLLBACK failed. TypeScript sets `discard` when a cleanup statement failed, apart from a statement whose only failure was its subscriber, because such a statement took effect. Rust `end` takes `clean` and marks the connection completed only when it is true, and `rollback` passes whether the cleanup succeeded. `docs/interfaces.md` states the rule; the TypeScript SQLite driver holds its one connection, which it cannot close. The PHP client opens one connection per `Db` (G5.47) and has no pool. Red: on MySQL with a pool of one connection, a transaction whose `RELEASE_LOCK` failed, or whose lock was released early so that `RELEASE_LOCK` returned 0, was followed by a use of the same session. The new Go test `TestFailedTransactionEndDiscardsConnection` found `session 69697 returned to the pool after its transaction end failed` for a rejected release at rollback and at commit and for a rejected ROLLBACK, and the lock was still held 5 s later. The new TypeScript case `mysql/failedEndDiscards` found the same for rollback and commit (session 69805). The new Rust test `failed_cleanup_discards_the_connection` failed with `rollback: session 69907 returned to the pool after its cleanup failed`. Green: those three pass; a transaction that ends cleanly keeps its session in each of them, and another connection takes the lock of a failed end at once. `TestTransactionReports*`, `TestSavepointReportsFailedEnd` and `TestCancelledTransactionClearsSessionState` pass, the TypeScript `model.mjs` passes, and the Rust `tx::tests` pass. `make owner-check` over the ten changed paths passed `version-check`, `repo-check`, `docs-rules-check`, `docs-check`, `docs-verify-idempotent`, `go-fmt-check`, `go-vet-check`, `rust-fmt-check` and the verification and coverage of `model_queries`, `parameter_chunking` and `interface_contract`.
  - [o] G5.45-1 Make owner-check run the transaction end tests of a changed file. Cause: `make owner-check` over the files of G5.45 selected no command that runs `clients/go/orm/transaction_end_test.go` ("an input of no verification command") or the tests inside `clients/rust/orm/src/tx.rs`. `tx::send_tests::sqlite_send_savepoint` then failed when run by hand with `declared test DSN is required`, because only a make target gives `ORM_SEND_SQLITE_DSN`. Through `make rust-send-savepoint-check` it passed, three of three twice. A file that holds tests, or that is a test of a feature, must select the command that runs them. Change: the feature `transactions` of `contracts/features.json` declares `transaction-end-go`, which runs `TestTransactionReports*`, `TestSavepointReportsFailedEnd`, `TestFailedTransactionEndDiscardsConnection` and `TestCancelledTransactionClearsSessionState` from `transaction_end_test.go`, `context_reset_test.go` and `cancelled_session_test.go`. It also declares `transaction-end-rust`, which runs the `tx::` tests of the Rust libraries with the run's environment from `tx.rs` and `tx_send_tests.rs`, and lists both Rust files among its tests. `failing_driver_test.go` stays a helper that selects its own check. Red: the new case `a changed file selects the transaction end tests that it holds or that test it` of `scripts/features/owners.test.mjs` found no `transactions` command for `clients/rust/orm/src/tx.rs`. Green: `tx.rs` and `tx_send_tests.rs` select `transactions/transaction-end-rust`, and the three Go files select `transactions/transaction-end-go`; `owners.test.mjs` passes its 16 cases. `make owner-check` ran both commands, with `TestFailedTransactionEndDiscardsConnection`, `tx::send_tests::sqlite_send_savepoint` and `tx::tests::failed_cleanup_discards_the_connection` passing, and passed all its 12 steps.
- [o] G5.46 Build Rust into the target directory of each checkout and refuse the target directory of another checkout. Cause: worktrees built Rust into `clients/rust/target` of the main checkout through `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` on the make command line, and `run.mjs` and owner-check passed that value on to every target. Cargo decides that an artifact is fresh from the paths of its sources relative to the package and their modification times. It records no checkout that built it: the dep-info of rustc names sources relative to the workspace, and the uplifted dep-info of cargo is rewritten with the current checkout's path. So a checkout whose sources are older than another checkout's build tests that other checkout's code, and nothing in the target directory can show which checkout built an artifact. A detector would have to record and compare build owners and modification times, and a missed or stale record would test the wrong code without a sign. Not sharing removes the cause. The cost is disk, temporary for each worktree: the target directory of this worktree reached 3.6 GiB in its owner check. Change: the Makefile stops with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=<dir> is outside this checkout <checkout>; each checkout builds Rust into its own target directory (AGENTS.md), because cargo would reuse what another checkout built as fresh` when the command line names a target directory outside the checkout; a run directory inside it stays accepted. `run.mjs` and owner-check no longer pass `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` to the targets they run, so each target uses the Makefile's directory of its checkout. AGENTS.md and AGENTS.ko.md state the rule, its reason, and that the leases still order the builds of one checkout's runs and that a worktree's target directory goes with the worktree. Comments that described the target directory as shared by several checkouts now say that the concurrent runs of one checkout share it. Red: the new case `cargo reuses what another checkout built into a shared target, and a target of its own builds this checkout` of `tests/cargo-test.test.mjs` built checkout a ("from a") into a shared target and then checkout b, whose source says "from b" with an older modification time: cargo rebuilt nothing and b ran "from a". With the Makefile of main, the new case `make refuses a Rust target directory outside its checkout` failed with `make ran with the target directory of another checkout`. Green: in its own target directory b runs "from b", make refuses the other checkout's directory with the message above and accepts its own and a run directory inside it, and `tests/cargo-test.test.mjs` passes its six cases. `make owner-check` over the 15 changed files, run without a `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` override and so building into this worktree's own target, passed `full-run-check`, `version-check`, `testcase-check`, `repo-check`, `docs-rules-check`, `go-fmt-check`, `go-vet-check`, `rust-fmt-check`, `feature-unit-check` and the verification and coverage of `interface_contract` and `conformance_verification`.
- [o] G5.49 Fix the two failures of the CI run of G5.44: `git-check` and `client-pooler-check`. Cause: the run passed 50 targets and nothing ran out of space, which confirmed G5.44, and failed `git-check` with `FAIL git-subjects ...: the process ended inside the case; its error is above` and `client-pooler-check` with `FAIL first_statement_lost/mysql: the Db holds the only other session of the case database: [1514,1515]`. The sub-items G5.49-1 to G5.49-3 carry the parts. Evidence: the Red/Green of each sub-item; `make owner-check` over the 11 changed files passed `full-run-check`, `version-check`, `testcase-check`, `repo-check`, `test-servers-check`, `git-check`, `docs-rules-check`, `feature-unit-check` and the verification of `transactions`.
  - [o] G5.49-1 Refuse a commit whose subject breaks `git.subject-format` before it exists. Cause: nothing checked a subject when it was written: `git-check` reads the history only after a push, when a pushed commit can no longer change. Change: the tracked hook `.githooks/commit-msg` runs `node scripts/git/check.mjs --message <file>`, which checks the subject of the message being committed (comment lines skipped, a merge not checked) and refuses a bad one with the rule and the reason. The Makefile sets `core.hooksPath` to `.githooks` at parse time when it differs, so every make run installs it in the checkout. `git-check` declares `.githooks/**` and the Makefile as inputs, and AGENTS.md states the hook. Red: without the hook and its installation, the new case `a commit whose subject breaks the rule is refused by the commit-msg hook that make installs` of `scripts/git/check.test.mjs` failed with `a subject of 53 characters was committed`. Green: the subject check passes; in a temporary repository whose `core.hooksPath` is this checkout's `.githooks`, the 53-character subject is refused with `git.subject-format: subject exceeds 50 characters: ... (contracts/rules.json; the commit is refused)` and no commit exists, the 46-character subject is committed, and `make -n` in a fresh repository sets its `core.hooksPath` to `.githooks`; `scripts/git/check.test.mjs` passes its five cases.
  - [o] G5.49-2 Put the error of a case that ended its process into its FAIL line. Cause: `sections()` of `tests/testcase.mjs` wrote `FAIL <case> ...: the process ended inside the case; its error is above` when a case ended its process. The summary of a `make check` run takes the FAIL line as the first failure line, so the git-check row showed no error, and the actual line `git.subject-format: G5.44: ...` was only in the target log. Change: while a case is open, `sections()` keeps the message of an uncaught error (`uncaughtExceptionMonitor`) or else the last line written to stderr, and the FAIL line reads `the process ended inside the case: <that error>` (`no error was written` when there is none). Red: with the runner of main, the case `testcase/sections` found no `FAIL fixture/section-crash ...: the process ended inside the case: uncaught section error`. Green: that line holds the uncaught error, and a case that writes `git.subject-format: G5.44: subject exceeds 50 characters` to stderr and calls `process.exit(1)` gets that line in its FAIL line; `tests/testcase.test.mjs` passes its six cases.
  - [o] G5.49-3 Find the session of the new `Db` in `first_statement_lost` as the session it opened, also behind a pooler. Cause: under `client-pooler-check` the case failed with `the Db holds the only other session of the case database: [1514,1515]` and then ended a session that was not the Db's. The fixture is installed through the pooler (ProxySQL, PgBouncer), which keeps its own server sessions open in the case database after its client closes, so the case database held more than the Db's session. The Db itself connects to the server directly. The case asserted the same thing behind a pooler, and the assertion stays: the first statement fails with `CONNECTION_LOST`, has one event, and is not sent again. Change: the case reads the sessions of the case database through the server before and after the `Db` connects, checks that the Db opened exactly one, and ends only that one. Red: with the pooler DSNs that `client-pooler-check` uses, the case failed on MySQL with `[70292,70293]` and on PostgreSQL with `[37655,37679]`, then with `no error` and the events `[null]`. Green: it passes on MySQL and PostgreSQL behind ProxySQL and PgBouncer and without them, and the other cases of `rollback_test.php` pass.
- [o] G5.50 Make `codec-check` compare only the codec outputs that it produced in its own run. Cause: `codec-check` ran only `php tests/codec/check.php`, which read `tests/codec/out/{go,rust,typescript}.json`. Other targets wrote those files whenever they ran the Go, Rust and TypeScript codec tests, and the local files dated from an earlier run of the day. So the check compared whatever an earlier run of any checkout state had left, not the code of this run. The Go writer also ignored the error of `os.MkdirAll` and wrote the file in place. Change: `codec-check` is now the parts `codec-check/go`, `codec-check/rust` and `codec-check/typescript`, which run the three codec tests with `ORM_CODEC_OUT` set to a directory of this make run (`CODEC_OUT`, `.runtime/run/codec-check-<make pid>`), and `codec-check/compare`, which runs the PHP checker on that directory after all three and removes it. The writers are independent, so under `make -k` a failed one does not stop the others. A writer writes only when `ORM_CODEC_OUT` is set, checks every error, and writes through a temporary file and a rename. The checker requires `ORM_CODEC_OUT` and reads exactly `go.json`, `rust.json` and `typescript.json` there. `tests/codec/out/` is no longer written or ignored, and `tests/codec/README.md` describes the run. Red: in a checkout of main holding the earlier outputs, a Go encoder that appends a byte before base64 made `TestCodecVectors` fail, yet `make codec-check` passed all three of its cases on the old files. Green: with the same broken encoder `make -k codec-check` fails in `codec-check/go` while `codec-check/rust` and `codec-check/typescript` still run. With the encoder restored, `make codec-check` passes `TestCodecVectors`, `codec::tests::vectors`, `codec-vector` and the PHP cases with `3 other-language outputs of 80 vectors`, and leaves no run directory. `make owner-check` over the seven changed files passed all its 13 steps.
- [o] G5.51 Fail owner-check for a changed path that selects no behaviour test, and connect every tracked file to the test that reads it or declare its scope. Cause: owner-check only printed `<path> is an input of no verification command, coverage part or helper; make check runs what reads it` and exited 0. A scan of the tracked files found 237 that selected no feature command, coverage part or helper and no owner target except the lint targets: repo-check matched every path through `**`, and go-fmt-check, go-vet-check, rust-fmt-check and docs-rules-check matched by extension. Among them were the generator and `cmd/orm-gen`, the TypeScript index, the generated error codes, `internal/*`, the physical tests of `tests/dialects`, `tests/codec` and `tests/conformance`. So `make owner-check PATHS=generator/gen_go.go` ran only lints and passed. Change: a target of `contracts/check-inputs.json` declares `inputs`, the files whose behaviour it tests, apart from `lints`, the patterns it only lints. repo-check now has the inputs `scripts/repo/**` and the lints `**`, docs-rules-check has the inputs `contracts/rules.json` and `scripts/docs/**` and the lints `**/*.md`, and the formatters have only lints. `contracts/check-inputs.json` also has `paths`, a scope `lint` or `suite` with a reason for each exact path that no test can own. Patterns, untracked paths, other scopes and empty reasons are declaration errors. A changed path that selects no feature command, coverage part, helper or target through its `inputs`, and has no declared scope, now fails owner-check with the path and how to declare it; a removed path is not checked. The 237 files are now connected. 27 targets that were suite-scoped and read specific files are owner targets with those inputs: go-model-check, client-unit-check, php-min-check, ts-min-check, rust-150-check, rust-driver-check, example-check, fuzz-check, dialect-facts-check, package-check, ts-model-check, codec-check and 15 dbspec targets. The six whole-suite targets stay suite. 40 new verification commands run the tests that no command ran; a Go command runs the `Test` and `Fuzz` functions of its files. Existing commands gained the fixtures, Faults.php, sqlite-paths.json and sources that their tests read, and 12 helpers own scripts and generated files. The helper `error-codes` regenerates `clients/go/orm/codes.go`, `clients/php/src/Code.php` and `clients/rust/orm/src/codes.rs` from `docs/errors.yaml` and compares them; nothing checked those generated files before. Sixteen paths declare a scope. lint: `.gitignore`, `LICENSE`, `clients/rust/rustfmt.toml`. suite, each with the mechanism by which it affects every target: `.github/runner`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.github/workflows/docs-pages.yml`, `.node-version`, `.php-version`, `clients/php/composer.lock`, `go.mod`, `go.sum`, `rust-toolchain.toml`, `internal/testdb/testdb.go`, the Rust `case-clock` and `case-database` libraries and `scripts/cargo-build-copy.sh`. Green: it selects go-model-check, and a file that nothing declares exits 1 with `selects no behaviour test`.   - [o] G5.51-1 Leave the ORM's own SQLite row lock table out of the database state that the conformance and decimal checks compare. Green: it passes with the other state tests, `decimal-physical` passes on MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQLite, and `conformance-runners` and `conformance-check-go` pass.
- [o] G5.52 Run every CI setup step and `make check` after a failed setup step, and record the targets that need what it installs as not-run. Cause: in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` only the step `database servers` had `continue-on-error`, and the other installs (Go, the Node dependencies, Rust, the two PHP releases, the PHP SQLite check, composer, the server programs) stopped the job at a failure. Then `make check`, the summary and the report did not run, so one failed install hid every target and left no report. `continue-on-error` also removed a failure from the job's result. In `.github/workflows/docs-pages.yml`, a failed install likewise skipped the build, the idempotence check and the smoke test. Change: every setup step of `ci.yml` has an id and `if: ${{ !cancelled() }}`, so it runs after a failed earlier one. `make check`, the summary and the report run with `if: ${{ !cancelled() }}`, and `make check` receives the step results as `ORM_CI_SETUP: ${{ toJSON(steps) }}`. `scripts/check/ci-setup.mjs` maps each step id to what it installs: go, node-modules, rust, php-min, php, composer, server-programs and databases. `rust-cache` installs nothing a target needs, and the checkout and Node are what the runner itself needs. The runner records each step that did not succeed as the setup step `ci/<id>`, and records a target that needs what it installs as not-run with `the CI setup step <id> failed; its output is in the job log of that step`, while every other target runs. A failed server-programs step also blocks databases. `contracts/check-inputs.json` declares these needs for the 49 targets, and owner-check refuses a need that no step provides. `docs-pages.yml` gives each step an id and `if: ${{ !cancelled() ... }}`, the builds run only after the Node dependencies, the smoke test only after the build and the browser, and the upload only when every step passed. No workflow uses `continue-on-error`. The new rule `ciSetupErrors` of `scripts/repo/ci.mjs` enforces this. The summary and report rule now requires `if: ${{ !cancelled() }}`, and AGENTS.md and AGENTS.ko.md state that a failed install never stops the suite. Red: with the runner of main, the new case `a failed CI setup step marks the targets that need it not-run and runs every other target` of `scripts/check/full-run.test.mjs` ran target a although its need rust had failed. The new case `a workflow runs every setup step and make check after a failed setup step and passes the results on` of `scripts/repo/check.test.mjs` finds errors in the workflows of G5.44. Green: the run records `ci/rust` failed, a not-run with that step and b and c passed, and `rust-cache` is not recorded; the rule passes the changed workflows and reports a missing id, an unknown id, a missing condition, `continue-on-error` and a missing `ORM_CI_SETUP`. `make owner-check` over the changed files passed `full-run-check`, `version-check`, `repo-check`, `docs-rules-check` and `feature-unit-check`; the workflows themselves run in the next CI run.
- [o] G5.53 Run the independent parts of a script, a recipe and a verification command after a failed part, and refuse recipes and commands that run independent checks in sequence. Cause: a read-only audit of main G5.46 found several places that still stopped at the first failure. `scripts/client-db-test.sh` ran its lanes in sequence in the current shell, so the `exit 1` of a failed lane's `finish` ended the run before the next lane. `scripts/perf-test.sh`, `scripts/package-check.sh` and `databases.sh drop` ran independent steps under `set -e`, so a failed MySQL drop left the PostgreSQL databases and the run directory behind. Seven recipes ran independent tests or lints in one recipe, whose first failure stopped the rest even under `make -k`: dbspec-php-check, dbspec-rust-check, conformance-result-check, dbspec-compare-check, rust-send-savepoint-check (clippy before the tests) and dbspec-stress-bench. conformance-check ran its own conformance run only after its three prerequisite checks. Six verification commands of `contracts/features.json` chained two tests with `&&`. Change: the sequential lanes each run in a subshell, and the run ends with `client-db-test: failed lanes: <lanes>`. perf-test, package-check (its four package checks are functions that each run in a `set -e` subshell) and the drop keep going and end with `perf-test: failed:`, `package-check: failed:` and `databases: drop failed:` and the failed steps. The seven recipes are split into parts `<target>/<part>`, and parts that share files use the run directory of the top target through a pattern-specific `RUN_DIR`, which the top target removes after every part passed. conformance-check has the part `conformance-check/run` beside its three checks. The chained commands are separate commands: generation-scan-typescript, generation-lib-rust, encryption-model-go, encryption-codec-rust, constraints-engine-go and constraints-driver-go, and catalog-lib-rust, which also takes over catalog-unit-rust of G5.51. The new repository rules `independentTestErrors` and `chainedCommandErrors` of `scripts/repo/ci.mjs` refuse a recipe with two or more different test or lint lines and a command that chains tests with `&&`. A second run of the same test, a compile without a run (`go test -c`, `cargo test --no-run`), an input written to a file and the build of a dependency's tool do not count. Red: the new cases of `scripts/check/continue.test.mjs` found that the TypeScript lane did not run after the failed PHP lane, that only the first of the four performance checks ran, that composer, cargo and go did not run after a failed `npm pack`, and that psql did not run and the directory stayed after a failed MySQL drop. The new repository case found the recipes of G5.52. Green: those cases pass, every lane, performance check, package and drop step runs and the failures are named; the repository case passes for the Makefile and `contracts/features.json` and refuses its fixtures. `make dbspec-php-check`, `dbspec-rust-check`, `conformance-result-check`, `rust-send-savepoint-check`, `dbspec-compare-check` and `dbspec-stress-bench` pass and leave no run directory, and `make run-databases TARGETS=conformance-check` passes. `make owner-check` over the ten changed files passed its 17 steps, among them package-check and the verification of the four features with split commands.
- [o] G5.54 Fail a test run that selects no test. Cause: a run that selected nothing passed. `node tests/go-test.mjs -v -run '^TestNoSuchName$' .` exited 0, `tests/cargo-test.mjs` ran every binary and passed when its name filters matched no test, and `ORM_CLIENT_DB_LANGS=typscript` ran no lane and printed `client-db-test: every lane passed:`. A scan of every Go `-run` selection of the Makefile, the scripts and `contracts/features.json` with `go test -list` found one that ran nothing: `decimal-go-physical` of `model_queries` and `model_writes` selected `^TestDecimalPhysical(MySQL|Postgres|SQLite)$` without `-tags decimalphysical`, the build tag of that test file, so its three tests never ran (0 listed without the tag, 3 with it). Change: `tests/go-test.mjs` runs the test step as `go test -json` and renders the `Output` of the test2json events, test output and build output alike, as the human-readable output. A run with `-run` fails with `go-test: -run <pattern> selected no test in <packages>` when no `"Action":"run"` event names a test. The count comes from the documented events, not from the `=== RUN` text of `-v`. `-run '^$'` states an empty selection and is not counted. `tests/cargo-test.mjs` lists each test binary with `--list` and the filters and arguments before it runs any; when they select no test in all binaries it runs none and fails with `cargo-test: the filters <filters> select no test in <n> test binaries`, and otherwise it prints the number selected. `scripts/client-db-test.sh` refuses a name of `ORM_CLIENT_DB_LANGS` that is no lane and a selection without a lane, with exit 2. The two `decimal-go-physical` commands pass `-tags decimalphysical`. Red: with the runners of main, the new case `a go test run whose -run selects no test fails` (a temporary module and the real go test) exited 0, the new case `cargo-test fails when the name filters select no test in any test binary` ran both stub binaries and exited 0, and `client-db-test refuses an unknown lane name and an empty selection` passed `typscript` and `,`. Green: those fail with the messages above, the selections `^TestPresent$` and `two` pass, and the case `the selected tests are counted from the run events of test2json` counts a run event without `=== RUN` text and ignores `=== RUN` text without a run event. The rescan finds no other empty Go selection, every Rust filter that owner-check ran selected tests, and `make owner-check` over the changed files passed its 11 steps, among them `decimal-go-physical`, which now runs its three tests.
- [o] G5.55 Leave nothing behind after a test step. Cause: the temporary directory held 1513 entries of orm runs (923M), among them `orm-testcase-*` 399, `orm-rust-sqlite-lock-*` 242, `orm-conformance-runners-*` 12 (399M) and `orm-interface-symbols-*` 10, and `.runtime/run` held two run directories of killed runs. Five causes made them. Some code never removed its files: `tests/testcase_test.php` removed the `.php` file but not the empty file that `tempnam` made for its name, and `clients/rust/orm/tests/sqlite_concurrency.rs` never removed its directory. Some code skipped its removal on failure: `tests/interfaces/check/main.go` called `os.Exit(1)` after `defer os.RemoveAll`, which then never ran; `tests/conformance/check/main.go` ended through `must` before its `os.RemoveAll`; and the `clients/rust/orm/src/tx.rs` tests removed their directories only at their end, so a failed assertion left them. A run killed by a signal ran no `finally` (`scripts/features/coverage.mjs`, `tests/cargo-test.mjs`). The runner copied a failed target's run directory into the report and left it on disk. The rest came from code that no longer exists (`orm-gen-cli-*`, `orm-ts-tools-*`, all made before 10-03). 133 places make temporary files, so fixing the places found would not stop the next one; and a process that a passed step left running was not seen at all. Change: `scripts/check/step.mjs` runs a step of the check runner and of `make owner-check` with a temporary directory of its own under the system temporary directory, given as `TMPDIR`, which every client's temporary file call follows, and as the leader of a process group of its own. After the step it lists and ends the processes left in the group, then lists the entries left in the directory and the run directories `.runtime/run/*-<make pid>`. A passed step that left any of them fails with `check: <label> left N processes: <pid> <command>` or `check: <label> left N temporary entries: <names>`. A failed step's temporary entries are copied into the report (`targets/<step>/tmp`) and its run directories into `targets/<step>/run` as before. In both cases they are then removed and the processes ended, and the runner ends the groups of running steps when it gets a signal. The setup steps (servers, databases, CI setup) keep the system temporary directory and the runner's group, because a server they start outlives the step, and a daemonized mysqld uses `TMPDIR` as its own temporary directory. The check against the old code found no state shared between steps through the temporary directory: the leases are in `.runtime`, the conformance lock is a literal `/tmp` path, and the fixed temporary names are named per run. The fixes: the interface and conformance checks end with `os.Exit(run())`, and `must` and `usage` return through `run` by a panic it recovers, so their `defer`s run and the conformance locks are released. `tx.rs` holds each test directory in a `Scratch` value and `sqlite_concurrency.rs` each database in a `Database` value with its own directory, both of which remove it on drop, also while a failed assertion unwinds. `tests/testcase_test.php` removes the `tempnam` file. `tests/cargo-test.mjs` makes its run directory under the temporary directory, so a killed run's copies go with the step. `make repo-check` refuses a Go function that calls `os.Exit` after a `defer` (`scripts/repo/gosource.mjs`). Two checks that read the temporary directory follow the step's: npm turns on Node's compile cache under `TMPDIR`, a cache shared between runs and not a leftover of a test, so the step gives npm `NODE_COMPILE_CACHE` in the system temporary directory, where it was before; and the socket case of `make repo-check`, which needs a root short enough for `mysql.sock`, makes its root under `/tmp`, because the step directory under the macOS temporary directory already leaves less room than that. AGENTS.md states the rule and the limit that a process that starts a session of its own leaves the group and is not seen. Red: with the runner of main, the new `scripts/check/step.test.mjs` cases failed: a step saw the system temporary directory as its own, a passed step that made `orm-leftover-probe` and one that backgrounded `sleep 37` passed and left both, and a failed step's entries were not kept. The extended setup-failure case of `full-run.test.mjs` found `.runtime/run/b-4242` still on disk after the report copy. The interface check of main, run with `-language rust` and a results directory that does not exist, failed and left `orm-interface-symbols-2642790756`. The conformance check of main, run with a failing build, left `orm-conformance-runners-3878254187`. `tests/testcase_test.php` left 7 files, and the `sqlite-concurrency-rust` command left `orm-rust-sqlite-lock-69336` after passing. The new repository rule found the five `os.Exit` calls of the interface check of main. Green: the step cases pass, naming `left 1 temporary entries: orm-leftover-probe` and `left 1 processes: <pid> sleep 37`, with the sleep ended and nothing left. The run directory is gone after the copy. The interface and conformance checks fail as before and leave nothing, and the conformance lock is released. `tests/testcase_test.php` and `sqlite-concurrency-rust` (6 tests) leave nothing. `scratch_is_removed_after_a_failed_assertion` passes. The repository rule finds nothing in the tree. `make owner-check` over the 19 changed paths passed its 17 steps, among them `full-run-check`, `repo-check`, `testcase-check`, the verification of `transactions`, `interface_contract` and `conformance_verification` and the coverage of `interface_contract`, with no step leaving a temporary entry or a process.
  - [o] G5.55-1 Remove the binary logs that a run's writes leave on the MySQL test servers at the end of the run. Cause: tests drop their databases, but the binary log entries their writes produced stay on the primary and the replica (with `log_replica_updates`, the replica writes its own). The servers held 5 binary logs of about 4.5 GB each side, and one run of `scripts/check/databases.sh` create and drop added 177 MB to each, so the disk grew with every run. The declared retention (`MYSQLD_BINLOG` in `scripts/test-servers.sh`) bounds the logs only when a server runs with it: the running pair reported `max_binlog_size` 1073741824 and `binlog_expire_logs_seconds` 2592000, the defaults, because it was started before that declaration. The only reader of the test servers' binary logs is the replica. Change: `databases.sh drop`, the last step of the check runner and of `make owner-check`, which also runs after a failed step, now also purges the binary logs. It rotates the primary's log, waits with `SOURCE_POS_WAIT` until the replica has applied the primary up to that point, purges the primary's logs up to the current one, rotates the replica's binary and relay logs, and purges the replica's binary logs up to its current one; `relay_log_purge` removes the applied relay logs. When the replica has not applied everything, it keeps the logs and fails the drop step, naming the file and position. This is safe for other runs on the shared servers, because only logs the replica has applied go and the current file stays. The drop step names each failed part by a label, because the commands carry DSNs, and it prints no DSN. The fake programs of `scripts/check/continue.test.mjs` quote the failing pattern, which a pattern with spaces and backquotes broke. Red: with the `databases.sh` of main, one create and drop left the primary at 5 files of 4,688,489,440 bytes after 4,511,362,870 before, and the replica at 4,688,585,640 after 4,511,458,821. Green: the new case "dropping the run databases purges the binary logs that the replica applied" passes; it runs the purge in order against a fake mysql and keeps the logs, with exit 1 and the reason, when `SOURCE_POS_WAIT` returns NULL. One create and drop with the new script left 1 file of 158 bytes on the primary and 1 of 317 bytes on the replica, the replica still ran both threads with no error, and the server directories went from about 4.5 GB each to 567 MB and 551 MB. The drop step of each later `make owner-check` printed `binary logs purged up to binlog.000014`.
- [o] G5.56 Let no ended holder block a lease or a lock. Cause: the CI run of G5.53 failed `conformance-result-check/typescript` with exit 3, `lease: refused the exclusive lease of .runtime/typescript.leases; held by: shared lease of pid 128962 (dead) … decimal-physical-check`; 22 s earlier the same lease was reported running. A `lease hold` lease is removed by the releaser process (`release-on-exit`) after it sees its holder end, so after a holder that ends normally the file stays until the releaser removes it. A waiter that woke in that interval saw the holder gone and the file present, called it dead and refused at once, because a dead lease was never taken over and `--wait` did not wait for it. The same rule also left a lease of a killed holder blocking every later run until someone ran `clear-dead`. Two other locks had the same defect: the conformance check locked a bench database with `mkdir /tmp/orm-conformance-<hash>.lock`, and `scripts/decimal-bench-sqlite.sh` with `mkdir .runtime/decimal-bench.lock`; a run that ended without removing its directory blocked every later run. Every other lease user (`CARGO_LEASED`, `HOLD_TYPESCRIPT`, `READ_TYPESCRIPT`, `WITH_TEST_ENV`, the runner's and owner-check's holds, `cargo-test`, the interface and conformance builds, `test-servers.sh`) goes through `tests/lease`, so the fix there covers them. Change: the releaser records its pid in the lease file before `hold` returns, so a lease whose holder ended while its releaser runs is being released. A waiting request then waits for a change of the directory or the end of the releaser, and `lease list` shows it as `ended, releaser <pid> running`. A lease whose holder and releaser both ended is dead and blocks no one. A dead shared lease is taken over by any request, because its holder only read the resource. A dead exclusive lease is taken over by the next exclusive request, which makes the resource again. A shared request is refused while a dead exclusive lease remains, with the reason that its holder may have left the resource half made. Each takeover is reported on stderr. The conformance check holds an `flock` of `orm-conformance-<hash>.lock` in the temporary directory, which the operating system releases however the process ends, and it checks after locking that the path still names the locked file, because a releasing run removes the file before it unlocks. A refusal names the holder's pid. `decimal-bench-sqlite.sh` prepares under an exclusive lease of `.runtime/decimal-bench.leases` (`lease run --wait`). CONTRIBUTING.md and its Korean translation state the rules. Red: with the lease program of main, the new cases of `scripts/test-servers.test.mjs` failed. A waiter facing an ended holder whose releaser still ran was refused at once. A dead shared lease refused an exclusive request. `hold` wrote no releaser into the lease file. The new Go test `TestLockOfAnEndedRunDoesNotHold`, with the `main.go` of main, failed with `mkdir /tmp/orm-conformance-ad16a2804aebc7ba.lock: file exists` after a run ended without releasing. The `decimal-bench-sqlite.sh` of main, with the directory a killed preparation leaves, exited 1 with `decimal SQLite bench preparation is already running`. Green: the waiter waits while the releaser runs and takes the lease over when the releaser ends without removing it. A dead shared lease is taken over by an exclusive request. A dead exclusive lease refuses a shared request with the reason and is taken over by an exclusive request. `hold` records a live releaser, which removes the lease when the holder ends. The conformance lock of an ended run no longer holds, and a running holder's lock refuses with its pid. `make decimal-bench-sqlite` with a dead exclusive lease reported `lease: took over the dead exclusive lease of pid 50342` and prepared the database. `make owner-check` over the 8 changed paths passed its 13 steps, among them `test-servers-check`, `full-run-check`, `repo-check`, the `decimal-bench-sqlite` helper and the verification of `conformance_verification`.
  - [o] G5.56-1 Start the lock helper test with the shared testcase package. Cause: `TestLockHelper` of `tests/conformance/check/lock_test.go`, added by G5.56, skipped or ran without `testcase.Start`, so `make repo-check` failed on main with `TestLockHelper does not start its case with internal/testcase, so it runs without a deadline or RUN line`. The owner-check of G5.56 passed because the repository check reads tracked files and the test file was not yet tracked then. Change: the helper starts its case with `testcase.Start` (Compute), and the test that runs it reads the helper's output line by line until `locked`, because the helper now prints its RUN line first. Red: on main, the repository case "every Go and Rust test starts its case with the shared testcase package" failed with that line. Green: it passes, and `TestLockOfAnEndedRunDoesNotHold` passes.
- [o] G5.57 Keep the errors that a FAIL line points at as first failure lines. Cause: in the CI run of G5.53, `performance_gate/preempted-timing` failed with `FAIL TestStressDocument elapsed=10.25s: the errors reported above`, and neither the summary nor the report said what those errors were. The Go testcase writes a failure as `    stress_test.go:80: <message>` and then only that FAIL line. The first failure lines took only failure and error-level lines, and the report's target log keeps its first and last part, so the reported line was in neither. Change: `failures()` of `scripts/check/report.mjs` keeps the source-location report lines (`<file>.go|rs|php|mjs|js|ts:<line>: <message>`) of the current case, reading through the lane prefix and the nested `STEP <case> elapsed=…: ` prefixes of a runner inside a runner. It forgets them when a case starts or passes, and puts them as first failure lines right before a failure line that ends with `the errors reported above`. They go into the run record and the summary, which the log cap does not shorten. Red: with the `report.mjs` of main, the new case "a FAIL that points at the errors reported above keeps those errors as first failure lines", on the nested lines of that run, found only the FAIL line. Green: the first failure lines are the `stress_test.go:80` report and then the FAIL line, without the log line of a case that passed before. The earlier first-failure-line cases pass, and `make owner-check` over the changed paths passed `full-run-check` and `repo-check`.
- [o] G5.58 Measure each stress parse against a reference workload of the same machine, printed and not yet asserted. Cause: the CI run of G5.53 failed the PHP stress parse at a median of 410.754 ms and, preempted, 444.392 ms of process CPU (`getrusage` user and system time, `clients/php/tests/case_clock.php`) against its 400 ms budget, and the same machine gave no evidence whether the budget or the code was wrong. The four budgets of docs/dbspec.md are absolute times fixed from the first measurement on the development machine, which measures 300 to 349 ms for the same parse. A budget that holds on one machine and fails on another states nothing about the parser. A ratio to a fixed workload measured by the same clock in the same process would cancel the speed of the machine, if that ratio is stable across machines. Change: the Go, PHP, TypeScript and Rust stress tests also time a reference workload five times: one pass over the bytes of the same stress document computing `h = h * 31 + b (mod 2^32)`, a sequential loop that no compiler can parallelize. They print its median CPU time and the ratio of the median parse to it, and assert nothing new. The budgets stay as they are until CI has printed its ratios. Red: the stress tests of main print no reference time and no ratio. Green: `make dbspec-stress-bench` passed and printed, on the development machine (two runs each), the reference median and ratio. Go: 2.106 and 2.561 ms, ratio 26.70 and 24.97 (parse 56.2 and 63.9 ms). PHP: 38.145 and 47.359 ms, ratio 8.24 and 6.35 (parse 314.3 and 300.6 ms). TypeScript: 2.282 and 2.509 ms, ratio 52.38 and 49.57 (parse 119.5 and 124.4 ms). Rust: 2.494 and 2.741 ms, ratio 23.98 and 19.87 (parse 59.8 and 54.4 ms). The checksum is 3992179881 in all four, so they did the same work. The ratio varies by up to 23 % between two runs on one machine (PHP, Rust), which bounds how tight a ratio budget can be.
- [o] G5.59 Drop the databases of a killed run before a run makes its own. Cause: a run drops its bench and decimal databases in its last step, which a killed run never reaches; the shared MySQL and PostgreSQL test servers held `orm_owner_81522_19e14363_bench` and `_decimal` from such a run, and nothing removed them. Change: `scripts/check/databases.sh create` first runs `drop_dead_runs` (also the action `drop-dead`). It lists the databases of both servers and drops each `orm_owner_<pid>_…` and `orm_check_<pid>_…` bench or decimal database whose process no longer runs on this host (`ps -p`), naming each. The test servers listen on 127.0.0.1 only, so every run is a process of this host. Databases of live runs, CI names (`ci_<id>`) and other databases stay. Red: the `databases.sh` of main has no such step; the new case "a run first drops the databases of runs whose process no longer runs" failed with its usage line. Green: against fake mysql and psql listing seven databases, it drops the three of the ended pid on each server and keeps the live run's database, the CI database and `orm_test`; the other cases of `scripts/check/continue.test.mjs` pass.
- [o] G5.60 Refuse an `os.Exit` reached through a helper after a `defer`. Cause: the rule of G5.55 refused only a direct `os.Exit` after a `defer` in the same function. Five programs reached it through a helper of the same package after `defer db.Close()`: `bench/install` (`fail`), `examples/complex/go` and `examples/thin-slice/go` (`check`, `dsn`), `tests/conformance/runner_go` (`check`) and `tests/dbspec/introspect/go` (`fail`). A failed step there ended the process without closing the connection, and in the examples, which show users how to write a program, the same pattern was taught. Change: `deferredExitErrors` of `scripts/repo/gosource.mjs` treats a function of the same package (directory) that calls `os.Exit`, directly or through such a function, as exiting, and refuses a call to it after a `defer`, naming the helper. The five programs end with `os.Exit(run())`. Their helpers panic with an exit code (`type exit int`) that `run` recovers into its result, so the deferred close runs, and a usage error inside `run` returns its code. Red: the widened rule found 18 calls in those five programs on main. Green: the new case with a `check` that calls a `fail` of another file of the package is refused with `main calls check, which calls os.Exit, after a defer`, and the tree has none. Each program still exits 2 on a usage error and 1 with its message on a failed connection or a missing `ORM_BENCH_MYSQL_DSN`.
- [o] G5.61 Check the process group of every process that a script starts in a group of its own. Cause: G5.55 checks the process group of each step, but a process that starts a group of its own leaves the step's group, so the step does not see what that group leaves. Five scripts start their commands with `spawn(…, { detached: true })` so that a deadline can kill the whole group: `scripts/decimal-physical-check.mjs`, `scripts/group-rows-physical-check.mjs`, `scripts/unselected-column-physical-check.mjs`, `scripts/features/coverage.mjs` and `tests/timing/preempted.test.mjs`. They killed the group only on a deadline or an output limit, so after a normal exit any process left in it ran on unseen. Change: `endGroup` of `scripts/check/step.mjs` lists the processes left in a group after its leader exits, ends them (TERM, then KILL after 3 s) and returns them. The five scripts call it when the command exits and fail the command with `<program> left N processes: <pid> <command>` when it returns any. `make repo-check` refuses a JavaScript file that starts a process with `detached: true` and never checks the group (`detachedGroupErrors` of `scripts/repo/gosource.mjs`), and it ignores the words in comments and string literals. The releaser of `tests/lease` is outside this rule, because it is meant to outlive its parent until the holder ends. Red: the new rule found the five scripts on main. Green: the rule finds none in the tree and refuses its fixture without `endGroup`. The new case "endGroup names and ends what a process of its own group left behind" gets `<pid> sleep 39` for a group whose leader exited while a background `sleep 39` ran, the sleep is ended, and a second call returns nothing. The step cases and `scripts/features/coverage.test.mjs` give the same results as on main.
- [o] G5.62 Start the test servers outside the caller's process group. Cause: `make test-servers` started PostgreSQL, its replica, ProxySQL and PgBouncer through `start_logged` of `scripts/test-servers.sh` with their log reader shells in the process group of the shell that ran make (all in pgid 75915 on 10-06; only the mysqld pair, which daemonizes, had groups of its own). Ending that group, as an interrupt in a terminal, a CI step kill or a tool that ends its command's group does, also ended those servers, which other runs share. Change: `start_logged` runs each server and its log reader through `tests/new-session` (`NEW_SESSION`, built by make like `STOP_PROCESS`), which opens a new session (`setsid`) and then runs the command, so the servers and readers run outside the starting command's process group; the ready handshake through the FIFO is unchanged. `make test-servers` and `make repo-check` build it. Red: with the `test-servers.sh` of main, the start_logged case of `scripts/repo/check.test.mjs` failed with `the server runs in the process group of the command that started it`. Green: the case passes, the fake server's process group differs from the test's, and the repository tests pass (66 cases); the real servers start in CI.
- [o] G5.63 Lock the table before a PostgreSQL insert publishes Locked. Cause: the CI run of G5.54 failed `catalog_connection/catalog-rust` with `generated identity failures: ["postgres: generated insert must lock the table before publishing Locked"]` at `orm-build/tests/row_insert_identity.rs:109:5`; the same case passed in the run of G5.53. On PostgreSQL, `insert_row` of `clients/rust/orm-build/src/catalog/row_insert.rs` took no lock of its own before publishing `MutationPhase::Locked`: the schema check (`metadata::describe`) reads only the catalogs, and whether its `pg_get_expr` held a lock on the table was a side effect, so a concurrent `LOCK TABLE … ACCESS EXCLUSIVE NOWAIT` sometimes succeeded, and a schema change could land between the check and the write. MySQL already takes its lock explicitly (`SELECT 1 … FOR UPDATE`). Change: on PostgreSQL the insert first runs `LOCK TABLE <table> IN ROW EXCLUSIVE MODE` (`postgres_insert_lock`), the lock an INSERT takes and one that conflicts with every schema change, before it checks the schema and publishes `Locked`. Red: on main the PostgreSQL insert held no explicit lock, and CI found the probe's lock granted. Green: the new unit case `a_postgres_insert_takes_the_insert_lock_of_its_table` pins the statement, quoting included; `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy -p orm-build --features live-db --lib --tests -D warnings` pass. The database case runs in CI.
- [o] G5.64 Require no local end-to-end check before a push. Cause: AGENTS.md required `make owner-check` before each commit, during an item allowed the Red/Green tests together with `make owner-check`, and asked to verify each `linux-runner` verification locally before a push. Those runs start real database servers, builds and suites on the development machine, which CI runs after every push anyway, and each of them held up the work for minutes. Change: AGENTS.md and its Korean translation state that during development only unit tests run, the Red/Green unit cases of what changed. End-to-end runs (real database servers, conformance across languages, browsers, containers, full builds), `make owner-check` and the full suite run in CI after the push, and no local check is required before a push. `make owner-check` stays a tool that no rule requires. A changed file's readers are run as their unit tests, and the `linux-runner` verifications run in CI. Red: the texts named `make owner-check` before each commit and a local `linux-runner` run before a push. Green: neither remains a requirement, and `node scripts/docs/rules.mjs` passes on both translations.
  - [o] G5.64-1 Check the new wording of the full-suite rule. Cause: `make repo-check` requires AGENTS.md and its translation to state the full-suite rule by fixed phrases (`FULL_SUITE_RULE` of `scripts/repo/ci.mjs`), among them "is no required step before a push" and "push 전에 필요한 단계가 아니다". G5.64 rewrote that sentence without updating the phrases, so main failed `make repo-check` with `AGENTS.md does not state the full-suite rule`; the docs-only commit ran only the document rules. Change: the rule requires "no local check is required" and "push 전에 필요한 로컬 검사는 없다", the wording G5.64 introduced. Red: on main the case "AGENTS states that the full suite runs on CI after a push" and the repository run failed on both translations. Green: both pass.
- [o] G5.65 Run every check offline and download only through `make install`. Cause: checks reached the network in the middle of a run. `rust-fetch`, a prerequisite of 20 Rust targets, ran `cargo fetch` online inside `make check`; `go test` and `go run` downloaded missing modules from the default proxy; `scripts/typescript/node-min.sh` downloaded the lowest Node with curl during `ts-min-check`; and nothing told a run apart that found its downloads in a cache from one that fetched them, so the same tree could pass or fail by what a registry answered. A missing crate failed with cargo's "retry without the offline flag". Change: the Makefile exports `CARGO_NET_OFFLINE=true`, `GOPROXY=off`, `npm_config_offline=true` and `COMPOSER_DISABLE_NETWORK=1`, and `ONLINE` unsets them for the download targets only: `make install` with `install-node`, `install-php`, `install-rust` (`rustup toolchain install` and `cargo fetch --locked` for the three Cargo.lock files), `install-go` (`go mod download`) and `install-node-min`. `scripts/check/downloads.mjs` checks without network that the crates (`cargo fetch --locked --offline`), the Go modules (`go mod download` under `GOPROXY=off`), the npm packages of both package-lock.json files and the Composer packages of composer.lock are present at their locked versions, and names each missing one with `run make install, which downloads it`, never with a retry online. The runner records it as the setup step `downloads`, and a target that needs a missing download (`rust`, `go`, `node-modules`, `composer`) is not run, with that reason. `make owner-check` stops with the same messages. `cargo-downloads-check` replaces `rust-fetch` as the prerequisite of the Rust targets, and `node-min.sh` downloads only under `make install-node-min`. CI downloads in its setup steps (`make install-rust`, a `go-modules` step with `make install-go`, `make install-node-min`), and the repository server-order rule allows `make install` targets before the servers. `make repo-check` refuses a Makefile without the four offline settings, a download command outside an install target and `$(ONLINE)` outside one (`offlineMakeErrors`). AGENTS.md, CONTRIBUTING.md and docs/docs-development.md with their translations state `make install` and the offline rule. Red: on main the Makefile exported none of the four settings and `rust-fetch` ran `cargo fetch` online, which the new rule names. Green: the four new cases of `scripts/check/downloads.test.mjs` pass: lock files against installed versions, a missing crate and Go module named with make install and no retry, `node-min.sh` refusing to download outside `install-node-min`, and the Makefile rule. The runner, continue, step, repository and owners unit tests pass (30, 9, 6, 65 and 19 cases), the check finds every download present in the main checkout, and it names the missing npm and Composer packages in a fresh worktree.
  - [o] G5.64-2 State in CONTRIBUTING.md that checks beyond unit tests run in CI. Cause: G5.64 rewrote AGENTS.md, but CONTRIBUTING.md and its translation still told a contributor to "run the relevant local checks" and to "run the full suite `make check` once, when every active checklist item is complete". Change: step 5 runs the unit tests of what changed, its Red/Green cases, with end-to-end runs, `make owner-check` and the full suite in CI after the push and no local check required before a push; step 6 states that `make check` runs in CI after every push, with its refusals as before. Red: the two sentences stood in both translations. Green: neither remains, and the document rules and the repository check (65 cases) pass.
- [o] G5.66 Run every CI step through a make target. Cause: the workflows ran commands that no make target ran, so CI did things a local run could not repeat and no rule checked. `ci.yml` ran `npm ci` twice and `npx playwright install`, `rustup toolchain install`, an inline `echo version=…` for the lowest PHP, an inline `php -r` SQLite probe, `composer install` and a 15-line apt, curl and AppArmor script for the database server programs; `docs-pages.yml` ran `npm ci` and `npx playwright install`. Change: those steps run `make install-node`, `make install-node-min`, `make install-browsers`, `make install-rust` (which runs `rustup toolchain install` and prints `rustc --version`), `make --no-print-directory ci-php-min-version >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"`, `make ci-php-sqlite` (`scripts/ci/php-sqlite.php`), `make install-php` and `make install-server-programs` (`scripts/ci/server-programs.sh`), and the node, browser and lowest-Node installs are separate setup steps (`node-modules`, `node-min`, `browsers`), each declared in `scripts/check/ci-setup.mjs`. `make repo-check` refuses a workflow step that runs anything but a make target (`ciMakeErrors` of `scripts/repo/ci.mjs`), except the `summary` step of `ci.yml`, which runs `node scripts/check/summary.mjs` also after a runner that did not finish and is named in `CI_RUN_EXCEPTIONS`. The server-order and rerun rules allow the declared CI setup steps, which install and probe and run no test, before the servers and outside `make check`. The PHP and Rust toolchain rules read the minimum PHP step and `rustup toolchain install` through those targets. The two new scripts are declared with scope `suite`. Red: the new rule found 24 commands outside make in the two workflows of main. Green: the new case "every CI step runs a make target" passes on its fixtures and finds nothing in the tree, the repository check passes (66 cases), and `make ci-php-min-version` prints `version=8.4`.
- [o] G5.67 Leave a report of the documentation workflow. Cause: `docs-pages.yml` ran `make docs-build`, `make docs-verify-idempotent` and `make docs-static-check` as plain steps, kept nothing but the job log, and wrote no summary, so a failure left no report to read and a step skipped after a failed one said nothing about why. Change: one step runs `make docs-ci`, which runs the three targets through the runner of `make check` (`scripts/check/run.mjs`) without test servers (`-`). The runner then has no server or database step, records a target that needs a database as not-run with `this run has no database servers`, checks only the downloads that its targets need, and runs every target after a failed one. It writes the log of each target and a summary to `.runtime/check/ci_<run id>_<attempt>_docs/report` and, for a run without a full-run record, also to the job summary. A `report` step uploads that directory under `!cancelled()`, and the pages artifact is uploaded only when the documentation checks passed. Red: the workflow of main kept no report, and the runner required a servers file. Green: the new case "a run without servers runs the targets that need no database and reports them" runs `docs-a`, records `db-b` as not-run with that reason, leaves out the server and database steps, keeps the downloads step passing although a crate its targets do not need is missing, and writes the summary and the target log. The runner (31 cases) and repository (66 cases) tests pass.
- [o] G5.68 Assert behaviour, not wall time, in two tests that broke under load. Cause: two tests bounded wall-clock time from above, which a loaded machine exceeds without any defect. `clients/typescript/tests/model.mjs` required a statement cancelled while it waited for a row lock to return within 4000 ms, and the start_logged case of `scripts/repo/check.test.mjs` required `start_logged` to return within 10 s and the slow server to be ready only after 2.5 s. The requirement is not a time in either: the statement must end because of the signal, and `start_logged` must return after the ready line and before the server ends. Change: the cancellation case checks that the statement returns `CANCELED` and that the signal had been aborted, while the other connection still holds the row, so neither a released lock nor a lock wait timeout could have ended it. The start_logged case checks that the fake server still runs when `start_logged` returns, and the slow server creates a marker file right before its ready line, which must exist when `start_logged` returns. The lower bounds that remain (a wait past a 200 ms bound, two builds through a slow go) do not break under load. Red: the two upper bounds of main. Green: the start_logged case passes on its behaviour, and the TypeScript case runs in CI.
  - [o] G5.63-1 Keep the PostgreSQL insert lock out of the Rust interface and declare its unit test. Cause: two CI runs failed `conformance-check` and `interface_contract` with `rust: unexpected clients/rust/orm-build/src/catalog/row_insert.rs::postgres_insert_lock`, because G5.63 declared the helper `pub(super)` and the interface check lists every non-private symbol against `contracts/interfaces.json`; and `feature-unit-check` failed `every tracked file selects a behaviour test or declares its scope` for `clients/rust/orm-build/tests/unit/row_insert_lock.rs`. Change: `postgres_insert_lock` is private to `row_insert`, whose child test module still reaches it, and the unit test file is an input of `catalog_connection` and of its `catalog-lib-rust` verification, beside the other catalog unit tests. Red: the two failures of those runs. Green: the unit case passes with the private helper, `node scripts/features/owners.mjs --list` selects `catalog-lib-rust` for the file, and the owners tests pass (19 cases).
- [o] G5.69 Remove what the documentation browser leaves in the temporary directory. Cause: a CI run failed `docs-check` with `check: docs-check left 1 temporary entries: .org.chromium.Chromium.1Yiv3c`. Headless Chromium creates `.org.chromium.Chromium.*` in TMPDIR and, on Linux, sometimes leaves it after `browser.close()`; since G5.55 a passed step that leaves an entry fails. Four scripts of `scripts/docs` launched Chromium with the step's TMPDIR. Change: `launchBrowser` of `scripts/docs/browser.mjs` launches Chromium with a temporary directory of its own as TMPDIR and removes it when the browser closes, also after a failed close, and `check.mjs`, `prepare.mjs`, `load.test.mjs` and `steps.test.mjs` launch through it. `npm run docs:static-check` runs the new `browser.test.mjs`. Red: on main the four scripts called `chromium.launch` with the step's TMPDIR, which the new case refuses, and CI found the leftover. Green: the new case "the documentation browser gets a temporary directory of its own, removed when it closes" passes against a fake Chromium that leaves a `.org.chromium.Chromium.left` entry, and no script of `scripts/docs` launches Chromium directly. The real browser runs in CI.
- [o] G5.70 Wait for this case's own lock wait before cancelling in the Go cancellation tests. Cause: a CI run failed `TestWithContextCancelsInsideTransaction/postgres/nested=true` with `blocked read: CANCELED: timeout: context already done: context canceled (code "CANCELED"), want ROLLBACK`. The case cancels its context once `awaitLockWait` sees a session of the case database waiting for a lock, which it counted with `count(*)` over all waiting sessions. The statement that the `nested=false` case had cancelled could still wait on the server after its connection closed, until the holder released the row, so the next case found that session, cancelled its context before its own statement started, and the statement ended as `context already done` with no rollback to fail. Change: the case reads the waiting sessions (`pg_stat_activity` pids, `performance_schema.data_locks` thread ids) before its statement starts, and `awaitLockWait` returns only when a session that was not among them waits (`newWaiter`); the other cancellation case does the same. Red: the run above; with the count of main, a session that waited before counts as this case's statement. Green: the new unit case `TestNewWaiterIgnoresSessionsThatAlreadyWaited` pins that a session waiting before is ignored and a new one is found, and `go vet` passes; the database cases run in CI.
- [o] G5.71 Leave out the failure lines of a case that passed. Cause: in a CI run the first failure lines of `feature-unit-check` were `FAIL sample/owner/typescript/none run 1 …: unexpected test output`, `duplicate observed case first` and more. Those lines are the output of samples that a case of `scripts/features/coverage.test.mjs` makes fail on purpose, and that case passed; the real failure, `every tracked file selects a behaviour test or declares its scope`, came after them and fell outside the 20 first failure lines, so the summary named no real cause. Change: `failures()` of `scripts/check/report.mjs` records, for each case, how many failure lines there were at its RUN line, and when the case ends with PASS it drops the lines collected since, because they are that case's output and not a failure of the target. Lines are collected without the limit and cut to the first 20 only when they are read, so a dropped block no longer pushes a later failure out. Red: with the `report.mjs` of main, the new case "the failure lines of a case that passed are not the first failure lines of the target" got the 25 sample lines in place of the real failure. Green: its only first failure line is the FAIL line of the tracked-file case, and the earlier first-failure-line cases pass (32 cases).
- [o] G5.72 Probe a catalog mutation's locks from inside its Locked publisher. Cause: a CI run failed `native_updates_lock_compare_verify_and_rollback` with `update completed before held-lock probe: true` at `orm-build/tests/row_mutation.rs:137`. The test drove the update's future until its publisher reported `Locked` and then stopped polling it, to probe the row lock from another connection; the identity test of `row_insert_identity.rs` probed the table lock the same way. Stopping the polls does not stop a future that needs no more polls: when the server answers each statement before the client reads (a loaded runner, a local server), the update reads every answer within the same poll and runs to its commit before the test gets to probe. Change: the probes run from the publisher itself at `Locked`, on a thread and a connection of their own (`tests/lock_probe/mod.rs`: `row_lock_held`, `table_lock_held`), and the publisher returns only after the probe; the mutation is thereby stopped exactly at `Locked` whatever the scheduling. After the probe the publisher sets the cancellation flag, so the mutation ends with `JOB_CANCELLED` and rolls back, which leaves the rows as the rest of each test expects. The new module is an input of the two catalog verifications. Red: the run above. Green: the new unit case `a_probe_inside_the_locked_publisher_sees_the_mutation_at_locked`, with a fake mutation that publishes `Locked` and finishes within its first poll, sees the mutation at `Locked`; `cargo clippy -p orm-build --features live-db --tests -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` pass. The database cases run in CI.
- [o] G5.74 Print an allocating reference beside the Go stress parse. Cause: the checksum reference of G5.58 does not track the Go parse across machines: its ratio was 25.0 and 26.7 on the development machine and 38.7 to 43.0 on the CI runner, where a CI run measured the parse at 102 ms against the 100 ms budget. A byte loop measures the speed of one tight loop, while the parse slices strings, allocates and hashes, and the two scale differently from one processor to another. Change: `TestStressDocument` of `engine/dbspec/stress_test.go` also times, five times on the locked thread's CPU clock, an allocating reference: split the document into lines and words and count each word in a map (`allocatingReference`, 22066 distinct words). It prints its median CPU time and the parse's ratio to it, and asserts nothing new, until the CI runner shows whether that ratio holds. Red: the test of main prints only the checksum reference. Green: the test passes and prints `stress allocating reference cpu median=… ratio=… words=22066`; on the development machine five runs gave ratios 5.26, 5.89, 4.11, 5.33 and 5.03 (allocating reference 10.2 to 13.7 ms), beside checksum ratios 25.4 to 30.9.
- [o] G5.73 Measure and report performance, and never fail a test on it. Cause: tests failed on measured time. The four stress tests failed when the median parse CPU time exceeded an absolute budget (Go 100 ms, PHP 400 ms, TypeScript 250 ms, Rust 300 ms); the PHP and Go budgets failed on the CI runner at 386–444 ms and 88–102 ms with no change in the parser; 20 Rust assertions and 5 PHP checks failed when a case's CPU time exceeded a limit; `make perf-check` failed when a hot-path ratio exceeded its bound; the Go introspection comparison failed above 5 s; and two Go cancellation tests failed when the cancelled statement returned after 4 s or 10 s. A reference-ratio budget was tried on the way: on macOS the PHP ratio moved from 6.35 to 10.1 and the Rust ratio from 17.3 to 24.0 across runs, because performance and efficiency cores change both times, while the CI runner gave 6.84–7.16 and 21.8–22.5. Performance is measured and reported and never fails a test; a warning is allowed. Change: each stress test prints the CPU and wall-clock time of each parse, the median, the median CPU time of the checksum reference, the ratio and the machine, and prints a `WARNING` line with the measurement, the reference and the machine when the ratio exceeds its reference value: 11 for PHP, 34 for Rust, 63 for Go and 185 for TypeScript, 1.5 times the highest ratio measured on the CI runner, so a warning marks a regression of about 50% or more. The CPU limits of the Rust and PHP cases, `cpuCaseEnd`, the Rust stress run, the hot-path gates of Go and PHP and the introspection comparison print a warning instead of failing; the two Go cancellation tests print the duration, since the statement returning `CANCELED` while the rows are still held already shows that the cancellation ended it; and the case-clock test compares its CPU time with its own wall-clock time. The testcase libraries gain `warning` (JavaScript, Rust), `Warn` (Go) and `testcase_warning` (PHP). The check runner keeps `WARNING` lines out of the first failure lines, records them per step, lists them in the summary under `warnings` and writes them as GitHub `::warning::` annotations. `make repo-check` refuses a test that fails on a measured time above a bound (`timeFailureErrors`: an `if` that fails when a measured time exceeds a fixed bound, or an assertion that a measured time stays below one; lower bounds, comparisons of two measurements and comparisons with 0 are correctness). The allocating reference of G5.74 is removed. docs/dbspec.md, docs/perf.md, docs/dialects.md, the performance_gate feature and AGENTS.md, with their translations, state the rule, the reference values and why macOS only prints. Red: the repository rule found 38 time failures on main, and on main the PHP stress test failed on the CI runner at 406–444 ms. Green: the rule finds none in the tree; with its reference forced to 1, the PHP stress test printed `WARNING dbspec_stress median parse CPU of 248.765 ms is 9.22 times the reference CPU of 26.986 ms, above the reference ratio 1.0 …` and passed; the Go and Rust stress tests pass and print their ratios (26.93, 18.44); the new cases "a WARNING line is kept as a warning, not a failure, and the summary lists it" and "a test does not fail on a measured time above a bound" pass; `go vet ./...`, `cargo clippy --workspace --tests --examples -D warnings` and the runner (33) and repository (66) tests pass.
- [o] G5.75 Let a new push cancel the run of the push before it. Cause: `ci.yml` declared no concurrency and `docs-pages.yml` kept its runs with `cancel-in-progress: false`, so every push started a run that went on after a newer push; with few runners, superseded runs of the same branch held them while only the newest head's run counts. Change: both workflows declare `concurrency: { group: ${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}, cancel-in-progress: true }`, and `make repo-check` refuses a workflow that runs on push without that workflow-level group (`concurrencyErrors` of `scripts/repo/ci.mjs`). Red: the new rule found `ci.yml` and `docs-pages.yml` of main. Green: the new case "every workflow that runs on push cancels the run of the push before it" passes on its fixtures (a workflow without the group or with `cancel-in-progress: false` is refused, one without push is not checked) and finds nothing in the tree, and the repository check passes.
- [o] G5.76 Record the PostgreSQL insert lock in the Rust symbol inventory and format the introspection test. Cause: the CI run of G5.75 failed three targets. `conformance-check` and the `interface_contract` coverage reported `rust: unexpected clients/rust/orm-build/src/catalog/row_insert.rs::postgres_insert_lock`: the Rust symbol extractor of `tests/interfaces/rust` lists every top-level function whatever its visibility, so making the helper private in G5.63-1 did not remove it, and the inventory `contracts/symbols/rust.json` lacked it. `go-fmt-check` failed on `tests/dialects/introspect_compare_physical_test.go`, whose new constant block G5.73 left unformatted. Change: the inventory records the helper (`go run ./tests/interfaces/check -language rust -record`), `contracts/interfaces.json` carries the new hash of the inventory, and the test file is formatted with gofmt. Before each commit the Go files are now checked with `gofmt -l`. Red: the run above. Green: `go run ./tests/interfaces/check -language rust` passes with 2781 native symbols, the Go, PHP and TypeScript interface checks pass, and `gofmt -l` lists no file.
- [o] G5.77 Keep large test copies out of memory, guard group signals and stream the runner's resources. Cause: the second attempt of the CI run of G5.76 lost its hosted runner about 52 minutes into `make check`, with no summary or report uploaded; GitHub names a process that ends the runner, CPU or memory starvation or a blocked network. Two changes of this series could do it. (b) Since G5.55, `tests/cargo-test.mjs` copied every test binary it runs into the step's TMPDIR under `/tmp`, which on the CI runner is a tmpfs of 7.8 GiB in memory, so up to 19 Rust test binaries per verification lived in RAM beside the database servers, replicas, ProxySQL, PgBouncer and parallel builds on a 16 GB machine. (a) The group signals of G5.55 and G5.61 send `kill(-group)`; nothing refused a group of 0 or 1 or the runner's own group, which would end the runner and its job. No log showed which, because the job log stopped without resource lines. Change: `tests/cargo-test.mjs` makes its run directory in the checkout's `.runtime/run` on disk, named after the step (`ORM_STEP`, which `runStep` now passes), and `runStep` removes a run directory named after its step as that step's leftover, so a killed copy is still removed. `signalGroup` of `scripts/check/step.mjs` refuses a group of 1 or less, the runner's own pid and its own process group, prints the refusal, and records every signal it sends. `scripts/check/resources.mjs` prints, every 30 s while the runner runs and at its start, `RESOURCES` lines to the job log: `free -m`, `df -h / /tmp`, the five processes with the largest RSS with their process groups, and the group signals sent since the last lines; the streamed log survives a lost runner. Red: on main the test copies went to `/tmp` and `signalGroup` had no guard; the control of the new case shows a raw signal to the runner's own group ends a fake runner with SIGTERM. Green: the new cases pass: "a group signal never reaches the process group of the runner" (a fake runner in its own group refuses five signals, its own group, 0, 1, -5 and its pid, runs a step that leaves a background process, and stays alive), "a run directory named after the step is a leftover of the step" and "the resource lines list the processes with the largest resident memory"; the cargo-test (7), step (9) and runner (33) tests pass. The next CI run shows the resource lines.
- [o] G5.78 Find test files from the working directory, not through runtime.Caller. Cause: engine/dbspec/harness_test.go (`repositoryRoot`) and tests/interfaces/check/grouped_contract_test.go found the repository through `runtime.Caller(0)`, which gives the source path compiled into the binary, not the checkout that runs it: a module path under `-trimpath`, and the directory of another checkout for a binary built there. Evidence: both tests take the working directory (`os.Getwd`), which go test sets to the package directory, and go up to the root; `callerPathErrors` of scripts/repo/gosource.mjs refuses a `runtime.Caller` or `runtime.Callers` call in a tracked Go file with its line and the fix, and the repository unit case found the two calls in the previous sources and none now. TestFileVectors, TestGetsCountUsesDedicatedGroupedRowsInEveryClient and make repo-check pass.
- [o] G5.79 Pin every toolchain the checks use. Cause: Node (`.node-version`) and Rust (`rust-toolchain.toml`) were exact and PHP (`.php-version`) was x.y, but CI installed the newest Go 1.27 (`go-version: "1.27"`), the newest Composer of setup-php, the newest libclang-dev and the newest PostgreSQL 17, and no check compared the Go or Composer that ran with a declaration, so a release of any of them changed the checks between two runs of the same commit. Evidence: `.go-version` (1.27.0) and `.composer-version` (2.10.3) declare the exact releases; ci.yml reads `go-version-file: .go-version` and every setup-php step installs `tools: composer:2.10.3`; `goVersionErrors` and `composerVersionErrors` of scripts/repo/toolchains.mjs refuse an inexact declaration, a go.mod `go` line of another x.y, a workflow that declares the release itself or omits it, and a running go or composer of another release, each with its fix (two new repository unit cases); scripts/ci/php-extension-tools.sh installs `libclang-21-dev`, the LLVM of Ubuntu 26.04, and names it in LIBCLANG_PATH; scripts/ci/server-programs.sh requires PostgreSQL 17 and prints the PgBouncer and SQLite releases. setup-php and the PostgreSQL apt repository offer only the newest patch of PHP x.y and PostgreSQL 17, so those stay pinned at x.y and the major release. make repo-check, full-run-check and feature-unit-check pass.
- [o] G5.80 Publish build outputs atomically: build into a temporary file or directory, then rename.
